--- SOURCE: rust_samples/arena.rs ---
// arena.rs
//
// A generic arena allocator: values live in a contiguous `Vec` of slots
// and are addressed by small, copyable *indices* instead of references.
// Indices stay valid while the arena lives (no borrow checker fights),
// freed slots are recycled, and each index carries a *generation* so a
// stale index left over from a previous occupant is detected instead of
// silently aliasing a different value.
//
// Design notes
// ------------
// * `Arena<T>` stores `Vec<Slot<T>>`; a slot is either occupied (with a
//   generation counter) or vacant (holding the next free slot's index,
//   forming an intrusive free list with no extra allocation).
// * `Index` is a plain `(u32 slot, u32 generation)` pair. It is `Copy`,
//   `Eq`, `Hash` and `Ord`, so it works as a `HashMap` key and can be
//   stored inside other arena values to build graphs, trees and entity
//   relationships without lifetimes or `Rc`.
// * Generations only ever increase, so a reused slot never matches an
//   old index. `Arena::get` returns `None` for a stale index.
// * No `unsafe` anywhere: the arena is safe Rust throughout. The only
//   cost is the bounds check on every access, which the compiler usually
//   folds away when the index was just produced by the arena itself.
// * Iteration yields `(Index, &T)` pairs in slot order, skipping vacant
//   slots; `iter_mut` and `retain` are provided too.
//
// The demo in `main` builds a tiny entity graph (a family tree) inside
// one arena, exercises removal and slot reuse, and proves stale indices
// are rejected. Unit tests cover insertion, removal, reuse, iteration,
// draining and the generation discipline.

use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::fmt;
use std::hash::Hash;
use std::iter::FromIterator;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Index: a typed, generational handle into an arena
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A handle to a value stored in an `Arena<T>`.
///
/// `slot` is the position in the arena's slot vector; `generation` is a
/// counter that is bumped every time the slot is recycled. Two indices
/// are equal only when both parts match, so an index captured before a
/// value was removed will *not* compare equal to the index of whatever
/// later reuses that slot.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct Index {
    slot: u32,
    generation: u32,
}

impl Index {
    /// Build an index directly. Prefer `Arena::insert`, which is the
    /// only way to obtain an index that actually points at a value.
    pub fn new(slot: u32, generation: u32) -> Index {
        Index { slot, generation }
    }

    pub fn slot(self) -> u32 {
        self.slot
    }

    pub fn generation(self) -> u32 {
        self.generation
    }
}

impl fmt::Debug for Index {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "Index({}:{})", self.slot, self.generation)
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Index {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}:{}", self.slot, self.generation)
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Slots
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// One slot of the arena. Occupied slots hold a value and the generation
/// the value was inserted under; vacant slots hold the index of the next
/// vacant slot, threading the free list through the vector itself.
#[derive(Debug)]
enum Slot<T> {
    Occupied { value: T, generation: u32 },
    Vacant { next_free: Option<u32> },
}

impl<T> Slot<T> {
    fn is_occupied(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Slot::Occupied { .. })
    }

    fn generation(&self) -> Option<u32> {
        match self {
            Slot::Occupied { generation, .. } => Some(*generation),
            Slot::Vacant { .. } => None,
        }
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Arena
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A generational arena: a growable, index-addressed pool of values of
/// one type.
///
/// Typical use: game entities, graph nodes, syntax-tree nodes, or any
/// collection where values need stable, cheap handles that outlive
/// borrows of the collection itself.
///
/// # Example
/// ```
/// let mut arena = Arena::new();
/// let a = arena.insert(10);
/// let b = arena.insert(20);
/// assert_eq!(arena[a], 10);
/// arena.remove(a);
/// assert_eq!(arena.get(a), None); // stale index rejected
/// assert_eq!(arena[b], 20);
/// ```
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Arena<T> {
    slots: Vec<Slot<T>>,
    /// Head of the intrusive free list (`None` when no slot is free).
    free_head: Option<u32>,
    /// Number of occupied slots.
    len: usize,
    /// Total number of generations ever issued; used to keep the
    /// generation counter monotonic even across `clear`.
    generation_seed: u32,
}

impl<T> Default for Arena<T> {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Arena::new()
    }
}

impl<T> Arena<T> {
    /// Create an empty arena.
    pub fn new() -> Arena<T> {
        Arena {
            slots: Vec::new(),
            free_head: None,
            len: 0,
            generation_seed: 0,
        }
    }

    /// Create an empty arena with room for at least `capacity` values
    /// without reallocating.
    pub fn with_capacity(capacity: usize) -> Arena<T> {
        Arena {
            slots: Vec::with_capacity(capacity),
            free_head: None,
            len: 0,
            generation_seed: 0,
        }
    }

    /// Number of live values.
    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.len
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.len == 0
    }

    /// Number of slots allocated (live + vacant). This is the high-water
    /// mark of the arena's storage.
    pub fn capacity(&self) -> usize {
        self.slots.capacity()
    }

    /// Number of slots, live or vacant.
    pub fn slot_count(&self) -> usize {
        self.slots.len()
    }

    /// Number of vacant slots available for reuse.
    pub fn free_count(&self) -> usize {
        self.slots.len() - self.len
    }

    /// Insert a value, returning its index. Reuses a vacant slot when
    /// one exists (the free list is LIFO, so recently freed slots are
    /// reused first), otherwise pushes a new slot.
    pub fn insert(&mut self, value: T) -> Index {
        self.generation_seed = self.generation_seed.wrapping_add(1);
        let generation = self.generation_seed;
        match self.free_head {
            Some(slot) => {
                // Pop the free list head and occupy that slot.
                let next = match &self.slots[slot as usize] {
                    Slot::Vacant { next_free } => *next_free,
                    Slot::Occupied { .. } => {
                        unreachable!("free list must only contain vacant slots")
                    }
                };
                self.free_head = next;
                self.slots[slot as usize] = Slot::Occupied { value, generation };
                self.len += 1;
                Index::new(slot, generation)
            }
            None => {
                let slot = self.slots.len() as u32;
                self.slots.push(Slot::Occupied { value, generation });
                self.len += 1;
                Index::new(slot, generation)
            }
        }
    }

    /// Insert many values, returning their indices in order.
    pub fn insert_many<I: IntoIterator<Item = T>>(&mut self, values: I) -> Vec<Index> {
        values.into_iter().map(|v| self.insert(v)).collect()
    }

    /// Look up a value by index. Returns `None` when the slot is out of
    /// range, vacant, or the generation does not match (stale index).
    pub fn get(&self, index: Index) -> Option<&T> {
        match self.slots.get(index.slot as usize) {
            Some(Slot::Occupied { value, generation })
                if *generation == index.generation =>
            {
                Some(value)
            }
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Mutable lookup. Same staleness rules as `get`.
    pub fn get_mut(&mut self, index: Index) -> Option<&mut T> {
        match self.slots.get_mut(index.slot as usize) {
            Some(Slot::Occupied { value, generation })
                if *generation == index.generation =>
            {
                Some(value)
            }
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// True when `index` currently addresses a live value.
    pub fn contains(&self, index: Index) -> bool {
        self.get(index).is_some()
    }

    /// Remove the value at `index`, returning it. Returns `None` for a
    /// stale or out-of-range index, and leaves the arena unchanged in
    /// that case. A successful removal vacates the slot and pushes it
    /// onto the free list, so the next insert reuses it.
    pub fn remove(&mut self, index: Index) -> Option<T> {
        let slot_no = index.slot as usize;
        let slot = self.slots.get_mut(slot_no)?;
        match slot {
            Slot::Occupied { value, generation } if *generation == index.generation => {
                // Replace the slot in place: move the value out, leave a
                // vacant slot pointing at the old free-list head.
                let old = std::mem::replace(
                    slot,
                    Slot::Vacant {
                        next_free: self.free_head,
                    },
                );
                self.free_head = Some(index.slot);
                self.len -= 1;
                match old {
                    Slot::Occupied { value, .. } => Some(value),
                    Slot::Vacant { .. } => unreachable!(),
                }
            }
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Remove a value, replacing it with `replacement` in the same slot
    /// and generation. Returns the old value, or `None` for a stale
    /// index (in which case nothing changes).
    pub fn replace(&mut self, index: Index, replacement: T) -> Option<T> {
        match self.slots.get_mut(index.slot as usize) {
            Some(Slot::Occupied { value, generation })
                if *generation == index.generation =>
            {
                Some(std::mem::replace(value, replacement))
            }
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Remove all values. Slots are kept (so the arena can be reused
    /// without reallocating) but every index issued before the clear
    /// becomes stale: the generation seed keeps increasing, so a cleared
    /// slot can never match an old index.
    pub fn clear(&mut self) {
        self.slots.clear();
        self.free_head = None;
        self.len = 0;
        // Deliberately do NOT reset generation_seed: old indices must
        // stay stale even though the slots are gone.
    }

    /// Iterate over live values in slot order: `(index, &value)`.
    pub fn iter(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (Index, &T)> {
        self.slots.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(i, slot)| match slot {
            Slot::Occupied { value, generation } => {
                Some((Index::new(i as u32, *generation), value))
            }
            Slot::Vacant { .. } => None,
        })
    }

    /// Mutable iteration over live values in slot order.
    pub fn iter_mut(&mut self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (Index, &mut T)> {
        self.slots.iter_mut().enumerate().filter_map(|(i, slot)| match slot {
            Slot::Occupied { value, generation } => {
                Some((Index::new(i as u32, *generation), value))
            }
            Slot::Vacant { .. } => None,
        })
    }

    /// Iterate over live indices only.
    pub fn indices(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = Index> + '_ {
        self.iter().map(|(idx, _)| idx)
    }

    /// Iterate over live values only.
    pub fn values(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &T> {
        self.iter().map(|(_, v)| v)
    }

    /// Mutable iteration over live values only.
    pub fn values_mut(&mut self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &mut T> {
        self.iter_mut().map(|(_, v)| v)
    }

    /// Keep only the values for which `pred` returns true. Removed
    /// values are dropped and their slots recycled. The predicate sees
    /// the index as well as the value.
    pub fn retain<F>(&mut self, mut pred: F)
    where
        F: FnMut(Index, &T) -> bool,
    {
        // Collect the indices to remove first: we cannot mutate the
        // arena while iterating over it, so the decision pass borrows
        // immutably and the removal pass mutates.
        let doomed: Vec<Index> = self
            .iter()
            .filter(|(idx, v)| !pred(*idx, v))
            .map(|(idx, _)| idx)
            .collect();
        for idx in doomed {
            self.remove(idx);
        }
    }

    /// Remove every value, yielding them in slot order. The arena is
    /// empty afterwards but keeps its allocated slots for reuse.
    pub fn drain(&mut self) -> Vec<T> {
        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(self.len);
        // Take the slots out wholesale, then rebuild the arena as a set
        // of vacant slots threaded into a free list. Rebuilding is
        // O(n) and avoids repeated `remove` bookkeeping.
        let old_slots = std::mem::take(&mut self.slots);
        let n = old_slots.len();
        for slot in old_slots {
            if let Slot::Occupied { value, .. } = slot {
                out.push(value);
            }
        }
        self.slots = (0..n)
            .map(|i| Slot::Vacant {
                next_free: if i + 1 < n { Some((i + 1) as u32) } else { None },
            })
            .collect();
        self.free_head = if n > 0 { Some(0) } else { None };
        self.len = 0;
        // Generation seed is left alone so drained indices stay stale.
        out
    }

    /// Find the first live value satisfying `pred`, returning its index
    /// and a reference.
    pub fn find<F>(&self, mut pred: F) -> Option<(Index, &T)>
    where
        F: FnMut(&T) -> bool,
    {
        self.iter().find(|(_, v)| pred(v))
    }

    /// Count live values satisfying `pred`.
    pub fn count_where<F>(&self, mut pred: F) -> usize
    where
        F: FnMut(&T) -> bool,
    {
        self.values().filter(|v| pred(v)).count()
    }

    /// Verify the arena's internal invariants. Used by tests and the
    /// demo; panics with a message on the first inconsistency found.
    /// Checks: `len` matches the occupied count, the free list contains
    /// exactly the vacant slots with no duplicates or cycles, and every
    /// generation is positive.
    pub fn check_invariants(&self) {
        let occupied = self
            .slots
            .iter()
            .filter(|s| s.is_occupied())
            .count();
        assert_eq!(occupied, self.len, "len does not match occupied slots");
        for slot in &self.slots {
            if let Some(g) = slot.generation() {
                assert!(g > 0, "occupied slot has generation 0");
            }
        }
        // Walk the free list, checking it visits each vacant slot once.
        let mut seen = vec![false; self.slots.len()];
        let mut count = 0;
        let mut cur = self.free_head;
        while let Some(slot_no) = cur {
            let i = slot_no as usize;
            assert!(i < self.slots.len(), "free list points out of range");
            assert!(!seen[i], "free list visits slot {} twice (cycle?)", i);
            seen[i] = true;
            count += 1;
            cur = match &self.slots[i] {
                Slot::Vacant { next_free } => *next_free,
                Slot::Occupied { .. } => panic!("free list points at occupied slot {}", i),
            };
        }
        assert_eq!(
            count,
            self.free_count(),
            "free list length does not match vacant slot count"
        );
        for (i, slot) in self.slots.iter().enumerate() {
            if !slot.is_occupied() {
                assert!(seen[i], "vacant slot {} is missing from the free list", i);
            }
        }
    }
}

// Indexing: `arena[idx]` panics on a stale or missing index, like
// indexing a slice. Prefer `get` when the index may be stale.
impl<T> std::ops::Index<Index> for Arena<T> {
    type Output = T;

    fn index(&self, index: Index) -> &T {
        self.get(index)
            .expect("arena index is stale or out of range")
    }
}

impl<T> std::ops::IndexMut<Index> for Arena<T> {
    fn index_mut(&mut self, index: Index) -> &mut T {
        self.get_mut(index)
            .expect("arena index is stale or out of range")
    }
}

impl<T> Extend<T> for Arena<T> {
    fn extend<I: IntoIterator<Item = T>>(&mut self, iter: I) {
        for v in iter {
            self.insert(v);
        }
    }
}

impl<T> FromIterator<T> for Arena<T> {
    fn from_iter<I: IntoIterator<Item = T>>(iter: I) -> Arena<T> {
        let mut arena = Arena::new();
        arena.extend(iter);
        arena
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Demo domain: a family tree stored in one arena
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A person in a family tree. Relationships are stored as arena indices
/// -- no references, no lifetimes, no `Rc` -- so the tree can be
/// rearranged freely while the arena owns every node.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
struct Person {
    name: String,
    age: u32,
    parent: Option<Index>,
    children: Vec<Index>,
}

impl Person {
    fn new(name: &str, age: u32) -> Person {
        Person {
            name: name.to_string(),
            age,
            parent: None,
            children: Vec::new(),
        }
    }
}

/// Link `child` under `parent` inside the same arena. Returns `false`
/// when either index is stale.
fn adopt(arena: &mut Arena<Person>, parent: Index, child: Index) -> bool {
    if !arena.contains(parent) || !arena.contains(child) {
        return false;
    }
    // Set the child's parent pointer, then record the child in the
    // parent's list. Two separate mutable borrows, one at a time -- the
    // borrow checker is happy because each borrow ends before the next.
    arena.get_mut(child).unwrap().parent = Some(parent);
    arena.get_mut(parent).unwrap().children.push(child);
    true
}

/// Walk from a person up to the oldest ancestor, following parent links.
/// Returns the chain from the person up to the root, inclusive. A cycle
/// (which `adopt` cannot create, but a careless manual edit could) is
/// cut off by the visited set.
fn ancestors(arena: &Arena<Person>, mut start: Index) -> Vec<Index> {
    let mut chain = Vec::new();
    let mut seen = std::collections::HashSet::new();
    while arena.contains(start) && seen.insert(start) {
        chain.push(start);
        match arena.get(start).unwrap().parent {
            Some(p) => start = p,
            None => break,
        }
    }
    chain
}

/// Count all descendants of `root` (children, grandchildren, ...).
fn count_descendants(arena: &Arena<Person>, root: Index) -> usize {
    let mut count = 0;
    let mut stack: Vec<Index> = arena.get(root).map(|p| p.children.clone()).unwrap_or_default();
    while let Some(idx) = stack.pop() {
        count += 1;
        if let Some(person) = arena.get(idx) {
            stack.extend(person.children.iter().copied());
        }
    }
    count
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Demonstration / entry point
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

fn main() {
    // --- Basic insert / get / remove --------------------------------------
    let mut numbers: Arena<i32> = Arena::with_capacity(8);
    let a = numbers.insert(10);
    let b = numbers.insert(20);
    let c = numbers.insert(30);
    assert_eq!(numbers.len(), 3);
    assert_eq!(numbers[a], 10);
    assert_eq!(numbers[b], 20);
    assert_eq!(numbers[c], 30);
    assert_eq!(numbers.get(a), Some(&10));
    numbers.check_invariants();

    // Remove the middle value. Its slot becomes vacant and is recycled
    // by the next insert -- but the generation changes, so the old
    // index is stale and can never alias the new occupant.
    let removed = numbers.remove(b);
    assert_eq!(removed, Some(20));
    assert_eq!(numbers.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(numbers.get(b), None, "stale index must not resolve");
    assert!(!numbers.contains(b));
    assert_eq!(numbers.free_count(), 1);
    numbers.check_invariants();

    let d = numbers.insert(99);
    assert_eq!(d.slot(), b.slot(), "freed slot is reused");
    assert_ne!(d.generation(), b.generation(), "generation must advance");
    assert_eq!(numbers[d], 99);
    assert_eq!(numbers.get(b), None, "old index stays stale after reuse");
    numbers.check_invariants();

    // Indexing with a stale index panics, like indexing a slice.
    let stale = std::panic::catch_unwind(|| numbers[b]);
    assert!(stale.is_err());

    // --- Mutation through indices ------------------------------------------
    *numbers.get_mut(a).unwrap() += 5;
    assert_eq!(numbers[a], 15);
    let old = numbers.replace(c, 300).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(old, 30);
    assert_eq!(numbers[c], 300);
    assert_eq!(numbers.replace(b, 0), None, "replace of stale index is a no-op");

    // --- Iteration ----------------------------------------------------------
    let collected: Vec<(Index, i32)> = numbers.iter().map(|(i, v)| (i, *v)).collect();
    assert_eq!(collected.len(), 3);
    let sum: i32 = numbers.values().sum();
    assert_eq!(sum, 15 + 99 + 300);
    let doubled: Vec<i32> = numbers.values().map(|v| v * 2).collect();
    assert_eq!(doubled, vec![30, 198, 600]);
    for v in numbers.values_mut() {
        *v += 1;
    }
    assert_eq!(numbers.values().sum::<i32>(), 15 + 99 + 300 + 3);

    let found = numbers.find(|v| *v > 200);
    assert_eq!(found.map(|(_, v)| *v), Some(301));
    assert_eq!(numbers.count_where(|v| *v % 2 == 0), 2); // 16 and 100

    // --- retain / drain -----------------------------------------------------
    numbers.retain(|_, v| *v >= 100); // keeps 100 and 301
    assert_eq!(numbers.len(), 2);
    numbers.check_invariants();
    let drained = numbers.drain();
    assert_eq!(drained.len(), 2);
    assert!(numbers.is_empty());
    assert_eq!(numbers.get(a), None, "drained indices are stale");
    numbers.check_invariants();
    // The arena still works after a drain; slots are recycled.
    let e = numbers.insert(7);
    assert_eq!(numbers[e], 7);
    numbers.check_invariants();

    // --- insert_many / FromIterator / Extend --------------------------------
    let mut words: Arena<&str> = ["alpha", "beta", "gamma"].iter().copied().collect();
    assert_eq!(words.len(), 3);
    words.extend(["delta", "epsilon"]);
    assert_eq!(words.len(), 5);
    let idxs = words.insert_many(["zeta", "eta"]);
    assert_eq!(idxs.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(words[idxs[0]], "zeta");

    // --- Family tree: indices as relationships ------------------------------
    let mut people: Arena<Person> = Arena::new();
    let grandma = people.insert(Person::new("Ada", 78));
    let mum = people.insert(Person::new("Bea", 52));
    let dad = people.insert(Person::new("Cal", 54));
    let kid1 = people.insert(Person::new("Dee", 24));
    let kid2 = people.insert(Person::new("Eli", 19));
    let cousin = people.insert(Person::new("Fay", 21));
    // Fay is unlinked: no parent, no descendants, and not an ancestor
    // of anyone -- she only exists as an independent arena entry.
    assert_eq!(people[cousin].parent, None);
    assert_eq!(count_descendants(&people, cousin), 0);
    assert_eq!(ancestors(&people, cousin), vec![cousin]);

    assert!(adopt(&mut people, grandma, mum));
    assert!(adopt(&mut people, mum, kid1));
    assert!(adopt(&mut people, mum, kid2));
    assert!(adopt(&mut people, dad, kid1)); // re-parenting overwrites
    assert!(!adopt(&mut people, grandma, Index::new(999, 1))); // stale child
    people.check_invariants();

    // Dee's parent is now Cal (the last adopt wins).
    assert_eq!(people[kid1].parent, Some(dad));
    assert_eq!(ancestors(&people, kid1).len(), 2); // Dee -> Cal
    assert_eq!(ancestors(&people, kid2), vec![kid2, mum, grandma]);
    assert_eq!(count_descendants(&people, grandma), 3); // Bea, Dee, Eli
    assert_eq!(count_descendants(&people, mum), 2);

    // Removing Bea leaves her children's parent pointers dangling as
    // *stale indices* -- and the arena makes that detectable: `get`
    // returns None instead of handing back a wrong person.
    let removed_bea = people.remove(mum);
    assert_eq!(removed_bea.unwrap().name, "Bea");
    let eli_parent = people[kid2].parent.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(people.get(eli_parent), None, "dangling parent is detectable");
    assert_eq!(ancestors(&people, kid2), vec![kid2], "walk stops at the stale link");
    people.check_invariants();

    // --- Indices as HashMap keys --------------------------------------------
    let mut ages: HashMap<Index, u32> = HashMap::new();
    for (idx, person) in people.iter() {
        ages.insert(idx, person.age);
    }
    assert_eq!(ages[&grandma], 78);
    assert_eq!(ages[&kid2], 19);
    assert_eq!(ages.len(), people.len());

    // --- Stress: churn through many inserts/removes -------------------------
    let mut stress: Arena<usize> = Arena::new();
    let mut live: Vec<(Index, usize)> = Vec::new();
    for i in 0..10_000 {
        let idx = stress.insert(i);
        live.push((idx, i));
        // Remove every third value as we go, forcing slot reuse, and
        // verify the removed value is exactly the one we stored.
        if i % 3 == 0 {
            let (victim, expected_value) = live.remove(0);
            assert_eq!(stress.remove(victim), Some(expected_value));
            assert_eq!(stress.get(victim), None, "removed index is stale");
        }
        if i % 1000 == 0 {
            stress.check_invariants();
        }
    }
    stress.check_invariants();
    assert_eq!(stress.len(), live.len());
    // Every surviving index still resolves to its original value.
    for (idx, value) in &live {
        assert_eq!(stress.get(*idx), Some(value));
    }
    let mut clean: Arena<usize> = Arena::new();
    let mut ids = Vec::new();
    for i in 0..5_000 {
        ids.push(clean.insert(i * 2));
    }
    for (n, id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {
        if n % 2 == 0 {
            assert_eq!(clean.remove(*id), Some(n * 2));
        }
    }
    assert_eq!(clean.len(), 2_500);
    clean.check_invariants();
    // Reuse: inserting 2,500 more values must recycle the freed slots.
    for i in 0..2_500 {
        clean.insert(100_000 + i);
    }
    assert_eq!(clean.len(), 5_000);
    assert_eq!(clean.slot_count(), 5_000, "no growth: freed slots were reused");
    clean.check_invariants();
    let total: usize = clean.values().sum();
    let expected: usize = (0..5_000).filter(|n| n % 2 == 1).map(|n| n * 2).sum::<usize>()
        + (0..2_500).map(|i| 100_000 + i).sum::<usize>();
    assert_eq!(total, expected);

    println!("arena: all assertions passed (stress total = {total})");
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Unit tests
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn insert_get_remove_roundtrip() {
        let mut a: Arena<String> = Arena::new();
        let i = a.insert("hello".to_string());
        assert_eq!(a.get(i).map(|s| s.as_str()), Some("hello"));
        assert_eq!(a.remove(i).as_deref(), Some("hello"));
        assert_eq!(a.get(i), None);
        assert!(a.is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn stale_index_never_aliases_new_value() {
        let mut a: Arena<i32> = Arena::new();
        let old = a.insert(1);
        a.remove(old);
        let new = a.insert(2);
        assert_eq!(new.slot(), old.slot());
        assert_ne!(new, old);
        assert_eq!(a.get(old), None);
        assert_eq!(a.get(new), Some(&2));
    }

    #[test]
    fn double_remove_returns_none() {
        let mut a: Arena<i32> = Arena::new();
        let i = a.insert(5);
        assert_eq!(a.remove(i), Some(5));
        assert_eq!(a.remove(i), None);
        assert_eq!(a.remove(Index::new(12345, 1)), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn iteration_skips_vacant_slots() {
        let mut a: Arena<i32> = Arena::new();
        let ids: Vec<Index> = (0..6).map(|v| a.insert(v)).collect();
        a.remove(ids[1]);
        a.remove(ids[4]);
        let values: Vec<i32> = a.values().copied().collect();
        assert_eq!(values, vec![0, 2, 3, 5]);
        assert_eq!(a.indices().count(), 4);
    }

    #[test]
    fn iter_mut_updates_values() {
        let mut a: Arena<i32> = Arena::new();
        for v in 0..4 {
            a.insert(v);
        }
        for v in a.values_mut() {
            *v *= 10;
        }
        assert_eq!(a.values().sum::<i32>(), 60);
    }

    #[test]
    fn retain_keeps_matching() {
        let mut a: Arena<i32> = Arena::new();
        for v in 0..10 {
            a.insert(v);
        }
        a.retain(|_, v| *v % 2 == 0);
        assert_eq!(a.values().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![0, 2, 4, 6, 8]);
        a.check_invariants();
    }

    #[test]
    fn drain_empties_and_invalidates() {
        let mut a: Arena<i32> = Arena::new();
        let ids: Vec<Index> = (0..5).map(|v| a.insert(v)).collect();
        let drained = a.drain();
        assert_eq!(drained, vec![0, 1, 2, 3, 4]);
        assert!(a.is_empty());
        for id in ids {
            assert_eq!(a.get(id), None);
        }
        a.check_invariants();
    }

    #[test]
    fn clear_invalidates_old_indices() {
        let mut a: Arena<i32> = Arena::new();
        let id = a.insert(42);
        a.clear();
        assert_eq!(a.get(id), None);
        assert_eq!(a.len(), 0);
        // Reuse after clear works and never matches the old index.
        let id2 = a.insert(43);
        assert_ne!(id2, id);
        assert_eq!(a.get(id2), Some(&43));
    }

    #[test]
    fn replace_swaps_value() {
        let mut a: Arena<i32> = Arena::new();
        let id = a.insert(1);
        assert_eq!(a.replace(id, 2), Some(1));
        assert_eq!(a[id], 2);
        a.remove(id);
        assert_eq!(a.replace(id, 3), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn index_is_usable_as_map_key() {
        let mut a: Arena<i32> = Arena::new();
        let id = a.insert(7);
        let mut map = HashMap::new();
        map.insert(id, "seven");
        assert_eq!(map[&id], "seven");
    }

    #[test]
    fn family_tree_links() {
        let mut people: Arena<Person> = Arena::new();
        let root = people.insert(Person::new("Root", 90));
        let child = people.insert(Person::new("Child", 60));
        assert!(adopt(&mut people, root, child));
        assert_eq!(ancestors(&people, child), vec![child, root]);
        assert_eq!(count_descendants(&people, root), 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn invariants_hold_under_churn() {
        let mut a: Arena<u32> = Arena::new();
        let mut live = Vec::new();
        for i in 0..1000u32 {
            live.push(a.insert(i));
            if i % 5 == 0 {
                let victim = live.remove(0);
                a.remove(victim);
            }
            a.check_invariants();
        }
        assert_eq!(a.len(), live.len());
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/binary_search_tree.rs ---
// binary_search_tree.rs
//
// A generic binary search tree (BST) over `T: Ord`, built from `Box`
// nodes, with insertion, search, deletion (all three cases), borrowing
// iterators (in-order, pre-order, post-order), range queries, floor and
// ceiling lookups, height and balance diagnostics, a structural
// invariant validator, and a `rebalance` operation that rebuilds the
// tree into a height-balanced shape from its own in-order traversal.
//
// The tree is deliberately *unbalanced* by insertion order (that is
// what a plain BST is), and the demo shows the pathology -- inserting
// sorted data makes a degenerate chain -- and then fixes it with
// `rebalance`. A sorted-input constructor builds a balanced tree
// directly, which is the standard way to bulk-load a BST.
//
// Correctness notes
// -----------------
// * Deletion of a node with two children replaces the node with its
//   in-order successor (the minimum of the right subtree) and then
//   deletes that successor from the right subtree. The successor has
//   no left child, so that second deletion is always the easy case.
// * All iterators borrow the tree and walk it with an explicit stack
//   of `&Node` references; no recursion at iteration time, so deep
//   trees do not blow the stack while iterating (insertion and
//   deletion are recursive and are documented as such).
// * `validate` walks the tree with a min/max range and proves the BST
//   ordering invariant holds at every node; the demo and the tests
//   call it after every mutating batch.
//
// The demo in `main` exercises every operation against known answers,
// including a 10,000-element randomised (deterministically shuffled)
// workload that is cross-checked against a sorted `Vec`.

use std::cmp::Ordering;
use std::fmt::Debug;
use std::iter::FromIterator;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Node and tree
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// One node of the tree. `left` holds values strictly less than `value`,
/// `right` holds values strictly greater; duplicates are rejected by
/// `insert` (it returns `false`) so the tree is a *set*.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
struct Node<T> {
    value: T,
    left: Option<Box<Node<T>>>,
    right: Option<Box<Node<T>>>,
}

impl<T: Ord> Node<T> {
    fn new(value: T) -> Node<T> {
        Node {
            value,
            left: None,
            right: None,
        }
    }

    /// Insert `value` into the subtree rooted here. Returns `true` when
    /// the value was new, `false` when it was already present.
    fn insert(&mut self, value: T) -> bool {
        match value.cmp(&self.value) {
            Ordering::Less => match self.left {
                Some(ref mut child) => child.insert(value),
                None => {
                    self.left = Some(Box::new(Node::new(value)));
                    true
                }
            },
            Ordering::Greater => match self.right {
                Some(ref mut child) => child.insert(value),
                None => {
                    self.right = Some(Box::new(Node::new(value)));
                    true
                }
            },
            Ordering::Equal => false,
        }
    }

    /// Search the subtree for `value`.
    fn contains(&self, value: &T) -> bool {
        match value.cmp(&self.value) {
            Ordering::Less => self.left.as_ref().map_or(false, |c| c.contains(value)),
            Ordering::Greater => self
                .right
                .as_ref()
                .map_or(false, |c| c.contains(value)),
            Ordering::Equal => true,
        }
    }

    /// Borrow the stored value, if present.
    fn get(&self, value: &T) -> Option<&T> {
        match value.cmp(&self.value) {
            Ordering::Less => self.left.as_ref().and_then(|c| c.get(value)),
            Ordering::Greater => self.right.as_ref().and_then(|c| c.get(value)),
            Ordering::Equal => Some(&self.value),
        }
    }

    /// Mutably borrow the stored value, if present.
    fn get_mut(&mut self, value: &T) -> Option<&mut T> {
        match value.cmp(&self.value) {
            Ordering::Less => self.left.as_mut().and_then(|c| c.get_mut(value)),
            Ordering::Greater => self.right.as_mut().and_then(|c| c.get_mut(value)),
            Ordering::Equal => Some(&mut self.value),
        }
    }

    /// Height of this subtree: a leaf has height 1.
    fn height(&self) -> usize {
        1 + usize::max(
            self.left.as_ref().map_or(0, |c| c.height()),
            self.right.as_ref().map_or(0, |c| c.height()),
        )
    }

    /// Number of nodes in this subtree.
    fn size(&self) -> usize {
        1 + self.left.as_ref().map_or(0, |c| c.size())
            + self.right.as_ref().map_or(0, |c| c.size())
    }

    /// The minimum value in this subtree.
    fn min_value(&self) -> &T {
        match self.left {
            Some(ref child) => child.min_value(),
            None => &self.value,
        }
    }

    /// The maximum value in this subtree.
    fn max_value(&self) -> &T {
        match self.right {
            Some(ref child) => child.max_value(),
            None => &self.value,
        }
    }

    /// True when the subtree is height-balanced in the AVL sense:
    /// at every node, the heights of the two children differ by at
    /// most one.
    fn is_balanced(&self) -> bool {
        let lh = self.left.as_ref().map_or(0, |c| c.height());
        let rh = self.right.as_ref().map_or(0, |c| c.height());
        lh.abs_diff(rh) <= 1
            && self.left.as_ref().map_or(true, |c| c.is_balanced())
            && self.right.as_ref().map_or(true, |c| c.is_balanced())
    }

    /// Validate the BST invariant for this subtree: every value in the
    /// left subtree is `< value` and every value in the right subtree
    /// is `> value`. `lo`/`hi` are the bounds inherited from ancestors.
    fn validate(&self, lo: Option<&T>, hi: Option<&T>) -> bool {
        if let Some(lo) = lo {
            if self.value <= *lo {
                return false;
            }
        }
        if let Some(hi) = hi {
            if self.value >= *hi {
                return false;
            }
        }
        self.left
            .as_ref()
            .map_or(true, |c| c.validate(lo, Some(&self.value)))
            && self
                .right
                .as_ref()
                .map_or(true, |c| c.validate(Some(&self.value), hi))
    }

    /// Remove `value` from the subtree rooted here. The subtree is
    /// passed as `&mut Option<Box<Node<T>>>` so that the "no children"
    /// and "one child" cases can replace the slot itself (with `None`
    /// or with the surviving child) instead of needing a parent
    /// pointer. Returns `true` when something was removed.
    fn remove(slot: &mut Option<Box<Node<T>>>, value: &T) -> bool {
        let node = match slot {
            Some(n) => n,
            None => return false,
        };
        match value.cmp(&node.value) {
            Ordering::Less => Node::remove(&mut node.left, value),
            Ordering::Greater => Node::remove(&mut node.right, value),
            Ordering::Equal => {
                // Found it. Three cases:
                match (node.left.take(), node.right.take()) {
                    // No children: drop the node, slot becomes None.
                    (None, None) => {
                        *slot = None;
                    }
                    // One child: the child takes the node's place.
                    (Some(child), None) | (None, Some(child)) => {
                        *slot = Some(child);
                    }
                    // Two children: replace this node's value with its
                    // in-order successor (minimum of the right subtree)
                    // and delete that successor from the right subtree.
                    // The successor has no left child, so the recursive
                    // deletion is always one of the easy cases.
                    (Some(left), Some(right)) => {
                        let mut right_slot = Some(right);
                        let successor =
                            extract_min_from(&mut right_slot).expect("right subtree nonempty");
                        *slot = Some(Box::new(Node {
                            value: successor,
                            left: Some(left),
                            right: right_slot,
                        }));
                    }
                }
                true
            }
        }
    }

}

/// Remove the minimum value from the subtree in `slot`, returning it.
/// The slot is updated in place. The minimum node has no left child,
/// so it is replaced by its (possibly absent) right child.
fn extract_min_from<T: Ord>(slot: &mut Option<Box<Node<T>>>) -> Option<T> {
    let node = slot.as_mut()?;
    match node.left {
        Some(_) => extract_min_from(&mut node.left),
        None => {
            // `node` is the minimum: replace the slot with its right
            // child and return the node's value.
            let mut old = slot.take().unwrap();
            *slot = old.right.take();
            Some(old.value)
        }
    }
}

/// A generic binary search tree (a set: duplicates are not stored).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct BinarySearchTree<T> {
    root: Option<Box<Node<T>>>,
    len: usize,
}

impl<T: Ord> BinarySearchTree<T> {
    pub fn new() -> BinarySearchTree<T> {
        BinarySearchTree { root: None, len: 0 }
    }

    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.len
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.len == 0
    }

    /// Insert `value`. Returns `true` when the value was new.
    pub fn insert(&mut self, value: T) -> bool {
        let inserted = match self.root {
            Some(ref mut node) => node.insert(value),
            None => {
                self.root = Some(Box::new(Node::new(value)));
                true
            }
        };
        if inserted {
            self.len += 1;
        }
        inserted
    }

    /// Insert every value from an iterator, returning how many were new.
    pub fn insert_all<I: IntoIterator<Item = T>>(&mut self, values: I) -> usize {
        let mut added = 0;
        for v in values {
            if self.insert(v) {
                added += 1;
            }
        }
        added
    }

    pub fn contains(&self, value: &T) -> bool {
        self.root.as_ref().map_or(false, |n| n.contains(value))
    }

    pub fn get(&self, value: &T) -> Option<&T> {
        self.root.as_ref().and_then(|n| n.get(value))
    }

    pub fn get_mut(&mut self, value: &T) -> Option<&mut T> {
        self.root.as_mut().and_then(|n| n.get_mut(value))
    }

    /// Remove `value`. Returns `true` when something was removed.
    pub fn remove(&mut self, value: &T) -> bool {
        let removed = Node::remove(&mut self.root, value);
        if removed {
            self.len -= 1;
        }
        removed
    }

    /// Remove every value satisfying `pred`, returning how many were
    /// removed. Implemented by collecting the victims first (the tree
    /// cannot be mutated while it is being traversed).
    pub fn remove_where<F>(&mut self, mut pred: F) -> usize
    where
        F: FnMut(&T) -> bool,
        T: Clone,
    {
        let victims: Vec<T> = self
            .in_order()
            .filter(|v| pred(v))
            .cloned()
            .collect();
        let n = victims.len();
        for v in victims {
            self.remove(&v);
        }
        n
    }

    /// Height of the tree; 0 when empty.
    pub fn height(&self) -> usize {
        self.root.as_ref().map_or(0, |n| n.height())
    }

    /// True when the tree is height-balanced in the AVL sense.
    pub fn is_balanced(&self) -> bool {
        self.root.as_ref().map_or(true, |n| n.is_balanced())
    }

    /// True when the BST ordering invariant holds everywhere.
    pub fn validate(&self) -> bool {
        self.root.as_ref().map_or(true, |n| n.validate(None, None))
            && self.root.as_ref().map_or(0, |n| n.size()) == self.len
    }

    pub fn min(&self) -> Option<&T> {
        self.root.as_ref().map(|n| n.min_value())
    }

    pub fn max(&self) -> Option<&T> {
        self.root.as_ref().map(|n| n.max_value())
    }

    /// Smallest value `>= target`, if any.
    pub fn ceiling(&self, target: &T) -> Option<&T> {
        let mut best: Option<&T> = None;
        let mut cur = self.root.as_ref();
        while let Some(node) = cur {
            match target.cmp(&node.value) {
                Ordering::Equal => return Some(&node.value),
                Ordering::Less => {
                    best = Some(&node.value);
                    cur = node.left.as_ref();
                }
                Ordering::Greater => {
                    cur = node.right.as_ref();
                }
            }
        }
        best
    }

    /// Largest value `<= target`, if any.
    pub fn floor(&self, target: &T) -> Option<&T> {
        let mut best: Option<&T> = None;
        let mut cur = self.root.as_ref();
        while let Some(node) = cur {
            match target.cmp(&node.value) {
                Ordering::Equal => return Some(&node.value),
                Ordering::Greater => {
                    best = Some(&node.value);
                    cur = node.right.as_ref();
                }
                Ordering::Less => {
                    cur = node.left.as_ref();
                }
            }
        }
        best
    }

    /// All values in `[lo, hi]` (inclusive), in ascending order.
    pub fn range(&self, lo: &T, hi: &T) -> Vec<&T> {
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        self.range_into(&self.root, lo, hi, &mut out);
        out
    }

    fn range_into<'a>(
        &'a self,
        node: &'a Option<Box<Node<T>>>,
        lo: &T,
        hi: &T,
        out: &mut Vec<&'a T>,
    ) {
        if let Some(n) = node {
            // Prune subtrees that cannot contain values in range.
            if n.value > *lo {
                self.range_into(&n.left, lo, hi, out);
            }
            if n.value >= *lo && n.value <= *hi {
                out.push(&n.value);
            }
            if n.value < *hi {
                self.range_into(&n.right, lo, hi, out);
            }
        }
    }

    /// Number of values strictly less than `target` (the rank).
    pub fn rank(&self, target: &T) -> usize {
        self.in_order().take_while(|v| *v < target).count()
    }

    /// The k-th smallest value (0-based), if it exists.
    pub fn kth_smallest(&self, k: usize) -> Option<&T> {
        self.in_order().nth(k)
    }

    /// Rebuild the tree into a height-balanced shape containing exactly
    /// the same values. Implemented by taking the sorted in-order
    /// sequence and building recursively from the middle outwards.
    pub fn rebalance(&mut self)
    where
        T: Clone,
    {
        let sorted: Vec<T> = self.in_order().cloned().collect();
        self.root = build_balanced(&sorted);
        // `len` is unchanged.
    }

    /// Remove all values.
    pub fn clear(&mut self) {
        self.root = None;
        self.len = 0;
    }

    /// In-order iterator: values in ascending order.
    pub fn in_order(&self) -> InOrderIter<'_, T> {
        InOrderIter::new(self.root.as_ref())
    }

    /// Pre-order iterator: node, then left, then right.
    pub fn pre_order(&self) -> PreOrderIter<'_, T> {
        PreOrderIter::new(self.root.as_ref())
    }

    /// Post-order iterator: left, then right, then node.
    pub fn post_order(&self) -> PostOrderIter<'_, T> {
        PostOrderIter::new(self.root.as_ref())
    }

    /// Collect all values in ascending order.
    pub fn to_sorted_vec(&self) -> Vec<T>
    where
        T: Clone,
    {
        self.in_order().cloned().collect()
    }
}

/// Build a height-balanced BST from a sorted slice, recursively taking
/// the middle element as the root of each subtree.
fn build_balanced<T: Ord + Clone>(sorted: &[T]) -> Option<Box<Node<T>>> {
    if sorted.is_empty() {
        return None;
    }
    let mid = sorted.len() / 2;
    let mut node = Box::new(Node::new(sorted[mid].clone()));
    node.left = build_balanced(&sorted[..mid]);
    node.right = build_balanced(&sorted[mid + 1..]);
    Some(node)
}

impl<T: Ord> BinarySearchTree<T> {
    /// Build a balanced tree from an arbitrary collection: sort the
    /// values, deduplicate, and build from the middle outwards. This
    /// is the right way to bulk-load a BST.
    pub fn from_sorted<I: IntoIterator<Item = T>>(values: I) -> BinarySearchTree<T>
    where
        T: Clone,
    {
        let mut sorted: Vec<T> = values.into_iter().collect();
        sorted.sort();
        sorted.dedup();
        let len = sorted.len();
        BinarySearchTree {
            root: build_balanced(&sorted),
            len,
        }
    }
}

impl<T: Ord> Extend<T> for BinarySearchTree<T> {
    fn extend<I: IntoIterator<Item = T>>(&mut self, iter: I) {
        for v in iter {
            self.insert(v);
        }
    }
}

impl<T: Ord> FromIterator<T> for BinarySearchTree<T> {
    fn from_iter<I: IntoIterator<Item = T>>(iter: I) -> BinarySearchTree<T> {
        let mut tree = BinarySearchTree::new();
        tree.extend(iter);
        tree
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Borrowing iterators (explicit stack, no recursion at iteration time)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// In-order iterator: yields values in ascending order.
pub struct InOrderIter<'a, T> {
    stack: Vec<&'a Node<T>>,
}

impl<'a, T> InOrderIter<'a, T> {
    fn new(root: Option<&'a Box<Node<T>>>) -> InOrderIter<'a, T> {
        let mut iter = InOrderIter { stack: Vec::new() };
        iter.push_left_spine(root);
        iter
    }

    fn push_left_spine(&mut self, mut node: Option<&'a Box<Node<T>>>) {
        while let Some(n) = node {
            self.stack.push(n);
            node = n.left.as_ref();
        }
    }
}

impl<'a, T> Iterator for InOrderIter<'a, T> {
    type Item = &'a T;

    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<&'a T> {
        let node = self.stack.pop()?;
        // After visiting a node, its right subtree's left spine is next.
        self.push_left_spine(node.right.as_ref());
        Some(&node.value)
    }
}

/// Pre-order iterator: node, then left subtree, then right subtree.
pub struct PreOrderIter<'a, T> {
    stack: Vec<&'a Node<T>>,
}

impl<'a, T> PreOrderIter<'a, T> {
    fn new(root: Option<&'a Box<Node<T>>>) -> PreOrderIter<'a, T> {
        let mut stack = Vec::new();
        if let Some(n) = root {
            stack.push(&**n);
        }
        PreOrderIter { stack }
    }
}

impl<'a, T> Iterator for PreOrderIter<'a, T> {
    type Item = &'a T;

    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<&'a T> {
        let node = self.stack.pop()?;
        // Push right first so left is popped first.
        if let Some(right) = node.right.as_ref() {
            self.stack.push(right);
        }
        if let Some(left) = node.left.as_ref() {
            self.stack.push(left);
        }
        Some(&node.value)
    }
}

/// Post-order iterator: left subtree, then right subtree, then node.
/// Implemented with a stack of `(node, visited)` pairs: a node is
/// pushed once unvisited, and again marked visited after its children
/// have been scheduled.
pub struct PostOrderIter<'a, T> {
    stack: Vec<(&'a Node<T>, bool)>,
}

impl<'a, T> PostOrderIter<'a, T> {
    fn new(root: Option<&'a Box<Node<T>>>) -> PostOrderIter<'a, T> {
        let mut stack = Vec::new();
        if let Some(n) = root {
            stack.push((&**n, false));
        }
        PostOrderIter { stack }
    }
}

impl<'a, T> Iterator for PostOrderIter<'a, T> {
    type Item = &'a T;

    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<&'a T> {
        while let Some((node, visited)) = self.stack.pop() {
            if visited {
                return Some(&node.value);
            }
            self.stack.push((node, true));
            if let Some(right) = node.right.as_ref() {
                self.stack.push((right, false));
            }
            if let Some(left) = node.left.as_ref() {
                self.stack.push((left, false));
            }
        }
        None
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// A tiny deterministic pseudo-random generator (for the stress workload)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// SplitMix64: a small, fast, deterministic PRNG. No external crates,
/// no OS randomness -- the same seed always gives the same sequence,
/// so the stress test is reproducible.
struct SplitMix64(u64);

impl SplitMix64 {
    fn new(seed: u64) -> SplitMix64 {
        SplitMix64(seed)
    }

    fn next_u64(&mut self) -> u64 {
        self.0 = self.0.wrapping_add(0x9E37_79B9_7F4A_7C15);
        let mut z = self.0;
        z = (z ^ (z >> 30)).wrapping_mul(0xBF58_476D_1CE4_E5B9);
        z = (z ^ (z >> 27)).wrapping_mul(0x94D0_49BB_1331_11EB);
        z ^ (z >> 31)
    }

    fn next_below(&mut self, n: u64) -> u64 {
        self.next_u64() % n
    }
}

/// Fisher-Yates shuffle driven by `SplitMix64`.
fn shuffle<T>(items: &mut [T], rng: &mut SplitMix64) {
    for i in (1..items.len()).rev() {
        let j = rng.next_below((i + 1) as u64) as usize;
        items.swap(i, j);
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Demonstration / entry point
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

fn main() {
    // --- Basic insertion, search, duplicates -------------------------------
    let mut tree: BinarySearchTree<i32> = BinarySearchTree::new();
    assert!(tree.is_empty());
    for v in [50, 30, 70, 20, 40, 60, 80] {
        assert!(tree.insert(v));
    }
    assert_eq!(tree.len(), 7);
    assert!(!tree.insert(50), "duplicate insert is rejected");
    assert_eq!(tree.len(), 7);
    assert!(tree.contains(&40));
    assert!(!tree.contains(&41));
    assert_eq!(tree.get(&60), Some(&60));
    assert_eq!(tree.get(&61), None);
    assert_eq!(tree.min(), Some(&20));
    assert_eq!(tree.max(), Some(&80));
    assert!(tree.validate());
    assert_eq!(tree.height(), 3);
    assert!(tree.is_balanced());

    // --- Traversals ---------------------------------------------------------
    let in_order: Vec<i32> = tree.in_order().copied().collect();
    assert_eq!(in_order, vec![20, 30, 40, 50, 60, 70, 80]);
    let pre_order: Vec<i32> = tree.pre_order().copied().collect();
    assert_eq!(pre_order, vec![50, 30, 20, 40, 70, 60, 80]);
    let post_order: Vec<i32> = tree.post_order().copied().collect();
    assert_eq!(post_order, vec![20, 40, 30, 60, 80, 70, 50]);

    // --- Floor / ceiling / rank / kth ---------------------------------------
    assert_eq!(tree.ceiling(&41), Some(&50));
    assert_eq!(tree.ceiling(&40), Some(&40));
    assert_eq!(tree.ceiling(&81), None);
    assert_eq!(tree.floor(&41), Some(&40));
    assert_eq!(tree.floor(&20), Some(&20));
    assert_eq!(tree.floor(&19), None);
    assert_eq!(tree.rank(&50), 3);
    assert_eq!(tree.rank(&20), 0);
    assert_eq!(tree.rank(&100), 7);
    assert_eq!(tree.kth_smallest(0), Some(&20));
    assert_eq!(tree.kth_smallest(6), Some(&80));
    assert_eq!(tree.kth_smallest(7), None);
    assert_eq!(
        tree.range(&30, &70).into_iter().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        vec![30, 40, 50, 60, 70]
    );
    assert_eq!(tree.range(&100, &200).len(), 0);

    // --- Deletion: leaf ------------------------------------------------------
    assert!(tree.remove(&20));
    assert_eq!(tree.len(), 6);
    assert!(!tree.contains(&20));
    assert!(tree.validate());
    assert_eq!(tree.in_order().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![30, 40, 50, 60, 70, 80]);

    // --- Deletion: node with one child ---------------------------------------
    // 30 now has only a right child (40).
    assert!(tree.remove(&30));
    assert_eq!(tree.in_order().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![40, 50, 60, 70, 80]);
    assert!(tree.validate());

    // --- Deletion: node with two children (the root) --------------------------
    assert!(tree.remove(&50));
    assert_eq!(tree.len(), 4);
    assert!(tree.validate());
    assert_eq!(tree.in_order().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![40, 60, 70, 80]);
    // The root is now the successor 60.
    assert_eq!(tree.pre_order().copied().next(), Some(60));

    // --- Deletion of a missing value is a no-op -------------------------------
    assert!(!tree.remove(&999));
    assert_eq!(tree.len(), 4);

    // --- remove_where ----------------------------------------------------------
    let removed = tree.remove_where(|v| *v >= 70);
    assert_eq!(removed, 2);
    assert_eq!(tree.in_order().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![40, 60]);
    assert!(tree.validate());

    // --- get_mut ---------------------------------------------------------------
    // NOTE: mutating a key in place can break the BST invariant, and
    // `validate` will catch it -- this is deliberate, to demonstrate
    // the check. Here the tree is root 60 with left child 40, so
    // changing the root to 30 (less than its left child) breaks the
    // invariant. The root is still findable by search, so it can be
    // restored. (A mutated key can also become *unfindable* by search
    // if it crosses its parent's boundary -- another reason keys
    // should be treated as immutable.)
    *tree.get_mut(&60).unwrap() = 30;
    assert!(!tree.validate(), "mutated key must break the invariant");
    *tree.get_mut(&30).unwrap() = 60;
    assert!(tree.validate());

    // --- Degenerate tree from sorted input, then rebalance ----------------------
    let mut chain: BinarySearchTree<i32> = BinarySearchTree::new();
    for v in 1..=15 {
        chain.insert(v);
    }
    assert_eq!(chain.height(), 15, "sorted insertion degenerates to a chain");
    assert!(!chain.is_balanced());
    assert!(chain.validate());
    chain.rebalance();
    assert_eq!(chain.height(), 4, "balanced height of 15 nodes is 4");
    assert!(chain.is_balanced());
    assert!(chain.validate());
    assert_eq!(chain.in_order().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), (1..=15).collect::<Vec<_>>());

    // --- Bulk load: from_sorted builds a balanced tree directly -----------------
    let bulk = BinarySearchTree::from_sorted([5, 1, 9, 3, 7, 5, 1]);
    assert_eq!(bulk.len(), 5, "duplicates are removed by from_sorted");
    assert!(bulk.is_balanced());
    assert!(bulk.validate());
    assert_eq!(bulk.to_sorted_vec(), vec![1, 3, 5, 7, 9]);

    // --- Strings work too (generic over Ord) -------------------------------------
    let mut words: BinarySearchTree<&str> = BinarySearchTree::new();
    for w in ["pear", "apple", "fig", "banana", "cherry"] {
        words.insert(w);
    }
    assert_eq!(words.in_order().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec!["apple", "banana", "cherry", "fig", "pear"]);
    assert_eq!(words.floor(&"blueberry"), Some(&"banana"));
    assert_eq!(words.ceiling(&"blueberry"), Some(&"cherry"));

    // --- Stress: 10,000 shuffled values, cross-checked with a Vec -----------------
    let mut rng = SplitMix64::new(0xC0FF_EE11_2233_4455);
    let mut values: Vec<i32> = (0..10_000).collect();
    shuffle(&mut values, &mut rng);
    let mut big: BinarySearchTree<i32> = values.iter().copied().collect();
    assert_eq!(big.len(), 10_000);
    assert!(big.validate());
    let sorted = big.to_sorted_vec();
    assert_eq!(sorted, (0..10_000).collect::<Vec<_>>());
    // Delete half the values in shuffled order, validating periodically.
    // Note: we remove every *even position* in the shuffled list, i.e.
    // a random half of the values -- so the expected survivors are
    // computed from the values actually removed, not from parity.
    let mut removed: std::collections::HashSet<i32> = std::collections::HashSet::new();
    shuffle(&mut values, &mut rng);
    for (n, v) in values.iter().enumerate() {
        if n % 2 == 0 {
            assert!(big.remove(v));
            removed.insert(*v);
        }
        if n % 2000 == 0 {
            assert!(big.validate());
        }
    }
    assert_eq!(big.len(), 5_000);
    assert!(big.validate());
    let remaining = big.to_sorted_vec();
    let expected: Vec<i32> = (0..10_000).filter(|v| !removed.contains(v)).collect();
    assert_eq!(remaining, expected);
    // Rebalance the survivor tree and check it again.
    big.rebalance();
    assert!(big.is_balanced());
    assert!(big.validate());
    assert_eq!(big.height(), 13, "balanced height of 5000 nodes is 13");
    assert_eq!(big.to_sorted_vec(), expected);

    // --- clear ----------------------------------------------------------------------
    big.clear();
    assert!(big.is_empty());
    assert_eq!(big.height(), 0);
    assert_eq!(big.min(), None);
    assert!(big.validate());

    println!("binary_search_tree: all assertions passed");
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Unit tests
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn sample_tree() -> BinarySearchTree<i32> {
        let mut t = BinarySearchTree::new();
        for v in [50, 30, 70, 20, 40, 60, 80] {
            t.insert(v);
        }
        t
    }

    #[test]
    fn insert_and_contains() {
        let t = sample_tree();
        assert_eq!(t.len(), 7);
        for v in [20, 30, 40, 50, 60, 70, 80] {
            assert!(t.contains(&v));
        }
        assert!(!t.contains(&0));
        assert!(t.validate());
    }

    #[test]
    fn duplicate_insert_rejected() {
        let mut t = BinarySearchTree::new();
        assert!(t.insert(1));
        assert!(!t.insert(1));
        assert_eq!(t.len(), 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn traversals_match_known_orders() {
        let t = sample_tree();
        assert_eq!(t.in_order().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![20, 30, 40, 50, 60, 70, 80]);
        assert_eq!(t.pre_order().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![50, 30, 20, 40, 70, 60, 80]);
        assert_eq!(t.post_order().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![20, 40, 30, 60, 80, 70, 50]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn remove_leaf_one_child_two_children() {
        let mut t = sample_tree();
        assert!(t.remove(&20)); // leaf
        assert!(t.remove(&30)); // one child after 20 is gone
        assert!(t.remove(&50)); // two children (root)
        assert_eq!(t.in_order().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![40, 60, 70, 80]);
        assert!(t.validate());
    }

    #[test]
    fn remove_root_with_two_children_repeatedly() {
        let mut t = sample_tree();
        for _ in 0..7 {
            let root = *t.in_order().nth(t.len() / 2).unwrap();
            assert!(t.remove(&root));
            assert!(t.validate());
        }
        assert!(t.is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn remove_missing_is_noop() {
        let mut t = sample_tree();
        assert!(!t.remove(&12345));
        assert_eq!(t.len(), 7);
    }

    #[test]
    fn floor_ceiling_edges() {
        let t = sample_tree();
        assert_eq!(t.floor(&10), None);
        assert_eq!(t.ceiling(&100), None);
        assert_eq!(t.floor(&80), Some(&80));
        assert_eq!(t.ceiling(&20), Some(&20));
        assert_eq!(t.floor(&55), Some(&50));
        assert_eq!(t.ceiling(&55), Some(&60));
    }

    #[test]
    fn range_query() {
        let t = sample_tree();
        let r: Vec<i32> = t.range(&35, &65).into_iter().copied().collect();
        assert_eq!(r, vec![40, 50, 60]);
        assert!(t.range(&90, &100).is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn rank_and_kth() {
        let t = sample_tree();
        for (k, v) in [20, 30, 40, 50, 60, 70, 80].iter().enumerate() {
            assert_eq!(t.kth_smallest(k), Some(v));
            assert_eq!(t.rank(v), k);
        }
        assert_eq!(t.kth_smallest(7), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rebalance_fixes_degenerate_tree() {
        let mut t: BinarySearchTree<i32> = BinarySearchTree::new();
        for v in 0..100 {
            t.insert(v);
        }
        assert_eq!(t.height(), 100);
        t.rebalance();
        assert!(t.is_balanced());
        assert_eq!(t.height(), 7);
        assert!(t.validate());
        assert_eq!(t.to_sorted_vec(), (0..100).collect::<Vec<_>>());
    }

    #[test]
    fn from_sorted_is_balanced_and_deduped() {
        let t = BinarySearchTree::from_sorted([3, 1, 2, 2, 3, 1]);
        assert_eq!(t.to_sorted_vec(), vec![1, 2, 3]);
        assert!(t.is_balanced());
    }

    #[test]
    fn shuffled_workload_matches_sorted_vec() {
        let mut rng = SplitMix64::new(42);
        let mut values: Vec<i32> = (0..1000).collect();
        shuffle(&mut values, &mut rng);
        let mut t: BinarySearchTree<i32> = values.iter().copied().collect();
        assert!(t.validate());
        assert_eq!(t.to_sorted_vec(), (0..1000).collect::<Vec<_>>());
        for v in values.iter().step_by(3) {
            assert!(t.remove(v));
        }
        assert!(t.validate());
        assert_eq!(t.len(), 1000 - 334);
    }

    #[test]
    fn remove_where_and_clear() {
        let mut t = sample_tree();
        assert_eq!(t.remove_where(|v| *v % 2 == 0), 7); // all are even
        assert!(t.is_empty());
        t.insert(1);
        t.clear();
        assert!(t.is_empty());
        assert_eq!(t.height(), 0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn empty_tree_behaviour() {
        let t: BinarySearchTree<i32> = BinarySearchTree::new();
        assert_eq!(t.min(), None);
        assert_eq!(t.max(), None);
        assert_eq!(t.height(), 0);
        assert!(t.is_balanced());
        assert!(t.validate());
        assert_eq!(t.in_order().count(), 0);
        assert_eq!(t.pre_order().count(), 0);
        assert_eq!(t.post_order().count(), 0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn string_keys() {
        let mut t: BinarySearchTree<String> = BinarySearchTree::new();
        for w in ["delta", "alpha", "charlie", "bravo"] {
            t.insert(w.to_string());
        }
        assert_eq!(t.to_sorted_vec(), vec!["alpha", "bravo", "charlie", "delta"]);
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/error_handling.rs ---
// error_handling.rs
//
// A self-contained demonstration of idiomatic Rust error handling:
//   * a custom error type implementing `std::error::Error`, with
//     `Display`, `Debug`, `source()`, and `From` conversions for the
//     standard library error types it wraps;
//   * a `Result`-based file-processing pipeline (read -> parse ->
//     validate -> aggregate -> report) where every fallible step
//     propagates with `?`;
//   * a hand-written CSV parser that reports precise line/column
//     positions for malformed input (unterminated quotes, stray
//     quotes, wrong field counts, bad numbers);
//   * error-context helpers (`with_context`-style), error chaining,
//     `downcast_ref`, and recovery strategies (skip-and-collect vs
//     fail-fast).
//
// Everything here is original code written for this demonstration.
// It uses only the Rust standard library and needs no crates.
//
// Run it:  rustc error_handling.rs -o error_handling && ./error_handling
// Tests:    rustc --test error_handling.rs -o error_handling_test && ./error_handling_test

use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::error::Error;
use std::fmt;
use std::fs;
use std::io;
use std::num::{ParseFloatError, ParseIntError};
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::str::Utf8Error;

// ===========================================================================
// 1. The error type
// ===========================================================================

/// Position of a problem inside a text input, 1-based lines and columns.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct Position {
    pub line: usize,
    pub column: usize,
}

impl Position {
    pub fn new(line: usize, column: usize) -> Self {
        Position { line, column }
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Position {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "line {}, column {}", self.line, self.column)
    }
}

/// Every failure the pipeline can produce, in one enum.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub enum PipelineError {
    /// An I/O failure while reading or writing a file.
    Io {
        path: PathBuf,
        source: io::Error,
    },
    /// The input was not valid UTF-8.
    InvalidUtf8 { source: Utf8Error },
    /// The CSV text was malformed at a precise position.
    Csv {
        position: Position,
        message: String,
        /// The raw line that failed, when available.
        line_text: Option<String>,
    },
    /// A field could not be parsed as an integer.
    BadInteger {
        position: Position,
        field: String,
        source: ParseIntError,
    },
    /// A field could not be parsed as a float.
    BadFloat {
        position: Position,
        field: String,
        source: ParseFloatError,
    },
    /// A row violated a business rule (wrong field count, empty key...).
    Validation {
        position: Position,
        message: String,
    },
    /// A value was outside its permitted range.
    OutOfRange {
        position: Position,
        field: String,
        value: f64,
        min: f64,
        max: f64,
    },
    /// Something unexpected happened; carries a free-form message and an
    /// optional boxed cause so callers can chain arbitrary errors.
    Other {
        message: String,
        source: Option<Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync>>,
    },
}

impl fmt::Display for PipelineError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        match self {
            PipelineError::Io { path, source } => {
                write!(f, "I/O error for {}: {}", path.display(), source)
            }
            PipelineError::InvalidUtf8 { source } => {
                write!(f, "input is not valid UTF-8: {}", source)
            }
            PipelineError::Csv {
                position,
                message,
                line_text,
            } => {
                write!(f, "CSV error at {}: {}", position, message)?;
                if let Some(text) = line_text {
                    write!(f, " (line text: {:?})", text)?;
                }
                Ok(())
            }
            PipelineError::BadInteger {
                position,
                field,
                source,
            } => write!(
                f,
                "bad integer in field {:?} at {}: {}",
                field, position, source
            ),
            PipelineError::BadFloat {
                position,
                field,
                source,
            } => write!(
                f,
                "bad float in field {:?} at {}: {}",
                field, position, source
            ),
            PipelineError::Validation { position, message } => {
                write!(f, "validation error at {}: {}", position, message)
            }
            PipelineError::OutOfRange {
                position,
                field,
                value,
                min,
                max,
            } => write!(
                f,
                "value {} for field {:?} at {} is outside [{}, {}]",
                value, field, position, min, max
            ),
            PipelineError::Other { message, .. } => write!(f, "{}", message),
        }
    }
}

impl Error for PipelineError {
    fn source(&self) -> Option<&(dyn Error + 'static)> {
        match self {
            PipelineError::Io { source, .. } => Some(source),
            PipelineError::InvalidUtf8 { source } => Some(source),
            PipelineError::BadInteger { source, .. } => Some(source),
            PipelineError::BadFloat { source, .. } => Some(source),
            PipelineError::Other {
                source: Some(cause), ..
            } => Some(cause.as_ref()),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
}

// --- `From` conversions: each wrapped error type converts automatically,
//     which is what makes `?` work on the wrapped operations. -------------

impl From<io::Error> for PipelineError {
    fn from(source: io::Error) -> Self {
        PipelineError::Other {
            message: format!("I/O error: {}", source),
            source: Some(Box::new(source)),
        }
    }
}

impl From<Utf8Error> for PipelineError {
    fn from(source: Utf8Error) -> Self {
        PipelineError::InvalidUtf8 { source }
    }
}

impl From<ParseIntError> for PipelineError {
    fn from(source: ParseIntError) -> Self {
        PipelineError::BadInteger {
            position: Position::new(0, 0),
            field: "<unknown>".to_string(),
            source,
        }
    }
}

impl From<ParseFloatError> for PipelineError {
    fn from(source: ParseFloatError) -> Self {
        PipelineError::BadFloat {
            position: Position::new(0, 0),
            field: "<unknown>".to_string(),
            source,
        }
    }
}

/// Convenience alias used throughout the pipeline.
pub type PipelineResult<T> = Result<T, PipelineError>;

// ===========================================================================
// 2. Error context helpers
// ===========================================================================

/// Attach a path to an I/O error produced inside a closure, so the caller
/// learns *which* file failed. This is the hand-rolled equivalent of the
/// `context()` helpers provided by crates such as `anyhow`.
pub fn read_file_with_path(path: &Path) -> PipelineResult<Vec<u8>> {
    fs::read(path).map_err(|source| PipelineError::Io {
        path: path.to_path_buf(),
        source,
    })
}

pub fn read_text_file(path: &Path) -> PipelineResult<String> {
    let bytes = read_file_with_path(path)?;
    String::from_utf8(bytes).map_err(|err| PipelineError::InvalidUtf8 {
        source: err.utf8_error(),
    })
}

pub fn write_text_file(path: &Path, contents: &str) -> PipelineResult<()> {
    fs::write(path, contents).map_err(|source| PipelineError::Io {
        path: path.to_path_buf(),
        source,
    })
}

/// Walk an error chain and collect every message, outermost first.
pub fn error_chain(err: &dyn Error) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut messages = vec![err.to_string()];
    let mut current = err.source();
    while let Some(cause) = current {
        messages.push(cause.to_string());
        current = cause.source();
    }
    messages
}

/// Render an error chain as a multi-line report, like the output of a
/// well-behaved command-line tool.
pub fn format_error_chain(err: &dyn Error) -> String {
    let chain = error_chain(err);
    let mut out = String::new();
    for (i, message) in chain.iter().enumerate() {
        if i == 0 {
            out.push_str(&format!("error: {}\n", message));
        } else {
            out.push_str(&format!("caused by: {}\n", message));
        }
    }
    out
}

// ===========================================================================
// 3. The CSV parser
// ===========================================================================

/// One parsed record: the fields plus the line it came from, so later
/// stages can report errors against the original input position.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct CsvRecord {
    pub fields: Vec<String>,
    pub line: usize,
}

/// Parser configuration. Kept deliberately small: delimiter, whether a
/// header row is expected, and whether blank lines are skipped.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct CsvConfig {
    pub delimiter: char,
    pub has_header: bool,
    pub skip_blank_lines: bool,
    pub trim_fields: bool,
}

impl Default for CsvConfig {
    fn default() -> Self {
        CsvConfig {
            delimiter: ',',
            has_header: true,
            skip_blank_lines: true,
            trim_fields: false,
        }
    }
}

/// The parser itself. It is a small state machine over characters:
///
///   * `StartField`  -- at the beginning of a field
///   * `InField`     -- inside an unquoted field
///   * `InQuoted`    -- inside a quoted field
///   * `InQuotedQuote` -- just saw a `"` inside a quoted field; a second
///     `"` is an escaped quote, anything else ends the quoted field
///
/// Line and column are tracked character by character, and every error
/// carries the position where it was detected.
pub struct CsvParser {
    config: CsvConfig,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum ParseState {
    StartField,
    InField,
    InQuoted,
    InQuotedQuote,
}

impl CsvParser {
    pub fn new(config: CsvConfig) -> Self {
        CsvParser { config }
    }

    /// Parse a whole document. Returns the header (if configured) and the
    /// data records. Fails fast on the first malformed construct.
    pub fn parse(&self, input: &str) -> PipelineResult<(Vec<String>, Vec<CsvRecord>)> {
        let mut records: Vec<CsvRecord> = Vec::new();
        let mut fields: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
        let mut current = String::new();
        let mut state = ParseState::StartField;
        let mut line: usize = 1;
        let mut column: usize = 1;
        let mut field_start_column: usize = 1;
        let mut record_start_line: usize = 1;
        // The position where the current quoted field opened, for errors.
        let mut quote_column: usize = 0;
        let mut quote_line: usize = 0;
        // Remember the text of the line currently being parsed, for errors.
        let mut current_line_text = String::new();

        let mut chars = input.chars().peekable();
        while let Some(ch) = chars.next() {
            let mut crlf = false;
            // Track the raw line text (without the newline itself).
            if ch != '\n' && ch != '\r' {
                current_line_text.push(ch);
            }

            match state {
                ParseState::StartField => {
                    if ch == '"' {
                        state = ParseState::InQuoted;
                        quote_column = column;
                        quote_line = line;
                        field_start_column = column;
                    } else if ch == self.config.delimiter {
                        self.finish_field(&mut fields, &mut current);
                        field_start_column = column + 1;
                    } else if ch == '\n' || ch == '\r' {
                        self.finish_record(
                            &mut records,
                            &mut fields,
                            &mut current,
                            record_start_line,
                        )?;
                        record_start_line = line + 1;
                        current_line_text.clear();
                        // Tolerate CRLF: a '\r' immediately followed by
                        // '\n' is one line ending, not two.
                        if ch == '\r' && chars.peek() == Some(&'\n') {
                            chars.next();
                            crlf = true;
                        }
                    } else {
                        current.push(ch);
                        state = ParseState::InField;
                        field_start_column = column;
                    }
                }
                ParseState::InField => {
                    if ch == self.config.delimiter {
                        self.finish_field(&mut fields, &mut current);
                        state = ParseState::StartField;
                        field_start_column = column + 1;
                    } else if ch == '\n' || ch == '\r' {
                        self.finish_record(
                            &mut records,
                            &mut fields,
                            &mut current,
                            record_start_line,
                        )?;
                        state = ParseState::StartField;
                        record_start_line = line + 1;
                        current_line_text.clear();
                        if ch == '\r' && chars.peek() == Some(&'\n') {
                            chars.next();
                            crlf = true;
                        }
                    } else if ch == '"' {
                        // A quote in the middle of an unquoted field.
