                        return Err(PipelineError::Csv {
                            position: Position::new(line, column),
                            message: "unexpected '\"' inside an unquoted field; \
                                      quote the whole field to include quotes"
                                .to_string(),
                            line_text: Some(current_line_text.clone()),
                        });
                    } else {
                        current.push(ch);
                    }
                }
                ParseState::InQuoted => {
                    if ch == '"' {
                        state = ParseState::InQuotedQuote;
                    } else {
                        // Newlines are legal inside quoted fields; the
                        // record's line number is where it *started*.
                        current.push(ch);
                    }
                }
                ParseState::InQuotedQuote => {
                    if ch == '"' {
                        // Doubled quote -> a literal quote character.
                        current.push('"');
                        state = ParseState::InQuoted;
                    } else if ch == self.config.delimiter {
                        self.finish_field(&mut fields, &mut current);
                        state = ParseState::StartField;
                        field_start_column = column + 1;
                    } else if ch == '\n' || ch == '\r' {
                        self.finish_record(
                            &mut records,
                            &mut fields,
                            &mut current,
                            record_start_line,
                        )?;
                        state = ParseState::StartField;
                        record_start_line = line + 1;
                        current_line_text.clear();
                        if ch == '\r' && chars.peek() == Some(&'\n') {
                            chars.next();
                            crlf = true;
                        }
                    } else {
                        return Err(PipelineError::Csv {
                            position: Position::new(line, column),
                            message: format!(
                                "expected delimiter, newline or '\"' after closing quote, found {:?}",
                                ch
                            ),
                            line_text: Some(current_line_text.clone()),
                        });
                    }
                }
            }

            if ch == '\n' || crlf {
                line += 1;
                column = 1;
            } else {
                column += 1;
            }
            // Keep the field-start column honest after a newline inside a
            // quoted field: it is only used for diagnostics, and the
            // record line is what matters there.
            let _ = field_start_column;
        }

        // End of input: the parser must not be mid-quote, and any pending
        // field/record must be flushed.
        match state {
            ParseState::InQuoted => {
                return Err(PipelineError::Csv {
                    position: Position::new(quote_line, quote_column),
                    message: "unterminated quoted field: reached end of input \
                              while still inside quotes"
                        .to_string(),
                    line_text: Some(current_line_text.clone()),
                });
            }
            ParseState::InQuotedQuote | ParseState::InField | ParseState::StartField => {
                if state != ParseState::StartField || !current.is_empty() || !fields.is_empty() {
                    self.finish_record(
                        &mut records,
                        &mut fields,
                        &mut current,
                        record_start_line,
                    )?;
                }
            }
        }

        // Blank-line handling: a record whose only field is empty and which
        // came from an empty line is dropped when configured.
        if self.config.skip_blank_lines {
            records.retain(|record| {
                !(record.fields.len() == 1 && record.fields[0].is_empty())
            });
        }

        if records.is_empty() {
            return Err(PipelineError::Csv {
                position: Position::new(1, 1),
                message: "no data records found in input".to_string(),
                line_text: None,
            });
        }

        let mut header = Vec::new();
        if self.config.has_header {
            header = records.remove(0).fields;
        }
        Ok((header, records))
    }

    fn finish_field(&self, fields: &mut Vec<String>, current: &mut String) {
        let mut value = std::mem::take(current);
        if self.config.trim_fields {
            value = value.trim().to_string();
        }
        fields.push(value);
    }

    fn finish_record(
        &self,
        records: &mut Vec<CsvRecord>,
        fields: &mut Vec<String>,
        current: &mut String,
        line: usize,
    ) -> PipelineResult<()> {
        self.finish_field(fields, current);
        let taken = std::mem::take(fields);
        records.push(CsvRecord {
            fields: taken,
            line,
        });
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Parse and additionally require every record to have exactly
    /// `expected_fields` fields, reporting the offending row precisely.
    pub fn parse_strict(
        &self,
        input: &str,
        expected_fields: usize,
    ) -> PipelineResult<(Vec<String>, Vec<CsvRecord>)> {
        let (header, records) = self.parse(input)?;
        if header.len() != expected_fields {
            return Err(PipelineError::Validation {
                position: Position::new(1, 1),
                message: format!(
                    "header has {} fields, expected {}",
                    header.len(),
                    expected_fields
                ),
            });
        }
        for record in &records {
            if record.fields.len() != expected_fields {
                return Err(PipelineError::Validation {
                    position: Position::new(record.line, 1),
                    message: format!(
                        "row has {} fields, expected {}",
                        record.fields.len(),
                        expected_fields
                    ),
                });
            }
        }
        Ok((header, records))
    }
}

// ===========================================================================
// 4. A typed row and the processing pipeline
// ===========================================================================

/// One validated sales row: region, product, units sold, unit price.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Sale {
    pub region: String,
    pub product: String,
    pub units: u32,
    pub unit_price: f64,
    /// The source line, kept for error reporting downstream.
    pub line: usize,
}

impl Sale {
    pub fn revenue(&self) -> f64 {
        self.units as f64 * self.unit_price
    }
}

/// Convert one raw record into a `Sale`, validating every field and
/// reporting the exact position of the first problem found.
pub fn record_to_sale(record: &CsvRecord) -> PipelineResult<Sale> {
    let pos = |column: usize| Position::new(record.line, column);
    let field = |index: usize| -> PipelineResult<&str> {
        record.fields.get(index).map(|s| s.as_str()).ok_or_else(|| {
            PipelineError::Validation {
                position: pos(1),
                message: format!(
                    "missing field {} (row has {} fields)",
                    index + 1,
                    record.fields.len()
                ),
            }
        })
    };

    let region = field(0)?.trim().to_string();
    if region.is_empty() {
        return Err(PipelineError::Validation {
            position: pos(1),
            message: "region must not be empty".to_string(),
        });
    }
    let product = field(1)?.trim().to_string();
    if product.is_empty() {
        return Err(PipelineError::Validation {
            position: pos(2),
            message: "product must not be empty".to_string(),
        });
    }
    let units_text = field(2)?.trim();
    let units: u32 = units_text.parse().map_err(|source| PipelineError::BadInteger {
        position: pos(3),
        field: units_text.to_string(),
        source,
    })?;
    if units == 0 {
        return Err(PipelineError::Validation {
            position: pos(3),
            message: "units must be greater than zero".to_string(),
        });
    }
    let price_text = field(3)?.trim();
    let unit_price: f64 = price_text.parse().map_err(|source| PipelineError::BadFloat {
        position: pos(4),
        field: price_text.to_string(),
        source,
    })?;
    if !(0.0..=10_000.0).contains(&unit_price) {
        return Err(PipelineError::OutOfRange {
            position: pos(4),
            field: "unit_price".to_string(),
            value: unit_price,
            min: 0.0,
            max: 10_000.0,
        });
    }

    Ok(Sale {
        region,
        product,
        units,
        unit_price,
        line: record.line,
    })
}

/// Aggregated results: totals per region, per product, and overall.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct SalesSummary {
    pub total_rows: usize,
    pub total_units: u64,
    pub total_revenue: f64,
    pub by_region: HashMap<String, (u64, f64)>,
    pub by_product: HashMap<String, (u64, f64)>,
}

impl SalesSummary {
    pub fn add(&mut self, sale: &Sale) {
        self.total_rows += 1;
        self.total_units += sale.units as u64;
        self.total_revenue += sale.revenue();
        let entry = self.by_region.entry(sale.region.clone()).or_default();
        entry.0 += sale.units as u64;
        entry.1 += sale.revenue();
        let entry = self.by_product.entry(sale.product.clone()).or_default();
        entry.0 += sale.units as u64;
        entry.1 += sale.revenue();
    }

    /// Render a human-readable report, regions and products sorted by
    /// revenue descending so the most important lines come first.
    pub fn render(&self) -> String {
        let mut out = String::new();
        out.push_str("Sales summary\n");
        out.push_str("-------------\n");
        out.push_str(&format!("rows:          {}\n", self.total_rows));
        out.push_str(&format!("total units:   {}\n", self.total_units));
        out.push_str(&format!("total revenue: ${:.2}\n", self.total_revenue));
        out.push_str("\nBy region (revenue descending):\n");
        let mut regions: Vec<_> = self.by_region.iter().collect();
        regions.sort_by(|a, b| b.1.1.partial_cmp(&a.1.1).unwrap_or(std::cmp::Ordering::Equal));
        for (region, (units, revenue)) in regions {
            out.push_str(&format!("  {:<12} {:>6} units  ${:>10.2}\n", region, units, revenue));
        }
        out.push_str("\nBy product (revenue descending):\n");
        let mut products: Vec<_> = self.by_product.iter().collect();
        products.sort_by(|a, b| b.1.1.partial_cmp(&a.1.1).unwrap_or(std::cmp::Ordering::Equal));
        for (product, (units, revenue)) in products {
            out.push_str(&format!("  {:<12} {:>6} units  ${:>10.2}\n", product, units, revenue));
        }
        out
    }
}

// --- Pipeline stages -------------------------------------------------------

/// Stage 1: read and parse the CSV document into typed rows (fail fast).
pub fn load_sales(input: &str) -> PipelineResult<Vec<Sale>> {
    let parser = CsvParser::new(CsvConfig::default());
    let (_header, records) = parser.parse_strict(input, 4)?;
    let mut sales = Vec::with_capacity(records.len());
    for record in &records {
        sales.push(record_to_sale(record)?);
    }
    Ok(sales)
}

/// Stage 2: aggregate typed rows into a summary.
pub fn summarise(sales: &[Sale]) -> SalesSummary {
    let mut summary = SalesSummary::default();
    for sale in sales {
        summary.add(sale);
    }
    summary
}

/// The whole pipeline for an in-memory document: parse, validate, aggregate.
pub fn process_sales_text(input: &str) -> PipelineResult<SalesSummary> {
    let sales = load_sales(input)?;
    Ok(summarise(&sales))
}

/// The whole pipeline for a file on disk: read, then process, then write
/// the report next to it. Every stage propagates errors with `?`, and the
/// caller sees a single `PipelineError` with the full chain attached.
pub fn process_sales_file(input_path: &Path, output_path: &Path) -> PipelineResult<SalesSummary> {
    let text = read_text_file(input_path)?;
    let summary = process_sales_text(&text)?;
    write_text_file(output_path, &summary.render())?;
    Ok(summary)
}

// --- Recovery strategy: skip-and-collect -----------------------------------

/// Outcome of the lenient pipeline: the good rows plus every error that
/// was found, so a caller can report all problems at once instead of
/// stopping at the first one.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct LenientOutcome {
    pub sales: Vec<Sale>,
    pub errors: Vec<PipelineError>,
    pub skipped_rows: usize,
}

/// Parse leniently: keep every row that validates, collect the errors for
/// the rows that do not, and never fail on a single bad row. Structural
/// CSV errors (unterminated quote etc.) still abort, because the rest of
/// the document cannot be trusted after one.
pub fn load_sales_lenient(input: &str) -> PipelineResult<LenientOutcome> {
    let parser = CsvParser::new(CsvConfig::default());
    let (_header, records) = parser.parse(input)?;
    let mut outcome = LenientOutcome::default();
    for record in &records {
        match record_to_sale(record) {
            Ok(sale) => outcome.sales.push(sale),
            Err(err) => {
                outcome.skipped_rows += 1;
                outcome.errors.push(err);
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(outcome)
}

// --- Recovery strategy: fall back to a default ------------------------------

/// Try the primary path; if the input file is missing, fall back to a
/// built-in sample instead of failing. Other errors still propagate.
/// This is the `or_else` / `unwrap_or_else` pattern at pipeline scale.
pub fn load_with_fallback(path: &Path, fallback: &str) -> PipelineResult<Vec<Sale>> {
    match read_text_file(path) {
        Ok(text) => load_sales(&text),
        Err(PipelineError::Io { source, .. }) if source.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
            load_sales(fallback)
        }
        Err(other) => Err(other),
    }
}

// ===========================================================================
// 5. Demonstration driver
// ===========================================================================

const SAMPLE_CSV: &str = "\
region,product,units,unit_price
North,Widget,10,2.50
South,Gadget,4,19.99
North,Gadget,7,19.99
\"East, Coastal\",Widget,3,2.50
South,\"Super \"\"Deluxe\"\" Gadget\",2,49.95
West,Widget,25,2.50
";

const BAD_CSV_UNTERMINATED: &str = "\
region,product,units,unit_price
North,Widget,10,2.50
South,\"Broken Gadget,4,19.99
";

const BAD_CSV_NUMBERS: &str = "\
region,product,units,unit_price
North,Widget,ten,2.50
South,Gadget,4,free
West,Widget,0,2.50
East,Widget,5,99999.00
";

fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn Error>> {
    // -- Demo 1: the happy path, end to end. -------------------------------
    println!("=== Demo 1: happy path ===");
    let summary = process_sales_text(SAMPLE_CSV)?;
    println!("{}", summary.render());
    assert_eq!(summary.total_rows, 6);
    assert_eq!(summary.total_units, 51);
    // 10*2.50 + 4*19.99 + 7*19.99 + 3*2.50 + 2*49.95 + 25*2.50
    let expected_revenue = 25.0 + 79.96 + 139.93 + 7.50 + 99.90 + 62.50;
    assert!((summary.total_revenue - expected_revenue).abs() < 0.001);
    assert_eq!(summary.by_region["North"].0, 17);
    assert_eq!(summary.by_product["Widget"].0, 38);

    // -- Demo 2: a structural CSV error, with position and chain. ----------
    println!("=== Demo 2: unterminated quote ===");
    match process_sales_text(BAD_CSV_UNTERMINATED) {
        Ok(_) => panic!("expected the unterminated-quote input to fail"),
        Err(err) => {
            println!("{}", format_error_chain(&err));
            // The error must point at the quoted field that never closed.
            let text = err.to_string();
            assert!(text.contains("unterminated"), "message: {}", text);
            assert!(text.contains("line 3"), "message: {}", text);
            // And the chain must be walkable through `source()`.
            assert!(error_chain(&err).len() >= 1);
        }
    }

    // -- Demo 3: lenient mode collects every row error at once. ------------
    println!("=== Demo 3: lenient parsing collects all row errors ===");
    let outcome = load_sales_lenient(BAD_CSV_NUMBERS)?;
    assert_eq!(outcome.sales.len(), 0);
    assert_eq!(outcome.errors.len(), 4);
    for err in &outcome.errors {
        println!("  skipped row: {}", err);
    }
    assert!(outcome.errors[0].to_string().contains("bad integer"));
    assert!(outcome.errors[1].to_string().contains("bad float"));
    assert!(outcome.errors[2].to_string().contains("greater than zero"));
    assert!(outcome.errors[3].to_string().contains("outside"));

    // -- Demo 4: the same errors, but with `downcast_ref` inspection. ------
    println!("=== Demo 4: inspecting the error type ===");
    let boxed: Box<dyn Error> = Box::new(outcome.errors[0].clone_for_demo());
    let _ = boxed; // (cloning is only possible because PipelineError is Debug+Clone-free here)
    let err = &outcome.errors[0];
    match err {
        PipelineError::BadInteger { field, .. } => {
            assert_eq!(field, "ten");
            println!("  first error is BadInteger for field {:?}", field);
        }
        other => panic!("expected BadInteger, got {:?}", other),
    }
    // `source()` on a BadInteger yields the underlying ParseIntError.
    let cause = err.source().expect("BadInteger must have a source");
    println!("  underlying cause: {}", cause);

    // -- Demo 5: file pipeline with a real temp file, plus fallback. -------
    println!("=== Demo 5: file pipeline and missing-file fallback ===");
    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("error_handling_demo");
    fs::create_dir_all(&dir)?;
    let input_path = dir.join("sales.csv");
    let output_path = dir.join("summary.txt");
    write_text_file(&input_path, SAMPLE_CSV)?;
    let summary = process_sales_file(&input_path, &output_path)?;
    assert_eq!(summary.total_rows, 6);
    let report = read_text_file(&output_path)?;
    assert!(report.contains("total revenue: $414.79"));
    println!("  wrote report to {}", output_path.display());

    // Missing input file -> fallback sample is used instead of failing.
    let missing = dir.join("does-not-exist.csv");
    let sales = load_with_fallback(&missing, SAMPLE_CSV)?;
    assert_eq!(sales.len(), 6);
    println!("  fallback loaded {} rows for missing file", sales.len());

    // A genuinely missing file with no fallback still errors, and the
    // error names the path.
    match read_text_file(&missing) {
        Err(PipelineError::Io { path, source }) => {
            assert_eq!(path, missing);
            assert_eq!(source.kind(), io::ErrorKind::NotFound);
            println!("  missing file error names its path: {}", path.display());
        }
        other => panic!("expected Io error, got {:?}", other),
    }

    // -- Demo 6: `?` inside a closure via `map`/`and_then` chains. --------
    println!("=== Demo 6: Result combinators ===");
    let numbers: PipelineResult<Vec<u32>> = ["3", "7", "11"]
        .iter()
        .map(|text| {
            text.parse::<u32>().map_err(|source| PipelineError::BadInteger {
                position: Position::new(1, 1),
                field: text.to_string(),
                source,
            })
        })
        .collect();
    assert_eq!(numbers?, vec![3, 7, 11]);
    let bad: PipelineResult<Vec<u32>> = ["3", "oops", "11"]
        .iter()
        .map(|text| {
            text.parse::<u32>().map_err(|source| PipelineError::BadInteger {
                position: Position::new(1, 1),
                field: text.to_string(),
                source,
            })
        })
        .collect();
    assert!(bad.is_err());

    println!("\nAll error_handling demos passed.");
    Ok(())
}

// PipelineError is not Clone (it holds a boxed trait object), so the demo
// above needs a small helper to obtain an owned copy of a specific variant
// for the downcasting illustration. It is defined here, after `main`, to
// keep the demo flow readable.
impl PipelineError {
    fn clone_for_demo(&self) -> PipelineError {
        match self {
            PipelineError::BadInteger {
                position,
                field,
                source,
            } => PipelineError::BadInteger {
                position: *position,
                field: field.clone(),
                source: source.clone(),
            },
            PipelineError::BadFloat {
                position,
                field,
                source,
            } => PipelineError::BadFloat {
                position: *position,
                field: field.clone(),
                source: source.clone(),
            },
            PipelineError::Validation { position, message } => PipelineError::Validation {
                position: *position,
                message: message.clone(),
            },
            PipelineError::Other { message, .. } => PipelineError::Other {
                message: message.clone(),
                source: None,
            },
            other => PipelineError::Other {
                message: other.to_string(),
                source: None,
            },
        }
    }
}

// ===========================================================================
// 6. Unit tests
// ===========================================================================

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn parser() -> CsvParser {
        CsvParser::new(CsvConfig::default())
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_plain_rows() {
        let (header, records) = parser().parse("a,b,c\n1,2,3\n4,5,6\n").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(header, vec!["a", "b", "c"]);
        assert_eq!(records.len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(records[0].fields, vec!["1", "2", "3"]);
        assert_eq!(records[1].line, 3);
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_quoted_fields_with_commas_and_escaped_quotes() {
        let (_h, records) = parser()
            .parse("name,note\n\"Doe, Jane\",\"said \"\"hi\"\" loudly\"\n")
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(records[0].fields[0], "Doe, Jane");
        assert_eq!(records[0].fields[1], "said \"hi\" loudly");
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_multiline_quoted_fields() {
        let (_h, records) = parser()
            .parse("a,b\n\"line one\nline two\",2\n")
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(records[0].fields[0], "line one\nline two");
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_crlf_and_empty_fields() {
        let (_h, records) = parser().parse("a,b,c\r\n,2,\r\n").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(records[0].fields, vec!["", "2", ""]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn skips_blank_lines() {
        let (_h, records) = parser().parse("a,b\n1,2\n\n3,4\n").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(records.len(), 2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_unterminated_quote_with_position() {
        let err = parser().parse("a,b\n\"oops,2\n").unwrap_err();
        match err {
            PipelineError::Csv { position, .. } => {
                assert_eq!(position.line, 2);
            }
            other => panic!("wrong error: {:?}", other),
        }
        assert!(err.to_string().contains("unterminated"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_stray_quote_in_unquoted_field() {
        let err = parser().parse("a,b\nhe\"llo,2\n").unwrap_err();
        assert!(err.to_string().contains("unexpected"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_junk_after_closing_quote() {
        let err = parser().parse("a,b\n\"x\"y,2\n").unwrap_err();
        assert!(err.to_string().contains("expected delimiter"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn strict_mode_rejects_wrong_field_count() {
        let err = parser()
            .parse_strict("a,b,c\n1,2\n", 3)
            .unwrap_err();
        match err {
            PipelineError::Validation { position, .. } => assert_eq!(position.line, 2),
            other => panic!("wrong error: {:?}", other),
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn record_to_sale_validates_every_field() {
        let good = CsvRecord {
            fields: vec![
                "North".into(),
                "Widget".into(),
                "10".into(),
                "2.50".into(),
            ],
            line: 5,
        };
        let sale = record_to_sale(&good).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(sale.units, 10);
        assert!((sale.revenue() - 25.0).abs() < 1e-9);

        let bad_units = CsvRecord {
            fields: vec!["N".into(), "W".into(), "x".into(), "1".into()],
            line: 6,
        };
        let err = record_to_sale(&bad_units).unwrap_err();
        assert!(matches!(err, PipelineError::BadInteger { .. }));
        assert!(err.source().is_some());

        let bad_price = CsvRecord {
            fields: vec!["N".into(), "W".into(), "1".into(), "20000".into()],
            line: 7,
        };
        assert!(matches!(
            record_to_sale(&bad_price).unwrap_err(),
            PipelineError::OutOfRange { .. }
        ));
    }

    #[test]
    fn summary_aggregates_by_region_and_product() {
        let summary = process_sales_text(SAMPLE_CSV).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(summary.total_rows, 6);
        assert_eq!(summary.by_region["South"].0, 6);
        assert!((summary.by_region["South"].1 - 179.86).abs() < 0.01);
        assert_eq!(summary.by_product.len(), 3);
    }

    #[test]
    fn lenient_mode_collects_errors_without_aborting() {
        let outcome = load_sales_lenient(BAD_CSV_NUMBERS).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(outcome.sales.len(), 0);
        assert_eq!(outcome.errors.len(), 4);
        assert_eq!(outcome.skipped_rows, 4);
    }

    #[test]
    fn error_chain_walks_sources() {
        let err = record_to_sale(&CsvRecord {
            fields: vec!["N".into(), "W".into(), "nope".into(), "1".into()],
            line: 2,
        })
        .unwrap_err();
        let chain = error_chain(&err);
        assert_eq!(chain.len(), 2);
        assert!(chain[0].contains("bad integer"));
        assert!(chain[1].contains("invalid digit"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn from_io_error_converts() {
        let io_err = io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::NotFound, "gone");
        let converted = PipelineError::from(io_err);
        assert!(converted.to_string().contains("I/O error"));
        assert!(converted.source().is_some());
    }

    #[test]
    fn from_parse_int_error_converts() {
        let parse_err = "abc".parse::<u32>().unwrap_err();
        let converted = PipelineError::from(parse_err);
        assert!(matches!(converted, PipelineError::BadInteger { .. }));
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/expression_lang.rs ---
// expression_lang.rs — A small expression language, written from scratch:
// lexer + recursive-descent parser + tree-walking evaluator.
// Features:
//   * numbers (integers and floats), strings with escapes, booleans, nil
//   * arrays [1, 2, 3] with indexing arr[0] and len(arr)
//   * variables and `let` bindings, assignment `x = expr`
//   * arithmetic + - * / % , comparison == != < <= > >=, logic && || !
//   * `if cond { ... } else { ... }` as an expression
//   * `fn name(a, b) { ... }` declarations, first-class closures,
//     recursion, and `return`
//   * blocks `{ ... }` as expressions, `//` and `/* */` comments
//   * built-ins: len, print, str, num, push, type_of
//   * a REPL-style demo in main() that evaluates programs and asserts
//     the results, plus a `run()` helper used by the unit tests.
// Standard library only. No code is copied from any book or tutorial.

use std::cell::RefCell;
use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::fmt;
use std::rc::Rc;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Lexer
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum TokenKind {
    Number(f64),
    Str(String),
    Ident(String),
    // Keywords
    Let,
    Fn,
    If,
    Else,
    Return,
    True,
    False,
    Nil,
    // Punctuation / operators
    Plus,
    Minus,
    Star,
    Slash,
    Percent,
    Bang,
    Eq,
    EqEq,
    NotEq,
    Lt,
    LtEq,
    Gt,
    GtEq,
    AndAnd,
    OrOr,
    LParen,
    RParen,
    LBrace,
    RBrace,
    LBracket,
    RBracket,
    Comma,
    Semicolon,
    Eof,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Token {
    pub kind: TokenKind,
    pub line: usize,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct LexError {
    pub message: String,
    pub line: usize,
}
impl fmt::Display for LexError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "lex error on line {}: {}", self.line, self.message)
    }
}
impl std::error::Error for LexError {}

pub struct Lexer {
    chars: Vec<char>,
    pos: usize,
    line: usize,
}

impl Lexer {
    pub fn new(source: &str) -> Self {
        Self { chars: source.chars().collect(), pos: 0, line: 1 }
    }
    fn peek(&self) -> Option<char> {
        self.chars.get(self.pos).copied()
    }
    fn peek_at(&self, off: usize) -> Option<char> {
        self.chars.get(self.pos + off).copied()
    }
    fn advance(&mut self) -> Option<char> {
        let c = self.peek();
        if let Some(c) = c {
            self.pos += 1;
            if c == '\n' {
                self.line += 1;
            }
        }
        c
    }
    fn err(&self, msg: impl Into<String>) -> LexError {
        LexError { message: msg.into(), line: self.line }
    }

    pub fn tokenize(&mut self) -> Result<Vec<Token>, LexError> {
        let mut tokens = Vec::new();
        loop {
            self.skip_space_and_comments()?;
            let line = self.line;
            let c = match self.peek() {
                None => {
                    tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::Eof, line });
                    break;
                }
                Some(c) => c,
            };
            let kind = match c {
                '0'..='9' => self.number()?,
                '"' => TokenKind::Str(self.string()?),
                'a'..='z' | 'A'..='Z' | '_' => {
                    let ident = self.identifier();
                    match ident.as_str() {
                        "let" => TokenKind::Let,
                        "fn" => TokenKind::Fn,
                        "if" => TokenKind::If,
                        "else" => TokenKind::Else,
                        "return" => TokenKind::Return,
                        "true" => TokenKind::True,
                        "false" => TokenKind::False,
                        "nil" => TokenKind::Nil,
                        _ => TokenKind::Ident(ident),
                    }
                }
                '+' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::Plus
                }
                '-' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::Minus
                }
                '*' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::Star
                }
                '/' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::Slash
                }
                '%' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::Percent
                }
                '(' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::LParen
                }
                ')' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::RParen
                }
                '{' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::LBrace
                }
                '}' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::RBrace
                }
                '[' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::LBracket
                }
                ']' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::RBracket
                }
                ',' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::Comma
                }
                ';' => {
                    self.advance();
                    TokenKind::Semicolon
                }
                '!' => {
                    self.advance();
                    if self.peek() == Some('=') {
                        self.advance();
                        TokenKind::NotEq
                    } else {
                        TokenKind::Bang
                    }
                }
                '=' => {
                    self.advance();
                    if self.peek() == Some('=') {
                        self.advance();
                        TokenKind::EqEq
                    } else {
                        TokenKind::Eq
                    }
                }
                '<' => {
                    self.advance();
                    if self.peek() == Some('=') {
                        self.advance();
                        TokenKind::LtEq
                    } else {
                        TokenKind::Lt
                    }
                }
                '>' => {
                    self.advance();
                    if self.peek() == Some('=') {
                        self.advance();
                        TokenKind::GtEq
                    } else {
                        TokenKind::Gt
                    }
                }
                '&' => {
                    self.advance();
                    if self.peek() == Some('&') {
                        self.advance();
                        TokenKind::AndAnd
                    } else {
                        return Err(self.err("single '&' is not an operator; use '&&'"));
                    }
                }
                '|' => {
                    self.advance();
                    if self.peek() == Some('|') {
                        self.advance();
                        TokenKind::OrOr
                    } else {
                        return Err(self.err("single '|' is not an operator; use '||'"));
                    }
                }
                other => return Err(self.err(format!("unexpected character {:?}", other))),
            };
            tokens.push(Token { kind, line });
        }
        Ok(tokens)
    }

    fn skip_space_and_comments(&mut self) -> Result<(), LexError> {
        loop {
            match self.peek() {
                Some(c) if c.is_whitespace() => {
                    self.advance();
                }
                Some('/') if self.peek_at(1) == Some('/') => {
                    while let Some(c) = self.peek() {
                        if c == '\n' {
                            break;
                        }
                        self.advance();
                    }
                }
                Some('/') if self.peek_at(1) == Some('*') => {
                    self.advance();
                    self.advance();
                    let mut depth = 1usize;
                    while depth > 0 {
                        match self.peek() {
                            None => return Err(self.err("unterminated block comment")),
                            Some('/') if self.peek_at(1) == Some('*') => {
                                self.advance();
                                self.advance();
                                depth += 1;
                            }
                            Some('*') if self.peek_at(1) == Some('/') => {
                                self.advance();
                                self.advance();
                                depth -= 1;
                            }
                            _ => {
                                self.advance();
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
                _ => break,
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn number(&mut self) -> Result<TokenKind, LexError> {
        let mut text = String::new();
        while let Some(c) = self.peek() {
            if c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '_' {
                if c != '_' {
                    text.push(c);
                }
                self.advance();
            } else {
                break;
            }
        }
        // Fractional part.
        if self.peek() == Some('.') && self.peek_at(1).map_or(false, |c| c.is_ascii_digit()) {
            text.push('.');
            self.advance();
            while let Some(c) = self.peek() {
                if c.is_ascii_digit() {
                    text.push(c);
                    self.advance();
                } else {
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
        // Exponent.
        if matches!(self.peek(), Some('e') | Some('E')) {
            let after = self.peek_at(1);
            if after.map_or(false, |c| c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '+' || c == '-') {
                text.push(self.advance().unwrap());
                if matches!(self.peek(), Some('+') | Some('-')) {
                    text.push(self.advance().unwrap());
                }
                while let Some(c) = self.peek() {
                    if c.is_ascii_digit() {
                        text.push(c);
                        self.advance();
                    } else {
                        break;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        text.parse::<f64>().map(TokenKind::Number).map_err(|_| self.err("invalid number"))
    }

    fn string(&mut self) -> Result<String, LexError> {
        self.advance(); // opening quote
        let mut out = String::new();
        loop {
            match self.advance() {
                None => return Err(self.err("unterminated string")),
                Some('"') => break,
                Some('\\') => match self.advance() {
                    None => return Err(self.err("unterminated string")),
                    Some('n') => out.push('\n'),
                    Some('t') => out.push('\t'),
                    Some('r') => out.push('\r'),
                    Some('\\') => out.push('\\'),
                    Some('"') => out.push('"'),
                    Some('0') => out.push('\0'),
                    Some(other) => {
                        return Err(self.err(format!("unknown string escape \\{}", other)));
                    }
                },
                Some(c) => out.push(c),
            }
        }
        Ok(out)
    }

    fn identifier(&mut self) -> String {
        let mut out = String::new();
        while let Some(c) = self.peek() {
            if c.is_alphanumeric() || c == '_' {
                out.push(c);
                self.advance();
            } else {
                break;
            }
        }
        out
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// AST
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum Expr {
    Number(f64),
    Str(String),
    Bool(bool),
    Nil,
    Var(String),
    Array(Vec<Expr>),
    Index { target: Box<Expr>, index: Box<Expr> },
    Unary { op: UnaryOp, expr: Box<Expr> },
    Binary { op: BinOp, left: Box<Expr>, right: Box<Expr> },
    Assign { name: String, value: Box<Expr> },
    If { cond: Box<Expr>, then_branch: Vec<Stmt>, else_branch: Option<Vec<Stmt>> },
    Block(Vec<Stmt>),
    Call { callee: Box<Expr>, args: Vec<Expr> },
    Closure { params: Vec<String>, body: Vec<Stmt> },
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum UnaryOp {
    Neg,
    Not,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum BinOp {
    Add,
    Sub,
    Mul,
    Div,
    Mod,
    Eq,
    NotEq,
    Lt,
    LtEq,
    Gt,
    GtEq,
    And,
    Or,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum Stmt {
    Let { name: String, value: Expr },
    FnDecl { name: String, params: Vec<String>, body: Vec<Stmt> },
    Return(Option<Expr>),
    Expr(Expr),
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Parser (recursive descent, precedence climbing by method per level)
// Precedence, lowest to highest:
//   assignment (=, right-assoc) -> or -> and -> equality -> comparison
//   -> additive -> multiplicative -> unary -> postfix (call/index) -> primary
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct ParseError {
    pub message: String,
    pub line: usize,
}
impl fmt::Display for ParseError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "parse error on line {}: {}", self.line, self.message)
    }
}
impl std::error::Error for ParseError {}

pub struct Parser {
    tokens: Vec<Token>,
    pos: usize,
}

impl Parser {
    pub fn new(tokens: Vec<Token>) -> Self {
        Self { tokens, pos: 0 }
    }
    fn peek(&self) -> &TokenKind {
        &self.tokens.get(self.pos).map(|t| &t.kind).unwrap_or(&TokenKind::Eof)
    }
    fn peek_line(&self) -> usize {
        self.tokens.get(self.pos).map(|t| t.line).unwrap_or(0)
    }
    fn advance(&mut self) -> Token {
        let t = self.tokens.get(self.pos).cloned().unwrap_or(Token { kind: TokenKind::Eof, line: 0 });
        if self.pos < self.tokens.len() {
            self.pos += 1;
        }
        t
    }
    fn check(&self, kind: &TokenKind) -> bool {
        self.peek() == kind
    }
    fn eat(&mut self, kind: &TokenKind) -> bool {
        if self.check(kind) {
            self.advance();
            true
        } else {
            false
        }
    }
    fn expect(&mut self, kind: &TokenKind, what: &str) -> Result<Token, ParseError> {
        if self.check(kind) {
            Ok(self.advance())
        } else {
            Err(ParseError {
                message: format!("expected {}, found {:?}", what, self.peek()),
                line: self.peek_line(),
            })
        }
    }
    fn err(&self, msg: impl Into<String>) -> ParseError {
        ParseError { message: msg.into(), line: self.peek_line() }
    }

    pub fn parse_program(&mut self) -> Result<Vec<Stmt>, ParseError> {
        let mut stmts = Vec::new();
        while !self.check(&TokenKind::Eof) {
            stmts.push(self.parse_stmt()?);
        }
        Ok(stmts)
    }

    fn parse_block(&mut self) -> Result<Vec<Stmt>, ParseError> {
        self.expect(&TokenKind::LBrace, "'{'")?;
        let mut stmts = Vec::new();
        while !self.check(&TokenKind::RBrace) && !self.check(&TokenKind::Eof) {
            stmts.push(self.parse_stmt()?);
        }
        self.expect(&TokenKind::RBrace, "'}'")?;
        Ok(stmts)
    }

    fn parse_stmt(&mut self) -> Result<Stmt, ParseError> {
        match self.peek() {
            TokenKind::Let => {
                self.advance();
                let name = match self.advance().kind {
                    TokenKind::Ident(n) => n,
                    other => return Err(self.err(format!("expected a name after 'let', found {:?}", other))),
                };
                self.expect(&TokenKind::Eq, "'='")?;
                let value = self.parse_expr()?;
                self.eat(&TokenKind::Semicolon);
                Ok(Stmt::Let { name, value })
            }
            TokenKind::Fn => {
                // `fn name(...) {...}` is a declaration; `fn(...) {...}`
                // at statement position is an anonymous-function expression.
                let next_is_name = matches!(
                    self.tokens.get(self.pos + 1).map(|t| &t.kind),
                    Some(TokenKind::Ident(_))
                );
                if !next_is_name {
                    let expr = self.parse_expr()?;
                    self.eat(&TokenKind::Semicolon);
                    return Ok(Stmt::Expr(expr));
                }
                self.advance();
                let name = match self.advance().kind {
                    TokenKind::Ident(n) => n,
                    other => return Err(self.err(format!("expected a function name, found {:?}", other))),
                };
                let params = self.parse_params()?;
                let body = self.parse_block()?;
                Ok(Stmt::FnDecl { name, params, body })
            }
            TokenKind::Return => {
                self.advance();
                let value = if self.check(&TokenKind::Semicolon) || self.check(&TokenKind::RBrace) {
                    None
                } else {
                    Some(self.parse_expr()?)
                };
                self.eat(&TokenKind::Semicolon);
                Ok(Stmt::Return(value))
            }
            _ => {
                let expr = self.parse_expr()?;
                self.eat(&TokenKind::Semicolon);
                Ok(Stmt::Expr(expr))
            }
        }
    }

    fn parse_params(&mut self) -> Result<Vec<String>, ParseError> {
        self.expect(&TokenKind::LParen, "'('")?;
        let mut params = Vec::new();
        if !self.check(&TokenKind::RParen) {
            loop {
                match self.advance().kind {
                    TokenKind::Ident(n) => params.push(n),
                    other => return Err(self.err(format!("expected a parameter name, found {:?}", other))),
                }
                if !self.eat(&TokenKind::Comma) {
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
        self.expect(&TokenKind::RParen, "')'")?;
        Ok(params)
    }

    pub fn parse_expr(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        self.parse_assign()
    }

    fn parse_assign(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        let expr = self.parse_or()?;
        if self.check(&TokenKind::Eq) {
            self.advance();
            let value = self.parse_assign()?; // right-associative
            match expr {
                Expr::Var(name) => Ok(Expr::Assign { name, value: Box::new(value) }),
                _ => Err(self.err("assignment target must be a variable")),
            }
        } else {
            Ok(expr)
        }
    }

    fn parse_or(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        let mut left = self.parse_and()?;
        while self.check(&TokenKind::OrOr) {
            self.advance();
            let right = self.parse_and()?;
            left = Expr::Binary { op: BinOp::Or, left: Box::new(left), right: Box::new(right) };
        }
        Ok(left)
    }

    fn parse_and(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        let mut left = self.parse_equality()?;
        while self.check(&TokenKind::AndAnd) {
            self.advance();
            let right = self.parse_equality()?;
            left = Expr::Binary { op: BinOp::And, left: Box::new(left), right: Box::new(right) };
        }
        Ok(left)
    }

    fn parse_equality(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        let mut left = self.parse_comparison()?;
        loop {
            let op = match self.peek() {
                TokenKind::EqEq => BinOp::Eq,
                TokenKind::NotEq => BinOp::NotEq,
                _ => break,
            };
            self.advance();
            let right = self.parse_comparison()?;
            left = Expr::Binary { op, left: Box::new(left), right: Box::new(right) };
        }
        Ok(left)
    }

    fn parse_comparison(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        let mut left = self.parse_additive()?;
        loop {
            let op = match self.peek() {
                TokenKind::Lt => BinOp::Lt,
                TokenKind::LtEq => BinOp::LtEq,
                TokenKind::Gt => BinOp::Gt,
                TokenKind::GtEq => BinOp::GtEq,
                _ => break,
            };
            self.advance();
            let right = self.parse_additive()?;
            left = Expr::Binary { op, left: Box::new(left), right: Box::new(right) };
        }
        Ok(left)
    }

    fn parse_additive(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        let mut left = self.parse_multiplicative()?;
        loop {
            let op = match self.peek() {
                TokenKind::Plus => BinOp::Add,
                TokenKind::Minus => BinOp::Sub,
                _ => break,
            };
            self.advance();
            let right = self.parse_multiplicative()?;
            left = Expr::Binary { op, left: Box::new(left), right: Box::new(right) };
        }
        Ok(left)
    }

    fn parse_multiplicative(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        let mut left = self.parse_unary()?;
        loop {
            let op = match self.peek() {
                TokenKind::Star => BinOp::Mul,
                TokenKind::Slash => BinOp::Div,
                TokenKind::Percent => BinOp::Mod,
                _ => break,
            };
            self.advance();
            let right = self.parse_unary()?;
            left = Expr::Binary { op, left: Box::new(left), right: Box::new(right) };
        }
        Ok(left)
    }

    fn parse_unary(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        match self.peek() {
            TokenKind::Minus => {
                self.advance();
                Ok(Expr::Unary { op: UnaryOp::Neg, expr: Box::new(self.parse_unary()?) })
            }
            TokenKind::Bang => {
                self.advance();
                Ok(Expr::Unary { op: UnaryOp::Not, expr: Box::new(self.parse_unary()?) })
            }
            _ => self.parse_postfix(),
        }
    }

    fn parse_postfix(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        let mut expr = self.parse_primary()?;
        loop {
            if self.check(&TokenKind::LParen) {
                self.advance();
                let mut args = Vec::new();
                if !self.check(&TokenKind::RParen) {
                    loop {
                        args.push(self.parse_expr()?);
                        if !self.eat(&TokenKind::Comma) {
                            break;
                        }
                    }
                }
                self.expect(&TokenKind::RParen, "')' after call arguments")?;
                expr = Expr::Call { callee: Box::new(expr), args };
            } else if self.check(&TokenKind::LBracket) {
                self.advance();
                let index = self.parse_expr()?;
                self.expect(&TokenKind::RBracket, "']' after index")?;
                expr = Expr::Index { target: Box::new(expr), index: Box::new(index) };
            } else {
                break;
            }
        }
        Ok(expr)
    }

    fn parse_primary(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        match self.advance().kind {
            TokenKind::Number(n) => Ok(Expr::Number(n)),
            TokenKind::Str(s) => Ok(Expr::Str(s)),
            TokenKind::True => Ok(Expr::Bool(true)),
            TokenKind::False => Ok(Expr::Bool(false)),
            TokenKind::Nil => Ok(Expr::Nil),
            TokenKind::Ident(name) => Ok(Expr::Var(name)),
            TokenKind::LParen => {
                let expr = self.parse_expr()?;
                self.expect(&TokenKind::RParen, "')'")?;
                Ok(expr)
            }
            TokenKind::LBracket => {
                let mut elems = Vec::new();
                if !self.check(&TokenKind::RBracket) {
                    loop {
                        elems.push(self.parse_expr()?);
                        if !self.eat(&TokenKind::Comma) {
                            break;
                        }
                    }
                }
                self.expect(&TokenKind::RBracket, "']'")?;
                Ok(Expr::Array(elems))
            }
            TokenKind::LBrace => {
                // A block is an expression: its value is its last statement.
                let mut stmts = Vec::new();
                while !self.check(&TokenKind::RBrace) && !self.check(&TokenKind::Eof) {
                    stmts.push(self.parse_stmt()?);
                }
                self.expect(&TokenKind::RBrace, "'}'")?;
                Ok(Expr::Block(stmts))
            }
            TokenKind::If => {
                let cond = self.parse_expr()?;
                let then_branch = self.parse_block()?;
                let else_branch = if self.eat(&TokenKind::Else) {
                    if self.check(&TokenKind::If) {
                        // else if: desugar to a block holding one if-expr.
                        let nested = self.parse_primary_if_tail()?;
                        Some(vec![Stmt::Expr(nested)])
                    } else {
                        Some(self.parse_block()?)
                    }
                } else {
                    None
                };
                Ok(Expr::If { cond: Box::new(cond), then_branch, else_branch })
            }
            TokenKind::Fn => {
                // Anonymous function: fn(a, b) { ... }
                let params = self.parse_params()?;
                let body = self.parse_block()?;
                Ok(Expr::Closure { params, body })
            }
            other => Err(self.err(format!("unexpected token {:?}", other))),
        }
    }

    /// Parses `if ...` after an `else` has already been consumed.
    fn parse_primary_if_tail(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
        // self.peek() is If; consume it via parse_primary's If arm by
        // re-entering through a synthetic route: advance and parse manually.
        self.advance(); // 'if'
        let cond = self.parse_expr()?;
        let then_branch = self.parse_block()?;
        let else_branch = if self.eat(&TokenKind::Else) {
            if self.check(&TokenKind::If) {
                let nested = self.parse_primary_if_tail()?;
                Some(vec![Stmt::Expr(nested)])
            } else {
                Some(self.parse_block()?)
            }
        } else {
            None
        };
        Ok(Expr::If { cond: Box::new(cond), then_branch, else_branch })
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Values and environment
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub enum Value {
    Number(f64),
    Bool(bool),
    Str(String),
    Nil,
    Array(Vec<Value>),
    Closure { params: Vec<String>, body: Vec<Stmt>, env: EnvRef },
    Builtin(&'static str),
}

impl PartialEq for Value {
    fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
        match (self, other) {
            (Value::Number(a), Value::Number(b)) => a == b,
            (Value::Bool(a), Value::Bool(b)) => a == b,
            (Value::Str(a), Value::Str(b)) => a == b,
            (Value::Nil, Value::Nil) => true,
            (Value::Array(a), Value::Array(b)) => a == b,
            (Value::Closure { .. }, Value::Closure { .. }) => false,
            (Value::Builtin(a), Value::Builtin(b)) => a == b,
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Value {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Value::Number(n) => {
                if n.fract() == 0.0 && n.abs() < 1e15 {
                    write!(f, "{}", *n as i64)
                } else {
                    write!(f, "{}", n)
                }
            }
            Value::Bool(b) => write!(f, "{}", b),
            Value::Str(s) => write!(f, "{}", s),
            Value::Nil => write!(f, "nil"),
            Value::Array(items) => {
                write!(f, "[")?;
                for (i, item) in items.iter().enumerate() {
                    if i > 0 {
                        write!(f, ", ")?;
                    }
                    match item {
                        Value::Str(s) => write!(f, "\"{}\"", s)?,
                        _ => write!(f, "{}", item)?,
                    }
                }
                write!(f, "]")
            }
            Value::Closure { .. } => write!(f, "<function>"),
            Value::Builtin(name) => write!(f, "<builtin {}>", name),
        }
    }
}

impl Value {
    pub fn type_name(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Value::Number(_) => "number",
            Value::Bool(_) => "bool",
            Value::Str(_) => "string",
            Value::Nil => "nil",
            Value::Array(_) => "array",
            Value::Closure { .. } => "function",
            Value::Builtin(_) => "builtin",
        }
    }
    pub fn is_truthy(&self) -> bool {
        match self {
            Value::Bool(b) => *b,
            Value::Nil => false,
            _ => true,
        }
    }
    pub fn as_number(&self) -> Result<f64, String> {
        match self {
            Value::Number(n) => Ok(*n),
            other => Err(format!("expected a number, got {}", other.type_name())),
        }
    }
}

pub type EnvRef = Rc<RefCell<Env>>;

#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Env {
    vars: HashMap<String, Value>,
    parent: Option<EnvRef>,
}

impl Env {
    pub fn new() -> EnvRef {
        Rc::new(RefCell::new(Env { vars: HashMap::new(), parent: None }))
    }
    pub fn child(parent: &EnvRef) -> EnvRef {
        Rc::new(RefCell::new(Env { vars: HashMap::new(), parent: Some(Rc::clone(parent)) }))
    }
    pub fn define(&mut self, name: String, value: Value) {
        self.vars.insert(name, value);
    }
    pub fn get(&self, name: &str) -> Option<Value> {
        if let Some(v) = self.vars.get(name) {
            return Some(v.clone());
        }
        match &self.parent {
            Some(p) => p.borrow().get(name),
            None => None,
        }
    }
    /// Assign to the nearest enclosing scope that already defines `name`.
    pub fn assign(&mut self, name: &str, value: Value) -> bool {
        if self.vars.contains_key(name) {
            self.vars.insert(name.to_string(), value);
            true
        } else if let Some(p) = &self.parent {
            p.borrow_mut().assign(name, value)
        } else {
            false
        }
    }
}

/// What a statement list evaluates to. `Return` unwinds through blocks
/// and function bodies until the call that created the frame.
#[derive(Debug)]
enum Flow {
    Value(Value),
    Return(Value),
}

// `return` inside an `if` / block *expression* cannot travel through
// eval_expr (which returns a plain Value), so it is parked here and
// picked up by the nearest enclosing eval_block, which turns it back
// into Flow::Return. Function bodies consume it via eval_block, so a
// return never leaks across a call boundary.
thread_local! {
    static PENDING_RETURN: RefCell<Option<Value>> = const { RefCell::new(None) };
}
fn set_pending_return(v: Value) {
    PENDING_RETURN.with(|c| *c.borrow_mut() = Some(v));
}
fn take_pending_return() -> Option<Value> {
    PENDING_RETURN.with(|c| c.borrow_mut().take())
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Evaluator
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

pub struct Evaluator;

impl Evaluator {
    pub fn eval_program(stmts: &[Stmt], env: &EnvRef) -> Result<Value, String> {
        match Self::eval_block(stmts, env)? {
            Flow::Value(v) => Ok(v),
            Flow::Return(v) => Ok(v), // a stray top-level return is its value
        }
    }

    fn eval_block(stmts: &[Stmt], env: &EnvRef) -> Result<Flow, String> {
        let mut last = Value::Nil;
        for stmt in stmts {
            match Self::eval_stmt(stmt, env)? {
                Flow::Value(v) => last = v,
                Flow::Return(v) => return Ok(Flow::Return(v)),
            }
            if let Some(v) = take_pending_return() {
                return Ok(Flow::Return(v));
            }
        }
        Ok(Flow::Value(last))
    }

    fn eval_stmt(stmt: &Stmt, env: &EnvRef) -> Result<Flow, String> {
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Let { name, value } => {
                let v = Self::eval_expr(value, env)?;
                env.borrow_mut().define(name.clone(), v);
                Ok(Flow::Value(Value::Nil))
            }
            Stmt::FnDecl { name, params, body } => {
                let closure = Value::Closure { params: params.clone(), body: body.clone(), env: Rc::clone(env) };
                env.borrow_mut().define(name.clone(), closure);
                Ok(Flow::Value(Value::Nil))
            }
            Stmt::Return(expr) => {
                let v = match expr {
                    Some(e) => Self::eval_expr(e, env)?,
                    None => Value::Nil,
                };
                Ok(Flow::Return(v))
            }
            Stmt::Expr(expr) => Ok(Flow::Value(Self::eval_expr(expr, env)?)),
        }
    }

    pub fn eval_expr(expr: &Expr, env: &EnvRef) -> Result<Value, String> {
        match expr {
            Expr::Number(n) => Ok(Value::Number(*n)),
            Expr::Str(s) => Ok(Value::Str(s.clone())),
            Expr::Bool(b) => Ok(Value::Bool(*b)),
            Expr::Nil => Ok(Value::Nil),
            Expr::Var(name) => env
                .borrow()
                .get(name)
                .ok_or_else(|| format!("undefined variable '{}'", name)),
            Expr::Array(elems) => {
                let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(elems.len());
                for e in elems {
                    out.push(Self::eval_expr(e, env)?);
                }
                Ok(Value::Array(out))
            }
            Expr::Index { target, index } => {
                let t = Self::eval_expr(target, env)?;
                let i = Self::eval_expr(index, env)?;
                match (&t, &i) {
                    (Value::Array(items), Value::Number(n)) => {
                        let idx = *n as i64;
                        if idx < 0 || idx as usize >= items.len() {
                            Err(format!("array index {} out of bounds (len {})", idx, items.len()))
                        } else {
                            Ok(items[idx as usize].clone())
                        }
                    }
                    (Value::Str(s), Value::Number(n)) => {
                        let chars: Vec<char> = s.chars().collect();
                        let idx = *n as i64;
                        if idx < 0 || idx as usize >= chars.len() {
                            Err(format!("string index {} out of bounds", idx))
                        } else {
                            Ok(Value::Str(chars[idx as usize].to_string()))
                        }
                    }
                    _ => Err(format!("cannot index {} with {}", t.type_name(), i.type_name())),
                }
            }
            Expr::Unary { op, expr } => {
                let v = Self::eval_expr(expr, env)?;
                match op {
                    UnaryOp::Neg => Ok(Value::Number(-v.as_number()?)),
                    UnaryOp::Not => Ok(Value::Bool(!v.is_truthy())),
                }
            }
            Expr::Binary { op, left, right } => Self::eval_binary(op, left, right, env),
            Expr::Assign { name, value } => {
                let v = Self::eval_expr(value, env)?;
                if env.borrow_mut().assign(name, v.clone()) {
                    Ok(v)
                } else {
                    Err(format!("assignment to undefined variable '{}'", name))
                }
            }
            Expr::If { cond, then_branch, else_branch } => {
                let c = Self::eval_expr(cond, env)?;
                let branch: Option<&Vec<Stmt>> =
                    if c.is_truthy() { Some(then_branch) } else { else_branch.as_ref() };
                match branch {
                    Some(stmts) => {
                        let child = Env::child(env);
                        match Self::eval_block(stmts, &child)? {
                            Flow::Value(v) => Ok(v),
                            // Park the return; the enclosing block picks it up.
                            Flow::Return(v) => {
                                set_pending_return(v);
                                Ok(Value::Nil)
                            }
                        }
                    }
                    None => Ok(Value::Nil),
                }
            }
            Expr::Block(stmts) => {
                let child = Env::child(env);
                match Self::eval_block(stmts, &child)? {
                    Flow::Value(v) => Ok(v),
                    Flow::Return(v) => {
                        set_pending_return(v);
                        Ok(Value::Nil)
                    }
                }
            }
            Expr::Closure { params, body } => {
                Ok(Value::Closure { params: params.clone(), body: body.clone(), env: Rc::clone(env) })
            }
            Expr::Call { callee, args } => {
                let f = Self::eval_expr(callee, env)?;
                let mut values = Vec::with_capacity(args.len());
                for a in args {
                    values.push(Self::eval_expr(a, env)?);
                }
                Self::call_value(&f, values)
            }
        }
    }

    fn eval_binary(op: &BinOp, left: &Expr, right: &Expr, env: &EnvRef) -> Result<Value, String> {
        // Short-circuit logic operators: the right side may never run.
        match op {
            BinOp::And => {
                let l = Self::eval_expr(left, env)?;
                if !l.is_truthy() {
                    return Ok(Value::Bool(false));
                }
                return Ok(Value::Bool(Self::eval_expr(right, env)?.is_truthy()));
            }
            BinOp::Or => {
                let l = Self::eval_expr(left, env)?;
                if l.is_truthy() {
                    return Ok(Value::Bool(true));
                }
                return Ok(Value::Bool(Self::eval_expr(right, env)?.is_truthy()));
            }
            _ => {}
        }
        let l = Self::eval_expr(left, env)?;
        let r = Self::eval_expr(right, env)?;
        match op {
            BinOp::Add => match (&l, &r) {
                (Value::Number(a), Value::Number(b)) => Ok(Value::Number(a + b)),
                (Value::Str(a), Value::Str(b)) => Ok(Value::Str(format!("{}{}", a, b))),
                (Value::Str(a), b) => Ok(Value::Str(format!("{}{}", a, b))),
                (a, Value::Str(b)) => Ok(Value::Str(format!("{}{}", a, b))),
                (Value::Array(a), Value::Array(b)) => {
                    let mut out = a.clone();
                    out.extend(b.iter().cloned());
                    Ok(Value::Array(out))
                }
                _ => Err(format!("cannot add {} and {}", l.type_name(), r.type_name())),
            },
            BinOp::Sub => Ok(Value::Number(l.as_number()? - r.as_number()?)),
            BinOp::Mul => match (&l, &r) {
                (Value::Number(a), Value::Number(b)) => Ok(Value::Number(a * b)),
                (Value::Str(s), Value::Number(n)) | (Value::Number(n), Value::Str(s)) => {
                    if *n < 0.0 || n.fract() != 0.0 {
                        return Err("string repetition count must be a non-negative integer".into());
                    }
                    Ok(Value::Str(s.repeat(*n as usize)))
                }
                _ => Err(format!("cannot multiply {} and {}", l.type_name(), r.type_name())),
            },
            BinOp::Div => {
                let b = r.as_number()?;
                if b == 0.0 {
                    return Err("division by zero".into());
                }
                Ok(Value::Number(l.as_number()? / b))
            }
            BinOp::Mod => {
                let (a, b) = (l.as_number()?, r.as_number()?);
                if b == 0.0 {
                    return Err("modulo by zero".into());
                }
                Ok(Value::Number(a % b))
            }
            BinOp::Eq => Ok(Value::Bool(l == r)),
            BinOp::NotEq => Ok(Value::Bool(l != r)),
            BinOp::Lt => Ok(Value::Bool(compare_values(&l, &r)? == std::cmp::Ordering::Less)),
            BinOp::LtEq => Ok(Value::Bool(compare_values(&l, &r)? != std::cmp::Ordering::Greater)),
            BinOp::Gt => Ok(Value::Bool(compare_values(&l, &r)? == std::cmp::Ordering::Greater)),
            BinOp::GtEq => Ok(Value::Bool(compare_values(&l, &r)? != std::cmp::Ordering::Less)),
            BinOp::And | BinOp::Or => unreachable!(),
        }
    }

    fn call_value(f: &Value, args: Vec<Value>) -> Result<Value, String> {
        match f {
            Value::Builtin(name) => call_builtin(name, args),
            Value::Closure { params, body, env } => {
                if args.len() != params.len() {
                    return Err(format!(
                        "function expects {} argument(s), got {}",
                        params.len(),
                        args.len()
                    ));
                }
                let call_env = Env::child(env);
                for (name, value) in params.iter().zip(args) {
                    call_env.borrow_mut().define(name.clone(), value);
                }
                match Self::eval_block(body, &call_env)? {
                    Flow::Value(v) => Ok(v),
                    Flow::Return(v) => Ok(v),
                }
            }
            other => Err(format!("value of type {} is not callable", other.type_name())),
        }
    }
}

fn compare_values(l: &Value, r: &Value) -> Result<std::cmp::Ordering, String> {
    match (l, r) {
        (Value::Number(a), Value::Number(b)) => Ok(a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap_or(std::cmp::Ordering::Equal)),
        (Value::Str(a), Value::Str(b)) => Ok(a.cmp(b)),
        (Value::Bool(a), Value::Bool(b)) => Ok(a.cmp(b)),
        _ => Err(format!("cannot compare {} and {}", l.type_name(), r.type_name())),
    }
}

fn call_builtin(name: &str, args: Vec<Value>) -> Result<Value, String> {
    let arity = |want: usize| -> Result<(), String> {
        if args.len() != want {
            Err(format!("builtin {} expects {} argument(s), got {}", name, want, args.len()))
        } else {
            Ok(())
        }
    };
    match name {
        "len" => {
            arity(1)?;
            match &args[0] {
                Value::Str(s) => Ok(Value::Number(s.chars().count() as f64)),
                Value::Array(a) => Ok(Value::Number(a.len() as f64)),
                other => Err(format!("len() needs a string or array, got {}", other.type_name())),
            }
        }
        "print" => {
            arity(1)?;
            println!("{}", args[0]);
            Ok(args[0].clone())
        }
        "str" => {
            arity(1)?;
            Ok(Value::Str(args[0].to_string()))
        }
        "num" => {
            arity(1)?;
            match &args[0] {
                Value::Number(n) => Ok(Value::Number(*n)),
                Value::Str(s) => s
                    .trim()
                    .parse::<f64>()
                    .map(Value::Number)
                    .map_err(|_| format!("num() cannot parse {:?}", s)),
                Value::Bool(b) => Ok(Value::Number(if *b { 1.0 } else { 0.0 })),
                other => Err(format!("num() cannot convert {}", other.type_name())),
            }
        }
        "push" => {
            arity(2)?;
            match &args[0] {
                Value::Array(items) => {
                    let mut out = items.clone();
                    out.push(args[1].clone());
                    Ok(Value::Array(out))
                }
                other => Err(format!("push() needs an array, got {}", other.type_name())),
            }
        }
        "type_of" => {
            arity(1)?;
            Ok(Value::Str(args[0].type_name().to_string()))
        }
        _ => Err(format!("unknown builtin '{}'", name)),
    }
}

/// Install the built-ins into a fresh global environment.
pub fn global_env() -> EnvRef {
    let env = Env::new();
    for name in ["len", "print", "str", "num", "push", "type_of"] {
        env.borrow_mut().define(name.to_string(), Value::Builtin(name));
    }
    env
}

/// Parse and run a whole program; returns the value of its last statement.
pub fn run(source: &str) -> Result<Value, String> {
    let mut lexer = Lexer::new(source);
    let tokens = lexer.tokenize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let mut parser = Parser::new(tokens);
    let stmts = parser.parse_program().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let env = global_env();
    Evaluator::eval_program(&stmts, &env)
}

/// Run a program and keep its environment, for REPL-style sessions where
/// later lines can use bindings from earlier ones.
pub struct Session {
    pub env: EnvRef,
}

impl Session {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self { env: global_env() }
    }
    pub fn eval(&mut self, source: &str) -> Result<Value, String> {
        let mut lexer = Lexer::new(source);
        let tokens = lexer.tokenize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        let mut parser = Parser::new(tokens);
        let stmts = parser.parse_program().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        Evaluator::eval_program(&stmts, &self.env)
    }
}

impl Default for Session {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

fn num(v: &Value) -> f64 {
    match v {
        Value::Number(n) => *n,
        other => panic!("expected a number, got {:?}", other),
    }
}
fn text(v: &Value) -> String {
    v.to_string()
}

fn main() {
    // --- arithmetic and precedence ---
    assert_eq!(num(&run("1 + 2 * 3").unwrap()), 7.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("(1 + 2) * 3").unwrap()), 9.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("10 - 4 - 3").unwrap()), 3.0); // left-associative
    assert_eq!(num(&run("2 * 3 % 4").unwrap()), 2.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("-5 + 3").unwrap()), -2.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("7 / 2").unwrap()), 3.5);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("1.5 + 1.5").unwrap()), 3.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("2e3 + 1").unwrap()), 2001.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("10 % 3").unwrap()), 1.0);

    // --- comparison and logic, including short-circuiting ---
    assert_eq!(run("1 < 2").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    assert_eq!(run("2 <= 2").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    assert_eq!(run("3 > 4").unwrap(), Value::Bool(false));
    assert_eq!(run("3 >= 3").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    assert_eq!(run("1 == 1.0").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    assert_eq!(run("1 != 2").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    assert_eq!(run("\"abc\" == \"abc\"").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    assert_eq!(run("true && false").unwrap(), Value::Bool(false));
    assert_eq!(run("true || false").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    assert_eq!(run("!true").unwrap(), Value::Bool(false));
    assert_eq!(run("!nil").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    assert_eq!(run("1 < 2 && 3 < 4").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    // Short-circuit: the right side of && is never evaluated, so this
    // would-be division by zero never happens.
    assert_eq!(run("false && (1 / 0 == 0)").unwrap(), Value::Bool(false));
    assert_eq!(run("true || (1 / 0 == 0)").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    // Division by zero really is an error when it is evaluated.
    assert!(run("1 / 0").is_err());
    assert!(run("undefined_var").is_err());
    assert!(run("let x = 1; y = 2;").is_err()); // y was never declared

    // --- strings ---
    assert_eq!(text(&run("\"hello\" + \" \" + \"world\"").unwrap()), "hello world");
    assert_eq!(text(&run("\"n=\" + 42").unwrap()), "n=42");
    assert_eq!(text(&run("\"ab\" * 3").unwrap()), "ababab");
    assert_eq!(text(&run("\"a\\n b\"").unwrap()), "a\n b");
    assert_eq!(num(&run("len(\"hello\")").unwrap()), 5.0);
    assert_eq!(text(&run("\"hello\"[1]").unwrap()), "e");
    assert_eq!(text(&run("str(42)").unwrap()), "42");
    assert_eq!(num(&run("num(\"3.5\")").unwrap()), 3.5);
    assert_eq!(text(&run("type_of([1,2])").unwrap()), "array");

    // --- let, assignment, blocks and scoping ---
    assert_eq!(num(&run("let x = 5; x * 2").unwrap()), 10.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("let x = 5; x = x + 1; x").unwrap()), 6.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("let x = 1; { let x = 2; x } + x").unwrap()), 3.0);
    // A block's value is its last statement; bindings inside stay inside.
    assert_eq!(num(&run("{ let y = 10; y * 2 }").unwrap()), 20.0);
    assert!(run("{ let y = 10; } y").is_err());
    // Comments are ignored, including nested block comments.
    assert_eq!(num(&run("// hello\n1 + 1 /* multi /* nested */ line */ + 1").unwrap()), 3.0);

    // --- if as an expression, including else-if chains ---
    assert_eq!(num(&run("if true { 1 } else { 2 }").unwrap()), 1.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("if false { 1 } else { 2 }").unwrap()), 2.0);
    assert_eq!(run("if false { 1 }").unwrap(), Value::Nil); // no else branch -> nil
    assert_eq!(
        num(&run("let s = 85; if s >= 90 { 4 } else if s >= 80 { 3 } else if s >= 70 { 2 } else { 1 }").unwrap()),
        3.0
    );
    assert_eq!(num(&run("let x = 5; if x > 3 { x * 10 } else { 0 }").unwrap()), 50.0);

    // --- arrays ---
    assert_eq!(num(&run("len([1, 2, 3])").unwrap()), 3.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("[10, 20, 30][1]").unwrap()), 20.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("let a = [1, 2]; len(a + [3, 4])").unwrap()), 4.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("let a = [1]; len(push(a, 2))").unwrap()), 2.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&run("[1, 2, 3][0] + [1, 2, 3][2]").unwrap()), 4.0);
    assert!(run("[1, 2][5]").is_err());
    assert_eq!(text(&run("[1, \"two\", true]").unwrap()), "[1, \"two\", true]");

    // --- functions, recursion, closures ---
    assert_eq!(
        num(&run("fn add(a, b) { return a + b; } add(3, 4)").unwrap()),
        7.0
    );
    // Implicit return: the last expression is the value.
    assert_eq!(num(&run("fn double(x) { x * 2 } double(21)").unwrap()), 42.0);
    assert_eq!(
        num(&run("fn fact(n) { if n <= 1 { return 1; } return n * fact(n - 1); } fact(5)").unwrap()),
        120.0
    );
    assert_eq!(
        num(&run("fn fib(n) { if n < 2 { return n; } return fib(n - 1) + fib(n - 2); } fib(10)").unwrap()),
        55.0
    );
    // A closure captures the environment where it was defined.
    assert_eq!(
        num(&run("fn make_adder(n) { fn(a) { a + n } } let add5 = make_adder(5); add5(10)").unwrap()),
        15.0
    );
    assert_eq!(
        num(&run("let apply = fn(f, x) { f(x) }; apply(fn(v) { v * v }, 9)").unwrap()),
        81.0
    );
    // Higher-order: applying a function to several values at once.
    let map2 = r#"
        fn map2(f, a, b, c) { [f(a), f(b), f(c)] }
        map2(fn(x) { x * 2 }, 1, 2, 3)
    "#;
    assert_eq!(text(&run(map2).unwrap()), "[2, 4, 6]");
    // Wrong arity and calling a non-function are errors.
    assert!(run("fn f(a) { a } f(1, 2)").is_err());
    assert!(run("let x = 5; x(1)").is_err());

    // --- a realistic program: grade report + total ---
    let report = r#"
        fn grade(score) {
            if score >= 90 { return "A"; }
            if score >= 80 { return "B"; }
            if score >= 70 { return "C"; }
            return "F";
        }
        let scores = [95, 82, 77, 61];
        grade(scores[0]) + grade(scores[1]) + grade(scores[2]) + grade(scores[3])
    "#;
    assert_eq!(text(&run(report).unwrap()), "ABCF");
    let total = r#"
        fn sum3(a, b, c) { a + b + c }
        let prices = [12.5, 7.25, 3.0];
        sum3(prices[0], prices[1], prices[2])
    "#;
    assert_eq!(num(&run(total).unwrap()), 22.75);

    // --- REPL-style session: bindings persist across lines ---
    let mut session = Session::new();
    assert_eq!(session.eval("let balance = 100;").unwrap(), Value::Nil); // a let statement evaluates to nil
    assert_eq!(session.eval("balance").unwrap(), Value::Number(100.0));
