    assert_eq!(num(&session.eval("balance = balance - 25;").unwrap()), 75.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&session.eval("balance * 2").unwrap()), 150.0);
    assert_eq!(text(&session.eval("let name = \"Ada\"; \"hi \" + name").unwrap()), "hi Ada");
    assert_eq!(num(&session.eval("fn sq(x) { x * x } sq(6)").unwrap()), 36.0);
    assert_eq!(num(&session.eval("sq(7)").unwrap()), 49.0); // fn persists too
    assert!(session.eval("1 +").is_err()); // a syntax error does not kill the session
    assert_eq!(num(&session.eval("balance").unwrap()), 75.0);

    println!("expression_lang: all assertions passed");
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn precedence() {
        assert_eq!(num(&run("2 + 3 * 4 - 1").unwrap()), 13.0);
        assert_eq!(num(&run("-(2 + 3)").unwrap()), -5.0);
        assert_eq!(run("!false && true").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    }

    #[test]
    fn bool_precedence() {
        assert_eq!(run("!false && true").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
        assert_eq!(run("1 + 2 == 3").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
    }

    #[test]
    fn lexer_numbers() {
        let mut lx = Lexer::new("42 3.14 1e3 1_000");
        let toks = lx.tokenize().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(toks[0].kind, TokenKind::Number(42.0));
        assert_eq!(toks[1].kind, TokenKind::Number(3.14));
        assert_eq!(toks[2].kind, TokenKind::Number(1000.0));
        assert_eq!(toks[3].kind, TokenKind::Number(1000.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn lexer_strings_and_comments() {
        let mut lx = Lexer::new("\"a\\n\" // hi\n/* x */ 1");
        let toks = lx.tokenize().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(toks[0].kind, TokenKind::Str("a\n".to_string()));
        assert_eq!(toks[1].kind, TokenKind::Number(1.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn scoping() {
        assert_eq!(num(&run("let x = 1; { let x = 2; } x").unwrap()), 1.0);
        assert!(run("let x = 1; { x = 2; } x").is_ok());
        assert_eq!(num(&run("let x = 1; { x = 2; } x").unwrap()), 2.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn recursion_factorial() {
        let src = "fn f(n) { if n <= 1 { return 1; } n * f(n - 1) } f(6)";
        assert_eq!(num(&run(src).unwrap()), 720.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn closures_capture() {
        let src = "let base = 10; let add_base = fn(x) { x + base }; add_base(5)";
        assert_eq!(num(&run(src).unwrap()), 15.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn errors_are_errors() {
        assert!(run("1 + ").is_err());
        assert!(run("(1").is_err());
        assert!(run("let = 3;").is_err());
        assert!(run("\"unterminated").is_err());
        assert!(run("fn f(a a) { a }").is_err());
        assert!(run("[1, 2][9]").is_err());
        assert!(run("len(1)").is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn session_persists() {
        let mut s = Session::new();
        s.eval("let x = 3;").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(num(&s.eval("x + 1").unwrap()), 4.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn if_expression_value() {
        assert_eq!(num(&run("let x = if 2 > 1 { 10 } else { 20 }; x").unwrap()), 10.0);
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/graph_algorithms.rs ---
// graph_algorithms.rs — Graph algorithms on an adjacency-list graph,
// written from scratch. Standard library only.
//   * BFS and DFS (recursive and iterative), with parent reconstruction
//   * Dijkstra's shortest paths (binary-heap priority queue)
//   * A* search with a caller-supplied admissible heuristic
//   * Topological sort (Kahn's algorithm and a DFS variant)
//   * Cycle detection (three-colour DFS, plus a returned cycle path)
//   * Strongly connected components (Tarjan), connected components,
//     Prim's minimum spanning tree, Floyd-Warshall all-pairs distances,
//     and shortest paths in a DAG (one topological pass)
// The demo builds a realistic city road network (nodes carry map
// coordinates, so A* can use Euclidean distance as its heuristic) and a
// task-dependency DAG, and asserts every result it prints.

use std::cmp::Ordering;
use std::collections::{BinaryHeap, HashMap, VecDeque};
use std::fmt;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Graph representation
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Edge {
    pub to: usize,
    pub weight: f64,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Graph {
    names: Vec<String>,
    coords: Vec<(f64, f64)>, // map coordinates, used by the A* heuristic
    adj: Vec<Vec<Edge>>,
    index: HashMap<String, usize>,
}

impl Graph {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self { names: Vec::new(), coords: Vec::new(), adj: Vec::new(), index: HashMap::new() }
    }

    pub fn add_node(&mut self, name: &str, x: f64, y: f64) -> usize {
        if let Some(&id) = self.index.get(name) {
            return id;
        }
        let id = self.names.len();
        self.names.push(name.to_string());
        self.coords.push((x, y));
        self.adj.push(Vec::new());
        self.index.insert(name.to_string(), id);
        id
    }

    pub fn node_id(&self, name: &str) -> Option<usize> {
        self.index.get(name).copied()
    }
    pub fn name(&self, id: usize) -> &str {
        &self.names[id]
    }
    pub fn coord(&self, id: usize) -> (f64, f64) {
        self.coords[id]
    }
    pub fn node_count(&self) -> usize {
        self.names.len()
    }
    pub fn edge_count(&self) -> usize {
        self.adj.iter().map(|v| v.len()).sum()
    }
    pub fn neighbours(&self, id: usize) -> &[Edge] {
        &self.adj[id]
    }
    pub fn euclidean(&self, a: usize, b: usize) -> f64 {
        let (x1, y1) = self.coords[a];
        let (x2, y2) = self.coords[b];
        ((x2 - x1).powi(2) + (y2 - y1).powi(2)).sqrt()
    }

    /// Directed edge; panics on a negative weight for Dijkstra/A* use,
    /// which require non-negative weights (use bellman_ford for negatives).
    pub fn add_edge(&mut self, from: usize, to: usize, weight: f64) {
        assert!(weight.is_finite(), "edge weight must be finite");
        self.adj[from].push(Edge { to, weight });
    }
    pub fn add_edge_by_name(&mut self, from: &str, to: &str, weight: f64) {
        let f = self.node_id(from).expect("unknown from-node");
        let t = self.node_id(to).expect("unknown to-node");
        self.add_edge(f, t, weight);
    }
    pub fn add_undirected_edge(&mut self, a: usize, b: usize, weight: f64) {
        self.add_edge(a, b, weight);
        self.add_edge(b, a, weight);
    }
    pub fn add_undirected_by_name(&mut self, a: &str, b: &str, weight: f64) {
        let x = self.node_id(a).expect("unknown node");
        let y = self.node_id(b).expect("unknown node");
        self.add_undirected_edge(x, y, weight);
    }

    /// Sort each adjacency list by destination id, so traversals are
    /// deterministic and independent of insertion order.
    pub fn sort_adjacency(&mut self) {
        for list in &mut self.adj {
            list.sort_by(|a, b| a.to.cmp(&b.to).then(a.weight.partial_cmp(&b.weight).unwrap_or(Ordering::Equal)));
        }
    }

    pub fn path_names(&self, path: &[usize]) -> Vec<String> {
        path.iter().map(|&id| self.names[id].clone()).collect()
    }
    pub fn path_cost(&self, path: &[usize]) -> Option<f64> {
        let mut total = 0.0;
        for w in path.windows(2) {
            let e = self.adj[w[0]].iter().find(|e| e.to == w[1])?;
            total += e.weight;
        }
        Some(total)
    }
}

impl Default for Graph {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Graph {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        for (id, name) in self.names.iter().enumerate() {
            let outs: Vec<String> = self.adj[id]
                .iter()
                .map(|e| format!("{} ({:.1})", self.names[e.to], e.weight))
                .collect();
            writeln!(f, "{} -> [{}]", name, outs.join(", "))?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// BFS
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct BfsResult {
    pub order: Vec<usize>,
    pub distance: Vec<Option<usize>>, // hops from the start
    pub parent: Vec<Option<usize>>,
}

pub fn bfs(graph: &Graph, start: usize) -> BfsResult {
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut distance = vec![None; n];
    let mut parent = vec![None; n];
    let mut order = Vec::new();
    let mut queue = VecDeque::new();
    distance[start] = Some(0);
    queue.push_back(start);
    while let Some(u) = queue.pop_front() {
        order.push(u);
        for edge in graph.neighbours(u) {
            let v = edge.to;
            if distance[v].is_none() {
                distance[v] = Some(distance[u].unwrap() + 1);
                parent[v] = Some(u);
                queue.push_back(v);
            }
        }
    }
    BfsResult { order, distance, parent }
}

/// Fewest-hop path from `start` to `goal`, or None if unreachable.
pub fn bfs_path(graph: &Graph, start: usize, goal: usize) -> Option<Vec<usize>> {
    let res = bfs(graph, start);
    if res.distance[goal].is_none() {
        return None;
    }
    let mut path = vec![goal];
    let mut cur = goal;
    while cur != start {
        cur = res.parent[cur]?;
        path.push(cur);
    }
    path.reverse();
    Some(path)
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// DFS
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

pub fn dfs_recursive(graph: &Graph, start: usize) -> Vec<usize> {
    let mut visited = vec![false; graph.node_count()];
    let mut order = Vec::new();
    dfs_rec(graph, start, &mut visited, &mut order);
    order
}

fn dfs_rec(graph: &Graph, u: usize, visited: &mut [bool], order: &mut Vec<usize>) {
    visited[u] = true;
    order.push(u);
    for edge in graph.neighbours(u) {
        if !visited[edge.to] {
            dfs_rec(graph, edge.to, visited, order);
        }
    }
}

pub fn dfs_iterative(graph: &Graph, start: usize) -> Vec<usize> {
    let mut visited = vec![false; graph.node_count()];
    let mut order = Vec::new();
    let mut stack = vec![start];
    visited[start] = true;
    while let Some(u) = stack.pop() {
        order.push(u);
        // Push neighbours in reverse so the smallest id is visited first,
        // matching the recursive order on a sorted adjacency list.
        for edge in graph.neighbours(u).iter().rev() {
            if !visited[edge.to] {
                visited[edge.to] = true;
                stack.push(edge.to);
            }
        }
    }
    order
}

/// DFS that also records discovery/finish times and parents.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct DfsTimes {
    pub order: Vec<usize>,
    pub discover: Vec<Option<usize>>,
    pub finish: Vec<Option<usize>>,
    pub parent: Vec<Option<usize>>,
}

pub fn dfs_with_times(graph: &Graph, start: usize) -> DfsTimes {
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut times = DfsTimes {
        order: Vec::new(),
        discover: vec![None; n],
        finish: vec![None; n],
        parent: vec![None; n],
    };
    let mut clock = 0usize;
    let mut visited = vec![false; n];
    dfs_time_rec(graph, start, &mut visited, &mut times, &mut clock);
    times
}

fn dfs_time_rec(graph: &Graph, u: usize, visited: &mut [bool], times: &mut DfsTimes, clock: &mut usize) {
    visited[u] = true;
    times.discover[u] = Some(*clock);
    *clock += 1;
    times.order.push(u);
    for edge in graph.neighbours(u) {
        if !visited[edge.to] {
            times.parent[edge.to] = Some(u);
            dfs_time_rec(graph, edge.to, visited, times, clock);
        }
    }
    times.finish[u] = Some(*clock);
    *clock += 1;
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Dijkstra
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
struct DijkstraState {
    cost: f64,
    node: usize,
}
impl Eq for DijkstraState {}
impl PartialOrd for DijkstraState {
    fn partial_cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Option<Ordering> {
        // Reverse: BinaryHeap is a max-heap, we need a min-heap.
        other.cost.partial_cmp(&self.cost).map(|o| o.then_with(|| other.node.cmp(&self.node)))
    }
}
impl Ord for DijkstraState {
    fn cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Ordering {
        self.partial_cmp(other).unwrap_or(Ordering::Equal)
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct DijkstraResult {
    pub distance: Vec<Option<f64>>,
    pub parent: Vec<Option<usize>>,
}

pub fn dijkstra(graph: &Graph, start: usize) -> DijkstraResult {
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut distance = vec![None; n];
    let mut parent = vec![None; n];
    let mut heap = BinaryHeap::new();
    distance[start] = Some(0.0);
    heap.push(DijkstraState { cost: 0.0, node: start });
    while let Some(DijkstraState { cost, node }) = heap.pop() {
        if let Some(best) = distance[node] {
            if cost > best + 1e-9 {
                continue; // stale heap entry
            }
        }
        for edge in graph.neighbours(node) {
            let next_cost = cost + edge.weight;
            let better = match distance[edge.to] {
                None => true,
                Some(d) => next_cost + 1e-9 < d,
            };
            if better {
                distance[edge.to] = Some(next_cost);
                parent[edge.to] = Some(node);
                heap.push(DijkstraState { cost: next_cost, node: edge.to });
            }
        }
    }
    DijkstraResult { distance, parent }
}

impl DijkstraResult {
    pub fn path_to(&self, start: usize, goal: usize) -> Option<Vec<usize>> {
        if self.distance[goal].is_none() {
            return None;
        }
        let mut path = vec![goal];
        let mut cur = goal;
        while cur != start {
            cur = self.parent[cur]?;
            path.push(cur);
        }
        path.reverse();
        Some(path)
    }
}

pub fn shortest_path(graph: &Graph, start: usize, goal: usize) -> Option<(Vec<usize>, f64)> {
    let res = dijkstra(graph, start);
    let path = res.path_to(start, goal)?;
    Some((path, res.distance[goal].unwrap()))
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// A*
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
struct AStarState {
    f: f64, // g + h
    g: f64,
    node: usize,
}
impl Eq for AStarState {}
impl PartialOrd for AStarState {
    fn partial_cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Option<Ordering> {
        other
            .f
            .partial_cmp(&self.f)
            .map(|o| o.then_with(|| other.g.partial_cmp(&self.g).unwrap_or(Ordering::Equal)))
    }
}
impl Ord for AStarState {
    fn cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Ordering {
        self.partial_cmp(other).unwrap_or(Ordering::Equal)
    }
}

/// A* from `start` to `goal`. `heuristic(node)` must estimate the remaining
/// cost; it is admissible (never overestimates) for the result to be
/// optimal. Returns (path, cost).
pub fn astar<F>(graph: &Graph, start: usize, goal: usize, heuristic: F) -> Option<(Vec<usize>, f64)>
where
    F: Fn(usize) -> f64,
{
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut g_score = vec![f64::INFINITY; n];
    let mut parent = vec![None; n];
    let mut closed = vec![false; n];
    let mut heap = BinaryHeap::new();
    g_score[start] = 0.0;
    heap.push(AStarState { f: heuristic(start), g: 0.0, node: start });
    while let Some(AStarState { g, node, .. }) = heap.pop() {
        if closed[node] {
            continue;
        }
        if g > g_score[node] + 1e-9 {
            continue;
        }
        closed[node] = true;
        if node == goal {
            let mut path = vec![goal];
            let mut cur = goal;
            while cur != start {
                cur = parent[cur]?;
                path.push(cur);
            }
            path.reverse();
            return Some((path, g));
        }
        for edge in graph.neighbours(node) {
            let tentative = g + edge.weight;
            if tentative + 1e-9 < g_score[edge.to] {
                g_score[edge.to] = tentative;
                parent[edge.to] = Some(node);
                heap.push(AStarState { f: tentative + heuristic(edge.to), g: tentative, node: edge.to });
            }
        }
    }
    None
}

/// A* on a graph whose nodes have coordinates, using straight-line
/// (Euclidean) distance as the heuristic.
pub fn astar_euclidean(graph: &Graph, start: usize, goal: usize) -> Option<(Vec<usize>, f64)> {
    astar(graph, start, goal, |node| graph.euclidean(node, goal))
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Topological sort and cycle detection
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Kahn's algorithm. Ok(order) for a DAG; Err(remaining nodes) when a
/// cycle leaves nodes with unsatisfied in-degree.
pub fn topological_sort(graph: &Graph) -> Result<Vec<usize>, Vec<usize>> {
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut indegree = vec![0usize; n];
    for u in 0..n {
        for e in graph.neighbours(u) {
            indegree[e.to] += 1;
        }
    }
    let mut queue: VecDeque<usize> = (0..n).filter(|&i| indegree[i] == 0).collect();
    let mut order = Vec::with_capacity(n);
    while let Some(u) = queue.pop_front() {
        order.push(u);
        for e in graph.neighbours(u) {
            indegree[e.to] -= 1;
            if indegree[e.to] == 0 {
                queue.push_back(e.to);
            }
        }
    }
    if order.len() == n {
        Ok(order)
    } else {
        Err((0..n).filter(|&i| indegree[i] > 0).collect())
    }
}

/// Topological order from a DFS post-order (reversed finish order).
pub fn topological_sort_dfs(graph: &Graph) -> Option<Vec<usize>> {
    if has_cycle(graph) {
        return None;
    }
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut visited = vec![false; n];
    let mut order = Vec::new();
    for start in 0..n {
        if !visited[start] {
            topo_dfs_rec(graph, start, &mut visited, &mut order);
        }
    }
    order.reverse();
    Some(order)
}

fn topo_dfs_rec(graph: &Graph, u: usize, visited: &mut [bool], order: &mut Vec<usize>) {
    visited[u] = true;
    for e in graph.neighbours(u) {
        if !visited[e.to] {
            topo_dfs_rec(graph, e.to, visited, order);
        }
    }
    order.push(u);
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum Colour {
    White,
    Grey,
    Black,
}

pub fn has_cycle(graph: &Graph) -> bool {
    find_cycle(graph).is_some()
}

/// Returns one directed cycle as a list of node ids (first == last is
/// not repeated; the cycle closes back to the first element), or None.
pub fn find_cycle(graph: &Graph) -> Option<Vec<usize>> {
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut colour = vec![Colour::White; n];
    let mut stack: Vec<usize> = Vec::new();
    for start in 0..n {
        if colour[start] == Colour::White {
            if let Some(cycle) = cycle_dfs(graph, start, &mut colour, &mut stack) {
                return Some(cycle);
            }
        }
    }
    None
}

fn cycle_dfs(graph: &Graph, u: usize, colour: &mut [Colour], stack: &mut Vec<usize>) -> Option<Vec<usize>> {
    colour[u] = Colour::Grey;
    stack.push(u);
    for e in graph.neighbours(u) {
        let v = e.to;
        match colour[v] {
            Colour::Grey => {
                // The cycle is the suffix of the DFS stack from v.
                let pos = stack.iter().position(|&x| x == v).unwrap();
                return Some(stack[pos..].to_vec());
            }
            Colour::White => {
                if let Some(cycle) = cycle_dfs(graph, v, colour, stack) {
                    return Some(cycle);
                }
            }
            Colour::Black => {}
        }
    }
    stack.pop();
    colour[u] = Colour::Black;
    None
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Strongly connected components (Tarjan), connected components, Prim, etc.
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Tarjan's algorithm: each returned component is a maximal set of nodes
/// mutually reachable from each other.
pub fn strongly_connected_components(graph: &Graph) -> Vec<Vec<usize>> {
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut index = vec![usize::MAX; n];
    let mut lowlink = vec![0usize; n];
    let mut on_stack = vec![false; n];
    let mut stack: Vec<usize> = Vec::new();
    let mut counter = 0usize;
    let mut components = Vec::new();
    for start in 0..n {
        if index[start] == usize::MAX {
            tarjan_rec(graph, start, &mut index, &mut lowlink, &mut on_stack, &mut stack, &mut counter, &mut components);
        }
    }
    components
}

#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
fn tarjan_rec(
    graph: &Graph,
    u: usize,
    index: &mut [usize],
    lowlink: &mut [usize],
    on_stack: &mut [bool],
    stack: &mut Vec<usize>,
    counter: &mut usize,
    components: &mut Vec<Vec<usize>>,
) {
    index[u] = *counter;
    lowlink[u] = *counter;
    *counter += 1;
    stack.push(u);
    on_stack[u] = true;
    for e in graph.neighbours(u) {
        let v = e.to;
        if index[v] == usize::MAX {
            tarjan_rec(graph, v, index, lowlink, on_stack, stack, counter, components);
            lowlink[u] = lowlink[u].min(lowlink[v]);
        } else if on_stack[v] {
            lowlink[u] = lowlink[u].min(index[v]);
        }
    }
    if lowlink[u] == index[u] {
        let mut comp = Vec::new();
        loop {
            let v = stack.pop().unwrap();
            on_stack[v] = false;
            comp.push(v);
            if v == u {
                break;
            }
        }
        components.push(comp);
    }
}

/// Weakly connected components (treats every edge as undirected).
pub fn connected_components(graph: &Graph) -> Vec<Vec<usize>> {
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut undirected: Vec<Vec<usize>> = vec![Vec::new(); n];
    for u in 0..n {
        for e in graph.neighbours(u) {
            undirected[u].push(e.to);
            undirected[e.to].push(u);
        }
    }
    let mut visited = vec![false; n];
    let mut components = Vec::new();
    for start in 0..n {
        if visited[start] {
            continue;
        }
        let mut comp = Vec::new();
        let mut stack = vec![start];
        visited[start] = true;
        while let Some(u) = stack.pop() {
            comp.push(u);
            for &v in &undirected[u] {
                if !visited[v] {
                    visited[v] = true;
                    stack.push(v);
                }
            }
        }
        comp.sort_unstable();
        components.push(comp);
    }
    components
}

/// Prim's algorithm. Returns (total weight, edges as (from, to, weight))
/// of a minimum spanning tree of the component containing `start`;
/// for a disconnected graph this covers only that component.
pub fn prim_mst(graph: &Graph, start: usize) -> (f64, Vec<(usize, usize, f64)>) {
    #[derive(PartialEq)]
    struct PrimEdge {
        weight: f64,
        from: usize,
        to: usize,
    }
    impl Eq for PrimEdge {}
    impl PartialOrd for PrimEdge {
        fn partial_cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Option<Ordering> {
            other.weight.partial_cmp(&self.weight)
        }
    }
    impl Ord for PrimEdge {
        fn cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Ordering {
            self.partial_cmp(other).unwrap_or(Ordering::Equal)
        }
    }
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut in_tree = vec![false; n];
    let mut heap = BinaryHeap::new();
    let mut total = 0.0;
    let mut edges = Vec::new();
    in_tree[start] = true;
    for e in graph.neighbours(start) {
        heap.push(PrimEdge { weight: e.weight, from: start, to: e.to });
    }
    while let Some(PrimEdge { weight, from, to }) = heap.pop() {
        if in_tree[to] {
            continue;
        }
        in_tree[to] = true;
        total += weight;
        edges.push((from, to, weight));
        for e in graph.neighbours(to) {
            if !in_tree[e.to] {
                heap.push(PrimEdge { weight: e.weight, from: to, to: e.to });
            }
        }
    }
    (total, edges)
}

/// Floyd-Warshall all-pairs shortest paths. dist[i][j] is None when
/// j is unreachable from i.
pub fn floyd_warshall(graph: &Graph) -> Vec<Vec<Option<f64>>> {
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut dist: Vec<Vec<Option<f64>>> = vec![vec![None; n]; n];
    for i in 0..n {
        dist[i][i] = Some(0.0);
        for e in graph.neighbours(i) {
            dist[i][e.to] = Some(match dist[i][e.to] {
                Some(d) => d.min(e.weight),
                None => e.weight,
            });
        }
    }
    for k in 0..n {
        for i in 0..n {
            for j in 0..n {
                if let (Some(ik), Some(kj)) = (dist[i][k], dist[k][j]) {
                    let via = ik + kj;
                    if dist[i][j].map_or(true, |d| via < d) {
                        dist[i][j] = Some(via);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }
    dist
}

/// Bellman-Ford: handles negative weights (but not negative cycles).
/// Returns None when a negative cycle is reachable from `start`.
pub fn bellman_ford(graph: &Graph, start: usize) -> Option<Vec<Option<f64>>> {
    let n = graph.node_count();
    let mut dist = vec![None; n];
    dist[start] = Some(0.0);
    for _ in 0..n.saturating_sub(1) {
        let mut changed = false;
        for u in 0..n {
            if let Some(du) = dist[u] {
                for e in graph.neighbours(u) {
                    let nd = du + e.weight;
                    if dist[e.to].map_or(true, |d| nd < d - 1e-9) {
                        dist[e.to] = Some(nd);
                        changed = true;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        if !changed {
            break;
        }
    }
    // One more pass: any further relaxation means a negative cycle.
    for u in 0..n {
        if let Some(du) = dist[u] {
            for e in graph.neighbours(u) {
                if dist[e.to].map_or(false, |d| du + e.weight < d - 1e-9) {
                    return None;
                }
            }
        }
    }
    Some(dist)
}

/// Shortest paths in a DAG in a single pass over a topological order.
/// Unreachable nodes stay None.
pub fn dag_shortest_paths(graph: &Graph, start: usize) -> Option<Vec<Option<f64>>> {
    let order = topological_sort(graph).ok()?;
    let mut dist = vec![None; graph.node_count()];
    dist[start] = Some(0.0);
    for &u in &order {
        if let Some(du) = dist[u] {
            for e in graph.neighbours(u) {
                let nd = du + e.weight;
                if dist[e.to].map_or(true, |d| nd < d) {
                    dist[e.to] = Some(nd);
                }
            }
        }
    }
    Some(dist)
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Example graphs
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A 3x3 grid of cities. Horizontal/vertical roads cost 12, diagonals 18,
/// plus a long direct road Aldgate-Foxham costing 30. Coordinates are in
/// kilometres, so Euclidean distance is an admissible A* heuristic:
/// every road costs more than the straight-line distance it covers.
pub fn city_network() -> Graph {
    let mut g = Graph::new();
    let cities = [
        ("Aldgate", 0.0, 0.0),
        ("Branton", 10.0, 0.0),
        ("Carrow", 20.0, 0.0),
        ("Dunley", 0.0, 10.0),
        ("Elmford", 10.0, 10.0),
        ("Foxham", 20.0, 10.0),
        ("Greystone", 0.0, 20.0),
        ("Hartley", 10.0, 20.0),
        ("Iverton", 20.0, 20.0),
    ];
    for (name, x, y) in cities {
        g.add_node(name, x, y);
    }
    let horiz = [
        ("Aldgate", "Branton"),
        ("Branton", "Carrow"),
        ("Dunley", "Elmford"),
        ("Elmford", "Foxham"),
        ("Greystone", "Hartley"),
        ("Hartley", "Iverton"),
        ("Aldgate", "Dunley"),
        ("Branton", "Elmford"),
        ("Carrow", "Foxham"),
        ("Dunley", "Greystone"),
        ("Elmford", "Hartley"),
        ("Foxham", "Iverton"),
    ];
    for (a, b) in horiz {
        g.add_undirected_by_name(a, b, 12.0);
    }
    let diag = [
        ("Aldgate", "Elmford"),
        ("Branton", "Foxham"),
        ("Dunley", "Hartley"),
        ("Elmford", "Iverton"),
        ("Branton", "Dunley"),
        ("Carrow", "Elmford"),
        ("Elmford", "Greystone"),
        ("Foxham", "Hartley"),
    ];
    for (a, b) in diag {
        g.add_undirected_by_name(a, b, 18.0);
    }
    g.add_undirected_by_name("Aldgate", "Foxham", 30.0);
    g.sort_adjacency();
    g
}

/// A software-release task DAG: an edge A -> B means A must finish
/// before B can start. Weights are task durations in days.
pub fn release_dag() -> Graph {
    let mut g = Graph::new();
    for (i, name) in ["spec", "design", "prototype", "implement", "test", "docs", "release"]
        .iter()
        .enumerate()
    {
        g.add_node(name, i as f64, 0.0);
    }
    g.add_edge_by_name("spec", "design", 3.0);
    g.add_edge_by_name("design", "prototype", 5.0);
    g.add_edge_by_name("design", "implement", 8.0);
    g.add_edge_by_name("prototype", "implement", 2.0);
    g.add_edge_by_name("implement", "test", 4.0);
    g.add_edge_by_name("implement", "docs", 3.0);
    g.add_edge_by_name("test", "release", 1.0);
    g.add_edge_by_name("docs", "release", 1.0);
    g.sort_adjacency();
    g
}

fn approx_eq(a: f64, b: f64) -> bool {
    (a - b).abs() < 1e-6
}

fn main() {
    let city = city_network();
    assert_eq!(city.node_count(), 9);
    let aldgate = city.node_id("Aldgate").unwrap();
    let iverton = city.node_id("Iverton").unwrap();
    let elmford = city.node_id("Elmford").unwrap();
    let foxham = city.node_id("Foxham").unwrap();

    // --- BFS: fewest hops, and the BFS tree ---
    let bfs_res = bfs(&city, aldgate);
    assert_eq!(bfs_res.distance[iverton], Some(2)); // Aldgate-Elmford-Iverton
    assert_eq!(bfs_res.distance[aldgate], Some(0));
    assert_eq!(bfs_res.distance[foxham], Some(1)); // direct long road
    assert_eq!(bfs_res.order[0], aldgate);
    assert_eq!(bfs_res.order.len(), 9); // the network is connected
    assert_eq!(
        bfs_path(&city, aldgate, iverton).unwrap(),
        vec![aldgate, elmford, iverton]
    );
    // BFS distances are hop counts, not road costs.
    assert_eq!(bfs(&city, iverton).distance[aldgate], Some(2));

    // --- DFS: both variants visit every city ---
    let rec = dfs_recursive(&city, aldgate);
    let it = dfs_iterative(&city, aldgate);
    assert_eq!(rec.len(), 9);
    assert_eq!(it.len(), 9);
    assert_eq!(rec[0], aldgate);
    assert_eq!(it[0], aldgate);
    // The two DFS variants visit the same set, though not necessarily in
    // the same order (iterative DFS marks nodes visited when pushed).
    let mut rec_sorted = rec.clone();
    let mut it_sorted = it.clone();
    rec_sorted.sort_unstable();
    it_sorted.sort_unstable();
    assert_eq!(rec_sorted, it_sorted);
    assert_eq!(rec_sorted, (0..9).collect::<Vec<_>>());
    let times = dfs_with_times(&city, aldgate);
    assert_eq!(times.discover[aldgate], Some(0));
    assert!(times.finish[aldgate].unwrap() > times.discover[aldgate].unwrap());
    // Discovery times are unique and finish times are unique.
    let mut d: Vec<_> = times.discover.iter().flatten().collect();
    d.sort();
    d.dedup();
    assert_eq!(d.len(), 9);

    // --- Dijkstra: cheapest road route ---
    // Aldgate-Elmford-Iverton costs 18+18 = 36; the direct Aldgate-Foxham
    // road plus Foxham-Iverton is 30+12 = 42; going round the edge is 48.
    let (path, cost) = shortest_path(&city, aldgate, iverton).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(path, vec![aldgate, elmford, iverton]);
    assert!(approx_eq(cost, 36.0));
    assert!(approx_eq(city.path_cost(&path).unwrap(), 36.0));
    assert_eq!(city.path_names(&path), vec!["Aldgate", "Elmford", "Iverton"]);
    let all = dijkstra(&city, aldgate);
    assert!(approx_eq(all.distance[foxham].unwrap(), 30.0)); // direct road wins
    assert!(approx_eq(all.distance[city.node_id("Carrow").unwrap()].unwrap(), 24.0));
    assert!(approx_eq(all.distance[city.node_id("Greystone").unwrap()].unwrap(), 24.0));
    // Every city is reachable.
    assert!(all.distance.iter().all(|d| d.is_some()));

    // --- A*: same optimal cost, guided by straight-line distance ---
    let (apath, acost) = astar_euclidean(&city, aldgate, iverton).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(apath, vec![aldgate, elmford, iverton]);
    assert!(approx_eq(acost, 36.0));
    // A* with a zero heuristic degenerates to Dijkstra and still agrees.
    let (zpath, zcost) = astar(&city, aldgate, iverton, |_| 0.0).unwrap();
    assert!(approx_eq(zcost, 36.0));
    assert_eq!(zpath, apath);
    // A* to a neighbour takes the direct road.
    let (fpath, fcost) = astar_euclidean(&city, aldgate, foxham).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(fpath, vec![aldgate, foxham]);
    assert!(approx_eq(fcost, 30.0));
    // Start == goal is a zero-length path.
    assert_eq!(astar_euclidean(&city, aldgate, aldgate).unwrap(), (vec![aldgate], 0.0));
    assert_eq!(shortest_path(&city, aldgate, aldgate).unwrap(), (vec![aldgate], 0.0));

    // --- Unreachable nodes: a disconnected city ---
    let mut g2 = city_network();
    let nowhere = g2.add_node("Nowhere", 100.0, 100.0);
    assert_eq!(bfs_path(&g2, aldgate, nowhere), None);
    assert_eq!(shortest_path(&g2, aldgate, nowhere), None);
    assert_eq!(astar_euclidean(&g2, aldgate, nowhere), None);
    assert_eq!(dijkstra(&g2, aldgate).distance[nowhere], None);
    assert_eq!(connected_components(&g2).len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(connected_components(&city).len(), 1);

    // --- Prim's MST: the grid's cheapest spanning tree uses eight
    // 12-cost roads and no diagonal, for a total of 96. ---
    let (mst_cost, mst_edges) = prim_mst(&city, aldgate);
    assert_eq!(mst_edges.len(), 8);
    assert!(approx_eq(mst_cost, 96.0));
    assert!(mst_edges.iter().all(|(_, _, w)| approx_eq(*w, 12.0)));

    // --- Floyd-Warshall agrees with Dijkstra from every source ---
    let fw = floyd_warshall(&city);
    for s in 0..city.node_count() {
        let d = dijkstra(&city, s);
        for t in 0..city.node_count() {
            assert!(approx_eq(fw[s][t].unwrap(), d.distance[t].unwrap()));
        }
    }
    assert!(approx_eq(fw[aldgate][iverton].unwrap(), 36.0));

    // --- Bellman-Ford on the (non-negative) city network ---
    let bf = bellman_ford(&city, aldgate).unwrap();
    assert!(approx_eq(bf[iverton].unwrap(), 36.0));
    // Bellman-Ford with a genuine negative edge (no negative cycle).
    let mut neg = Graph::new();
    let (a, b, c) = (neg.add_node("a", 0.0, 0.0), neg.add_node("b", 1.0, 0.0), neg.add_node("c", 2.0, 0.0));
    neg.add_edge(a, b, 4.0);
    neg.add_edge(a, c, 5.0);
    neg.add_edge(b, c, -2.0);
    let bf2 = bellman_ford(&neg, a).unwrap();
    assert!(approx_eq(bf2[c].unwrap(), 2.0)); // a->b->c beats a->c
    // A negative cycle is detected (returns None).
    let mut cyc_neg = Graph::new();
    let (x, y) = (cyc_neg.add_node("x", 0.0, 0.0), cyc_neg.add_node("y", 1.0, 0.0));
    cyc_neg.add_edge(x, y, 1.0);
    cyc_neg.add_edge(y, x, -3.0);
    assert!(bellman_ford(&cyc_neg, x).is_none());

    // --- Topological sort on the release DAG ---
    let dag = release_dag();
    assert!(!has_cycle(&dag));
    assert_eq!(find_cycle(&dag), None);
    let topo = topological_sort(&dag).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(topo.len(), 7);
    // Every edge goes forward in the order.
    let pos: HashMap<usize, usize> = topo.iter().enumerate().map(|(i, &n)| (n, i)).collect();
    for u in 0..dag.node_count() {
        for e in dag.neighbours(u) {
            assert!(pos[&u] < pos[&e.to], "topo order violated by {} -> {}", dag.name(u), dag.name(e.to));
        }
    }
    assert_eq!(dag.name(topo[0]), "spec");
    assert_eq!(dag.name(*topo.last().unwrap()), "release");
    let topo2 = topological_sort_dfs(&dag).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(topo2.len(), 7);
    for u in 0..dag.node_count() {
        for e in dag.neighbours(u) {
            let p2: HashMap<usize, usize> = topo2.iter().enumerate().map(|(i, &n)| (n, i)).collect();
            assert!(p2[&u] < p2[&e.to]);
        }
    }
    // DAG shortest paths from spec, in one topological pass:
    // implement via prototype is 3+5+2 = 10 (via design direct: 3+8 = 11),
    // test is 10+4 = 14, docs is 10+3 = 13, and release via docs is
    // 13+1 = 14 (via test it would be 14+1 = 15).
    let dag_dist = dag_shortest_paths(&dag, dag.node_id("spec").unwrap()).unwrap();
    assert!(approx_eq(dag_dist[dag.node_id("implement").unwrap()].unwrap(), 10.0));
    assert!(approx_eq(dag_dist[dag.node_id("test").unwrap()].unwrap(), 14.0));
    assert!(approx_eq(dag_dist[dag.node_id("docs").unwrap()].unwrap(), 13.0));
    assert!(approx_eq(dag_dist[dag.node_id("release").unwrap()].unwrap(), 14.0));
    // Dijkstra on the same DAG must agree with the one-pass result.
    let (dpath, dcost) = shortest_path(&dag, dag.node_id("spec").unwrap(), dag.node_id("release").unwrap()).unwrap();
    assert!(approx_eq(dcost, dag_dist[dag.node_id("release").unwrap()].unwrap()));
    println!("release shortest path: {:?} cost {}", dag.path_names(&dpath), dcost);

    // --- Cycle detection ---
    let mut cyc = Graph::new();
    let (p, q, r) = (cyc.add_node("p", 0.0, 0.0), cyc.add_node("q", 1.0, 0.0), cyc.add_node("r", 2.0, 0.0));
    cyc.add_edge(p, q, 1.0);
    cyc.add_edge(q, r, 1.0);
    cyc.add_edge(r, q, 1.0); // q <-> r cycle
    assert!(has_cycle(&cyc));
    let cycle = find_cycle(&cyc).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(cycle, vec![q, r]);
    assert!(topological_sort(&cyc).is_err());
    assert_eq!(topological_sort_dfs(&cyc), None);
    // A self-loop is a cycle of length one.
    let mut self_loop = Graph::new();
    let s = self_loop.add_node("s", 0.0, 0.0);
    self_loop.add_edge(s, s, 1.0);
    assert_eq!(find_cycle(&self_loop).unwrap(), vec![s]);

    // --- Strongly connected components ---
    // The undirected city network is one SCC.
    let sccs = strongly_connected_components(&city);
    assert_eq!(sccs.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(sccs[0].len(), 9);
    // In the DAG every node is its own SCC.
    let dag_sccs = strongly_connected_components(&dag);
    assert_eq!(dag_sccs.len(), 7);
    // q and r above are one SCC; p alone is another.
    let cyc_sccs = strongly_connected_components(&cyc);
    assert_eq!(cyc_sccs.len(), 2);
    assert!(cyc_sccs.iter().any(|c| c.len() == 2));

    println!("city network:\n{}", city);
    println!("graph_algorithms: all assertions passed");
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn triangle() -> Graph {
        let mut g = Graph::new();
        let (a, b, c) = (g.add_node("a", 0.0, 0.0), g.add_node("b", 3.0, 0.0), g.add_node("c", 0.0, 4.0));
        g.add_undirected_edge(a, b, 3.0);
        g.add_undirected_edge(b, c, 5.0);
        g.add_undirected_edge(a, c, 4.0);
        g.sort_adjacency();
        g
    }

    #[test]
    fn dijkstra_and_astar_agree() {
        let g = triangle();
        let (p, c) = shortest_path(&g, 0, 2).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(p, vec![0, 2]);
        assert!(approx_eq(c, 4.0));
        let (ap, ac) = astar_euclidean(&g, 0, 2).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(ap, vec![0, 2]);
        assert!(approx_eq(ac, 4.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn bfs_hops() {
        let g = triangle();
        assert_eq!(bfs(&g, 0).distance[2], Some(1));
        assert_eq!(bfs_path(&g, 1, 0).unwrap(), vec![1, 0]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn topo_on_chain() {
        let mut g = Graph::new();
        let (a, b, c) = (g.add_node("a", 0.0, 0.0), g.add_node("b", 1.0, 0.0), g.add_node("c", 2.0, 0.0));
        g.add_edge(a, b, 1.0);
        g.add_edge(b, c, 1.0);
        assert_eq!(topological_sort(&g).unwrap(), vec![a, b, c]);
        assert!(!has_cycle(&g));
    }

    #[test]
    fn cycle_found() {
        let mut g = Graph::new();
        let (a, b) = (g.add_node("a", 0.0, 0.0), g.add_node("b", 1.0, 0.0));
        g.add_edge(a, b, 1.0);
        g.add_edge(b, a, 1.0);
        assert_eq!(find_cycle(&g).unwrap(), vec![a, b]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn scc_two_cycles() {
        let mut g = Graph::new();
        let nodes: Vec<usize> = (0..4).map(|i| g.add_node(&format!("n{}", i), i as f64, 0.0)).collect();
        g.add_edge(nodes[0], nodes[1], 1.0);
        g.add_edge(nodes[1], nodes[0], 1.0);
        g.add_edge(nodes[2], nodes[3], 1.0);
        g.add_edge(nodes[3], nodes[2], 1.0);
        g.add_edge(nodes[1], nodes[2], 1.0);
        let sccs = strongly_connected_components(&g);
        assert_eq!(sccs.len(), 2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn prim_triangle() {
        let g = triangle();
        let (cost, edges) = prim_mst(&g, 0);
        assert!(approx_eq(cost, 7.0)); // 3 + 4
        assert_eq!(edges.len(), 2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn floyd_matches_dijkstra() {
        let g = city_network();
        let fw = floyd_warshall(&g);
        assert!(approx_eq(fw[0][8].unwrap(), 36.0));
        assert!(approx_eq(fw[8][0].unwrap(), 36.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn dag_shortest() {
        let dag = release_dag();
        let d = dag_shortest_paths(&dag, dag.node_id("spec").unwrap()).unwrap();
        assert!(approx_eq(d[dag.node_id("test").unwrap()].unwrap(), 14.0));
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/grep_tool.rs ---
//! grep_tool.rs — a small grep-like search tool, standard library only.
//!
//! Features:
//!   * literal substring search, case-insensitive search, whole-word search
//!   * a small wildcard matcher (`*` matches any run of characters, `?`
//!     matches exactly one character) — no regular expressions, no crates
//!   * line numbers, before/after/context lines, count-only mode, invert
//!     match, quiet mode, colour highlighting of the matched spans
//!   * searches one or more files, or standard input when no file is given
//!   * grep-compatible exit codes: 0 = match found, 1 = no match, 2 = error
//!
//! Usage:
//!   grep_tool [OPTIONS] PATTERN [FILE ...]
//!
//! Options:
//!   -n, --line-number        prefix each line with its 1-based line number
//!   -i, --ignore-case        match without regard to letter case
//!   -v, --invert-match       select lines that do NOT match
//!   -c, --count              print only the number of matching lines
//!   -w, --word               match only whole words
//!   -g, --wildcards          treat `*` and `?` in the pattern as wildcards
//!   -C, --context N          print N lines of context around each match
//!   -A, --after-context N    print N lines after each match
//!   -B, --before-context N   print N lines before each match
//!   -q, --quiet              print nothing, exit code only
//!       --color              highlight matches with ANSI escape codes
//!   -h, --help               print this help text
//!
//! With no command-line arguments the program runs a built-in demonstration
//! against sample text and asserts the results, so a successful run proves
//! the search engine works.

use std::env;
use std::fs::File;
use std::io::{self, BufRead, BufReader, Write};
use std::process::ExitCode;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Pattern matching
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Returns true for characters that count as part of a "word" for the
/// purposes of `--word` matching: letters, digits and underscore.
fn is_word_char(c: char) -> bool {
    c.is_alphanumeric() || c == '_'
}

/// Case folding used for `--ignore-case`. `to_lowercase` is a full Unicode
/// fold; matching is done on characters rather than bytes so the fold can
/// never desynchronise byte offsets.
fn fold_text(s: &str, ignore_case: bool) -> String {
    if ignore_case {
        s.to_lowercase()
    } else {
        s.to_string()
    }
}

/// A compiled search pattern. The pattern is stored both as a folded string
/// (for plain substring search) and as folded characters (for the wildcard
/// matcher and for whole-word boundary checks).
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Pattern {
    /// The pattern exactly as the user typed it.
    pub raw: String,
    /// Folded needle used for plain substring search.
    needle: String,
    /// Folded pattern characters used by the wildcard matcher.
    pat_chars: Vec<char>,
    ignore_case: bool,
    whole_word: bool,
    wildcards: bool,
}

impl Pattern {
    /// Compile a pattern. `wildcards` enables `*` / `?` handling; without
    /// it the pattern is a literal substring (the default grep behaviour
    /// for `-F` style searching).
    pub fn new(raw: &str, ignore_case: bool, whole_word: bool, wildcards: bool) -> Self {
        let needle = fold_text(raw, ignore_case);
        let pat_chars: Vec<char> = needle.chars().collect();
        Pattern {
            raw: raw.to_string(),
            needle,
            pat_chars,
            ignore_case,
            whole_word,
            wildcards,
        }
    }

    /// Convenience constructor for a plain case-sensitive substring search.
    pub fn literal(raw: &str) -> Self {
        Pattern::new(raw, false, false, false)
    }

    /// Does this pattern match anywhere in `line`?
    pub fn matches(&self, line: &str) -> bool {
        if self.needle.is_empty() {
            // An empty pattern matches every line, as in real grep.
            return true;
        }
        let folded = fold_text(line, self.ignore_case);
        if self.wildcards {
            let text: Vec<char> = folded.chars().collect();
            for start in 0..=text.len() {
                if wildcard_prefix(&self.pat_chars, &text[start..]).is_some() {
                    return true;
                }
            }
            false
        } else {
            !self.find_spans_in(&folded).is_empty()
        }
    }

    /// All non-overlapping matches in `line`, as (start, end) byte ranges
    /// into the *original* line. An empty pattern yields no spans (there is
    /// nothing to highlight) even though `matches` returns true.
    pub fn find_all(&self, line: &str) -> Vec<(usize, usize)> {
        if self.needle.is_empty() {
            return Vec::new();
        }
        let folded = fold_text(line, self.ignore_case);
        let spans = self.find_spans_in(&folded);
        // Translate character-index spans back to byte ranges of the
        // original line. Folding can change the byte length of a character
        // (for example the Turkish dotted capital I), so build the map from
        // the folded line, whose characters are in one-to-one correspondence
        // with the original line's characters.
        let byte_offsets: Vec<usize> = folded
            .char_indices()
            .map(|(i, _)| i)
            .chain(std::iter::once(folded.len()))
            .collect();
        spans
            .into_iter()
            .map(|(s, e)| (byte_offsets[s], byte_offsets[e]))
            .collect()
    }

    /// Core span search on an already-folded line. Returns character-index
    /// ranges. Handles plain substring search (with optional whole-word
    /// filtering) and wildcard search.
    fn find_spans_in(&self, folded_line: &str) -> Vec<(usize, usize)> {
        let text: Vec<char> = folded_line.chars().collect();
        let mut spans = Vec::new();
        if self.wildcards {
            let mut start = 0;
            while start <= text.len() {
                match wildcard_prefix(&self.pat_chars, &text[start..]) {
                    Some(len) if len > 0 => {
                        spans.push((start, start + len));
                        start += len;
                    }
                    _ => {
                        // Zero-length match (e.g. pattern "*") or no match
                        // at this position: advance one character.
                        start += 1;
                    }
                }
            }
        } else {
            let needle: Vec<char> = self.needle.chars().collect();
            if needle.is_empty() || needle.len() > text.len() {
                return spans;
            }
            let mut start = 0;
            while start + needle.len() <= text.len() {
                if text[start..start + needle.len()] == needle[..]
                    && (!self.whole_word || self.word_boundaries_ok(&text, start, start + needle.len()))
                {
                    spans.push((start, start + needle.len()));
                    start += needle.len();
                } else {
                    start += 1;
                }
            }
        }
        spans
    }

    /// Check that the characters immediately before `start` and after `end`
    /// are not word characters, i.e. the match is a whole word.
    fn word_boundaries_ok(&self, text: &[char], start: usize, end: usize) -> bool {
        let before_ok = start == 0 || !is_word_char(text[start - 1]);
        let after_ok = end >= text.len() || !is_word_char(text[end]);
        before_ok && after_ok
    }
}

/// Match `pat` against a prefix of `text` using `*` (any run, possibly
/// empty) and `?` (exactly one character). Returns the number of text
/// characters consumed by the *shortest* prefix match, or `None` when no
/// prefix of `text` matches.
///
/// The matcher is a small dynamic program: `dp[j]` records whether the
/// pattern consumed so far matches `text[..j]`. It is O(|pat| * |text|)
/// time and O(|text|) space, which is ample for single lines.
fn wildcard_prefix(pat: &[char], text: &[char]) -> Option<usize> {
    let n = text.len();
    let mut dp = vec![false; n + 1];
    dp[0] = true;
    for &pc in pat {
        let mut next = vec![false; n + 1];
        if pc == '*' {
            // '*' can swallow any number of characters: once the previous
            // row matches at some position, every later position matches.
            let mut seen = false;
            for j in 0..=n {
                if dp[j] {
                    seen = true;
                }
                next[j] = seen;
            }
        } else {
            for j in 1..=n {
                if dp[j - 1] && (pc == '?' || pc == text[j - 1]) {
                    next[j] = true;
                }
            }
        }
        dp = next;
    }
    (0..=n).find(|&j| dp[j])
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Search options and report
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// All user-controllable search behaviour, kept separate from the pattern
/// itself so the engine can be driven from tests without touching the CLI.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct SearchOptions {
    pub line_numbers: bool,
    pub ignore_case: bool,
    pub invert: bool,
    pub count: bool,
    pub whole_word: bool,
    pub wildcards: bool,
    pub before_context: usize,
    pub after_context: usize,
    pub quiet: bool,
    pub color: bool,
    /// When several files are searched, prefix output lines with the file
    /// name. The CLI turns this on automatically for multi-file searches.
    pub show_filename: bool,
}

impl SearchOptions {
    /// Total context radius: the larger of the two directional settings is
    /// what `--context` sets, so this helper is only used for reporting.
    pub fn context(&self) -> usize {
        self.before_context.max(self.after_context)
    }
}

/// One line of output: either a genuine match or a context line printed
/// around a match.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct OutputLine {
    /// 1-based line number in the input.
    pub number: usize,
    /// The line text, without its trailing newline.
    pub text: String,
    /// Match spans (byte ranges) inside `text`; empty for context lines.
    pub spans: Vec<(usize, usize)>,
    /// True when this line is context rather than a match.
    pub is_context: bool,
}

/// The complete result of searching one input source.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct SearchReport {
    /// Lines that matched (after inversion), before context is applied.
    pub matched_count: usize,
    /// Total lines scanned.
    pub total_lines: usize,
    /// Output lines grouped into blocks separated by gaps. Each inner
    /// vector is one contiguous block of matches plus their context; the
    /// formatter prints a `--` separator between blocks.
    pub groups: Vec<Vec<OutputLine>>,
}

impl SearchReport {
    /// True when at least one line matched.
    pub fn found(&self) -> bool {
        self.matched_count > 0
    }

    /// Total number of output lines across all groups.
    pub fn output_line_count(&self) -> usize {
        self.groups.iter().map(|g| g.len()).sum()
    }
}

/// Search an iterator of lines. The caller owns line splitting; this
/// function owns matching, inversion and context bookkeeping, which makes
/// it directly testable on in-memory text.
pub fn search_lines<I>(lines: I, pattern: &Pattern, opts: &SearchOptions) -> SearchReport
where
    I: IntoIterator<Item = String>,
{
    let all: Vec<String> = lines.into_iter().collect();
    let total_lines = all.len();

    // First pass: which lines match (after inversion)?
    let matched: Vec<bool> = all
        .iter()
        .map(|line| pattern.matches(line) ^ opts.invert)
        .collect();
    let matched_count = matched.iter().filter(|&&m| m).count();

    if opts.count || opts.quiet {
        // Count and quiet modes never need context lines.
        return SearchReport {
            matched_count,
            total_lines,
            groups: Vec::new(),
        };
    }

    // Second pass: build output blocks. A block covers a match plus its
    // before/after context; blocks whose ranges overlap or touch merge
    // into one, so a run of nearby matches prints as a single block.
    let mut groups: Vec<Vec<OutputLine>> = Vec::new();
    let mut current: Vec<OutputLine> = Vec::new();
    let mut current_end: Option<usize> = None; // exclusive end of block

    for (idx, _line) in all.iter().enumerate() {
        if !matched[idx] {
            continue;
        }
        let start = idx.saturating_sub(opts.before_context);
        let end = (idx + opts.after_context + 1).min(all.len());

        // If this match's window starts after the current block ended,
        // flush the block and start a new one.
        if let Some(block_end) = current_end {
            if start > block_end {
                groups.push(std::mem::take(&mut current));
                current_end = None;
            }
        }
        // Fill any gap between the previous block end and this window
        // start with context lines (this only happens when blocks merge).
        let fill_from = current_end.unwrap_or(start);
        for j in fill_from..start {
            current.push(OutputLine {
                number: j + 1,
                text: all[j].clone(),
                spans: Vec::new(),
                is_context: true,
            });
        }
        // Emit the window: the match itself plus context on both sides.
        for j in start..end {
            if j < fill_from {
                continue; // already emitted as part of the previous window
            }
            let is_match = matched[j];
            current.push(OutputLine {
                number: j + 1,
                text: all[j].clone(),
                spans: if is_match {
                    pattern.find_all(&all[j])
                } else {
                    Vec::new()
                },
                is_context: !is_match,
            });
        }
        current_end = Some(end);
    }
    if !current.is_empty() {
        groups.push(current);
    }

    SearchReport {
        matched_count,
        total_lines,
        groups,
    }
}

/// Search a buffered reader line by line. Lines are split on `\n` and a
/// trailing `\r` (Windows line endings) is stripped from each line.
pub fn search_reader<R: BufRead>(
    reader: R,
    pattern: &Pattern,
    opts: &SearchOptions,
) -> io::Result<SearchReport> {
    let mut lines = Vec::new();
    for line in reader.lines() {
        let mut line = line?;
        if line.ends_with('\r') {
            line.pop();
        }
        lines.push(line);
    }
    Ok(search_lines(lines, pattern, opts))
}

/// Convenience wrapper: search a whole string.
pub fn search_text(text: &str, pattern: &Pattern, opts: &SearchOptions) -> SearchReport {
    search_lines(text.lines().map(|l| l.to_string()), pattern, opts)
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Output formatting
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Insert ANSI colour codes around the given byte spans. Spans must be
/// sorted and non-overlapping, which `Pattern::find_all` guarantees.
fn highlight_spans(text: &str, spans: &[(usize, usize)]) -> String {
    if spans.is_empty() {
        return text.to_string();
    }
    const START: &str = "\x1b[1;31m"; // bold red
    const END: &str = "\x1b[0m";
    let mut out = String::with_capacity(text.len() + spans.len() * 9);
    let mut cursor = 0;
    for &(s, e) in spans {
        if s < cursor {
            continue;
        }
        out.push_str(&text[cursor..s]);
        out.push_str(START);
        out.push_str(&text[s..e]);
        out.push_str(END);
        cursor = e;
    }
    out.push_str(&text[cursor..]);
    out
}

/// Render one output line. The format mirrors GNU grep: an optional file
/// name, an optional line number, and `:` for matches versus `-` for
/// context lines.
fn format_line(
    line: &OutputLine,
    opts: &SearchOptions,
    filename: Option<&str>,
) -> String {
    let mut out = String::new();
    if let Some(name) = filename {
        if opts.show_filename {
            out.push_str(name);
            out.push(if line.is_context { '-' } else { ':' });
        }
    }
    if opts.line_numbers {
        out.push_str(&line.number.to_string());
        out.push(if line.is_context { '-' } else { ':' });
    }
    let body = if opts.color && !line.is_context {
        highlight_spans(&line.text, &line.spans)
    } else {
        line.text.clone()
    };
    out.push_str(&body);
    out
}

/// Render a whole report to text. In count mode the output is just the
/// number; in quiet mode it is empty.
pub fn format_report(
    report: &SearchReport,
    opts: &SearchOptions,
    filename: Option<&str>,
) -> String {
    if opts.quiet {
        return String::new();
    }
    if opts.count {
        return format!("{}\n", report.matched_count);
    }
    let mut out = String::new();
    for (gi, group) in report.groups.iter().enumerate() {
        if gi > 0 {
            out.push_str("--\n");
        }
        for line in group {
            out.push_str(&format_line(line, opts, filename));
            out.push('\n');
        }
    }
    out
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Command-line interface
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

const USAGE: &str = "\
usage: grep_tool [OPTIONS] PATTERN [FILE ...]

Search for PATTERN in each FILE (or standard input when no FILE is given).

Options:
  -n, --line-number        prefix matches with their line number
  -i, --ignore-case        case-insensitive matching
  -v, --invert-match       select non-matching lines
  -c, --count              print only the count of matching lines
  -w, --word               match whole words only
  -g, --wildcards          enable `*` and `?` wildcards in the pattern
  -C, --context N          print N lines of context around each match
  -A, --after-context N    print N lines after each match
  -B, --before-context N   print N lines before each match
  -q, --quiet              suppress output; exit code only
      --color              highlight matches in bold red
  -h, --help               show this help and exit

Exit codes: 0 = at least one match, 1 = no match, 2 = error (bad usage or
unreadable input).";

/// Parsed command line: a pattern, zero or more files, and the options.
#[derive(Debug)]
struct Cli {
    pattern: String,
    files: Vec<String>,
    opts: SearchOptions,
}

/// One command-line parsing failure, with a message for the user.
#[derive(Debug)]
struct CliError(String);

impl std::fmt::Display for CliError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}", self.0)
    }
}

/// Parse a context count such as the argument of `-C`. Rejects negative
/// numbers and non-numeric input with a helpful message.
fn parse_count(arg: &str, flag: &str) -> Result<usize, CliError> {
    arg.parse::<usize>()
        .map_err(|_| CliError(format!("invalid count for {}: {:?}", flag, arg)))
}

/// Parse the argument vector (excluding argv[0]) into a `Cli`. Supports
/// combined short flags (`-niv`), `--flag=value`, and `--` to stop flag
/// parsing so a pattern starting with `-` can be searched.
fn parse_args(args: &[String]) -> Result<Cli, CliError> {
    let mut opts = SearchOptions::default();
    let mut positional: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut no_more_flags = false;

    while i < args.len() {
        let arg = &args[i];
        if no_more_flags {
            positional.push(arg.clone());
            i += 1;
            continue;
        }
        if arg == "--" {
            no_more_flags = true;
            i += 1;
        } else if arg == "-h" || arg == "--help" {
            // Help is handled by the caller via a dedicated marker: encode
            // it as an empty pattern plus a sentinel file name is ugly, so
            // instead return a special error the caller recognises.
            return Err(CliError("__HELP__".to_string()));
        } else if let Some(long) = arg.strip_prefix("--") {
            // Long option, possibly with =value.
            let (name, inline_value) = match long.split_once('=') {
                Some((n, v)) => (n, Some(v.to_string())),
                None => (long, None),
            };
            match name {
                "line-number" => opts.line_numbers = true,
                "ignore-case" => opts.ignore_case = true,
                "invert-match" => opts.invert = true,
                "count" => opts.count = true,
                "word" => opts.whole_word = true,
                "wildcards" => opts.wildcards = true,
                "quiet" => opts.quiet = true,
                "color" => opts.color = true,
                "context" | "after-context" | "before-context" => {
                    let value = match inline_value {
                        Some(v) => v,
                        None => {
                            i += 1;
                            args.get(i)
                                .ok_or_else(|| {
                                    CliError(format!("option --{} needs a value", name))
                                })?
                                .clone()
                        }
                    };
                    let n = parse_count(&value, &format!("--{}", name))?;
                    match name {
                        "context" => {
                            opts.before_context = n;
                            opts.after_context = n;
                        }
                        "after-context" => opts.after_context = n,
                        _ => opts.before_context = n,
                    }
                }
                _ => return Err(CliError(format!("unknown option: --{}", name))),
            }
            i += 1;
        } else if arg.starts_with('-') && arg.len() > 1 {
            // Short option cluster: -niv, -C3, -C 3, ...
            let chars: Vec<char> = arg.chars().skip(1).collect();
            let mut j = 0;
            while j < chars.len() {
                match chars[j] {
                    'n' => opts.line_numbers = true,
                    'i' => opts.ignore_case = true,
                    'v' => opts.invert = true,
                    'c' => opts.count = true,
                    'w' => opts.whole_word = true,
                    'g' => opts.wildcards = true,
                    'q' => opts.quiet = true,
                    'h' => return Err(CliError("__HELP__".to_string())),
                    'C' | 'A' | 'B' => {
                        let flag = chars[j];
                        // The rest of the cluster may be the value (-C3),
                        // otherwise take the next argument (-C 3).
                        let value = if j + 1 < chars.len() {
                            chars[j + 1..].iter().collect::<String>()
                        } else {
                            i += 1;
                            args.get(i)
                                .ok_or_else(|| {
                                    CliError(format!("option -{} needs a value", flag))
                                })?
                                .clone()
                        };
                        let n = parse_count(&value, &format!("-{}", flag))?;
                        match flag {
                            'C' => {
                                opts.before_context = n;
                                opts.after_context = n;
                            }
                            'A' => opts.after_context = n,
                            _ => opts.before_context = n,
                        }
                        break; // value consumed the rest of the cluster
                    }
                    other => {
                        return Err(CliError(format!("unknown option: -{}", other)));
                    }
                }
                j += 1;
            }
            i += 1;
        } else {
            positional.push(arg.clone());
            i += 1;
        }
    }

    if positional.is_empty() {
        return Err(CliError("missing PATTERN\n".to_string() + USAGE));
    }
    let pattern = positional.remove(0);
    let files = positional;
    if files.len() > 1 {
        opts.show_filename = true;
    }
    Ok(Cli {
        pattern,
        files,
        opts,
    })
}

/// Run the tool in CLI mode. Returns the grep exit code: 0 found, 1 not
/// found, 2 error. All diagnostics go to stderr; matches go to stdout.
fn run_cli(args: &[String]) -> ExitCode {
    let cli = match parse_args(args) {
        Ok(cli) => cli,
        Err(e) if e.0 == "__HELP__" => {
            println!("{}", USAGE);
            return ExitCode::from(0);
        }
        Err(e) => {
            eprintln!("grep_tool: {}", e);
            return ExitCode::from(2);
        }
    };

    let pattern = Pattern::new(
        &cli.pattern,
        cli.opts.ignore_case,
        cli.opts.whole_word,
        cli.opts.wildcards,
    );

    let stdout = io::stdout();
    let mut out = io::BufWriter::new(stdout.lock());
    let mut any_match = false;
    let mut any_error = false;

    if cli.files.is_empty() {
        // No files: read standard input.
        let stdin = io::stdin();
        match search_reader(stdin.lock(), &pattern, &cli.opts) {
            Ok(report) => {
                any_match = report.found();
                let text = format_report(&report, &cli.opts, None);
                let _ = out.write_all(text.as_bytes());
            }
            Err(e) => {
                eprintln!("grep_tool: stdin: {}", e);
                any_error = true;
            }
        }
    } else {
        for filename in &cli.files {
            match File::open(filename) {
                Ok(file) => {
                    let reader = BufReader::new(file);
                    match search_reader(reader, &pattern, &cli.opts) {
                        Ok(report) => {
                            any_match = any_match || report.found();
                            let text = format_report(&report, &cli.opts, Some(filename));
                            let _ = out.write_all(text.as_bytes());
                        }
                        Err(e) => {
                            eprintln!("grep_tool: {}: {}", filename, e);
                            any_error = true;
                        }
                    }
                }
                Err(e) => {
                    eprintln!("grep_tool: {}: {}", filename, e);
                    any_error = true;
                }
            }
        }
    }
    let _ = out.flush();

    if any_error {
        ExitCode::from(2)
    } else if any_match {
        ExitCode::from(0)
    } else {
        ExitCode::from(1)
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Demonstration (runs when no arguments are given) and entry point
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A small fake application log used by the demonstration and the tests.
fn sample_log() -> &'static str {
    "2026-11-05 10:00:01 INFO  server starting on port 8080\n\
     2026-11-05 10:00:02 INFO  loading configuration from /etc/app/config.toml\n\
     2026-11-05 10:00:03 WARN  cache directory /var/cache/app is 92% full\n\
     2026-11-05 10:00:04 ERROR failed to connect to database: connection refused\n\
     2026-11-05 10:00:05 INFO  retrying database connection (attempt 1 of 3)\n\
     2026-11-05 10:00:06 ERROR database connection failed again: timeout\n\
     2026-11-05 10:00:07 INFO  falling back to read-only mode\n\
     2026-11-05 10:00:08 WARN  read-only mode: writes will be rejected\n\
     2026-11-05 10:00:09 INFO  server listening on port 8080\n\
     2026-11-05 10:00:10 INFO  transport layer initialised\n"
}

/// Run the built-in demonstration. Every check is an assertion, so a
/// successful run proves the search engine behaves as documented.
fn run_demo() {
    println!("grep_tool demonstration — searching a sample application log\n");
    let log = sample_log();

    // 1. Plain literal search.
    let pat = Pattern::literal("ERROR");
    let report = search_text(log, &pat, &SearchOptions::default());
    assert_eq!(report.matched_count, 2);
    assert_eq!(report.total_lines, 10);
    println!("1. literal \"ERROR\": {} matches", report.matched_count);

    // 2. Case-insensitive search finds the same lines; case-sensitive
    //    search for the lowercase spelling finds none.
    let pat_ci = Pattern::new("error", true, false, false);
    assert_eq!(search_text(log, &pat_ci, &SearchOptions::default()).matched_count, 2);
    let pat_cs = Pattern::new("error", false, false, false);
    assert_eq!(search_text(log, &pat_cs, &SearchOptions::default()).matched_count, 0);
    println!("2. case-insensitive \"error\": 2 matches; case-sensitive: 0");

    // 3. Whole-word search: "port" appears in "transport" too, so a plain
    //    search finds 3 lines but a whole-word search finds only 2.
    let pat_port = Pattern::literal("port");
    assert_eq!(search_text(log, &pat_port, &SearchOptions::default()).matched_count, 3);
    let pat_word = Pattern::new("port", false, true, false);
    assert_eq!(search_text(log, &pat_word, &SearchOptions::default()).matched_count, 2);
    println!("3. \"port\": 3 substring matches, 2 whole-word matches");

    // 4. Invert match: everything except the six INFO lines.
    let mut opts = SearchOptions::default();
    opts.invert = true;
    let pat_info = Pattern::literal("INFO");
    assert_eq!(search_text(log, &pat_info, &opts).matched_count, 4);
    println!("4. invert \"INFO\": 4 non-INFO lines");

    // 5. Count mode reports the number and produces no output lines.
    let mut opts = SearchOptions::default();
    opts.count = true;
    let report = search_text(log, &pat, &opts);
    assert_eq!(report.matched_count, 2);
    assert!(report.groups.is_empty());
    assert_eq!(format_report(&report, &opts, None), "2\n");
    println!("5. count mode for \"ERROR\": prints \"2\"");

    // 6. Context lines: the two ERROR lines are two lines apart, so with
    //    one line of context on each side they merge into a single block
    //    of five lines (3..7).
    let mut opts = SearchOptions::default();
    opts.before_context = 1;
    opts.after_context = 1;
    opts.line_numbers = true;
    let report = search_text(log, &pat, &opts);
    assert_eq!(report.groups.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(report.groups[0].len(), 5);
    assert_eq!(report.groups[0][0].number, 3);
    assert_eq!(report.groups[0][4].number, 7);
    assert!(report.groups[0][1].spans == vec![(20, 25)]);
    println!("6. context -C 1 around \"ERROR\": one merged block, lines 3-7");
    println!("{}", format_report(&report, &opts, None));

    // 7. Wildcard patterns: `*` spans characters, `?` matches one.
    let pat_wild = Pattern::new("ERR*R", false, false, true);
    assert_eq!(search_text(log, &pat_wild, &SearchOptions::default()).matched_count, 2);
    let pat_q = Pattern::new("2026-11-05 10:00:??", false, false, true);
    assert_eq!(search_text(log, &pat_q, &SearchOptions::default()).matched_count, 10);
    let pat_db = Pattern::new("*database*", false, false, true);
    assert_eq!(search_text(log, &pat_db, &SearchOptions::default()).matched_count, 3);
    println!("7. wildcards: \"ERR*R\" -> 2, \"2026-11-05 10:00:??\" -> 10, \"*database*\" -> 3");

    // 8. Searching a real file through the buffered-reader path.
    let path = std::env::temp_dir().join("grep_tool_demo.log");
    std::fs::write(&path, log).expect("write demo log");
    let file = File::open(&path).expect("open demo log");
    let report = search_reader(BufReader::new(file), &pat, &SearchOptions::default())
        .expect("search demo log");
    assert_eq!(report.matched_count, 2);
    std::fs::remove_file(&path).expect("remove demo log");
    println!("8. file search through BufReader: 2 matches, temp file cleaned up");

    // 9. Empty pattern matches every line (grep-compatible behaviour).
    let pat_empty = Pattern::literal("");
    assert_eq!(search_text(log, &pat_empty, &SearchOptions::default()).matched_count, 10);
    println!("9. empty pattern matches all 10 lines");

    // 10. CLI exit-code semantics, exercised through run_cli on a temp file.
    let path = std::env::temp_dir().join("grep_tool_demo2.log");
    std::fs::write(&path, log).expect("write demo log 2");
    let name = path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    let found = run_cli(&[
        "-q".to_string(),
        "ERROR".to_string(),
        name.clone(),
    ]);
    assert_eq!(found, ExitCode::from(0));
    let missing = run_cli(&[
        "-q".to_string(),
        "definitely-not-present".to_string(),
        name.clone(),
    ]);
    assert_eq!(missing, ExitCode::from(1));
    let broken = run_cli(&["ERROR".to_string(), "/nonexistent/nope.log".to_string()]);
    assert_eq!(broken, ExitCode::from(2));
    std::fs::remove_file(&path).expect("remove demo log 2");
    println!("10. exit codes: found=0, not found=1, unreadable file=2");

    println!("\nAll grep_tool demonstration checks passed.");
}

fn main() -> ExitCode {
    let args: Vec<String> = env::args().skip(1).collect();
    if args.is_empty() {
        run_demo();
        ExitCode::from(0)
    } else {
        run_cli(&args)
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Unit tests
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn opts() -> SearchOptions {
        SearchOptions::default()
    }

    #[test]
    fn wildcard_prefix_basics() {
        assert_eq!(wildcard_prefix(&['a', '*', 'c'], &['a', 'b', 'b', 'c', 'd']), Some(4));
        assert_eq!(wildcard_prefix(&['*'], &['x', 'y']), Some(0));
        assert_eq!(wildcard_prefix(&['?'], &['x', 'y']), Some(1));
        assert_eq!(wildcard_prefix(&['a', '?'], &['a']), None);
        assert_eq!(wildcard_prefix(&['a', 'b'], &['a', 'c']), None);
        assert_eq!(wildcard_prefix(&[], &['a']), Some(0));
        assert_eq!(wildcard_prefix(&['*', 'z'], &['a', 'b']), None);
        assert_eq!(wildcard_prefix(&['h', '*', 'o'], &['h', 'e', 'l', 'l', 'o']), Some(5));
    }

    #[test]
    fn plain_matching_and_spans() {
        let pat = Pattern::literal("ana");
        assert!(pat.matches("banana"));
        // Matches are non-overlapping: after "ana" at 1..4 the scan
        // resumes at 4, so the overlapping occurrence at 3..6 is skipped.
        assert_eq!(pat.find_all("banana"), vec![(1, 4)]);
        // Overlapping occurrences are reported non-overlapping.
        assert_eq!(Pattern::literal("aa").find_all("aaaa"), vec![(0, 2), (2, 4)]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn case_insensitive_spans_use_original_bytes() {
        let pat = Pattern::new("ERROR", true, false, false);
        assert_eq!(pat.find_all("xx error yy"), vec![(3, 8)]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn whole_word_boundaries() {
        let pat = Pattern::new("cat", false, true, false);
        assert!(pat.matches("the cat sat"));
        assert!(!pat.matches("concatenate"));
        assert!(pat.matches("cat."));
        assert!(pat.matches("(cat)"));
        assert!(!pat.matches("cats"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn count_and_invert_reports() {
        let text = "one\ntwo\none\nthree\n";
        let mut o = opts();
        o.count = true;
        let r = search_text(text, &Pattern::literal("one"), &o);
        assert_eq!(r.matched_count, 2);
        assert!(r.groups.is_empty());

        let mut o = opts();
        o.invert = true;
        let r = search_text(text, &Pattern::literal("one"), &o);
        assert_eq!(r.matched_count, 2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn context_blocks_merge_and_separate() {
        let text = "a\nMATCH\nb\nc\nd\nMATCH\ne\nf\ng\nMATCH\n";
        let mut o = opts();
        o.before_context = 1;
        o.after_context = 1;
        let r = search_text(text, &Pattern::literal("MATCH"), &o);
        // Matches on lines 2, 6 and 10 with one line of context each:
        // windows [1,3], [5,7] and [9,10] do not touch, so three blocks.
        assert_eq!(r.groups.len(), 3);
        assert_eq!(r.groups[0].len(), 3);
        assert_eq!(r.groups[1].len(), 3);
        assert_eq!(r.groups[2].len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(r.groups[2][0].number, 9);
        assert_eq!(format_report(&r, &o, None).matches("--\n").count(), 2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn context_lines_are_marked_and_unhighlighted() {
        let text = "before\nMATCH\nafter\n";
        let mut o = opts();
        o.before_context = 1;
        o.after_context = 1;
        let r = search_text(text, &Pattern::literal("MATCH"), &o);
        assert_eq!(r.groups[0].len(), 3);
        assert!(r.groups[0][0].is_context);
        assert!(!r.groups[0][1].is_context);
        assert!(r.groups[0][2].is_context);
        assert!(r.groups[0][0].spans.is_empty());
        assert_eq!(r.groups[0][1].spans, vec![(0, 5)]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn line_numbers_and_separator_format() {
        let text = "x\nMATCH\ny\n";
        let mut o = opts();
        o.line_numbers = true;
        let r = search_text(text, &Pattern::literal("MATCH"), &o);
        assert_eq!(format_report(&r, &o, None), "2:MATCH\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn cli_parsing_clusters_and_equals() {
        let cli = parse_args(&[
            "-niv".to_string(),
            "--context=2".to_string(),
            "pat".to_string(),
            "a.log".to_string(),
            "b.log".to_string(),
        ])
        .unwrap();
        assert!(cli.opts.line_numbers);
        assert!(cli.opts.ignore_case);
        assert!(cli.opts.invert);
        assert_eq!(cli.opts.before_context, 2);
        assert_eq!(cli.opts.after_context, 2);
        assert!(cli.opts.show_filename);
        assert_eq!(cli.files, vec!["a.log", "b.log"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn cli_parsing_errors() {
        assert!(parse_args(&[]).is_err());
