        assert!(parse_args(&["--bogus".to_string(), "x".to_string()]).is_err());
        assert!(parse_args(&["-C".to_string(), "notanumber".to_string(), "x".to_string()]).is_err());
        assert!(parse_args(&["-C".to_string()]).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn cli_double_dash_pattern() {
        let cli = parse_args(&["--".to_string(), "-weird".to_string()]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(cli.pattern, "-weird");
    }

    #[test]
    fn search_reader_handles_crlf() {
        let data = b"one\r\ntwo\r\none\r\n";
        let cursor = io::Cursor::new(data.to_vec());
        let r = search_reader(cursor, &Pattern::literal("one"), &opts()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(r.matched_count, 2);
        assert_eq!(r.groups[0][0].text, "one");
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/http_router.rs ---
//! http_router.rs — an HTTP request parser and router without any socket
//! code. Standard library only.
//!
//! This file covers the HTTP layer *above* the network: given the raw
//! bytes of a request (as text), parse them into a structured `Request`,
//! dispatch the request through a `Router` to a handler, and serialize
//! the handler's `Response` back into wire format. Nothing here opens a
//! socket, binds a port or spawns a thread — a real server would read
//! bytes from a connection, call `Request::parse`, hand the request to
//! `Router::handle`, and write `Response::to_bytes` back.
//!
//! Contents:
//!   * `Method` — the HTTP method, with the common methods as variants
//!     and anything else as `Method::Other`.
//!   * `percent_decode` — `%XX` decoding, with optional `+`-as-space
//!     for query strings and form bodies.
//!   * `Request` — method, decoded path, query parameters (kept in
//!     order, duplicates preserved), version, headers (case-insensitive
//!     lookup) and body, plus `form_params` for
//!     `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` bodies.
//!   * `Router` — routes of `METHOD pattern => handler` where patterns
//!     are `/`-separated segments, `:name` captures one segment, and a
//!     trailing `/*` captures the rest. Dispatch returns the handler
//!     and its captured parameters; `handle` turns a miss into a 404,
//!     and a path that matches under a different method into a 405
//!     with an `Allow` header.
//!   * `Response` — status, headers and body, with builders for the
//!     common cases and a `to_bytes` serializer that emits a correct
//!     `HTTP/1.1` response including an automatic `Content-Length`.
//!
//! With no arguments the program runs a demonstration: several raw
//! requests are parsed and asserted on, routed through a small router,
//! and their responses serialized and checked byte-for-byte.

use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::fmt;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Method
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// An HTTP request method. The seven methods this router treats as
/// first-class are variants; anything else (a custom method, or a
/// future standard one) is `Other` and still routes normally.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum Method {
    Get,
    Post,
    Put,
    Delete,
    Patch,
    Head,
    Options,
    Other(String),
}

impl Method {
    /// Parse a method token. Matching is case-sensitive, as the HTTP
    /// specification requires method tokens to be case-sensitive.
    pub fn parse(token: &str) -> Method {
        match token {
            "GET" => Method::Get,
            "POST" => Method::Post,
            "PUT" => Method::Put,
            "DELETE" => Method::Delete,
            "PATCH" => Method::Patch,
            "HEAD" => Method::Head,
            "OPTIONS" => Method::Options,
            other => Method::Other(other.to_string()),
        }
    }

    /// The method as it appears on the wire.
    pub fn as_str(&self) -> &str {
        match self {
            Method::Get => "GET",
            Method::Post => "POST",
            Method::Put => "PUT",
            Method::Delete => "DELETE",
            Method::Patch => "PATCH",
            Method::Head => "HEAD",
            Method::Options => "OPTIONS",
            Method::Other(s) => s,
        }
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Method {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}", self.as_str())
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Percent decoding
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// One hexadecimal digit's value, or `None`.
fn hex_value(b: u8) -> Option<u8> {
    match b {
        b'0'..=b'9' => Some(b - b'0'),
        b'a'..=b'f' => Some(b - b'a' + 10),
        b'A'..=b'F' => Some(b - b'A' + 10),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Decode `%XX` sequences in `input`. A malformed sequence (a `%` not
/// followed by two hex digits, or a `%` at the very end) is left as a
/// literal `%` rather than failing the whole decode. When
/// `plus_as_space` is true, `+` decodes to a space — the rule for query
/// strings and `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` bodies; in a path a
/// `+` stays a `+`. Invalid UTF-8 after decoding is replaced with the
/// Unicode replacement character, never an error.
pub fn percent_decode(input: &str, plus_as_space: bool) -> String {
    let bytes = input.as_bytes();
    let mut out: Vec<u8> = Vec::with_capacity(bytes.len());
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < bytes.len() {
        match bytes[i] {
            b'%' if i + 2 < bytes.len() => {
                match (hex_value(bytes[i + 1]), hex_value(bytes[i + 2])) {
                    (Some(hi), Some(lo)) => {
                        out.push(hi * 16 + lo);
                        i += 3;
                    }
                    _ => {
                        out.push(b'%');
                        i += 1;
                    }
                }
            }
            b'+' if plus_as_space => {
                out.push(b' ');
                i += 1;
            }
            b => {
                out.push(b);
                i += 1;
            }
        }
    }
    String::from_utf8_lossy(&out).into_owned()
}

/// Percent-encode a string for use in a query value: everything except
/// the unreserved characters `A-Z a-z 0-9 - _ . ~` becomes `%XX`, and a
/// space becomes `+`. Provided so the demo can round-trip values.
pub fn percent_encode_query(value: &str) -> String {
    let mut out = String::new();
    for b in value.bytes() {
        match b {
            b'A'..=b'Z' | b'a'..=b'z' | b'0'..=b'9' | b'-' | b'_' | b'.' | b'~' => {
                out.push(b as char)
            }
            b' ' => out.push('+'),
            _ => out.push_str(&format!("%{:02X}", b)),
        }
    }
    out
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Request parsing
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A request that failed to parse: what was wrong. No position is
/// tracked — HTTP errors are reported by kind, and the demo asserts on
/// the message text.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct RequestParseError {
    pub message: String,
}

impl fmt::Display for RequestParseError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "request parse error: {}", self.message)
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for RequestParseError {}

/// A parsed HTTP request.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Request {
    /// The request method.
    pub method: Method,
    /// The full request target exactly as sent (path plus query string).
    pub raw_target: String,
    /// The percent-decoded path, without the query string.
    pub path: String,
    /// Query parameters in order; duplicates are preserved.
    pub query: Vec<(String, String)>,
    /// The HTTP version token, e.g. `HTTP/1.1`.
    pub version: String,
    /// Headers in order, as `(name, value)` with the name as sent.
    pub headers: Vec<(String, String)>,
    /// The request body, as raw bytes.
    pub body: Vec<u8>,
}

impl Request {
    /// Parse a complete raw request (request line, headers, blank line,
    /// body). Accepts both CRLF and bare-LF line endings. The body is
    /// delimited by `Content-Length` when that header is present;
    /// otherwise everything after the blank line is the body.
    pub fn parse(raw: &str) -> Result<Request, RequestParseError> {
        let err = |msg: &str| RequestParseError {
            message: msg.to_string(),
        };
        // Split head from body at the first blank line.
        let (head, body) = match find_blank_line(raw) {
            Some((head, body)) => (head, body),
            None => (raw, ""),
        };
        let mut lines = head.split('\n');
        let request_line = lines.next().unwrap_or("").trim_end_matches('\r');
        if request_line.is_empty() {
            return Err(err("empty request (no request line)"));
        }
        let mut parts = request_line.split_whitespace();
        let method_token = parts.next().ok_or_else(|| err("request line has no method"))?;
        let target = parts.next().ok_or_else(|| err("request line has no target"))?;
        let version = parts.next().ok_or_else(|| err("request line has no HTTP version"))?;
        if parts.next().is_some() {
            return Err(err("request line has too many parts"));
        }
        if !version.starts_with("HTTP/") {
            return Err(err(&format!("bad HTTP version {:?}", version)));
        }
        let method = Method::parse(method_token);

        // Target: path and query string.
        let (raw_path, raw_query) = match target.split_once('?') {
            Some((p, q)) => (p, Some(q)),
            None => (target, None),
        };
        let path = percent_decode(raw_path, false);
        let query = match raw_query {
            Some(q) => parse_query_string(q),
            None => Vec::new(),
        };

        // Headers: `Name: value`, one per line.
        let mut headers: Vec<(String, String)> = Vec::new();
        for line in lines {
            let line = line.trim_end_matches('\r');
            if line.is_empty() {
                continue;
            }
            let (name, value) = line
                .split_once(':')
                .ok_or_else(|| err(&format!("malformed header line {:?}", line)))?;
            let name = name.trim();
            if name.is_empty() {
                return Err(err("header with an empty name"));
            }
            headers.push((name.to_string(), value.trim().to_string()));
        }

        // Body: honour Content-Length when present.
        let body_bytes = body.as_bytes();
        let body_vec = match header_lookup(&headers, "content-length") {
            Some(len_str) => {
                let len: usize = len_str
                    .parse()
                    .map_err(|_| err("Content-Length is not a number"))?;
                if body_bytes.len() < len {
                    return Err(err(&format!(
                        "body is {} bytes but Content-Length says {}",
                        body_bytes.len(),
                        len
                    )));
                }
                body_bytes[..len].to_vec()
            }
            None => body_bytes.to_vec(),
        };

        Ok(Request {
            method,
            raw_target: target.to_string(),
            path,
            query,
            version: version.to_string(),
            headers,
            body: body_vec,
        })
    }

    /// First query value for `name`, or `None`.
    pub fn query_param(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        self.query
            .iter()
            .find(|(k, _)| k == name)
            .map(|(_, v)| v.as_str())
    }

    /// All query values for `name`, in order.
    pub fn query_all(&self, name: &str) -> Vec<&str> {
        self.query
            .iter()
            .filter(|(k, _)| k == name)
            .map(|(_, v)| v.as_str())
            .collect()
    }

    /// First header value for `name`, matched case-insensitively.
    pub fn header(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        header_lookup(&self.headers, name)
    }

    /// All header values for `name`, matched case-insensitively.
    pub fn headers_all(&self, name: &str) -> Vec<&str> {
        self.headers
            .iter()
            .filter(|(k, _)| k.eq_ignore_ascii_case(name))
            .map(|(_, v)| v.as_str())
            .collect()
    }

    /// The body as text, lossily decoded.
    pub fn body_text(&self) -> String {
        String::from_utf8_lossy(&self.body).into_owned()
    }

    /// Parse the body as `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` data into
    /// key/value pairs. Used for HTML form submissions.
    pub fn form_params(&self) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
        parse_query_string(&self.body_text())
    }

    /// True when the `Content-Type` header names form data.
    pub fn is_form(&self) -> bool {
        self.header("content-type")
            .map(|ct| ct.starts_with("application/x-www-form-urlencoded"))
            .unwrap_or(false)
    }
}

/// Find the blank line separating head from body. Returns the head
/// (without the blank line) and the body. Handles CRLF (`\r\n\r\n`)
/// and LF (`\n\n`) endings.
fn find_blank_line(raw: &str) -> Option<(&str, &str)> {
    if let Some(pos) = raw.find("\r\n\r\n") {
        Some((&raw[..pos], &raw[pos + 4..]))
    } else if let Some(pos) = raw.find("\n\n") {
        Some((&raw[..pos], &raw[pos + 2..]))
    } else {
        None
    }
}

/// Case-insensitive header lookup over a header list.
fn header_lookup<'a>(headers: &'a [(String, String)], name: &str) -> Option<&'a str> {
    headers
        .iter()
        .find(|(k, _)| k.eq_ignore_ascii_case(name))
        .map(|(_, v)| v.as_str())
}

/// Parse a query string (`a=1&b=2`) into ordered pairs. `+` decodes to
/// a space and `%XX` sequences are decoded; a pair without `=` gets an
/// empty value, and an empty string yields no pairs.
pub fn parse_query_string(raw: &str) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    if raw.is_empty() {
        return out;
    }
    for pair in raw.split('&') {
        if pair.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }
        match pair.split_once('=') {
            Some((k, v)) => out.push((
                percent_decode(k, true),
                percent_decode(v, true),
            )),
            None => out.push((percent_decode(pair, true), String::new())),
        }
    }
    out
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Routing
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Captured path parameters, e.g. `id` for the route `/users/:id`.
pub type Params = HashMap<String, String>;

/// One route: a method, a path pattern and the handler to call.
/// Patterns are `/`-separated segments. A segment starting with `:`
/// captures that path segment under the segment's name, and a trailing
/// `/*` captures the rest of the path under `rest`.
pub struct Route {
    pub method: Method,
    pub pattern: &'static str,
    pub handler: fn(&Request, &Params) -> Response,
}

/// A table of routes. Routes are tried in order, so a more specific
/// route should be listed before a more general one.
pub struct Router {
    routes: Vec<Route>,
}

impl Router {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Router { routes: Vec::new() }
    }

    /// Add a route, builder-style, so a router reads like a route table.
    pub fn route(
        mut self,
        method: Method,
        pattern: &'static str,
        handler: fn(&Request, &Params) -> Response,
    ) -> Self {
        self.routes.push(Route {
            method,
            pattern,
            handler,
        });
        self
    }

    /// Number of registered routes.
    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.routes.len()
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.routes.is_empty()
    }

    /// Find the first route whose method and pattern match the request,
    /// returning its handler and captured parameters.
    pub fn dispatch(&self, req: &Request) -> Option<(fn(&Request, &Params) -> Response, Params)> {
        for route in &self.routes {
            if route.method != req.method {
                continue;
            }
            if let Some(params) = match_pattern(route.pattern, &req.path) {
                return Some((route.handler, params));
            }
        }
        None
    }

    /// The methods that have a route for this path, in registration
    /// order. Used for the `Allow` header of a 405 response.
    pub fn allowed_methods(&self, path: &str) -> Vec<Method> {
        let mut methods = Vec::new();
        for route in &self.routes {
            if match_pattern(route.pattern, path).is_some() && !methods.contains(&route.method) {
                methods.push(route.method.clone());
            }
        }
        methods
    }

    /// Handle a request end to end: dispatch it, or answer 405 when
    /// the path matches under another method, or 404 when nothing
    /// matches at all.
    pub fn handle(&self, req: &Request) -> Response {
        if let Some((handler, params)) = self.dispatch(req) {
            return handler(req, &params);
        }
        let allowed = self.allowed_methods(&req.path);
        if !allowed.is_empty() {
            let allow = allowed
                .iter()
                .map(|m| m.as_str())
                .collect::<Vec<&str>>()
                .join(", ");
            return Response::new(405)
                .header("Allow", &allow)
                .with_text(&format!("method {} not allowed for {}", req.method, req.path));
        }
        Response::not_found().with_text(&format!("no route for {} {}", req.method, req.path))
    }
}

impl Default for Router {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Router::new()
    }
}

/// Match a route pattern against a concrete path. Returns the captured
/// parameters on a match.
pub fn match_pattern(pattern: &str, path: &str) -> Option<Params> {
    let pattern_segments: Vec<&str> = pattern.split('/').filter(|s| !s.is_empty()).collect();
    let path_segments: Vec<&str> = path.split('/').filter(|s| !s.is_empty()).collect();
    let mut params = Params::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < pattern_segments.len() {
        let segment = pattern_segments[i];
        if segment == "*" {
            // Trailing wildcard: capture the rest of the path.
            let rest = path_segments[i..].join("/");
            params.insert("rest".to_string(), rest);
            return Some(params);
        }
        if i >= path_segments.len() {
            return None;
        }
        if let Some(name) = segment.strip_prefix(':') {
            params.insert(name.to_string(), path_segments[i].to_string());
        } else if segment != path_segments[i] {
            return None;
        }
        i += 1;
    }
    if path_segments.len() != pattern_segments.len() {
        return None;
    }
    Some(params)
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Response
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// An HTTP response: a status code, headers and a body. Built with the
/// constructor and the chaining helpers, then serialized with
/// `to_bytes` for the wire.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Response {
    pub status: u16,
    pub headers: Vec<(String, String)>,
    pub body: Vec<u8>,
}

impl Response {
    /// A response with this status, no headers and an empty body.
    pub fn new(status: u16) -> Self {
        Response {
            status,
            headers: Vec::new(),
            body: Vec::new(),
        }
    }

    /// 200 with a plain-text body.
    pub fn text(body: &str) -> Self {
        Response::new(200)
            .header("Content-Type", "text/plain; charset=utf-8")
            .body(body.as_bytes().to_vec())
    }

    /// 200 with a JSON body.
    pub fn json(body: &str) -> Self {
        Response::new(200)
            .header("Content-Type", "application/json")
            .body(body.as_bytes().to_vec())
    }

    /// 404 with an empty body (callers usually chain `.text(...)`).
    pub fn not_found() -> Self {
        Response::new(404)
    }

    /// 301 redirect to `location`.
    pub fn redirect(location: &str) -> Self {
        Response::new(301)
            .header("Location", location)
            .with_text(&format!("redirecting to {}", location))
    }

    /// Set a plain-text body, builder-style (like `text`, but chainable
    /// after other builder calls).
    pub fn with_text(mut self, body: &str) -> Self {
        self.headers
            .retain(|(k, _)| !k.eq_ignore_ascii_case("content-type"));
        self.headers.push((
            "Content-Type".to_string(),
            "text/plain; charset=utf-8".to_string(),
        ));
        self.body = body.as_bytes().to_vec();
        self
    }

    /// Add a header, builder-style. Duplicate names are kept as
    /// separate headers, which HTTP allows.
    pub fn header(mut self, name: &str, value: &str) -> Self {
        self.headers.push((name.to_string(), value.to_string()));
        self
    }

    /// Replace the body, builder-style.
    pub fn body(mut self, body: Vec<u8>) -> Self {
        self.body = body;
        self
    }

    /// The reason phrase for a status code (the standard phrase where
    /// one is registered, `Unknown` otherwise).
    pub fn reason_phrase(status: u16) -> &'static str {
        match status {
            200 => "OK",
            201 => "Created",
            204 => "No Content",
            301 => "Moved Permanently",
            302 => "Found",
            304 => "Not Modified",
            400 => "Bad Request",
            401 => "Unauthorized",
            403 => "Forbidden",
            404 => "Not Found",
            405 => "Method Not Allowed",
            408 => "Request Timeout",
            409 => "Conflict",
            411 => "Length Required",
            413 => "Payload Too Large",
            415 => "Unsupported Media Type",
            422 => "Unprocessable Entity",
            429 => "Too Many Requests",
            500 => "Internal Server Error",
            501 => "Not Implemented",
            502 => "Bad Gateway",
            503 => "Service Unavailable",
            _ => "Unknown",
        }
    }

    /// The status line, e.g. `HTTP/1.1 200 OK`.
    pub fn status_line(&self) -> String {
        format!("HTTP/1.1 {} {}", self.status, Self::reason_phrase(self.status))
    }

    /// First header value for `name`, case-insensitive.
    pub fn header_value(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        header_lookup(&self.headers, name)
    }

    /// The body as text, lossily decoded.
    pub fn body_text(&self) -> String {
        String::from_utf8_lossy(&self.body).into_owned()
    }

    /// Serialize the whole response to wire format: status line, headers
    /// (with an automatic `Content-Length` when the caller did not set
    /// one), a blank line, then the body.
    pub fn to_bytes(&self) -> Vec<u8> {
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        out.extend_from_slice(self.status_line().as_bytes());
        out.extend_from_slice(b"\r\n");
        for (name, value) in &self.headers {
            out.extend_from_slice(name.as_bytes());
            out.extend_from_slice(b": ");
            out.extend_from_slice(value.as_bytes());
            out.extend_from_slice(b"\r\n");
        }
        if self.header_value("content-length").is_none() {
            out.extend_from_slice(format!("Content-Length: {}\r\n", self.body.len()).as_bytes());
        }
        out.extend_from_slice(b"\r\n");
        out.extend_from_slice(&self.body);
        out
    }

    /// `to_bytes` as a (lossy) string, for tests and the demo.
    pub fn to_string(&self) -> String {
        String::from_utf8_lossy(&self.to_bytes()).into_owned()
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Demonstration
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Demo route handlers.
fn home(_req: &Request, _params: &Params) -> Response {
    Response::text("welcome to the demo server")
}

fn list_users(req: &Request, _params: &Params) -> Response {
    let page = req.query_param("page").unwrap_or("1");
    let limit = req.query_param("limit").unwrap_or("20");
    Response::json(&format!("{{\"page\": {}, \"limit\": {}}}", page, limit))
}

fn get_user(_req: &Request, params: &Params) -> Response {
    let id = params.get("id").map(|s| s.as_str()).unwrap_or("?");
    Response::text(&format!("user {}", id))
}

fn create_user(req: &Request, _params: &Params) -> Response {
    let form = req.form_params();
    let name = form
        .iter()
        .find(|(k, _)| k == "name")
        .map(|(_, v)| v.clone())
        .unwrap_or_else(|| "unknown".to_string());
    Response::new(201)
        .header("Content-Type", "application/json")
        .body(format!("{{\"created\": true, \"name\": \"{}\"}}", name).into_bytes())
}

fn search(req: &Request, _params: &Params) -> Response {
    let q = req.query_param("q").unwrap_or("");
    let tag = req.query_param("tag").unwrap_or("");
    Response::text(&format!("results for {:?} (tag {:?})", q, tag))
}

fn static_file(_req: &Request, params: &Params) -> Response {
    let rest = params.get("rest").map(|s| s.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
    Response::text(&format!("file: {}", rest))
}

fn echo_agent(req: &Request, _params: &Params) -> Response {
    Response::text(&format!("agent: {}", req.header("user-agent").unwrap_or("-")))
}

fn demo_router() -> Router {
    Router::new()
        .route(Method::Get, "/", home)
        .route(Method::Get, "/users", list_users)
        .route(Method::Post, "/users", create_user)
        .route(Method::Get, "/users/:id", get_user)
        .route(Method::Get, "/search", search)
        .route(Method::Get, "/static/*", static_file)
        .route(Method::Get, "/agent", echo_agent)
}

fn run_demo() {
    println!("http_router demonstration — request parsing and routing\n");

    // 1. Parse a GET with a query string and headers (CRLF endings).
    let raw = "GET /users?page=2&limit=10 HTTP/1.1\r\n\
               Host: example.com\r\n\
               Accept: application/json\r\n\
               X-Custom: demo-value\r\n\
               \r\n";
    let req = Request::parse(raw).expect("parse GET request");
    assert_eq!(req.method, Method::Get);
    assert_eq!(req.raw_target, "/users?page=2&limit=10");
    assert_eq!(req.path, "/users");
    assert_eq!(req.version, "HTTP/1.1");
    assert_eq!(req.query_param("page"), Some("2"));
    assert_eq!(req.query_param("limit"), Some("10"));
    assert_eq!(req.query_param("missing"), None);
    assert_eq!(req.header("host"), Some("example.com"));
    assert_eq!(req.header("HOST"), Some("example.com"), "headers match case-insensitively");
    assert_eq!(req.header("x-custom"), Some("demo-value"));
    assert!(req.body.is_empty());
    println!("1. GET /users?page=2&limit=10 parsed: query + headers (case-insensitive)");

    // 2. Parse a POST with a form body (LF endings, no Content-Length).
    let raw = "POST /users HTTP/1.1\n\
               Host: example.com\n\
               Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded\n\
               \n\
               name=Grace+Hopper&city=New+York";
    let req = Request::parse(raw).expect("parse POST request");
    assert_eq!(req.method, Method::Post);
    assert!(req.is_form());
    let form = req.form_params();
    assert_eq!(form, vec![
        ("name".to_string(), "Grace Hopper".to_string()),
        ("city".to_string(), "New York".to_string()),
    ]);
    println!("2. POST form body parsed: name=Grace Hopper, city=New York");

    // 3. Percent-decoding in the query string, including duplicates and
    //    a valueless parameter.
    let raw = "GET /search?q=hello%20world&tag=rust%2Blang&empty&dup=1&dup=2 HTTP/1.1\r\n\r\n";
    let req = Request::parse(raw).expect("parse search request");
    assert_eq!(req.query_param("q"), Some("hello world"));
    assert_eq!(req.query_param("tag"), Some("rust+lang"), "+ in a query value stays a plus once decoded from %2B");
    assert_eq!(req.query_param("empty"), Some(""));
    assert_eq!(req.query_all("dup"), vec!["1", "2"]);
    assert_eq!(percent_decode("/a%20b+c", false), "/a b+c", "plus stays plus in a path");
    assert_eq!(percent_decode("a+b", true), "a b", "plus becomes a space in a query");
    assert_eq!(percent_decode("100%25", true), "100%");
    assert_eq!(percent_decode("bad%zzend", true), "bad%zzend", "malformed escapes stay literal");
    println!("3. percent-decoding: %20, %2B, duplicates, valueless params, malformed escapes");

    // 4. Percent-encoding round-trips through decoding.
    let original = "hello world & more/special?chars";
    let encoded = percent_encode_query(original);
    assert_eq!(encoded, "hello+world+%26+more%2Fspecial%3Fchars");
    assert_eq!(percent_decode(&encoded, true), original);
    println!("4. percent-encoding round-trips: {:?} -> {:?}", original, encoded);

    // 5. Content-Length delimits the body; extra bytes are ignored.
    let raw = "POST /echo HTTP/1.1\r\nContent-Length: 10\r\n\r\nname=GraceEXTRA-BYTES";
    let req = Request::parse(raw).expect("parse content-length request");
    assert_eq!(req.body_text(), "name=Grace");
    assert_eq!(req.form_params(), vec![("name".to_string(), "Grace".to_string())]);
    println!("5. Content-Length: 10 keeps only the first 10 body bytes");

    // 6. Route dispatch: list, detail with a captured parameter, create,
    //    search, wildcard static files, and header echo.
    let router = demo_router();
    assert_eq!(router.len(), 7);
    let get = |target: &str| {
        let raw = format!("GET {} HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: x\r\n\r\n", target);
        let req = Request::parse(&raw).expect("parse demo request");
        router.handle(&req)
    };
    assert_eq!(get("/").body_text(), "welcome to the demo server");
    assert_eq!(get("/users?page=2&limit=10").body_text(), "{\"page\": 2, \"limit\": 10}");
    assert_eq!(get("/users").body_text(), "{\"page\": 1, \"limit\": 20}");
    assert_eq!(get("/users/42").body_text(), "user 42");
    assert_eq!(get("/search?q=hello%20world&tag=rust").body_text(), "results for \"hello world\" (tag \"rust\")");
    assert_eq!(get("/static/css/site.css").body_text(), "file: css/site.css");
    let agent_req = Request::parse("GET /agent HTTP/1.1\r\nUser-Agent: DemoAgent/1.0\r\n\r\n").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(router.handle(&agent_req).body_text(), "agent: DemoAgent/1.0");
    println!("6. router dispatches 7 routes: params, query, wildcard, header echo");

    // 7. Create a user with a POST, then 404 and 405 behaviour.
    let raw = "POST /users HTTP/1.1\r\nContent-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded\r\n\r\nname=Ada";
    let req = Request::parse(raw).expect("parse create request");
    let resp = router.handle(&req);
    assert_eq!(resp.status, 201);
    assert_eq!(resp.header_value("content-type"), Some("application/json"));
    assert_eq!(resp.body_text(), "{\"created\": true, \"name\": \"Ada\"}");
    let resp = get("/no-such-page");
    assert_eq!(resp.status, 404);
    assert!(resp.body_text().contains("no route for GET /no-such-page"));
    let raw = "DELETE /users/42 HTTP/1.1\r\n\r\n";
    let req = Request::parse(raw).expect("parse delete request");
    let resp = router.handle(&req);
    assert_eq!(resp.status, 405);
    assert_eq!(resp.header_value("allow"), Some("GET"));
    assert!(resp.body_text().contains("not allowed"));
    println!("7. POST creates (201); unknown path -> 404; wrong method -> 405 with Allow: GET");

    // 8. Response serialization, checked byte-for-byte.
    let resp = Response::text("hi");
    assert_eq!(
        resp.to_string(),
        "HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\nContent-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8\r\nContent-Length: 2\r\n\r\nhi"
    );
    let resp = Response::json("{\"ok\":true}");
    assert_eq!(resp.status_line(), "HTTP/1.1 200 OK");
    assert_eq!(resp.header_value("content-length"), None, "length is added at serialization time");
    assert!(resp.to_string().contains("Content-Length: 11\r\n"));
    let resp = Response::redirect("/new-home");
    assert_eq!(resp.status, 301);
    assert_eq!(resp.header_value("location"), Some("/new-home"));
    assert_eq!(Response::reason_phrase(418), "Unknown");
    println!("8. response wire format verified byte-for-byte, incl. auto Content-Length");

    // 9. Malformed requests are rejected with clear errors.
    for (name, raw) in [
        ("empty", ""),
        ("no target", "GET"),
        ("no version", "GET /users"),
        ("too many parts", "GET /users HTTP/1.1 extra"),
        ("bad version", "GET /users FOO/1.0"),
        ("header without colon", "GET / HTTP/1.1\r\nBadHeader\r\n\r\n"),
        ("bad content-length", "POST / HTTP/1.1\r\nContent-Length: abc\r\n\r\nxx"),
        ("short body", "POST / HTTP/1.1\r\nContent-Length: 100\r\n\r\nshort"),
    ] {
        assert!(Request::parse(raw).is_err(), "case {:?} must fail", name);
    }
    println!("9. 8 malformed requests rejected");

    // 10. A custom method routes like any other.
    let router = Router::new().route(Method::Other("PURGE".to_string()), "/cache/*", static_file);
    let req = Request::parse("PURGE /cache/item-1 HTTP/1.1\r\n\r\n").expect("parse PURGE");
    assert_eq!(req.method, Method::Other("PURGE".to_string()));
    assert_eq!(router.handle(&req).body_text(), "file: item-1");
    println!("10. custom method PURGE routes normally");

    println!("\nAll http_router demonstration checks passed.");
}

fn main() {
    run_demo();
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Unit tests
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn get_req(target: &str) -> Request {
        Request::parse(&format!("GET {} HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: x\r\n\r\n", target)).unwrap()
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_get_basics() {
        let req = get_req("/hello?x=1&y=two");
        assert_eq!(req.method, Method::Get);
        assert_eq!(req.path, "/hello");
        assert_eq!(req.query_param("x"), Some("1"));
        assert_eq!(req.query_param("y"), Some("two"));
        assert_eq!(req.version, "HTTP/1.1");
        assert!(req.headers.iter().any(|(k, v)| k == "Host" && v == "x"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_lf_and_crlf() {
        let lf = Request::parse("GET /a HTTP/1.0\nHost: h\n\nbody").unwrap();
        let crlf = Request::parse("GET /a HTTP/1.0\r\nHost: h\r\n\r\nbody").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(lf.body_text(), "body");
        assert_eq!(crlf.body_text(), "body");
        assert_eq!(lf.version, "HTTP/1.0");
    }

    #[test]
    fn headers_case_insensitive_and_repeated() {
        let req = Request::parse(
            "GET / HTTP/1.1\r\nX-Test: one\r\nx-test: two\r\nContent-Type: text/plain\r\n\r\n",
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(req.header("X-TEST"), Some("one"));
        assert_eq!(req.headers_all("x-test"), vec!["one", "two"]);
        assert_eq!(req.header("content-type"), Some("text/plain"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn query_string_edge_cases() {
        assert_eq!(parse_query_string(""), Vec::<(String, String)>::new());
        assert_eq!(
            parse_query_string("a=1&&b=&c"),
            vec![
                ("a".to_string(), "1".to_string()),
                ("b".to_string(), "".to_string()),
                ("c".to_string(), "".to_string()),
            ]
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_query_string("q=a+b%20c"),
            vec![("q".to_string(), "a b c".to_string())]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn percent_decode_table() {
        assert_eq!(percent_decode("%41%42%43", true), "ABC");
        assert_eq!(percent_decode("%2f%2F", true), "//");
        assert_eq!(percent_decode("%", true), "%");
        assert_eq!(percent_decode("%2", true), "%2");
        assert_eq!(percent_decode("caf%C3%A9", true), "café");
    }

    #[test]
    fn body_and_content_length() {
        let req = Request::parse("POST / HTTP/1.1\r\nContent-Length: 5\r\n\r\nhello world").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(req.body_text(), "hello");
        let req = Request::parse("POST / HTTP/1.1\r\nContent-Length: 0\r\n\r\nhello").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(req.body_text(), "");
        assert!(Request::parse("POST / HTTP/1.1\r\nContent-Length: 9\r\n\r\nshort").is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn pattern_matching() {
        assert!(match_pattern("/users/:id", "/users/7").is_some());
        assert_eq!(match_pattern("/users/:id", "/users/7").unwrap()["id"], "7");
        assert!(match_pattern("/users/:id", "/users").is_none());
        assert!(match_pattern("/users/:id", "/users/7/extra").is_none());
        assert_eq!(
            match_pattern("/files/*", "/files/a/b.txt").unwrap()["rest"],
            "a/b.txt"
        );
        assert!(match_pattern("/", "/").is_some());
        assert!(match_pattern("/a/:x/b/:y", "/a/1/b/2").unwrap()["y"] == "2");
    }

    #[test]
    fn router_404_and_405() {
        let router = Router::new()
            .route(Method::Get, "/only-get", home)
            .route(Method::Post, "/only-post", home);
        assert_eq!(router.handle(&get_req("/missing")).status, 404);
        let req = Request::parse("DELETE /only-get HTTP/1.1\r\n\r\n").unwrap();
        let resp = router.handle(&req);
        assert_eq!(resp.status, 405);
        assert_eq!(resp.header_value("allow"), Some("GET"));
        let req = Request::parse("PUT /only-post HTTP/1.1\r\n\r\n").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(router.handle(&req).status, 405);
        assert_eq!(router.handle(&get_req("/only-get")).status, 200);
    }

    #[test]
    fn router_dispatch_order() {
        let router = Router::new()
            .route(Method::Get, "/users/new", home)
            .route(Method::Get, "/users/:id", get_user);
        assert_eq!(router.handle(&get_req("/users/new")).body_text(), "welcome to the demo server");
        assert_eq!(router.handle(&get_req("/users/9")).body_text(), "user 9");
    }

    #[test]
    fn response_serialization() {
        let resp = Response::new(201).header("X-A", "1").body(b"created".to_vec());
        assert_eq!(
            resp.to_string(),
            "HTTP/1.1 201 Created\r\nX-A: 1\r\nContent-Length: 7\r\n\r\ncreated"
        );
        // An explicit Content-Length is respected, not duplicated.
        let resp = Response::new(200)
            .header("Content-Length", "999")
            .body(b"hi".to_vec());
        assert_eq!(resp.to_string().matches("Content-Length").count(), 1);
        assert_eq!(Response::reason_phrase(404), "Not Found");
        assert_eq!(Response::reason_phrase(500), "Internal Server Error");
    }

    #[test]
    fn method_parsing() {
        assert_eq!(Method::parse("GET"), Method::Get);
        assert_eq!(Method::parse("get"), Method::Other("get".to_string()));
        assert_eq!(Method::parse("BREW"), Method::Other("BREW".to_string()));
        assert_eq!(Method::Delete.as_str(), "DELETE");
    }

    #[test]
    fn form_detection() {
        let req = Request::parse(
            "POST /f HTTP/1.1\r\nContent-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded; charset=utf-8\r\n\r\na=1",
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert!(req.is_form());
        let req = Request::parse("POST /f HTTP/1.1\r\nContent-Type: text/plain\r\n\r\na=1").unwrap();
        assert!(!req.is_form());
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/json_value.rs ---
//! json_value.rs — a hand-written JSON value parser and serializer,
//! standard library only.
//!
//! This is a small, complete JSON implementation in the JSON sense of
//! RFC 8259, written from scratch as a recursive-descent parser:
//!
//!   * `Value` — a JSON value: null, boolean, number, string, array or
//!     object. Objects preserve insertion order (a `Vec` of key/value
//!     pairs) and duplicate keys are resolved last-wins, as most JSON
//!     tooling does.
//!   * `parse` — a recursive-descent parser with precise error reporting
//!     (line, column and byte offset), strict number grammar, full string
//!     escape handling including `\uXXXX` surrogate pairs, and a nesting
//!     depth limit so hostile input cannot overflow the stack.
//!   * Serialization — compact output and indented pretty-printing, both
//!     with correct string escaping. `parse(v.to_string()) == v` for
//!     every value the parser can produce (round-trip property).
//!
//! Numbers are stored as `f64`. Integers whose magnitude fits in 53 bits
//! serialize without a decimal point, so `42` stays `42` and `3.5` stays
//! `3.5`. Non-finite numbers cannot come from the parser; the checked
//! constructor `Value::number` refuses them, and the serializer renders
//! a non-finite number that was built by hand as `null` rather than
//! emitting invalid JSON.
//!
//! With no arguments the program runs a demonstration: it parses a
//! realistic nested document, queries and mutates it, round-trips it
//! through both serializers, and checks a battery of malformed inputs,
//! asserting every step.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::fmt;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The Value type
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A JSON value. Objects keep their keys in insertion order.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum Value {
    Null,
    Bool(bool),
    Number(f64),
    String(String),
    Array(Vec<Value>),
    Object(Vec<(String, Value)>),
}

impl Value {
    /// Checked number constructor: returns `None` for NaN and infinities,
    /// which are not valid JSON numbers.
    pub fn number(n: f64) -> Option<Value> {
        if n.is_finite() {
            Some(Value::Number(n))
        } else {
            None
        }
    }

    /// Build an object from key/value pairs. Later pairs with the same
    /// key replace earlier ones, matching the parser's behaviour.
    pub fn object(pairs: Vec<(&str, Value)>) -> Value {
        let mut obj = Value::Object(Vec::new());
        for (k, v) in pairs {
            obj.set(k, v);
        }
        obj
    }

    /// Build an array from values.
    pub fn array(items: Vec<Value>) -> Value {
        Value::Array(items)
    }

    // -- type tests -------------------------------------------------------

    pub fn is_null(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Value::Null)
    }

    pub fn is_bool(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Value::Bool(_))
    }

    pub fn is_number(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Value::Number(_))
    }

    pub fn is_string(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Value::String(_))
    }

    pub fn is_array(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Value::Array(_))
    }

    pub fn is_object(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Value::Object(_))
    }

    // -- typed accessors --------------------------------------------------

    pub fn as_bool(&self) -> Option<bool> {
        match self {
            Value::Bool(b) => Some(*b),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn as_number(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        match self {
            Value::Number(n) => Some(*n),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// The number as an `i64` when it is integral and in range.
    pub fn as_i64(&self) -> Option<i64> {
        match self {
            Value::Number(n)
                if n.is_finite() && n.fract() == 0.0 && *n >= i64::MIN as f64
                    && *n <= i64::MAX as f64 =>
            {
                Some(*n as i64)
            }
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn as_str(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        match self {
            Value::String(s) => Some(s),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn as_array(&self) -> Option<&[Value]> {
        match self {
            Value::Array(items) => Some(items),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn as_array_mut(&mut self) -> Option<&mut Vec<Value>> {
        match self {
            Value::Array(items) => Some(items),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn as_object(&self) -> Option<&[(String, Value)]> {
        match self {
            Value::Object(pairs) => Some(pairs),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn as_object_mut(&mut self) -> Option<&mut Vec<(String, Value)>> {
        match self {
            Value::Object(pairs) => Some(pairs),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Number of elements for arrays and objects, characters for strings,
    /// 0 for null and 1 for booleans and numbers.
    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        match self {
            Value::Null => 0,
            Value::Bool(_) | Value::Number(_) => 1,
            Value::String(s) => s.chars().count(),
            Value::Array(items) => items.len(),
            Value::Object(pairs) => pairs.len(),
        }
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.len() == 0
    }

    // -- object access ----------------------------------------------------

    /// Look up a key in an object. Returns `None` for non-objects and
    /// for missing keys.
    pub fn get(&self, key: &str) -> Option<&Value> {
        match self {
            Value::Object(pairs) => pairs
                .iter()
                .rev()
                .find(|(k, _)| k == key)
                .map(|(_, v)| v),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Mutable lookup, same semantics as `get`.
    pub fn get_mut(&mut self, key: &str) -> Option<&mut Value> {
        match self {
            Value::Object(pairs) => pairs
                .iter_mut()
                .rev()
                .find(|(k, _)| k == key)
                .map(|(_, v)| v),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Insert or replace a key in an object. Does nothing for non-objects
    /// (use `Value::object` to build one). A replaced key keeps its
    /// original position, so insertion order is stable.
    pub fn set(&mut self, key: &str, value: Value) {
        if let Value::Object(pairs) = self {
            if let Some(slot) = pairs.iter_mut().find(|(k, _)| k == key) {
                slot.1 = value;
            } else {
                pairs.push((key.to_string(), value));
            }
        }
    }

    /// Remove a key from an object, returning its old value.
    pub fn remove(&mut self, key: &str) -> Option<Value> {
        if let Value::Object(pairs) = self {
            if let Some(pos) = pairs.iter().position(|(k, _)| k == key) {
                return Some(pairs.remove(pos).1);
            }
        }
        None
    }

    /// True when the object contains `key`.
    pub fn contains_key(&self, key: &str) -> bool {
        self.get(key).is_some()
    }

    /// Index into an array. Returns `None` for non-arrays and for
    /// out-of-range indices.
    pub fn index(&self, idx: usize) -> Option<&Value> {
        match self {
            Value::Array(items) => items.get(idx),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Push a value onto an array. Does nothing for non-arrays.
    pub fn push(&mut self, value: Value) {
        if let Value::Array(items) = self {
            items.push(value);
        }
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Paths, merging and conversion helpers
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

impl Value {
    /// Resolve a dotted path such as `server.tls.enabled` or
    /// `users[1].name`. Array indices are written `[n]`. Returns `None`
    /// when any step is missing or has the wrong type. An empty path
    /// resolves to the value itself.
    pub fn get_path(&self, path: &str) -> Option<&Value> {
        let mut current = self;
        for segment in PathSegments::new(path) {
            match segment {
                PathSegment::Key(key) => current = current.get(key)?,
                PathSegment::Index(idx) => current = current.index(idx)?,
            }
        }
        Some(current)
    }

    /// Mutable version of `get_path`.
    pub fn get_path_mut(&mut self, path: &str) -> Option<&mut Value> {
        let mut current = self;
        for segment in PathSegments::new(path) {
            match segment {
                PathSegment::Key(key) => current = current.get_mut(key)?,
                PathSegment::Index(idx) => {
                    current = current.as_array_mut()?.get_mut(idx)?;
                }
            }
        }
        Some(current)
    }

    /// Deep-merge `other` into `self`. When both values are objects, keys
    /// from `other` are merged recursively; a key present in both as an
    /// object is merged rather than replaced. In every other case `other`
    /// replaces `self` wholesale. This is the usual "defaults overlaid
    /// with overrides" operation for configuration documents.
    pub fn merge(&mut self, other: Value) {
        match (self, other) {
            (Value::Object(base_pairs), Value::Object(other_pairs)) => {
                for (key, other_value) in other_pairs {
                    match base_pairs.iter_mut().find(|(k, _)| *k == key) {
                        Some((_, base_value)) => base_value.merge(other_value),
                        None => base_pairs.push((key, other_value)),
                    }
                }
            }
            (base, other) => *base = other,
        }
    }

    /// Convert to a `BTreeMap` when this value is an object, for callers
    /// that want sorted keys. Returns `None` for non-objects.
    pub fn into_sorted_map(self) -> Option<BTreeMap<String, Value>> {
        match self {
            Value::Object(pairs) => Some(pairs.into_iter().collect()),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
}

/// One step of a dotted path.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum PathSegment<'a> {
    Key(&'a str),
    Index(usize),
}

/// Split a path like `a.b[2].c` into segments. Parsing is lenient about
/// malformed brackets: a `[` that does not parse as `[digits]` is treated
/// as part of a literal key, so `get_path` simply fails to resolve it
/// instead of erroring.
struct PathSegments<'a> {
    rest: &'a str,
}

impl<'a> PathSegments<'a> {
    fn new(path: &'a str) -> Self {
        PathSegments { rest: path }
    }
}

impl<'a> Iterator for PathSegments<'a> {
    type Item = PathSegment<'a>;

    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<PathSegment<'a>> {
        if self.rest.is_empty() {
            return None;
        }
        // Skip a leading dot between segments.
        if let Some(stripped) = self.rest.strip_prefix('.') {
            self.rest = stripped;
            if self.rest.is_empty() {
                return None;
            }
        }
        if let Some(stripped) = self.rest.strip_prefix('[') {
            // Try to read [digits].
            let digits_end = stripped.find(']')?;
            let digits = &stripped[..digits_end];
            if !digits.is_empty() && digits.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit()) {
                if let Ok(idx) = digits.parse::<usize>() {
                    self.rest = &stripped[digits_end + 1..];
                    return Some(PathSegment::Index(idx));
                }
            }
            // Not a valid index: fall through and treat '[' as a key char.
        }
        let end = self
            .rest
            .find(|c| c == '.' || c == '[')
            .unwrap_or(self.rest.len());
        let key = &self.rest[..end];
        self.rest = &self.rest[end..];
        Some(PathSegment::Key(key))
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Serialization
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Format a number as JSON. Integral values within the exactly
/// representable integer range print without a decimal point; everything
/// else uses Rust's shortest-round-trip decimal formatting, which is
/// always valid JSON. Non-finite numbers are rendered as `null`.
fn format_number(n: f64) -> String {
    if !n.is_finite() {
        return "null".to_string();
    }
    if n.fract() == 0.0 && n.abs() < 9_007_199_254_740_992.0 {
        format!("{}", n as i64)
    } else {
        format!("{}", n)
    }
}

/// Escape a string for inclusion in JSON output, including the quotes.
fn write_json_string(out: &mut String, s: &str) {
    out.push('"');
    for c in s.chars() {
        match c {
            '"' => out.push_str("\\\""),
            '\\' => out.push_str("\\\\"),
            '\u{0008}' => out.push_str("\\b"),
            '\u{000C}' => out.push_str("\\f"),
            '\n' => out.push_str("\\n"),
            '\r' => out.push_str("\\r"),
            '\t' => out.push_str("\\t"),
            c if (c as u32) < 0x20 => {
                out.push_str(&format!("\\u{:04x}", c as u32));
            }
            c => out.push(c),
        }
    }
    out.push('"');
}

impl Value {
    /// Compact serialization with no unnecessary whitespace.
    pub fn to_string(&self) -> String {
        let mut out = String::new();
        self.write_compact(&mut out);
        out
    }

    fn write_compact(&self, out: &mut String) {
        match self {
            Value::Null => out.push_str("null"),
            Value::Bool(true) => out.push_str("true"),
            Value::Bool(false) => out.push_str("false"),
            Value::Number(n) => out.push_str(&format_number(*n)),
            Value::String(s) => write_json_string(out, s),
            Value::Array(items) => {
                out.push('[');
                for (i, item) in items.iter().enumerate() {
                    if i > 0 {
                        out.push(',');
                    }
                    item.write_compact(out);
                }
                out.push(']');
            }
            Value::Object(pairs) => {
                out.push('{');
                for (i, (k, v)) in pairs.iter().enumerate() {
                    if i > 0 {
                        out.push(',');
                    }
                    write_json_string(out, k);
                    out.push(':');
                    v.write_compact(out);
                }
                out.push('}');
            }
        }
    }

    /// Pretty-printed serialization with two-space indentation.
    pub fn to_string_pretty(&self) -> String {
        self.to_string_pretty_indent(2)
    }

    /// Pretty-printed serialization with a chosen indent width in spaces.
    /// A width of 0 produces compact output with newlines but no indent.
    pub fn to_string_pretty_indent(&self, indent: usize) -> String {
        let mut out = String::new();
        self.write_pretty(&mut out, indent, 0);
        out
    }

    fn write_pretty(&self, out: &mut String, indent: usize, level: usize) {
        match self {
            Value::Array(items) if items.is_empty() => out.push_str("[]"),
            Value::Object(pairs) if pairs.is_empty() => out.push_str("{}"),
            Value::Array(items) => {
                out.push_str("[\n");
                for (i, item) in items.iter().enumerate() {
                    out.push_str(&" ".repeat(indent * (level + 1)));
                    item.write_pretty(out, indent, level + 1);
                    if i + 1 < items.len() {
                        out.push(',');
                    }
                    out.push('\n');
                }
                out.push_str(&" ".repeat(indent * level));
                out.push(']');
            }
            Value::Object(pairs) => {
                out.push_str("{\n");
                for (i, (k, v)) in pairs.iter().enumerate() {
                    out.push_str(&" ".repeat(indent * (level + 1)));
                    write_json_string(out, k);
                    out.push_str(": ");
                    v.write_pretty(out, indent, level + 1);
                    if i + 1 < pairs.len() {
                        out.push(',');
                    }
                    out.push('\n');
                }
                out.push_str(&" ".repeat(indent * level));
                out.push('}');
            }
            // Scalars print the same in both modes.
            other => other.write_compact(out),
        }
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Value {
    /// `Display` is compact serialization, so `format!("{}", value)`
    /// produces valid JSON.
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}", self.to_string())
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The parser
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Maximum nesting depth the parser will accept. JSON can nest
/// arbitrarily, but unbounded recursion is a stack-overflow vector for
/// untrusted input, so deeply nested documents are rejected with a clear
/// error instead of crashing.
const MAX_DEPTH: usize = 128;

/// A parse failure, with the exact position where parsing stopped.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ParseError {
    /// 1-based line number.
    pub line: usize,
    /// 1-based column number (in bytes, within the line).
    pub col: usize,
    /// 0-based byte offset into the input.
    pub pos: usize,
    /// Human-readable description of what was expected or wrong.
    pub message: String,
}

impl fmt::Display for ParseError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(
            f,
            "line {}, column {}: {}",
            self.line, self.col, self.message
        )
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for ParseError {}

/// Parse a complete JSON document. The whole input must be one value;
/// trailing characters after the value are an error.
pub fn parse(input: &str) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {
    let mut parser = Parser {
        src: input,
        bytes: input.as_bytes(),
        pos: 0,
    };
    parser.skip_whitespace();
    let value = parser.parse_value(0)?;
    parser.skip_whitespace();
    if parser.pos != parser.bytes.len() {
        return Err(parser.error("trailing characters after JSON value"));
    }
    Ok(value)
}

/// The parser state: the source text and the current byte offset.
struct Parser<'a> {
    src: &'a str,
    bytes: &'a [u8],
    pos: usize,
}

impl<'a> Parser<'a> {
    /// Build an error at the current position, computing line and column
    /// from the source text.
    fn error(&self, message: &str) -> ParseError {
        let before = &self.src[..self.pos];
        let line = before.bytes().filter(|&b| b == b'\n').count() + 1;
        let col = self.pos - before.rfind('\n').map(|i| i + 1).unwrap_or(0) + 1;
        ParseError {
            line,
            col,
            pos: self.pos,
            message: message.to_string(),
        }
    }

    fn peek(&self) -> Option<u8> {
        self.bytes.get(self.pos).copied()
    }

    fn peek_at(&self, ahead: usize) -> Option<u8> {
        self.bytes.get(self.pos + ahead).copied()
    }

    fn skip_whitespace(&mut self) {
        while matches!(self.peek(), Some(b' ') | Some(b'\t') | Some(b'\n') | Some(b'\r')) {
            self.pos += 1;
        }
    }

    /// Parse one value at the current position, dispatching on the next
    /// byte. `depth` counts nesting so the limit can be enforced.
    fn parse_value(&mut self, depth: usize) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {
        if depth > MAX_DEPTH {
            return Err(self.error("nesting too deep (limit 128)"));
        }
        match self.peek() {
            Some(b'{') => self.parse_object(depth),
            Some(b'[') => self.parse_array(depth),
            Some(b'"') => Ok(Value::String(self.parse_string()?)),
            Some(b't') => {
                self.expect_literal("true")?;
                Ok(Value::Bool(true))
            }
            Some(b'f') => {
                self.expect_literal("false")?;
                Ok(Value::Bool(false))
            }
            Some(b'n') => {
                self.expect_literal("null")?;
                Ok(Value::Null)
            }
            Some(b'-') | Some(b'0'..=b'9') => self.parse_number(),
            Some(other) => Err(self.error(&format!(
                "unexpected character '{}' (expected a value)",
                other as char
            ))),
            None => Err(self.error("unexpected end of input (expected a value)")),
        }
    }

    /// Consume the exact literal `word` (e.g. `true`) or fail.
    fn expect_literal(&mut self, word: &str) -> Result<(), ParseError> {
        if self.src[self.pos..].starts_with(word) {
            self.pos += word.len();
            Ok(())
        } else {
            Err(self.error(&format!("invalid literal (expected `{}`)", word)))
        }
    }

    /// Parse a number following the strict JSON grammar:
    /// `-?(0|[1-9][0-9]*)(\.[0-9]+)?([eE][+-]?[0-9]+)?`.
    /// A leading zero followed by another digit is rejected by the
    /// caller: after `0` the next character must be a delimiter, and
    /// anything else produces an error in the enclosing context.
    fn parse_number(&mut self) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {
        let start = self.pos;
        if self.peek() == Some(b'-') {
            self.pos += 1;
        }
        match self.peek() {
            Some(b'0') => {
                self.pos += 1;
            }
            Some(b'1'..=b'9') => {
                while matches!(self.peek(), Some(b'0'..=b'9')) {
                    self.pos += 1;
                }
            }
            _ => return Err(self.error("expected a digit in number")),
        }
        if self.peek() == Some(b'.') {
            self.pos += 1;
            if !matches!(self.peek(), Some(b'0'..=b'9')) {
                return Err(self.error("expected a digit after the decimal point"));
            }
            while matches!(self.peek(), Some(b'0'..=b'9')) {
                self.pos += 1;
            }
        }
        if matches!(self.peek(), Some(b'e') | Some(b'E')) {
            self.pos += 1;
            if matches!(self.peek(), Some(b'+') | Some(b'-')) {
                self.pos += 1;
            }
            if !matches!(self.peek(), Some(b'0'..=b'9')) {
                return Err(self.error("expected a digit in the exponent"));
            }
            while matches!(self.peek(), Some(b'0'..=b'9')) {
                self.pos += 1;
            }
        }
        let text = &self.src[start..self.pos];
        match text.parse::<f64>() {
            Ok(n) if n.is_finite() => Ok(Value::Number(n)),
            _ => Err(self.error("number is out of range")),
        }
    }

    /// Parse a double-quoted string, handling all escapes. Raw bytes
    /// above 0x7F are copied through unchanged: the input is valid UTF-8
    /// and copying it byte-for-byte preserves that. Control characters
    /// must be escaped — a raw newline inside a string is an error.
    fn parse_string(&mut self) -> Result<String, ParseError> {
        debug_assert_eq!(self.peek(), Some(b'"'));
        self.pos += 1;
        let mut out: Vec<u8> = Vec::new();
        loop {
            let b = match self.peek() {
                Some(b) => b,
                None => return Err(self.error("unterminated string")),
            };
            match b {
                b'"' => {
                    self.pos += 1;
                    break;
                }
                b'\\' => {
                    self.pos += 1;
                    let esc = match self.peek() {
                        Some(e) => e,
                        None => return Err(self.error("unterminated escape sequence")),
                    };
                    self.pos += 1;
                    match esc {
                        b'"' => out.push(b'"'),
                        b'\\' => out.push(b'\\'),
                        b'/' => out.push(b'/'),
                        b'b' => out.push(0x08),
                        b'f' => out.push(0x0C),
                        b'n' => out.push(b'\n'),
                        b'r' => out.push(b'\r'),
                        b't' => out.push(b'\t'),
                        b'u' => self.parse_unicode_escape(&mut out)?,
                        _ => {
                            return Err(self.error(&format!(
                                "invalid escape sequence `\\{}`",
                                esc as char
                            )))
                        }
                    }
                }
                0x00..=0x1F => {
                    return Err(self.error("unescaped control character in string"));
                }
                _ => {
                    out.push(b);
                    self.pos += 1;
                }
            }
        }
        String::from_utf8(out).map_err(|_| self.error("invalid UTF-8 in string"))
    }

    /// Parse a `\uXXXX` escape (the backslash and `u` are already
    /// consumed). Handles surrogate pairs: a high surrogate must be
    /// followed immediately by a `\uDC00`–`\uDFFF` low surrogate, and a
    /// lone low surrogate is an error.
    fn parse_unicode_escape(&mut self, out: &mut Vec<u8>) -> Result<(), ParseError> {
        let first = self.parse_hex4()?;
        let code_point = if (0xD800..0xDC00).contains(&first) {
            // High surrogate: expect a low surrogate right after.
            if self.peek() == Some(b'\\') && self.peek_at(1) == Some(b'u') {
                self.pos += 2;
                let second = self.parse_hex4()?;
                if !(0xDC00..0xE000).contains(&second) {
                    return Err(self.error("high surrogate not followed by a low surrogate"));
                }
                0x10000 + ((first - 0xD800) << 10) + (second - 0xDC00)
            } else {
                return Err(self.error("unpaired high surrogate in string"));
            }
        } else if (0xDC00..0xE000).contains(&first) {
            return Err(self.error("unpaired low surrogate in string"));
        } else {
            first
        };
        let ch = char::from_u32(code_point)
            .ok_or_else(|| self.error("invalid Unicode code point"))?;
        let mut buf = [0u8; 4];
        out.extend_from_slice(ch.encode_utf8(&mut buf).as_bytes());
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Read exactly four hexadecimal digits as a `u32`.
    fn parse_hex4(&mut self) -> Result<u32, ParseError> {
        let mut value: u32 = 0;
        for _ in 0..4 {
            let digit = match self.peek() {
                Some(b) => b,
                None => return Err(self.error("truncated \\u escape")),
            };
            let d = match digit {
                b'0'..=b'9' => (digit - b'0') as u32,
                b'a'..=b'f' => (digit - b'a' + 10) as u32,
                b'A'..=b'F' => (digit - b'A' + 10) as u32,
                _ => return Err(self.error("invalid hex digit in \\u escape")),
            };
            value = value * 16 + d;
            self.pos += 1;
        }
        Ok(value)
    }

    fn parse_array(&mut self, depth: usize) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {
        debug_assert_eq!(self.peek(), Some(b'['));
        self.pos += 1;
        let mut items = Vec::new();
        self.skip_whitespace();
        if self.peek() == Some(b']') {
            self.pos += 1;
            return Ok(Value::Array(items));
        }
        loop {
            self.skip_whitespace();
            let item = self.parse_value(depth + 1)?;
            items.push(item);
            self.skip_whitespace();
            match self.peek() {
                Some(b',') => {
                    self.pos += 1;
                }
                Some(b']') => {
                    self.pos += 1;
                    break;
                }
                Some(_) => {
                    return Err(self.error("expected `,` or `]` in array"));
                }
                None => return Err(self.error("unterminated array")),
            }
        }
        Ok(Value::Array(items))
    }

    fn parse_object(&mut self, depth: usize) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {
        debug_assert_eq!(self.peek(), Some(b'{'));
        self.pos += 1;
        let mut pairs: Vec<(String, Value)> = Vec::new();
        self.skip_whitespace();
        if self.peek() == Some(b'}') {
            self.pos += 1;
            return Ok(Value::Object(pairs));
        }
        loop {
            self.skip_whitespace();
            if self.peek() != Some(b'"') {
                return Err(self.error("expected a string key in object"));
            }
            let key = self.parse_string()?;
            self.skip_whitespace();
            if self.peek() != Some(b':') {
                return Err(self.error("expected `:` after object key"));
            }
            self.pos += 1;
            self.skip_whitespace();
            let value = self.parse_value(depth + 1)?;
            // Last-wins for duplicate keys, keeping the first position.
            match pairs.iter_mut().find(|(k, _)| *k == key) {
                Some((_, slot)) => *slot = value,
                None => pairs.push((key, value)),
            }
            self.skip_whitespace();
            match self.peek() {
                Some(b',') => {
                    self.pos += 1;
                }
                Some(b'}') => {
                    self.pos += 1;
                    break;
                }
                Some(_) => {
                    return Err(self.error("expected `,` or `}` in object"));
                }
                None => return Err(self.error("unterminated object")),
            }
        }
        Ok(Value::Object(pairs))
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Demonstration
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A realistic nested configuration document used by the demonstration.
const DEMO_DOC: &str = r#"{
  "server": {
    "host": "localhost",
    "port": 8080,
    "tls": { "enabled": true, "cert": "/etc/ssl/cert.pem" },
    "workers": 4
  },
  "features": ["logging", "metrics", "cache"],
  "limits": { "max_connections": 1000, "timeout_seconds": 2.5, "ratio": 0.125 },
  "notes": "café ☕ \u0041",
  "pi": 3.14159,
  "neg": -17,
  "exp": 1.5e3,
  "nothing": null
}"#;

/// Run the demonstration. Every step is asserted, so a successful run
/// proves parsing, querying, mutation, serialization and error
/// reporting all work.
fn run_demo() {
    println!("json_value demonstration — hand-written JSON parser/serializer\n");

    // 1. Parse the document and query it.
    let mut doc = parse(DEMO_DOC).expect("parse demo document");
    assert_eq!(doc.get_path("server.port").and_then(|v| v.as_i64()), Some(8080));
    assert_eq!(doc.get_path("server.host").and_then(|v| v.as_str()), Some("localhost"));
    assert_eq!(doc.get_path("server.tls.enabled").and_then(|v| v.as_bool()), Some(true));
    assert_eq!(doc.get_path("features[1]").and_then(|v| v.as_str()), Some("metrics"));
    assert_eq!(doc.get_path("features[2]").and_then(|v| v.as_str()), Some("cache"));
    assert_eq!(doc.get_path("limits.timeout_seconds").and_then(|v| v.as_number()), Some(2.5));
    assert_eq!(doc.get_path("exp").and_then(|v| v.as_number()), Some(1500.0));
    assert_eq!(doc.get_path("neg").and_then(|v| v.as_i64()), Some(-17));
    assert!(doc.get_path("nothing").map(|v| v.is_null()).unwrap_or(false));
    assert_eq!(doc.get_path("notes").and_then(|v| v.as_str()), Some("café ☕ A"));
    assert!(doc.get_path("server.missing").is_none());
    assert!(doc.get_path("features[9]").is_none());
    assert!(doc.get_path("server.port.x").is_none());
    println!("1. parsed document; dotted paths resolve (server.port=8080, features[1]=metrics)");

    // 2. Compact serialization and the round-trip property.
    let compact = doc.to_string();
    assert!(compact.contains("\"port\":8080"));
    assert!(compact.contains("\"notes\":\"café ☕ A\""));
    assert!(!compact.contains('\n'), "compact output has no newlines");
    let reparsed = parse(&compact).expect("reparse compact output");
    assert_eq!(reparsed, doc, "round-trip must preserve the value");
    println!("2. compact serialization round-trips exactly ({} bytes)", compact.len());

    // 3. Pretty-printing with an exact expected layout.
    let small = Value::object(vec![
        ("a", Value::Number(1.0)),
        ("b", Value::array(vec![Value::Bool(true), Value::Null])),
        ("c", Value::object(vec![])),
    ]);
    let expected = "{\n  \"a\": 1,\n  \"b\": [\n    true,\n    null\n  ],\n  \"c\": {}\n}";
    assert_eq!(small.to_string_pretty(), expected);
    assert_eq!(parse(&small.to_string_pretty()).unwrap(), small);
    println!("3. pretty-printing matches the expected layout and re-parses");

    // 4. Mutation: change a value through a path, push an array element,
    //    remove a key, and confirm the document changed as expected.
    if let Some(port) = doc.get_path_mut("server.port") {
        *port = Value::Number(9090.0);
    }
    assert_eq!(doc.get_path("server.port").and_then(|v| v.as_i64()), Some(9090));
    if let Some(features) = doc.get_mut("features") {
        features.push(Value::String("tracing".to_string()));
    }
    assert_eq!(doc.get_path("features[3]").and_then(|v| v.as_str()), Some("tracing"));
    assert_eq!(doc.remove("nothing"), Some(Value::Null));
    assert!(!doc.contains_key("nothing"));
    assert_eq!(doc.len(), 7);
    println!("4. mutation: port -> 9090, feature appended, \"nothing\" removed");

    // 5. Deep merge: defaults overlaid with overrides. Nested objects
    //    merge key by key instead of being replaced wholesale.
    let mut defaults = parse(
        r#"{"server": {"host": "0.0.0.0", "port": 80, "tls": {"enabled": false}},
            "limits": {"timeout_seconds": 30, "retries": 3}}"#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let overrides = parse(
        r#"{"server": {"port": 8443, "tls": {"enabled": true}}, "debug": true}"#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    defaults.merge(overrides);
    assert_eq!(defaults.get_path("server.host").and_then(|v| v.as_str()), Some("0.0.0.0"));
    assert_eq!(defaults.get_path("server.port").and_then(|v| v.as_i64()), Some(8443));
    assert_eq!(defaults.get_path("server.tls.enabled").and_then(|v| v.as_bool()), Some(true));
    assert_eq!(defaults.get_path("limits.retries").and_then(|v| v.as_i64()), Some(3));
    assert_eq!(defaults.get_path("debug").and_then(|v| v.as_bool()), Some(true));
    println!("5. deep merge: nested objects merge key-by-key, scalars replace");

    // 6. Number formatting: integers stay integers, fractions and
    //    exponents normalise to their numeric value.
    assert_eq!(parse("42").unwrap().to_string(), "42");
    assert_eq!(parse("-0").unwrap().to_string(), "0");
    assert_eq!(parse("3.50").unwrap().to_string(), "3.5");
    assert_eq!(parse("1e3").unwrap().to_string(), "1000");
    assert_eq!(parse("0.125").unwrap().to_string(), "0.125");
    assert_eq!(parse("-2.5e-2").unwrap().to_string(), "-0.025");
    assert_eq!(Value::number(f64::NAN), None, "NaN is not a JSON number");
    assert_eq!(Value::Number(f64::INFINITY).to_string(), "null");
    println!("6. numbers: 42, 3.50 -> 3.5, 1e3 -> 1000; NaN refused, infinity -> null");

    // 7. String escapes round-trip, including a surrogate pair (𝄞 U+1D11E)
    //    and control characters.
    let tricky = Value::String("quote \" backslash \\ newline \n tab \t bell \u{0007} 𝄞".to_string());
    let encoded = tricky.to_string();
    assert_eq!(parse(&encoded).unwrap(), tricky);
    assert_eq!(parse(r#""\uD834\uDD1E""#).unwrap().as_str(), Some("𝄞"));
    assert_eq!(parse(r#""\u0041\u00e9""#).unwrap().as_str(), Some("Aé"));
    println!("7. string escapes round-trip (surrogate pair, control chars)");

    // 8. Malformed inputs are rejected, with positions.
    let bad = [
        "",
        "tru",
        "{",
        "[1,]",
        "{\"a\":}",
        "01",
        "1.",
        "\"unterminated",
        "\"bad \\x escape\"",
        "\"\\uD800\"",
        "\"\\uDC00\"",
        "1e999",
        "{\"a\" 1}",
        "[1 2]",
        "null null",
        "{\"a\":1} extra",
        "[1,2",
        "{\"a\":1,}",
        "\"raw\nnewline\"",
    ];
    for input in bad {
        assert!(parse(input).is_err(), "input {:?} must fail to parse", input);
    }
    let err = parse("{\n  \"a\": 1,\n  \"b\": @\n}").unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!((err.line, err.col), (3, 8));
    println!("8. {} malformed inputs rejected; error position reported as line {}, col {}", bad.len(), err.line, err.col);

    // 9. Depth limit: 200 nested arrays are refused instead of overflowing.
    let deep = "[".repeat(200) + &"]".repeat(200);
    let err = parse(&deep).unwrap_err();
    assert!(err.message.contains("nesting too deep"));
    println!("9. 200-deep nesting refused: \"{}\"", err.message);

    // 10. Duplicate keys: last value wins, first position kept.
    let dup = parse("{\"a\": 1, \"b\": 2, \"a\": 3}").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(dup.get_path("a").and_then(|v| v.as_i64()), Some(3));
    assert_eq!(dup.to_string(), "{\"a\":3,\"b\":2}");
    println!("10. duplicate keys resolve last-wins: {{\"a\": 1, \"b\": 2, \"a\": 3}} -> {}", dup);

    println!("\nAll json_value demonstration checks passed.");
}

fn main() {
    run_demo();
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Unit tests
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn parse_literals() {
        assert_eq!(parse("null").unwrap(), Value::Null);
        assert_eq!(parse("true").unwrap(), Value::Bool(true));
        assert_eq!(parse("false").unwrap(), Value::Bool(false));
        assert!(parse("nul").is_err());
        assert!(parse("True").is_err());
        assert_eq!(parse("  null  ").unwrap(), Value::Null);
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_numbers_table() {
        let cases: &[(&str, f64)] = &[
            ("0", 0.0),
            ("-0", 0.0),
            ("42", 42.0),
            ("-17", -17.0),
            ("3.14", 3.14),
            ("-0.5", -0.5),
            ("1e3", 1000.0),
            ("1E3", 1000.0),
            ("1.5e-3", 0.0015),
            ("2e+2", 200.0),
            ("123456789", 123456789.0),
        ];
        for (text, want) in cases {
            assert_eq!(parse(text).unwrap(), Value::Number(*want), "{}", text);
        }
        for bad in ["01", "1.", ".5", "-", "1e", "1e+", "--1", "NaN", "Infinity", "1e999"] {
            assert!(parse(bad).is_err(), "{:?} must fail", bad);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_strings_and_escapes() {
        assert_eq!(parse(r#""hello""#).unwrap().as_str(), Some("hello"));
        assert_eq!(parse(r#""a\"b\\c\/d""#).unwrap().as_str(), Some("a\"b\\c/d"));
        assert_eq!(
            parse(r#""\b\f\n\r\t""#).unwrap().as_str(),
            Some("\u{0008}\u{000C}\n\r\t")
        );
        assert_eq!(parse(r#""é""#).unwrap().as_str(), Some("é"));
        assert_eq!(parse(r#""\u00e9""#).unwrap().as_str(), Some("é"));
        assert_eq!(parse(r#""\uD834\uDD1E""#).unwrap().as_str(), Some("𝄞"));
        assert!(parse("\"\\uD834\"").is_err());
        assert!(parse("\"\\uDD1E\"").is_err());
        assert!(parse("\"\\uD834\\u0041\"").is_err());
        assert!(parse("\"\\u12\"").is_err());
        assert!(parse("\"\\uZZZZ\"").is_err());
        assert!(parse("\"\\q\"").is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_containers() {
        assert_eq!(parse("[]").unwrap(), Value::Array(vec![]));
        assert_eq!(parse("{}").unwrap(), Value::Object(vec![]));
        assert_eq!(
            parse("[1, \"two\", true, null, [3], {\"k\": 4}]").unwrap(),
            Value::array(vec![
                Value::Number(1.0),
