                Value::String("two".to_string()),
                Value::Bool(true),
                Value::Null,
                Value::array(vec![Value::Number(3.0)]),
                Value::object(vec![("k", Value::Number(4.0))]),
            ])
        );
        assert!(parse("[1,,2]").is_err());
        assert!(parse("[1 2]").is_err());
        assert!(parse("{\"a\"}").is_err());
        assert!(parse("{\"a\":1 \"b\":2}").is_err());
        assert!(parse("{a: 1}").is_err());
        assert!(parse("[1,]").is_err());
        assert!(parse("{\"a\":1,}").is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn round_trip_documents() {
        let docs = [
            "null",
            "true",
            "3.14159",
            "\"text with \\n escapes\"",
            "[1,2,3]",
            "{\"a\":[1,{\"b\":null}],\"c\":{}}",
            "[[[[\"deep\"]]]]",
            "{\"emoji\":\"🎉\",\"nested\":{\"x\":[true,false]}}",
        ];
        for doc in docs {
            let v = parse(doc).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(parse(&v.to_string()).unwrap(), v, "{}", doc);
            assert_eq!(parse(&v.to_string_pretty()).unwrap(), v, "pretty {}", doc);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn pretty_print_exact() {
        let v = parse("{\"a\":[1,2],\"b\":{\"c\":null}}").unwrap();
        let expected = "{\n  \"a\": [\n    1,\n    2\n  ],\n  \"b\": {\n    \"c\": null\n  }\n}";
        assert_eq!(v.to_string_pretty(), expected);
        assert_eq!(v.to_string_pretty_indent(4), "{\n    \"a\": [\n        1,\n        2\n    ],\n    \"b\": {\n        \"c\": null\n    }\n}");
        assert_eq!(parse("[]").unwrap().to_string_pretty(), "[]");
        assert_eq!(parse("{}").unwrap().to_string_pretty(), "{}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn string_escaping_on_output() {
        let v = Value::String("a\"b\\c\nd\te\u{0001}f".to_string());
        assert_eq!(v.to_string(), "\"a\\\"b\\\\c\\nd\\te\\u0001f\"");
        assert_eq!(parse(&v.to_string()).unwrap(), v);
    }

    #[test]
    fn object_api() {
        let mut obj = Value::object(vec![("a", Value::Number(1.0))]);
        obj.set("b", Value::Bool(true));
        obj.set("a", Value::Number(2.0));
        assert_eq!(obj.to_string(), "{\"a\":2,\"b\":true}");
        assert!(obj.contains_key("a"));
        assert_eq!(obj.remove("a"), Some(Value::Number(2.0)));
        assert_eq!(obj.remove("a"), None);
        assert_eq!(obj.len(), 1);
        // set/push on the wrong type are no-ops, not panics.
        let mut n = Value::Number(5.0);
        n.set("x", Value::Null);
        n.push(Value::Null);
        assert_eq!(n, Value::Number(5.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn paths() {
        let v = parse("{\"a\": {\"b\": [10, 20, {\"c\": \"deep\"}]}}").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(v.get_path("a.b[0]").and_then(|x| x.as_i64()), Some(10));
        assert_eq!(v.get_path("a.b[2].c").and_then(|x| x.as_str()), Some("deep"));
        assert_eq!(v.get_path("a").and_then(|x| x.get("b")).is_some(), true);
        assert!(v.get_path("a.b[3]").is_none());
        assert!(v.get_path("a.b.c").is_none());
        assert!(v.get_path("").is_some());
        let mut v = v;
        *v.get_path_mut("a.b[1]").unwrap() = Value::Number(99.0);
        assert_eq!(v.get_path("a.b[1]").and_then(|x| x.as_i64()), Some(99));
        assert!(v.get_path_mut("a.b[5]").is_none());
    }

    #[test]
    fn merge_semantics() {
        let mut base = parse("{\"a\": {\"x\": 1, \"y\": 2}, \"b\": [1], \"c\": 1}").unwrap();
        base.merge(parse("{\"a\": {\"y\": 20, \"z\": 30}, \"b\": [2], \"d\": 4}").unwrap());
        assert_eq!(base.to_string(), "{\"a\":{\"x\":1,\"y\":20,\"z\":30},\"b\":[2],\"c\":1,\"d\":4}");
        let mut scalar = Value::Number(1.0);
        scalar.merge(Value::String("s".to_string()));
        assert_eq!(scalar, Value::String("s".to_string()));
    }

    #[test]
    fn error_positions() {
        let err = parse("{\n\"a\": 1,\n\"b\": @}").unwrap_err();
        assert_eq!(err.line, 3);
        assert_eq!(err.col, 6);
        assert!(err.to_string().contains("line 3"));
        let err = parse("[1,\n2,\n@]").unwrap_err();
        assert_eq!(err.line, 3);
        let err = parse("").unwrap_err();
        assert_eq!((err.line, err.col), (1, 1));
    }

    #[test]
    fn depth_limit() {
        let deep = "[".repeat(200) + &"]".repeat(200);
        assert!(parse(&deep).is_err());
        let ok = "[".repeat(50) + &"]".repeat(50);
        assert!(parse(&ok).is_ok());
    }

    #[test]
    fn accessors_and_len() {
        assert_eq!(Value::Null.len(), 0);
        assert_eq!(Value::Bool(true).len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(Value::String("héllo".to_string()).len(), 5);
        assert_eq!(parse("[1,2]").unwrap().len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(Value::Number(7.5).as_i64(), None);
        assert_eq!(Value::Number(7.0).as_i64(), Some(7));
        assert_eq!(Value::Number(-3.0).as_i64(), Some(-3));
        assert!(Value::Bool(true).as_number().is_none());
        assert!(Value::Null.as_str().is_none());
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/kv_store.rs ---
//! kv_store.rs — a small persistent key-value store, standard library only.
//!
//! Design: an append-only log file plus an in-memory index.
//!
//!   * Every mutation is appended to the log as one line. `put` writes a
//!     `PUT` record, `delete` writes a `DEL` tombstone. Nothing is ever
//!     overwritten in place, so a crash can only damage the tail.
//!   * The in-memory index maps each live key to the byte offset of its
//!     latest record, so `get` is one seek plus one line read.
//!   * Each record carries a 64-bit FNV-1a checksum over its key and value.
//!     On open the log is replayed ("recovery"): records are validated in
//!     order and the index rebuilt. The first corrupt or incomplete record
//!     ends the replay and the file is truncated there, which discards a
//!     torn tail left by a crash mid-write while keeping every earlier
//!     committed record.
//!   * `compact` rewrites only the live entries into a fresh file and
//!     atomically renames it over the log, reclaiming the space used by
//!     overwritten values and tombstones.
//!
//! Record format (one record per line, fields separated by tabs):
//!
//!   PUT\t<escaped key>\t<escaped value>\t<16 hex checksum digits>
//!   DEL\t<escaped key>\t<16 hex checksum digits>
//!
//! Keys and values are arbitrary strings. Tab, newline, carriage return
//! and backslash are escaped (`\t`, `\n`, `\r`, `\\`) so they can never be
//! confused with record structure; any other character, including spaces
//! and non-ASCII text, passes through unchanged.
//!
//! With no arguments the program runs an end-to-end demonstration against
//! temporary files and asserts every step, so a successful run proves the
//! store works.

use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::fs::{File, OpenOptions};
use std::io::{self, BufRead, BufReader, Seek, SeekFrom, Write};
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Errors
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Everything that can go wrong in the store, kept in one small enum so
/// callers can distinguish I/O failures from corrupt log data.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub enum StoreError {
    /// An underlying operating-system / file error.
    Io(io::Error),
    /// A log record failed to parse or its checksum did not match.
    Corrupt(String),
}

impl std::fmt::Display for StoreError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        match self {
            StoreError::Io(e) => write!(f, "I/O error: {}", e),
            StoreError::Corrupt(msg) => write!(f, "corrupt log record: {}", msg),
        }
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for StoreError {
    fn source(&self) -> Option<&(dyn std::error::Error + 'static)> {
        match self {
            StoreError::Io(e) => Some(e),
            StoreError::Corrupt(_) => None,
        }
    }
}

impl From<io::Error> for StoreError {
    fn from(e: io::Error) -> Self {
        StoreError::Io(e)
    }
}

/// Short alias used throughout the store's public API.
pub type StoreResult<T> = Result<T, StoreError>;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Escaping, checksums and record encoding
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Escape the four characters that would otherwise break the tab-separated
/// line format. Everything else is left untouched, so UTF-8 text survives
/// byte-for-byte.
pub fn escape(s: &str) -> String {
    let mut out = String::with_capacity(s.len());
    for c in s.chars() {
        match c {
            '\\' => out.push_str("\\\\"),
            '\t' => out.push_str("\\t"),
            '\n' => out.push_str("\\n"),
            '\r' => out.push_str("\\r"),
            other => out.push(other),
        }
    }
    out
}

/// Inverse of `escape`. An escape sequence that is not one of the four
/// recognised ones is corrupt data, not something to guess at.
pub fn unescape(s: &str) -> StoreResult<String> {
    let mut out = String::with_capacity(s.len());
    let mut chars = s.chars();
    while let Some(c) = chars.next() {
        if c != '\\' {
            out.push(c);
            continue;
        }
        match chars.next() {
            Some('\\') => out.push('\\'),
            Some('t') => out.push('\t'),
            Some('n') => out.push('\n'),
            Some('r') => out.push('\r'),
            Some(other) => {
                return Err(StoreError::Corrupt(format!(
                    "unknown escape sequence \\{}",
                    other
                )))
            }
            None => {
                return Err(StoreError::Corrupt(
                    "dangling backslash at end of field".to_string(),
                ))
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(out)
}

/// FNV-1a 64-bit hash. It is not cryptographic — it only has to catch a
/// torn or accidentally garbled record, for which a fast non-cryptographic
/// checksum is the right tool. The hash covers the key, a separator byte
/// that can never appear in the hashed data, and the value.
fn checksum(key: &str, value: &str) -> u64 {
    const FNV_OFFSET: u64 = 0xcbf29ce484222325;
    const FNV_PRIME: u64 = 0x100000001b3;
    let mut hash = FNV_OFFSET;
    for &b in key.as_bytes() {
        hash ^= b as u64;
        hash = hash.wrapping_mul(FNV_PRIME);
    }
    hash ^= 0xff;
    hash = hash.wrapping_mul(FNV_PRIME);
    for &b in value.as_bytes() {
        hash ^= b as u64;
        hash = hash.wrapping_mul(FNV_PRIME);
    }
    hash
}

/// One decoded log record.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum Record {
    Put { key: String, value: String },
    Delete { key: String },
}

/// Encode a `PUT` record, including its trailing newline.
fn encode_put(key: &str, value: &str) -> String {
    format!(
        "PUT\t{}\t{}\t{:016x}\n",
        escape(key),
        escape(value),
        checksum(key, value)
    )
}

/// Encode a `DEL` tombstone record, including its trailing newline.
fn encode_del(key: &str) -> String {
    format!("DEL\t{}\t{:016x}\n", escape(key), checksum(key, ""))
}

/// Decode one log line (without its trailing newline) and verify its
/// checksum. Any structural problem or checksum mismatch is an error,
/// which the recovery replay treats as the start of a torn tail.
fn parse_record(line: &str) -> StoreResult<Record> {
    let corrupt = |msg: &str| StoreError::Corrupt(msg.to_string());
    let fields: Vec<&str> = line.split('\t').collect();
    match fields.as_slice() {
        ["PUT", key, value, sum] => {
            let key = unescape(key)?;
            let value = unescape(value)?;
            let expected = u64::from_str_radix(sum, 16)
                .map_err(|_| corrupt("PUT record has a non-hex checksum"))?;
            if expected != checksum(&key, &value) {
                return Err(corrupt("PUT record checksum mismatch"));
            }
            Ok(Record::Put { key, value })
        }
        ["DEL", key, sum] => {
            let key = unescape(key)?;
            let expected = u64::from_str_radix(sum, 16)
                .map_err(|_| corrupt("DEL record has a non-hex checksum"))?;
            if expected != checksum(&key, "") {
                return Err(corrupt("DEL record checksum mismatch"));
            }
            Ok(Record::Delete { key })
        }
        _ => Err(corrupt(&format!(
            "record has {} tab-separated fields, expected 3 or 4",
            fields.len()
        ))),
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Store statistics
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Counters kept in memory for the lifetime of an open store. They are
/// informational only and are not persisted; `log_bytes` and `entries`
/// are recomputed from the log on every open.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Stats {
    /// Live entries (keys present in the index).
    pub entries: usize,
    /// Bytes currently used by the log file on disk.
    pub log_bytes: u64,
    /// `put` calls since this store was opened.
    pub puts: u64,
    /// `delete` calls since this store was opened.
    pub deletes: u64,
    /// `get` calls since this store was opened.
    pub gets: u64,
    /// Records discarded by recovery as a corrupt tail (0 on a clean open).
    pub recovered_truncated_records: u64,
    /// Compactions performed since this store was opened.
    pub compactions: u64,
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The store itself
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A persistent key-value store backed by an append-only log file.
///
/// The handle owns the open log file. Dropping it closes the file; all
/// completed writes are already in the operating system's page cache, so
/// they survive a normal close and a process crash. Call `sync` to force
/// them to stable storage when durability across a power loss matters.
pub struct KvStore {
    path: PathBuf,
    file: File,
    /// Live keys only: key -> byte offset of the key's latest PUT record.
    index: HashMap<String, u64>,
    stats: Stats,
}

impl KvStore {
    /// Open the store at `path`, creating the log file if it does not
    /// exist. Replays the log to rebuild the index, truncating any torn
    /// or corrupt tail left by a crash. This is the only constructor:
    /// recovery is not optional, it is what "open" means.
    pub fn open<P: AsRef<Path>>(path: P) -> StoreResult<Self> {
        let path = path.as_ref().to_path_buf();
        let mut file = OpenOptions::new()
            .read(true)
            .write(true)
            .create(true)
            .open(&path)?;
        let (index, truncated) = replay(&mut file)?;
        let log_bytes = file.metadata()?.len();
        let stats = Stats {
            entries: index.len(),
            log_bytes,
            recovered_truncated_records: truncated,
            ..Default::default()
        };
        file.seek(SeekFrom::End(0))?;
        Ok(KvStore {
            path,
            file,
            index,
            stats,
        })
    }

    /// Path of the log file.
    pub fn path(&self) -> &Path {
        &self.path
    }

    /// Number of live entries.
    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.index.len()
    }

    /// True when the store holds no entries.
    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.index.is_empty()
    }

    /// Current statistics snapshot.
    pub fn stats(&self) -> Stats {
        let mut s = self.stats.clone();
        s.entries = self.index.len();
        s.log_bytes = self.file.metadata().map(|m| m.len()).unwrap_or(0);
        s
    }

    /// Bytes used by the log file on disk right now.
    pub fn log_size(&self) -> StoreResult<u64> {
        Ok(self.file.metadata()?.len())
    }

    /// Force all buffered writes out to stable storage.
    pub fn sync(&mut self) -> StoreResult<()> {
        self.file.sync_all()?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// True when `key` has a live value.
    pub fn contains_key(&self, key: &str) -> bool {
        self.index.contains_key(key)
    }

    /// Sorted list of all live keys.
    pub fn keys(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        let mut keys: Vec<String> = self.index.keys().cloned().collect();
        keys.sort();
        keys
    }

    /// Store `value` under `key`, overwriting any previous value. The
    /// write is appended to the log and the index updated; the old record
    /// stays in the log as dead space until the next compaction.
    pub fn put(&mut self, key: &str, value: &str) -> StoreResult<()> {
        let record = encode_put(key, value);
        let offset = self.append_record(&record)?;
        self.index.insert(key.to_string(), offset);
        self.stats.puts += 1;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Store several entries in one call. Each entry is still its own
    /// log record, so a crash part-way through leaves a prefix of the
    /// batch committed — exactly the guarantee the single-record API
    /// gives, applied per entry.
    pub fn put_many(&mut self, entries: &[(&str, &str)]) -> StoreResult<()> {
        for (key, value) in entries {
            self.put(key, value)?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Look up `key`. Returns `Ok(None)` when the key is absent, which is
    /// not an error. The value is read back from the log at the offset
    /// recorded in the index and its checksum verified, so a `get` can
    /// still detect on-disk corruption that happened after the store
    /// was opened.
    pub fn get(&mut self, key: &str) -> StoreResult<Option<String>> {
        self.stats.gets += 1;
        let offset = match self.index.get(key) {
            Some(&o) => o,
            None => return Ok(None),
        };
        self.file.seek(SeekFrom::Start(offset))?;
        let mut reader = BufReader::new(&mut self.file);
        let mut line = String::new();
        reader.read_line(&mut line)?;
        let line = line.strip_suffix('\n').unwrap_or(&line);
        match parse_record(line)? {
            Record::Put { value, .. } => Ok(Some(value)),
            Record::Delete { .. } => Err(StoreError::Corrupt(format!(
                "index points at a tombstone for key {:?}",
                key
            ))),
        }
    }

    /// Delete `key`. Returns true when the key was present. A tombstone
    /// record is appended so the deletion survives a reopen; deleting an
    /// absent key writes nothing.
    pub fn delete(&mut self, key: &str) -> StoreResult<bool> {
        if !self.index.contains_key(key) {
            return Ok(false);
        }
        let record = encode_del(key);
        self.append_record(&record)?;
        self.index.remove(key);
        self.stats.deletes += 1;
        Ok(true)
    }

    /// Remove every entry. Implemented as a compaction to an empty file:
    /// the log is replaced with a zero-byte file, so no trace of the old
    /// entries remains on disk.
    pub fn clear(&mut self) -> StoreResult<()> {
        let tmp_path = self.tmp_path();
        {
            let tmp = File::create(&tmp_path)?;
            tmp.sync_all()?;
        }
        self.swap_log_file(&tmp_path)?;
        self.index.clear();
        self.stats.compactions += 1;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// All live entries with keys starting with `prefix`, in key order.
    pub fn scan_prefix(&mut self, prefix: &str) -> StoreResult<Vec<(String, String)>> {
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for key in self.keys() {
            if key.starts_with(prefix) {
                if let Some(value) = self.get(&key)? {
                    out.push((key, value));
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(out)
    }

    /// All live entries in key order.
    pub fn iter(&mut self) -> StoreResult<Vec<(String, String)>> {
        self.scan_prefix("")
    }

    /// Append one encoded record to the end of the log and return the
    /// byte offset it was written at.
    fn append_record(&mut self, record: &str) -> StoreResult<u64> {
        let offset = self.file.seek(SeekFrom::End(0))?;
        self.file.write_all(record.as_bytes())?;
        Ok(offset)
    }

    /// Path of the temporary file used by compaction and clear: the log
    /// path with a `.tmp` suffix, in the same directory so the final
    /// rename stays on one filesystem.
    fn tmp_path(&self) -> PathBuf {
        let mut name = self.path.as_os_str().to_owned();
        name.push(".tmp");
        PathBuf::from(name)
    }

    /// Rewrite the log so it contains only the live entries, in key
    /// order, then atomically rename it over the old log. Dead records
    /// from overwritten values and tombstones are dropped, so the file
    /// shrinks. The new index is built from the rewritten offsets.
    pub fn compact(&mut self) -> StoreResult<()> {
        let tmp_path = self.tmp_path();
        let mut new_index: HashMap<String, u64> = HashMap::new();
        let mut offset: u64 = 0;
        {
            let mut tmp = File::create(&tmp_path)?;
            for key in self.keys() {
                let value = self
                    .get(&key)?
                    .ok_or_else(|| StoreError::Corrupt(format!("lost value for {:?}", key)))?;
                let record = encode_put(&key, &value);
                tmp.write_all(record.as_bytes())?;
                new_index.insert(key, offset);
                offset += record.len() as u64;
            }
            tmp.sync_all()?;
        }
        self.swap_log_file(&tmp_path)?;
        self.index = new_index;
        self.stats.compactions += 1;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Replace the open log file with the file at `new_path` (already
    /// fully written and synced) and rename `new_path` over the log
    /// path. On Unix the rename succeeds even though the new file is
    /// open; the handle simply keeps referring to the same inode under
    /// its new name.
    fn swap_log_file(&mut self, new_path: &Path) -> StoreResult<()> {
        let new_file = OpenOptions::new().read(true).write(true).open(new_path)?;
        let old_file = std::mem::replace(&mut self.file, new_file);
        drop(old_file);
        std::fs::rename(new_path, &self.path)?;
        self.file.seek(SeekFrom::End(0))?;
        Ok(())
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Recovery replay
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Replay the log from the beginning, rebuilding the index. Returns the
/// index and the number of records discarded as a corrupt tail.
///
/// The replay is deliberately strict: the first record that fails to
/// parse or fails its checksum ends the replay, and the file is truncated
/// at that record's offset. In an append-only log, everything after a
/// torn write is suspect — a later record cannot be trusted once an
/// earlier one is damaged — so truncation is the only safe recovery.
/// A clean log replays with zero discarded records.
fn replay(file: &mut File) -> StoreResult<(HashMap<String, u64>, u64)> {
    file.seek(SeekFrom::Start(0))?;
    let mut reader = BufReader::new(&mut *file);
    let mut index: HashMap<String, u64> = HashMap::new();
    let mut offset: u64 = 0;
    let mut discarded: u64 = 0;

    loop {
        let mut line = String::new();
        let bytes_read = reader.read_line(&mut line)?;
        if bytes_read == 0 {
            break; // clean end of file
        }
        let record_len = line.len() as u64;
        // A record without its trailing newline was cut short by a crash
        // mid-write: it is a torn tail even if it happens to parse.
        let complete = line.ends_with('\n');
        let body = line.strip_suffix('\n').unwrap_or(&line);
        match parse_record(body) {
            Ok(Record::Put { key, .. }) if complete => {
                index.insert(key, offset);
            }
            Ok(Record::Delete { key }) if complete => {
                index.remove(&key);
            }
            _ => {
                // Corrupt, incomplete or bad-checksum record: everything
                // from here on is discarded.
                discarded = 1;
                // Count any further lines as discarded too, for the stat.
                let mut rest = String::new();
                while reader.read_line(&mut rest)? > 0 {
                    discarded += 1;
                    rest.clear();
                }
                drop(reader);
                file.set_len(offset)?;
                file.seek(SeekFrom::End(0))?;
                return Ok((index, discarded));
            }
        }
        offset += record_len;
    }
    Ok((index, discarded))
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Demonstration
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A fresh temporary path for a demo store, unique per run.
fn demo_path(name: &str) -> PathBuf {
    std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("kv_store_demo_{}_{}.log", name, std::process::id()))
}

/// Run the end-to-end demonstration. Every step is asserted, so a
/// successful run proves persistence, recovery and compaction work.
fn run_demo() {
    println!("kv_store demonstration — persistent log-structured store\n");

    // 1. Basic put / get / overwrite / contains / len.
    let path = demo_path("basic");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&path);
    let mut store = KvStore::open(&path).expect("open store");
    assert!(store.is_empty());
    store.put("user:1:name", "Ada Lovelace").expect("put");
    store.put("user:1:city", "London").expect("put");
    store.put("user:2:name", "Alan Turing").expect("put");
    store.put("counter", "41").expect("put");
    assert_eq!(store.len(), 4);
    assert_eq!(store.get("user:1:name").unwrap(), Some("Ada Lovelace".to_string()));
    assert_eq!(store.get("missing").unwrap(), None);
    assert!(store.contains_key("counter"));
    assert!(!store.contains_key("missing"));
    store.put("counter", "42").expect("overwrite");
    assert_eq!(store.get("counter").unwrap(), Some("42".to_string()));
    assert_eq!(store.len(), 4, "overwrite must not add an entry");
    println!("1. put/get/overwrite: 4 entries, counter updated 41 -> 42");

    // 2. Tricky keys and values: tabs, newlines, backslashes, empty
    //    values, empty keys and non-ASCII text must round-trip exactly.
    store.put("we\tird\nkey", "value with\ttab and\nnewline \\ backslash").expect("put tricky");
    store.put("", "empty key").expect("put empty key");
    store.put("empty value", "").expect("put empty value");
    store.put("greeting", "héllo wörld — ünïcode ✓").expect("put unicode");
    assert_eq!(
        store.get("we\tird\nkey").unwrap(),
        Some("value with\ttab and\nnewline \\ backslash".to_string())
    );
    assert_eq!(store.get("").unwrap(), Some("empty key".to_string()));
    assert_eq!(store.get("empty value").unwrap(), Some("".to_string()));
    assert_eq!(store.get("greeting").unwrap(), Some("héllo wörld — ünïcode ✓".to_string()));
    println!("2. tricky keys/values (tabs, newlines, unicode, empty) round-trip");

    // 3. Prefix scan and batch put.
    store
        .put_many(&[("user:3:name", "Grace Hopper"), ("user:3:city", "New York")])
        .expect("put_many");
    let users = store.scan_prefix("user:").expect("scan");
    assert_eq!(users.len(), 5);
    assert_eq!(users[0].0, "user:1:city");
    assert!(users.iter().all(|(k, _)| k.starts_with("user:")));
    println!("3. prefix scan \"user:\": {} entries, sorted", users.len());

    // 4. Delete: the key disappears and a tombstone is logged.
    assert!(store.delete("user:2:name").expect("delete"));
    assert!(!store.delete("user:2:name").expect("delete again"));
    assert_eq!(store.get("user:2:name").unwrap(), None);
    assert!(!store.contains_key("user:2:name"));
    println!("4. delete removes the key; deleting again reports false");

    // 5. Persistence: close and reopen, everything is still there.
    let size_before = store.log_size().expect("log size");
    drop(store);
    let mut store = KvStore::open(&path).expect("reopen store");
    assert_eq!(store.get("user:1:name").unwrap(), Some("Ada Lovelace".to_string()));
    assert_eq!(store.get("counter").unwrap(), Some("42".to_string()));
    assert_eq!(store.get("user:2:name").unwrap(), None);
    assert_eq!(store.get("greeting").unwrap(), Some("héllo wörld — ünïcode ✓".to_string()));
    assert_eq!(store.stats().recovered_truncated_records, 0, "clean reopen");
    println!("5. reopen: all entries intact, 0 records truncated (clean log)");

    // 6. Crash simulation: append a torn partial record, as a process
    //    killed mid-write would leave behind. Recovery truncates it.
    {
        let mut raw = OpenOptions::new().append(true).open(&path).expect("open raw");
        raw.write_all(b"PUT\tcrash\tpartial-value-without-newline")
            .expect("write torn tail");
        raw.sync_all().expect("sync torn tail");
    }
    drop(store);
    let mut store = KvStore::open(&path).expect("reopen after crash");
    assert_eq!(store.stats().recovered_truncated_records, 1);
    assert_eq!(store.get("crash").unwrap(), None);
    assert_eq!(store.get("counter").unwrap(), Some("42".to_string()));
    assert!(store.log_size().expect("size") < size_before + 100);
    println!("6. torn tail recovered: 1 record truncated, earlier data intact");

    // 7. Checksum corruption: a record whose bytes were flipped must be
    //    treated as a torn tail, not trusted.
    {
        let mut raw = OpenOptions::new().append(true).open(&path).expect("open raw");
        // A well-formed record with a deliberately wrong checksum.
        raw.write_all(b"PUT\tforged\tvalue\t0000000000000000\n")
            .expect("write forged record");
    }
    drop(store);
    let store = KvStore::open(&path).expect("reopen after forgery");
    assert_eq!(store.stats().recovered_truncated_records, 1);
    assert_eq!(store.len(), 9);
    println!("7. forged record (bad checksum) rejected by recovery");

    // 8. Compaction: grow the log with churn, then compact and check the
    //    file shrinks while every live entry survives a reopen.
    let mut store = KvStore::open(&path).expect("reopen for compaction");
    for i in 0..50 {
        store.put(&format!("churn:{}", i), "x").expect("churn put");
    }
    for i in 0..50 {
        store.delete(&format!("churn:{}", i)).expect("churn delete");
    }
    for i in 0..20 {
        store.put("counter", &format!("{}", 100 + i)).expect("overwrite churn");
    }
    let bloated = store.log_size().expect("bloated size");
    let live_before = store.iter().expect("iter before compact");
    store.compact().expect("compact");
    let compacted = store.log_size().expect("compacted size");
    assert!(compacted < bloated, "compaction must shrink the log");
    let live_after = store.iter().expect("iter after compact");
    assert_eq!(live_before, live_after);
    assert_eq!(store.get("counter").unwrap(), Some("119".to_string()));
    drop(store);
    let mut store = KvStore::open(&path).expect("reopen after compact");
    assert_eq!(store.iter().expect("iter after reopen"), live_after);
    println!(
        "8. compaction: log {} -> {} bytes, {} live entries preserved",
        bloated,
        compacted,
        live_after.len()
    );

    // 9. Clear removes everything, including from disk.
    store.clear().expect("clear");
    assert!(store.is_empty());
    assert_eq!(store.log_size().expect("size after clear"), 0);
    drop(store);
    let store = KvStore::open(&path).expect("reopen after clear");
    assert!(store.is_empty());
    std::fs::remove_file(&path).expect("remove demo log");
    println!("9. clear: store empty on disk and after reopen");

    println!("\nAll kv_store demonstration checks passed.");
}

fn main() {
    run_demo();
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Unit tests
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn tmp_store(name: &str) -> (KvStore, PathBuf) {
        let path = std::env::temp_dir()
            .join(format!("kv_store_test_{}_{}.log", name, std::process::id()));
        let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&path);
        let store = KvStore::open(&path).expect("open test store");
        (store, path)
    }

    #[test]
    fn escape_round_trip() {
        for s in ["", "plain", "a\tb\nc\rd\\e", "ünïcode ✓", " trailing ", "\t\n\r\\"] {
            assert_eq!(unescape(&escape(s)).unwrap(), s);
        }
        assert!(unescape("bad\\xescape").is_err());
        assert!(unescape("dangling\\").is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn checksum_is_stable_and_sensitive() {
        assert_eq!(checksum("k", "v"), checksum("k", "v"));
        assert_ne!(checksum("k", "v"), checksum("k", "w"));
        assert_ne!(checksum("k", "v"), checksum("j", "v"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn record_encode_parse_round_trip() {
        for (k, v) in [("a", "1"), ("with\ttab", "new\nline"), ("", ""), ("ü", "✓")] {
            let line = encode_put(k, v);
            let body = line.strip_suffix('\n').unwrap();
            assert_eq!(parse_record(body).unwrap(), Record::Put {
                key: k.to_string(),
                value: v.to_string()
            });
        }
        let del = encode_del("gone");
        assert_eq!(
            parse_record(del.strip_suffix('\n').unwrap()).unwrap(),
            Record::Delete { key: "gone".to_string() }
        );
        // A flipped checksum must fail.
        assert!(parse_record("PUT\tk\tv\t0000000000000000").is_err());
        assert!(parse_record("PUT\tonly-two-fields").is_err());
        assert!(parse_record("NOPE\ta\tb\tc").is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn put_get_delete_and_reopen() {
        let (mut store, path) = tmp_store("basic");
        store.put("a", "1").unwrap();
        store.put("b", "2").unwrap();
        store.put("a", "3").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(store.get("a").unwrap(), Some("3".to_string()));
        assert!(store.delete("b").unwrap());
        assert_eq!(store.get("b").unwrap(), None);
        drop(store);
        let mut store = KvStore::open(&path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(store.get("a").unwrap(), Some("3".to_string()));
        assert_eq!(store.get("b").unwrap(), None);
        assert_eq!(store.len(), 1);
        drop(store);
        std::fs::remove_file(&path).unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn torn_tail_is_truncated_on_open() {
        let (mut store, path) = tmp_store("torn");
        store.put("good", "yes").unwrap();
        drop(store);
        {
            let mut raw = OpenOptions::new().append(true).open(&path).unwrap();
            raw.write_all(b"PUT\tbad\tno-newline").unwrap();
        }
        let mut store = KvStore::open(&path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(store.stats().recovered_truncated_records, 1);
        assert_eq!(store.get("good").unwrap(), Some("yes".to_string()));
        // The store is usable again after recovery.
        store.put("after", "recovery").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(store.get("after").unwrap(), Some("recovery".to_string()));
        drop(store);
        std::fs::remove_file(&path).unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn garbage_mid_log_truncates_everything_after_it() {
        let (mut store, path) = tmp_store("garbage");
        store.put("before", "1").unwrap();
        drop(store);
        {
            let mut raw = OpenOptions::new().append(true).open(&path).unwrap();
            raw.write_all(b"this is not a record at all\nPUT\tafter\t1\t0000000000000000\n")
                .unwrap();
        }
        let store = KvStore::open(&path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(store.stats().recovered_truncated_records, 2);
        assert_eq!(store.len(), 1);
        drop(store);
        std::fs::remove_file(&path).unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn compaction_preserves_live_data_and_shrinks() {
        let (mut store, path) = tmp_store("compact");
        for i in 0..30 {
            store.put(&format!("k{:02}", i), &format!("v{}", i)).unwrap();
        }
        for i in 0..15 {
            store.delete(&format!("k{:02}", i)).unwrap();
        }
        for _ in 0..10 {
            store.put("k29", "final").unwrap();
        }
        let before = store.log_size().unwrap();
        let live = store.iter().unwrap();
        store.compact().unwrap();
        let after = store.log_size().unwrap();
        assert!(after < before);
        assert_eq!(store.iter().unwrap(), live);
        assert_eq!(store.get("k29").unwrap(), Some("final".to_string()));
        drop(store);
        let mut store = KvStore::open(&path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(store.iter().unwrap(), live);
        drop(store);
        std::fs::remove_file(&path).unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn prefix_scan_and_keys_are_sorted() {
        let (mut store, path) = tmp_store("scan");
        store.put_many(&[("b:2", "x"), ("a:1", "x"), ("b:1", "x"), ("c:1", "x")]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(store.keys(), vec!["a:1", "b:1", "b:2", "c:1"]);
        let found = store.scan_prefix("b:").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(found.len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(found[0].0, "b:1");
        drop(store);
        std::fs::remove_file(&path).unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn clear_empties_store() {
        let (mut store, path) = tmp_store("clear");
        store.put("a", "1").unwrap();
        store.clear().unwrap();
        assert!(store.is_empty());
        assert_eq!(store.log_size().unwrap(), 0);
        drop(store);
        let store = KvStore::open(&path).unwrap();
        assert!(store.is_empty());
        drop(store);
        std::fs::remove_file(&path).unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn empty_key_and_value() {
        let (mut store, path) = tmp_store("empty");
        store.put("", "").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(store.get("").unwrap(), Some("".to_string()));
        drop(store);
        let mut store = KvStore::open(&path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(store.get("").unwrap(), Some("".to_string()));
        drop(store);
        std::fs::remove_file(&path).unwrap();
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/lru_cache.rs ---
// lru_cache.rs
//
// A least-recently-used (LRU) cache built from a `HashMap` plus a
// doubly linked list threaded through a `Vec` by *indices* -- no
// `unsafe`, no `Rc`/`RefCell`, no raw pointers.
//
// Layout
// ------
// * `map: HashMap<K, usize>` -- key to the index of its node in `nodes`.
// * `nodes: Vec<Option<Node<K, V>>>` -- the linked-list nodes. A slot
//   is `Some(node)` when it holds a live entry and `None` when it is
//   vacant. A node holds the key, the value, and `prev`/`next` indices
//   (`None` at the ends). Vacant slots are remembered in `free` so
//   they can be reused instead of growing the vector forever.
// * `head` -- the most recently used node; `tail` -- the least recently
//   used node, i.e. the eviction candidate.
//
// The linked list is only ever rewired through four small helpers
// (`unlink`, `push_front`, `move_to_front`, `evict_tail`), each of
// which keeps the `HashMap`, the `Vec` and the `head`/`tail` pointers
// consistent. `check_invariants` (used by the demo and the tests)
// walks the list from the head, verifies that every node is visited
// exactly once, that the map agrees with the list, and that the
// ordering is head-to-tail.
//
// Why indices instead of `Box` links? A `Box`-based list needs either
// `Rc<RefCell<...>>` (runtime borrow checks) or `unsafe` (raw pointers
// to siblings). Indices give the same O(1) splicing with plain safe
// code, and nodes stay in one contiguous allocation, which is good for
// cache locality -- fitting for a cache.
//
// Why `Option<Node>` slots? Eviction and removal have to *move* the
// key and value out of a slot, and generic `K`/`V` have no dummy value
// to swap in. `Option::take` solves it: a vacated slot becomes `None`
// and the node is returned by value.
//
// API
// ---
// * `get` -- returns a reference and promotes the entry to most
//   recently used; records a hit or a miss in the statistics.
// * `peek` -- reads without promoting (useful for inspection).
// * `put` -- inserts or updates; an update promotes the entry, and an
//   insert beyond capacity evicts the tail and returns the evicted
//   `(K, V)` pair so the caller can react (write it back, log it...).
// * `remove`, `contains`, `clear`, `resize`.
// * `iter` / `keys` / `values` -- in most-recently-used-first order.
// * `stats` -- hits, misses, evictions, and the derived hit rate.
//
// The demo in `main` checks the eviction order and the statistics
// against hand-computed expectations, then runs a small benchmark:
// a deterministic pseudo-random workload over a large key space, with
// timings and a hit-rate report. Unit tests cover promotion, eviction,
// updates, removal, resize, zero capacity, and the invariants under
// churn.

use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::fmt::Debug;
use std::hash::Hash;
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Node
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// One entry of the linked list. `prev`/`next` are indices into the
/// cache's `nodes` vector (`None` means "no neighbour on that side").
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
struct Node<K, V> {
    key: K,
    value: V,
    prev: Option<usize>,
    next: Option<usize>,
}

impl<K, V> Node<K, V> {
    fn new(key: K, value: V) -> Node<K, V> {
        Node {
            key,
            value,
            prev: None,
            next: None,
        }
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Statistics
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Counters for cache activity. `Clone`/`Copy` so callers can snapshot
/// them cheaply.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Stats {
    pub hits: u64,
    pub misses: u64,
    pub evictions: u64,
    pub insertions: u64,
    pub updates: u64,
}

impl Stats {
    /// Fraction of lookups that hit, or `None` when there were none.
    pub fn hit_rate(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        let total = self.hits + self.misses;
        if total == 0 {
            None
        } else {
            Some(self.hits as f64 / total as f64)
        }
    }

    pub fn lookups(&self) -> u64 {
        self.hits + self.misses
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// LruCache
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A fixed-capacity LRU cache.
///
/// `K` must be hashable, equatable and cloneable (the key is stored in
/// the node as well as in the map, so it is cloned on insert). `V` has
/// no bounds at all.
///
/// A cache with capacity 0 never stores anything: `put` returns the
/// would-be entry as immediately evicted.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct LruCache<K, V> {
    map: HashMap<K, usize>,
    nodes: Vec<Option<Node<K, V>>>,
    /// Indices of vacant (`None`) slots in `nodes`, for reuse (LIFO).
    free: Vec<usize>,
    head: Option<usize>,
    tail: Option<usize>,
    capacity: usize,
    stats: Stats,
}

impl<K, V> LruCache<K, V> {
    /// Borrow the node at `idx`. Panics only on an internal bug: every
    /// index in the map or the list must point at a live node.
    fn node(&self, idx: usize) -> &Node<K, V> {
        self.nodes[idx].as_ref().expect("live index points at vacant slot")
    }

    fn node_mut(&mut self, idx: usize) -> &mut Node<K, V> {
        self.nodes[idx].as_mut().expect("live index points at vacant slot")
    }
}

impl<K, V> LruCache<K, V>
where
    K: Eq + Hash + Clone,
{
    /// Create a cache that holds at most `capacity` entries.
    pub fn new(capacity: usize) -> LruCache<K, V> {
        LruCache {
            map: HashMap::with_capacity(capacity),
            nodes: Vec::with_capacity(capacity),
            free: Vec::new(),
            head: None,
            tail: None,
            capacity,
            stats: Stats::default(),
        }
    }

    pub fn capacity(&self) -> usize {
        self.capacity
    }

    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.map.len()
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.map.is_empty()
    }

    pub fn stats(&self) -> Stats {
        self.stats
    }

    pub fn contains(&self, key: &K) -> bool {
        self.map.contains_key(key)
    }

    /// Number of node slots allocated (live + vacant). Under churn
    /// this stays close to the capacity, because freed slots are
    /// reused instead of growing the vector.
    pub fn slot_count(&self) -> usize {
        self.nodes.len()
    }

    // -- linked-list plumbing ------------------------------------------------

    /// Detach the node at `idx` from the list, fixing its neighbours
    /// and `head`/`tail`. The node itself is left with stale
    /// `prev`/`next` fields; callers must overwrite, re-link or
    /// remove it before it is linked again.
    fn unlink(&mut self, idx: usize) {
        let (prev, next) = {
            let node = self.node(idx);
            (node.prev, node.next)
        };
        match prev {
            Some(p) => self.node_mut(p).next = next,
            None => self.head = next, // was the head
        }
        match next {
            Some(n) => self.node_mut(n).prev = prev,
            None => self.tail = prev, // was the tail
        }
    }

    /// Insert the node at `idx` at the front (most recently used end).
    /// The node must not currently be linked.
    fn push_front(&mut self, idx: usize) {
        self.node_mut(idx).prev = None;
        self.node_mut(idx).next = self.head;
        if let Some(old_head) = self.head {
            self.node_mut(old_head).prev = Some(idx);
        } else {
            // The list was empty, so this node is also the tail.
            self.tail = Some(idx);
        }
        self.head = Some(idx);
    }

    /// Move an already-linked node to the front. A no-op when it is
    /// already the head.
    fn move_to_front(&mut self, idx: usize) {
        if self.head == Some(idx) {
            return;
        }
        self.unlink(idx);
        self.push_front(idx);
    }

    /// Allocate a slot for a new node, reusing a freed slot when one
    /// exists, otherwise pushing a fresh node onto `nodes`.
    fn alloc_slot(&mut self, node: Node<K, V>) -> usize {
        match self.free.pop() {
            Some(idx) => {
                self.nodes[idx] = Some(node);
                idx
            }
            None => {
                self.nodes.push(Some(node));
                self.nodes.len() - 1
            }
        }
    }

    // -- public API ------------------------------------------------------------

    /// Look up `key`. On a hit the entry is promoted to most recently
    /// used and a reference to its value is returned; on a miss `None`
    /// is returned. Both outcomes are recorded in the statistics.
    pub fn get(&mut self, key: &K) -> Option<&V> {
        match self.map.get(key) {
            Some(&idx) => {
                self.stats.hits += 1;
                self.move_to_front(idx);
                Some(&self.node(idx).value)
            }
            None => {
                self.stats.misses += 1;
                None
            }
        }
    }

    /// Look up `key` and return a mutable reference to its value,
    /// promoting the entry like `get` does.
    pub fn get_mut(&mut self, key: &K) -> Option<&mut V> {
        match self.map.get(key) {
            Some(&idx) => {
                self.stats.hits += 1;
                self.move_to_front(idx);
                Some(&mut self.node_mut(idx).value)
            }
            None => {
                self.stats.misses += 1;
                None
            }
        }
    }

    /// Look up `key` without promoting it and without touching the
    /// statistics. Useful for inspection and for "peek before you
    /// commit" patterns.
    pub fn peek(&self, key: &K) -> Option<&V> {
        self.map.get(key).map(|&idx| &self.node(idx).value)
    }

    /// Look up `key`, promoting it, or insert `value` (created by
    /// `default`) when it is absent. Returns a mutable reference to
    /// the value either way.
    pub fn get_or_insert_with<F>(&mut self, key: K, default: F) -> &mut V
    where
        F: FnOnce() -> V,
    {
        if self.contains(&key) {
            return self.get_mut(&key).unwrap();
        }
        let value = default();
        self.put(key.clone(), value);
        self.get_mut(&key).unwrap()
    }

    /// Insert or update `key -> value`.
    ///
    /// * Update: the value is replaced, the entry is promoted to most
    ///   recently used, and `None` is returned.
    /// * Insert: the entry becomes most recently used. When the cache
    ///   is already full, the least recently used entry is evicted and
    ///   returned as `Some((key, value))`.
    /// * Capacity 0: nothing is stored; the entry is returned as
    ///   immediately evicted.
    pub fn put(&mut self, key: K, value: V) -> Option<(K, V)> {
        if let Some(&idx) = self.map.get(&key) {
            // Update in place: replace the value, promote, no eviction.
            self.node_mut(idx).value = value;
            self.move_to_front(idx);
            self.stats.updates += 1;
            return None;
        }
        if self.capacity == 0 {
            self.stats.evictions += 1;
            return Some((key, value));
        }
        let evicted = if self.len() >= self.capacity {
            self.evict_tail()
        } else {
            None
        };
        let idx = self.alloc_slot(Node::new(key.clone(), value));
        self.map.insert(key, idx);
        self.push_front(idx);
        self.stats.insertions += 1;
        evicted
    }

    /// Evict the least recently used entry, returning it. `None` when
    /// the cache is empty. This is the primitive `put` uses when the
    /// cache is full, exposed publicly so callers can evict manually.
    pub fn evict_tail(&mut self) -> Option<(K, V)> {
        let tail_idx = self.tail?;
        self.unlink(tail_idx);
        let node = self.nodes[tail_idx]
            .take()
            .expect("tail points at vacant slot");
        self.map.remove(&node.key);
        self.free.push(tail_idx);
        self.stats.evictions += 1;
        Some((node.key, node.value))
    }

    /// Remove `key`, returning its value. `None` when absent.
    pub fn remove(&mut self, key: &K) -> Option<V> {
        let idx = self.map.remove(key)?;
        self.unlink(idx);
        let node = self.nodes[idx]
            .take()
            .expect("map points at vacant slot");
        self.free.push(idx);
        Some(node.value)
    }

    /// Remove all entries and reset the statistics.
    pub fn clear(&mut self) {
        self.map.clear();
        self.nodes.clear();
        self.free.clear();
        self.head = None;
        self.tail = None;
        self.stats = Stats::default();
    }

    /// Change the capacity. Shrinking below the current size evicts
    /// least recently used entries until the size fits, returning the
    /// evicted pairs in eviction order (least recent first).
    pub fn resize(&mut self, new_capacity: usize) -> Vec<(K, V)> {
        self.capacity = new_capacity;
        let mut evicted = Vec::new();
        while self.len() > self.capacity {
            match self.evict_tail() {
                Some(pair) => evicted.push(pair),
                None => break,
            }
        }
        evicted
    }

    /// Iterate over entries in most-recently-used-first order.
    pub fn iter(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&K, &V)> {
        LruIter {
            cache: self,
            next: self.head,
        }
    }

    pub fn keys(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &K> {
        self.iter().map(|(k, _)| k)
    }

    pub fn values(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &V> {
        self.iter().map(|(_, v)| v)
    }

    /// The least recently used entry (the next eviction candidate).
    pub fn lru_entry(&self) -> Option<(&K, &V)> {
        self.tail
            .map(|idx| (&self.node(idx).key, &self.node(idx).value))
    }

    /// The most recently used entry.
    pub fn mru_entry(&self) -> Option<(&K, &V)> {
        self.head
            .map(|idx| (&self.node(idx).key, &self.node(idx).value))
    }

    /// Verify the internal invariants; panics on the first problem.
    /// Checks: the list from `head` visits exactly `len` nodes, ends
    /// at `tail`, has no cycles or repeated nodes, the map agrees
    /// with the list (same keys, same indices), and the free list
    /// contains exactly the slots not in the list.
    pub fn check_invariants(&self) {
        let mut seen = vec![false; self.nodes.len()];
        let mut count = 0;
        let mut cur = self.head;
        let mut prev: Option<usize> = None;
        while let Some(idx) = cur {
            assert!(idx < self.nodes.len(), "list index out of range");
            assert!(!seen[idx], "list visits node {} twice (cycle?)", idx);
            seen[idx] = true;
            count += 1;
            let node = self.node(idx);
            assert_eq!(node.prev, prev, "prev link wrong at node {}", idx);
            assert_eq!(
                self.map.get(&node.key),
                Some(&idx),
                "map disagrees with list at node {}",
                idx
            );
            prev = Some(idx);
            cur = node.next;
        }
        assert_eq!(count, self.len(), "list length != map length");
        assert_eq!(prev, self.tail, "tail is not the last list node");
        if self.len() == 0 {
            assert_eq!(self.head, None);
            assert_eq!(self.tail, None);
        }
        // Free slots are exactly the unvisited ones, with no duplicates.
        let mut free_seen = vec![false; self.nodes.len()];
        for &f in &self.free {
            assert!(f < self.nodes.len(), "free slot out of range");
            assert!(!seen[f], "free slot {} is still in the list", f);
            assert!(!free_seen[f], "free slot {} listed twice", f);
            free_seen[f] = true;
        }
        for (i, s) in seen.iter().enumerate() {
            if !s {
                assert!(free_seen[i], "slot {} is neither in the list nor free", i);
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Iterator over cache entries, most recently used first.
pub struct LruIter<'a, K, V> {
    cache: &'a LruCache<K, V>,
    next: Option<usize>,
}

impl<'a, K, V> Iterator for LruIter<'a, K, V> {
    type Item = (&'a K, &'a V);

    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<(&'a K, &'a V)> {
        let idx = self.next?;
        let node = self.cache.node(idx);
        self.next = node.next;
        Some((&node.key, &node.value))
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Deterministic PRNG (SplitMix64) for the benchmark workload
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// SplitMix64: small, fast, deterministic. The same seed always gives
/// the same sequence, so the benchmark is reproducible.
struct SplitMix64(u64);

impl SplitMix64 {
    fn new(seed: u64) -> SplitMix64 {
        SplitMix64(seed)
    }

    fn next_u64(&mut self) -> u64 {
        self.0 = self.0.wrapping_add(0x9E37_79B9_7F4A_7C15);
        let mut z = self.0;
        z = (z ^ (z >> 30)).wrapping_mul(0xBF58_476D_1CE4_E5B9);
        z = (z ^ (z >> 27)).wrapping_mul(0x94D0_49BB_1331_11EB);
        z ^ (z >> 31)
    }

    fn next_below(&mut self, n: u64) -> u64 {
        self.next_u64() % n
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Benchmark helpers
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Format a `Duration` as a human-readable string.
fn fmt_duration(d: Duration) -> String {
    let nanos = d.as_nanos();
    if nanos < 1_000 {
        format!("{nanos} ns")
    } else if nanos < 1_000_000 {
        format!("{:.1} us", nanos as f64 / 1_000.0)
    } else if nanos < 1_000_000_000 {
        format!("{:.1} ms", nanos as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
    } else {
        format!("{:.2} s", nanos as f64 / 1_000_000_000.0)
    }
}

/// Run `op` `n` times and return the total elapsed time.
fn bench<F: FnMut()>(n: u64, mut op: F) -> Duration {
    let start = Instant::now();
    for _ in 0..n {
        op();
    }
    start.elapsed()
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Demonstration / entry point
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

fn main() {
    // --- Basic put / get / eviction order ----------------------------------
    let mut cache: LruCache<&str, i32> = LruCache::new(3);
    assert_eq!(cache.put("a", 1), None);
    assert_eq!(cache.put("b", 2), None);
    assert_eq!(cache.put("c", 3), None);
    assert_eq!(cache.len(), 3);
    cache.check_invariants();
    // MRU-first order right after insertion: c, b, a.
    assert_eq!(
        cache.keys().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        vec!["c", "b", "a"]
    );
    assert_eq!(cache.mru_entry(), Some((&"c", &3)));
    assert_eq!(cache.lru_entry(), Some((&"a", &1)));

    // Accessing "a" promotes it to MRU; the order becomes a, c, b.
    assert_eq!(cache.get(&"a"), Some(&1));
    assert_eq!(
        cache.keys().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        vec!["a", "c", "b"]
    );
    cache.check_invariants();

    // Inserting "d" evicts the LRU entry, "b".
    let evicted = cache.put("d", 4);
    assert_eq!(evicted, Some(("b", 2)));
    assert_eq!(cache.len(), 3);
    assert!(!cache.contains(&"b"));
    assert_eq!(cache.get(&"b"), None); // a miss, recorded in stats
    assert_eq!(
        cache.keys().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        vec!["d", "a", "c"]
    );
    cache.check_invariants();

    // --- Updates promote and do not evict ------------------------------------
    assert_eq!(cache.put("c", 30), None);
    assert_eq!(cache.peek(&"c"), Some(&30));
    assert_eq!(
        cache.keys().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        vec!["c", "d", "a"]
    );
    assert_eq!(cache.len(), 3);
    cache.check_invariants();

    // --- peek does not promote, get does ---------------------------------------
    assert_eq!(cache.peek(&"a"), Some(&1));
    assert_eq!(
        cache.keys().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        vec!["c", "d", "a"],
        "peek must not reorder"
    );
    assert_eq!(cache.get(&"a"), Some(&1));
    assert_eq!(
        cache.keys().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        vec!["a", "c", "d"]
    );

    // --- remove / evict_tail ------------------------------------------------------
    assert_eq!(cache.remove(&"c"), Some(30));
    assert_eq!(cache.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(cache.remove(&"c"), None);
    assert_eq!(
        cache.keys().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        vec!["a", "d"]
    );
    let tail = cache.evict_tail();
    assert_eq!(tail, Some(("d", 4)), "tail is the LRU entry");
    assert_eq!(cache.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(cache.evict_tail(), Some(("a", 1)));
    assert!(cache.is_empty());
    assert_eq!(cache.evict_tail(), None);
    cache.check_invariants();

    // --- Statistics ------------------------------------------------------------------
    // Hits: get("a") twice. Misses: get("b") after eviction.
    // Evictions: "b" on insert of "d", plus "d" and "a" via the two
    // explicit evict_tail calls in the section above.
    let stats = cache.stats();
    assert_eq!(stats.hits, 2, "two successful gets of a");
    assert_eq!(stats.misses, 1, "one get of evicted b");
    assert_eq!(stats.evictions, 3, "b by put, d and a by evict_tail");
    assert_eq!(stats.insertions, 4);
    assert_eq!(stats.updates, 1);
    assert!((stats.hit_rate().unwrap() - 2.0 / 3.0).abs() < 1e-12);

    // --- get_or_insert_with ------------------------------------------------------------
    let mut nums: LruCache<u32, String> = LruCache::new(2);
    assert_eq!(nums.get_or_insert_with(7, || "seven".to_string()), "seven");
    assert_eq!(nums.get_or_insert_with(7, || "SEVEN".to_string()), "seven");
    assert_eq!(nums.len(), 1);

    // --- resize ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
    let mut sized: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(5);
    for i in 0..5 {
        sized.put(i, i * 10);
    }
    let evicted = sized.resize(2);
    assert_eq!(evicted, vec![(0, 0), (1, 10), (2, 20)]);
    assert_eq!(sized.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(sized.keys().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![4, 3]);
    sized.check_invariants();
    assert!(sized.resize(10).is_empty());
    assert_eq!(sized.capacity(), 10);

    // --- capacity 0 ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
    let mut zero: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(0);
    assert_eq!(zero.put(1, 1), Some((1, 1)));
    assert_eq!(zero.len(), 0);
    assert_eq!(zero.get(&1), None);
    assert_eq!(zero.stats().evictions, 1);

    // --- clear -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    sized.clear();
    assert!(sized.is_empty());
    assert_eq!(sized.stats(), Stats::default());
    sized.check_invariants();

    // --- Slot reuse: the node vector must not grow unboundedly under churn ---------------------------
    let mut churn: LruCache<u32, u32> = LruCache::new(4);
    for i in 0..10_000 {
        churn.put(i % 8, i);
        churn.check_invariants();
    }
    assert_eq!(churn.len(), 4);
    // Only a handful of slots are ever needed: capacity 4 plus the
    // free-list recycling means the vector stays small.
    assert!(churn.slot_count() <= 8, "slots: {}", churn.slot_count());

    println!("lru_cache: correctness assertions passed");
    println!();

    // --- Benchmark -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    run_benchmark();
}

/// Benchmark: a deterministic workload over a large key space.
/// Prints timings and the hit rate; asserts the hit rate is in the
/// range the workload design predicts.
fn run_benchmark() {
    const CAPACITY: usize = 1_000;
    const KEY_SPACE: u64 = 10_000;
    const OPS: u64 = 200_000;

    // Warm-up: fill the cache.
    let mut cache: LruCache<u64, u64> = LruCache::new(CAPACITY);
    for k in 0..CAPACITY as u64 {
        cache.put(k, k * 2);
    }

    // Workload: 80% of accesses hit a "hot" set of 200 keys, 20% hit
    // the cold remainder. The hot set fits in the cache, so the
    // expected hit rate is close to 80% (plus whatever cold keys
    // happen to still be cached).
    let mut rng = SplitMix64::new(0x1234_5EED_1BAD);
    let start = Instant::now();
    for _ in 0..OPS {
        let roll = rng.next_below(100);
        let key = if roll < 80 {
            rng.next_below(200) // hot
        } else {
            200 + rng.next_below(KEY_SPACE - 200) // cold
        };
        match cache.get(&key) {
            Some(_) => {}
            None => {
                cache.put(key, key * 2);
            }
        }
    }
    let elapsed = start.elapsed();
    let stats = cache.stats();
    let hit_rate = stats.hit_rate().unwrap();
    println!("benchmark: {OPS} mixed get/put ops on capacity {CAPACITY}");
    println!("  elapsed: {}", fmt_duration(elapsed));
    println!(
        "  {:.1} ns/op",
        elapsed.as_nanos() as f64 / OPS as f64
    );
    println!(
        "  hits: {}, misses: {}, evictions: {}, hit rate: {:.1}%",
        stats.hits,
        stats.misses,
        stats.evictions,
        hit_rate * 100.0
    );
    assert!(
        (0.70..0.95).contains(&hit_rate),
        "hit rate {:.3} outside the expected 0.70..0.95 band",
        hit_rate
    );
    assert_eq!(cache.len(), CAPACITY);
    cache.check_invariants();

    // Pure-put throughput: inserting fresh keys (every put evicts).
    let mut cache2: LruCache<u64, u64> = LruCache::new(CAPACITY);
    let put_time = bench(OPS, || {
        // `bench` needs a fresh key each time; use a static-ish
        // counter via the closure's captured state instead.
        cache2.put(0, 0);
    });
    // The closure above re-inserts the same key (an update, not an
    // eviction), so measure a true evicting put stream separately:
    let mut cache3: LruCache<u64, u64> = LruCache::new(CAPACITY);
    let mut key = 0u64;
    let evict_time = bench(OPS, || {
        cache3.put(key, key);
        key += 1;
    });
    println!(
        "benchmark: {OPS} repeated updates in {} ({:.1} ns/op)",
        fmt_duration(put_time),
        put_time.as_nanos() as f64 / OPS as f64
    );
    println!(
        "benchmark: {OPS} evicting puts in {} ({:.1} ns/op)",
        fmt_duration(evict_time),
        evict_time.as_nanos() as f64 / OPS as f64
    );
    assert_eq!(cache3.len(), CAPACITY);
    cache3.check_invariants();

    // Pure-get throughput on a hot cache (all hits).
    let mut cache4: LruCache<u64, u64> = LruCache::new(CAPACITY);
    for k in 0..CAPACITY as u64 {
        cache4.put(k, k);
    }
    let mut rng2 = SplitMix64::new(99);
    let get_time = bench(OPS, || {
        let k = rng2.next_below(CAPACITY as u64);
        let _ = cache4.get(&k);
    });
    println!(
        "benchmark: {OPS} get hits in {} ({:.1} ns/op)",
        fmt_duration(get_time),
        get_time.as_nanos() as f64 / OPS as f64
    );
    assert_eq!(cache4.stats().hits, OPS);

    println!("lru_cache: benchmark complete");
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Unit tests
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn eviction_order_is_lru() {
        let mut c: LruCache<i32, &str> = LruCache::new(2);
        c.put(1, "one");
        c.put(2, "two");
        c.get(&1); // 1 becomes MRU; 2 is now LRU
        assert_eq!(c.put(3, "three"), Some((2, "two")));
        assert_eq!(c.keys().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec![3, 1]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn update_promotes_without_eviction() {
        let mut c: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(2);
        c.put(1, 10);
        c.put(2, 20);
        assert_eq!(c.put(1, 11), None);
        assert_eq!(c.put(3, 30), Some((2, 20)));
        assert_eq!(c.peek(&1), Some(&11));
    }

    #[test]
    fn peek_does_not_promote() {
        let mut c: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(2);
        c.put(1, 10);
        c.put(2, 20);
        assert_eq!(c.peek(&1), Some(&10));
        assert_eq!(c.put(3, 30), Some((1, 10)), "1 was still LRU");
    }

    #[test]
    fn get_mut_promotes_and_mutates() {
        let mut c: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(2);
        c.put(1, 10);
        c.put(2, 20);
        *c.get_mut(&1).unwrap() += 5;
        assert_eq!(c.peek(&1), Some(&15));
        assert_eq!(c.put(3, 30), Some((2, 20)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn remove_frees_and_reuses_slots() {
        let mut c: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(3);
        for i in 0..3 {
            c.put(i, i);
        }
        assert_eq!(c.remove(&1), Some(1));
        assert_eq!(c.remove(&1), None);
        c.put(9, 90);
        assert_eq!(c.len(), 3);
        c.check_invariants();
    }

    #[test]
    fn zero_capacity_never_stores() {
        let mut c: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(0);
        assert_eq!(c.put(1, 1), Some((1, 1)));
        assert!(c.is_empty());
        assert_eq!(c.get(&1), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn capacity_one_keeps_only_latest() {
        let mut c: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(1);
        assert_eq!(c.put(1, 1), None);
        assert_eq!(c.put(2, 2), Some((1, 1)));
        assert_eq!(c.put(2, 22), None);
        assert_eq!(c.peek(&2), Some(&22));
        assert_eq!(c.len(), 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn resize_shrinks_and_grows() {
        let mut c: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(4);
        for i in 0..4 {
            c.put(i, i);
        }
        let evicted = c.resize(1);
        assert_eq!(evicted, vec![(0, 0), (1, 1), (2, 2)]);
        assert_eq!(c.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(c.resize(10).len(), 0);
        c.check_invariants();
    }

    #[test]
    fn iteration_is_mru_first() {
        let mut c: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(4);
        for i in 0..4 {
            c.put(i, i * 10);
        }
        c.get(&1);
        c.get(&3);
        assert_eq!(
            c.iter().map(|(k, _)| *k).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
            vec![3, 1, 2, 0]
        );
        assert_eq!(
            c.values().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
            vec![30, 10, 20, 0]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn stats_track_hits_and_misses() {
        let mut c: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(2);
        c.put(1, 1);
        assert_eq!(c.get(&1), Some(&1));
        assert_eq!(c.get(&2), None);
        assert_eq!(c.get(&2), None);
        let s = c.stats();
        assert_eq!(s.hits, 1);
        assert_eq!(s.misses, 2);
        assert!((s.hit_rate().unwrap() - 1.0 / 3.0).abs() < 1e-12);
    }

    #[test]
    fn invariants_hold_under_churn() {
        let mut c: LruCache<u32, u32> = LruCache::new(16);
        let mut rng = SplitMix64::new(7);
        for i in 0..50_000 {
            let op = rng.next_below(10);
            let key = rng.next_below(40) as u32;
            match op {
                0..=5 => {
                    c.put(key, i as u32);
                }
                6..=8 => {
                    let _ = c.get(&key);
                }
                _ => {
                    c.remove(&key);
                }
            }
            if i % 1000 == 0 {
                c.check_invariants();
            }
        }
        c.check_invariants();
        assert!(c.len() <= 16);
    }

    #[test]
    fn string_keys_and_values() {
        let mut c: LruCache<String, Vec<i32>> = LruCache::new(2);
        c.put("a".to_string(), vec![1, 2]);
        c.put("b".to_string(), vec![3]);
        assert_eq!(c.get(&"a".to_string()), Some(&vec![1, 2]));
        assert_eq!(c.put("c".to_string(), vec![4]), Some(("b".to_string(), vec![3])));
    }

    #[test]
    fn clear_resets_everything() {
        let mut c: LruCache<i32, i32> = LruCache::new(3);
        c.put(1, 1);
        c.put(2, 2);
        c.clear();
        assert!(c.is_empty());
        assert_eq!(c.stats(), Stats::default());
        assert_eq!(c.lru_entry(), None);
        assert_eq!(c.mru_entry(), None);
        c.put(3, 3);
        assert_eq!(c.len(), 1);
        c.check_invariants();
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/mini_vm.rs ---
//! mini_vm.rs — a small bytecode virtual machine with a compiler for a
//! tiny source language. Standard library only.
//!
//! The pipeline has four stages, each its own small module of code:
//!
//!   1. Lexer — turns source text into tokens (numbers, strings,
//!      identifiers, keywords, operators, `//` comments).
//!   2. Parser — a recursive-descent parser that builds an AST. The
//!      language has `let` bindings, assignment, `if`/`else`, `while`,
//!      `fn` declarations with parameters and `return`, `print`
//!      statements, blocks, arithmetic, comparison and short-circuit
//!      logic operators, function calls and string concatenation.
//!   3. Compiler — walks the AST and emits bytecode into `Chunk`s: one
//!      chunk per function (chunk 0 is the top-level program). Locals
//!      live in stack slots, top-level bindings live in a globals table,
//!      jumps are emitted as placeholders and patched once the target
//!      is known. Each `fn` declaration compiles its body into a new
//!      chunk and stores a `Function` value under the function's name.
//!   4. VM — a stack machine with call frames. Values are tagged
//!      (`Nil`, `Bool`, `Int`, `Float`, `Str`, `Function`, `Builtin`),
//!      arithmetic coerces integers and floats, and the builtins `len`,
//!      `str` and `typeof` are ordinary values held in the globals
//!      table. `print` output is captured in `VM::output` so programs
//!      can be run and their output asserted by the host.
//!
//! Example program:
//!
//! ```text
//! let limit = 10;
//! fn fib(n) {
//!     if n < 2 { return n; }
//!     return fib(n - 1) + fib(n - 2);
//! }
//! let i = 0;
//! while i < limit {
//!     print fib(i);
//!     i = i + 1;
//! }
//! ```
//!
//! With no arguments the program runs a demonstration: several source
//! programs are compiled and executed through the VM and their captured
//! output is asserted, and a handful of programs that must fail (an
//! undefined variable, a wrong argument count, division by zero) are
//! checked to fail with the right error. A successful run proves the
//! whole pipeline works end to end.

use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::fmt;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Values
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A value in the VM. Numbers come in two flavours: `Int` (exact integer
/// arithmetic) and `Float`. Mixed arithmetic coerces to `Float`, so
/// `7 / 2` is `3` but `7.0 / 2` is `3.5`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum Value {
    Nil,
    Bool(bool),
    Int(i64),
    Float(f64),
    Str(String),
    /// Index into the VM's function (chunk) table.
    Function(usize),
    /// Index into the VM's builtins table.
    Builtin(usize),
}

impl Value {
    /// Truthiness, as in most dynamic languages: only `false` and `nil`
    /// are falsy; everything else (including 0 and "") is truthy.
    pub fn is_truthy(&self) -> bool {
        !matches!(self, Value::Nil | Value::Bool(false))
    }

    /// The value's type name, as reported by the `typeof` builtin.
    pub fn type_name(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Value::Nil => "nil",
            Value::Bool(_) => "bool",
            Value::Int(_) => "int",
            Value::Float(_) => "float",
            Value::Str(_) => "string",
            Value::Function(_) => "function",
            Value::Builtin(_) => "builtin",
        }
    }

    /// Numeric coercion: the value as an `f64`, when it is a number.
    pub fn as_f64(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        match self {
            Value::Int(n) => Some(*n as f64),
            Value::Float(f) => Some(*f),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Display form used by `print` and by error messages.
    pub fn display(&self) -> String {
        match self {
            Value::Nil => "nil".to_string(),
            Value::Bool(b) => b.to_string(),
            Value::Int(n) => n.to_string(),
            Value::Float(f) => {
                if f.fract() == 0.0 && f.is_finite() && f.abs() < 9_007_199_254_740_992.0 {
                    format!("{}", *f as i64)
                } else {
                    format!("{}", f)
                }
            }
            Value::Str(s) => s.clone(),
            Value::Function(idx) => format!("<fn#{}>", idx),
            Value::Builtin(idx) => format!("<builtin#{}>", idx),
        }
    }
}

/// Numeric equality with cross-type coercion: `Int(1)` equals
/// `Float(1.0)`. All other comparisons are by value and type.
fn values_equal(a: &Value, b: &Value) -> bool {
    match (a, b) {
        (Value::Int(x), Value::Int(y)) => x == y,
        (Value::Float(x), Value::Float(y)) => x == y,
        (Value::Int(x), Value::Float(y)) | (Value::Float(y), Value::Int(x)) => {
            (*x as f64) == *y
        }
        _ => a == b,
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Lexer
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// The kinds of token the lexer produces.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum TokenKind {
    // Literals.
    Number(Value), // Int or Float
    Text(String),
    Ident(String),
    // Keywords.
    Let,
    Fn,
    Return,
    If,
    Else,
    While,
    Print,
    True,
    False,
    Nil,
    // Operators and punctuation.
    Plus,
    Minus,
    Star,
    Slash,
    Percent,
    Bang,
    Equal,
    EqualEqual,
    BangEqual,
    Less,
    LessEqual,
    Greater,
    GreaterEqual,
    AndAnd,
    OrOr,
    LeftParen,
    RightParen,
    LeftBrace,
    RightBrace,
    Comma,
    Semicolon,
    Eof,
}

/// One token: its kind, the exact source text, and its line number.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Token {
    pub kind: TokenKind,
    pub lexeme: String,
    pub line: usize,
}

/// A lexer failure: what went wrong and on which line.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct LexError {
    pub line: usize,
    pub message: String,
}

impl fmt::Display for LexError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "line {}: {}", self.line, self.message)
    }
}

/// Turn source text into a token vector. The final token is always `Eof`.
pub fn lex(source: &str) -> Result<Vec<Token>, LexError> {
    let chars: Vec<char> = source.chars().collect();
    let mut tokens = Vec::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut line = 1;

    let error = |line: usize, message: &str| LexError {
        line,
        message: message.to_string(),
    };

    while i < chars.len() {
        let c = chars[i];
        match c {
            // Whitespace and newlines.
            ' ' | '\t' | '\r' => i += 1,
            '\n' => {
                line += 1;
                i += 1;
            }
            // Line comments: `//` runs to the end of the line.
            '/' if chars.get(i + 1) == Some(&'/') => {
                while i < chars.len() && chars[i] != '\n' {
                    i += 1;
                }
            }
            // Single-character tokens.
            '(' => {
                tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::LeftParen, lexeme: "(".into(), line });
                i += 1;
            }
            ')' => {
                tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::RightParen, lexeme: ")".into(), line });
                i += 1;
            }
            '{' => {
                tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::LeftBrace, lexeme: "{".into(), line });
                i += 1;
            }
            '}' => {
                tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::RightBrace, lexeme: "}".into(), line });
                i += 1;
            }
            ',' => {
                tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::Comma, lexeme: ",".into(), line });
                i += 1;
            }
            ';' => {
                tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::Semicolon, lexeme: ";".into(), line });
                i += 1;
            }
            '+' => {
                tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::Plus, lexeme: "+".into(), line });
                i += 1;
            }
            '-' => {
                tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::Minus, lexeme: "-".into(), line });
                i += 1;
            }
            '*' => {
                tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::Star, lexeme: "*".into(), line });
                i += 1;
            }
            '/' => {
                tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::Slash, lexeme: "/".into(), line });
                i += 1;
            }
            '%' => {
                tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::Percent, lexeme: "%".into(), line });
                i += 1;
            }
            // Two-character operators.
            '=' => {
                if chars.get(i + 1) == Some(&'=') {
                    tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::EqualEqual, lexeme: "==".into(), line });
                    i += 2;
                } else {
                    tokens.push(Token { kind: TokenKind::Equal, lexeme: "=".into(), line });
                    i += 1;
                }
            }
            '!' => {
