    while let Some(c) = chars.next() {
        if c == '\\' {
            match chars.next() {
                Some('n') => out.push('\n'),
                Some('t') => out.push('\t'),
                Some('\\') => out.push('\\'),
                Some(other) => {
                    // Unknown escape: keep it verbatim.
                    out.push('\\');
                    out.push(other);
                }
                None => out.push('\\'),
            }
        } else {
            out.push(c);
        }
    }
    Cow::Owned(out)
}

/// Trim redundant whitespace and collapse runs of spaces, again with
/// `Cow`: unchanged input stays borrowed.
pub fn collapse_spaces(input: &str) -> Cow<'_, str> {
    let needs_work = input.starts_with(' ')
        || input.ends_with(' ')
        || input.contains("  ");
    if !needs_work {
        return Cow::Borrowed(input);
    }
    let collapsed: String = input
        .split_whitespace()
        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
        .join(" ");
    Cow::Owned(collapsed)
}

/// An owned, normalised record. This is what leaves the borrowing world:
/// the pipeline *moves* each `LogLine` in, copies the slices it still
/// needs into owned `String`s, and drops the borrow of the input text.
/// After this stage the original input buffer may be freed.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct OwnedRecord {
    pub timestamp: String,
    pub level: Level,
    pub component: String,
    /// Normalised message. Kept as `Cow<'static, str>` in spirit -- in
    /// practice the record owns the string, but the normaliser's Cow
    /// decided whether an allocation was needed at all.
    pub message: String,
    /// Fields are stored sorted by key so two records with the same
    /// fields in different orders compare equal.
    pub fields: Vec<(String, String)>,
}

impl OwnedRecord {
    /// Consume a borrowed line and produce an owned record. The line is
    /// moved in (by value) -- the caller gives up the borrow.
    pub fn from_line(line: LogLine<'_>) -> OwnedRecord {
        // Two Cow stages chained: each borrows when it can. The
        // intermediate must be bound to a name, or the temporary is
        // dropped at the end of the statement while still borrowed.
        let unescaped = unescape_message(line.message);
        let message = collapse_spaces(&unescaped);
        let mut fields: Vec<(String, String)> = line
            .fields
            .iter()
            .map(|f| (f.key.to_string(), f.value.to_string()))
            .collect();
        fields.sort();
        OwnedRecord {
            timestamp: line.timestamp.to_string(),
            level: line.level,
            component: line.component.to_string(),
            message: message.into_owned(),
            fields,
        }
    }

    pub fn field_value(&self, key: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        self.fields
            .iter()
            .find(|(k, _)| k == key)
            .map(|(_, v)| v.as_str())
    }

    /// Render the record back into log format (lossy: field order is
    /// the sorted order, not the original one).
    pub fn render(&self) -> String {
        let mut out = format!(
            "{} {} [{}] {}",
            self.timestamp, self.level, self.component, self.message
        );
        for (k, v) in &self.fields {
            out.push_str(&format!(" {k}={v}"));
        }
        out
    }
}

/// Move a whole vector of borrowed lines into owned records, consuming
/// the vector. `into_iter` moves each line out; nothing is cloned.
pub fn own_all(lines: Vec<LogLine<'_>>) -> Vec<OwnedRecord> {
    lines.into_iter().map(OwnedRecord::from_line).collect()
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Stage 4 (consume): aggregation
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Per-component statistics accumulated from owned records.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ComponentStats {
    pub total: usize,
    pub by_level: HashMap<Level, usize>,
    /// Sum of the numeric `ms` field, when present, and how many lines
    /// contributed to it.
    pub ms_sum: i64,
    pub ms_count: usize,
    /// Distinct users seen, in first-seen order.
    pub users: Vec<String>,
}

impl ComponentStats {
    pub fn record(&mut self, rec: &OwnedRecord) {
        self.total += 1;
        *self.by_level.entry(rec.level).or_insert(0) += 1;
        if let Some(ms) = rec.field_value("ms").and_then(|v| v.parse::<i64>().ok()) {
            self.ms_sum += ms;
            self.ms_count += 1;
        }
        if let Some(user) = rec.field_value("user") {
            if !self.users.iter().any(|u| u == user) {
                self.users.push(user.to_string());
            }
        }
    }

    pub fn avg_ms(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        if self.ms_count == 0 {
            None
        } else {
            Some(self.ms_sum as f64 / self.ms_count as f64)
        }
    }

    pub fn errors(&self) -> usize {
        self.by_level.get(&Level::Error).copied().unwrap_or(0)
    }
}

/// The summary produced by consuming a stream of owned records.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct Summary {
    pub total: usize,
    pub by_level: HashMap<Level, usize>,
    pub by_component: HashMap<String, ComponentStats>,
    /// The single worst (highest-severity, then earliest) record, kept
    /// by value -- the aggregator *takes ownership* of it.
    pub worst: Option<OwnedRecord>,
}

impl Summary {
    /// Consume one record. `mut self` by reference; the record is moved in.
    pub fn ingest(&mut self, rec: OwnedRecord) {
        self.total += 1;
        *self.by_level.entry(rec.level).or_insert(0) += 1;
        let stats = self
            .by_component
            .entry(rec.component.clone())
            .or_default();
        stats.record(&rec);

        let worse = match &self.worst {
            None => true,
            Some(w) => rec.level > w.level,
        };
        if worse {
            self.worst = Some(rec);
        }
    }

    /// Consume an entire iterator of records. The iterator is moved in
    /// and drained; after this call the caller owns nothing of it.
    pub fn ingest_all<I: IntoIterator<Item = OwnedRecord>>(&mut self, recs: I) {
        for rec in recs {
            self.ingest(rec);
        }
    }

    pub fn errors(&self) -> usize {
        self.by_level.get(&Level::Error).copied().unwrap_or(0)
    }

    pub fn warnings(&self) -> usize {
        self.by_level.get(&Level::Warn).copied().unwrap_or(0)
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Stage 5 (borrowed): reporting
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Render a summary as a human-readable report. Borrows the summary --
/// rendering never needs to own it.
pub fn render_report(summary: &Summary) -> String {
    let mut out = String::new();
    out.push_str(&format!("total lines: {}\n", summary.total));
    out.push_str(&format!("errors:      {}\n", summary.errors()));
    out.push_str(&format!("warnings:    {}\n", summary.warnings()));
    let mut components: Vec<&String> = summary.by_component.keys().collect();
    components.sort();
    for name in components {
        let stats = &summary.by_component[name];
        let avg = match stats.avg_ms() {
            Some(v) => format!("{v:.1} ms avg"),
            None => "no ms data".to_string(),
        };
        out.push_str(&format!(
            "[{name}] total={} errors={} {avg} users={:?}\n",
            stats.total,
            stats.errors(),
            stats.users
        ));
    }
    if let Some(worst) = &summary.worst {
        out.push_str(&format!("worst: {}\n", worst.render()));
    }
    out
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// A reusable pipeline object: stages chained by ownership
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A small builder-style pipeline. `Pipeline` owns the parsed lines
/// after `parse`, and each method consumes `self` and returns a new
/// state, so a stage cannot accidentally be run twice on the same data.
pub struct Pipeline<'a> {
    lines: Vec<LogLine<'a>>,
}

impl<'a> Pipeline<'a> {
    pub fn parse(source: &'a str) -> Result<Pipeline<'a>, ParseError> {
        let mut parser = LogParser::new(source);
        let lines = parser.parse_all()?;
        Ok(Pipeline { lines })
    }

    /// Keep only lines satisfying `pred`. Borrows each line to test it,
    /// then moves the survivors into a fresh vector (`retain` would do
    /// the same in place; `into_iter().filter().collect()` shows the
    /// move explicitly).
    pub fn filter<F>(self, pred: F) -> Pipeline<'a>
    where
        F: Fn(&LogLine<'a>) -> bool,
    {
        let lines = self
            .lines
            .into_iter()
            .filter(|l| pred(l))
            .collect();
        Pipeline { lines }
    }

    /// Number of lines currently held.
    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.lines.len()
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.lines.is_empty()
    }

    /// Borrow the lines for inspection without giving them up.
    pub fn lines(&self) -> &[LogLine<'a>] {
        &self.lines
    }

    /// Terminal stage: move the lines into owned records.
    pub fn into_records(self) -> Vec<OwnedRecord> {
        own_all(self.lines)
    }

    /// Terminal stage: move the lines straight into a summary.
    pub fn into_summary(self) -> Summary {
        let mut summary = Summary::default();
        summary.ingest_all(self.into_records());
        summary
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Demonstration / entry point
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

const SAMPLE_LOG: &str = "\
# Sample application log
2026-11-05T10:15:00Z INFO  [auth] user=alice action=login ok=true
2026-11-05T10:15:01Z WARN  [db]   slow query detected ms=812
2026-11-05T10:15:02Z ERROR [auth] user=bob action=login ok=false
2026-11-05T10:15:03Z INFO  [auth] user=alice action=logout ok=true
2026-11-05T10:15:04Z DEBUG [db]   cache hit key=users:alice
2026-11-05T10:15:05Z WARN  [db]   slow query detected ms=1204
2026-11-05T10:15:06Z ERROR [db]   connection lost ms=0
2026-11-05T10:15:07Z INFO  [web]  request served path=/index ms=42
";

fn main() {
    // --- Stage 1: parse (borrowed) -------------------------------------
    let mut parser = LogParser::new(SAMPLE_LOG);
    let lines = parser.parse_all().expect("sample log must parse");
    assert_eq!(lines.len(), 8);
    assert_eq!(parser.lines_consumed(), 9); // 8 lines + 1 comment line

    // The lines borrow SAMPLE_LOG; the borrow is visible in the types:
    // `lines: Vec<LogLine<'_>>` where `'_` is tied to SAMPLE_LOG.
    let first = &lines[0];
    assert_eq!(first.timestamp, "2026-11-05T10:15:00Z");
    assert_eq!(first.level, Level::Info);
    assert_eq!(first.component, "auth");
    // The first line's message is empty: every token after the component
    // is a key=value field, so there is no free-text message.
    assert_eq!(first.message, "");
    // Check a real message-bearing line instead.
    let slow = &lines[1];
    assert_eq!(slow.message, "slow query detected");
    assert_eq!(slow.field_value("ms"), Some("812"));
    assert_eq!(slow.field("ms").unwrap().as_i64(), Some(812));

    // --- Borrow rules, demonstrated --------------------------------------
    // Many shared borrows at once are fine:
    let a = &lines[0];
    let b = &lines[0];
    assert_eq!(a, b);
    // A mutable borrow is exclusive; it ends when the borrow is last used.
    let mut owned_lines = lines;
    let m = &mut owned_lines[2];
    m.fields.push(Field::new("retry", "1"));
    assert_eq!(owned_lines[2].field_value("retry"), Some("1"));

    // --- Stage 2: filter (borrowed) --------------------------------------
    let auth_errors = component_at_least(&owned_lines, "auth", Level::Error);
    assert_eq!(auth_errors.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(auth_errors[0].field_value("user"), Some("bob"));

    let db_lines = filter_lines(&owned_lines, |l| l.component == "db");
    assert_eq!(db_lines.len(), 4);

    // --- Cow: borrowed when unchanged, owned when changed ---------------
    let plain = unescape_message("no escapes here");
    assert!(matches!(plain, Cow::Borrowed(_)));
    let escaped = unescape_message("first\\nsecond\\tthird\\\\end");
    assert!(matches!(escaped, Cow::Owned(_)));
    assert_eq!(escaped.as_ref(), "first\nsecond\tthird\\end");
    let spaced = collapse_spaces("  too   much   space  ");
    assert_eq!(spaced.as_ref(), "too much space");
    let already_tidy = collapse_spaces("tidy");
    assert!(matches!(already_tidy, Cow::Borrowed(_)));

    // --- Stage 3: move into owned records --------------------------------
    // `own_all` consumes the vector; `owned_lines` cannot be used after.
    let records = own_all(owned_lines);
    assert_eq!(records.len(), 8);
    // The records no longer borrow SAMPLE_LOG at all: they own Strings.
    let rec = &records[1];
    assert_eq!(rec.component, "db");
    assert_eq!(rec.message, "slow query detected");
    assert_eq!(rec.field_value("ms"), Some("812"));
    // Fields are sorted by key in the owned record.
    assert!(records[0].fields.windows(2).all(|w| w[0].0 <= w[1].0));

    // Cloning is explicit and visible: a clone is a deep copy.
    let cloned = records[0].clone();
    assert_eq!(cloned, records[0]);

    // --- Stage 4: consume into a summary ---------------------------------
    let mut summary = Summary::default();
    // `into_iter` moves records out one by one; `records` is gone after.
    summary.ingest_all(records);
    assert_eq!(summary.total, 8);
    assert_eq!(summary.errors(), 2);
    assert_eq!(summary.warnings(), 2);
    let db = &summary.by_component["db"];
    assert_eq!(db.total, 4);
    assert_eq!(db.errors(), 1);
    assert_eq!(db.ms_count, 3);
    assert!((db.avg_ms().unwrap() - 672.0).abs() < 1e-9); // (812+1204+0)/3
    let auth = &summary.by_component["auth"];
    assert_eq!(auth.users, vec!["alice".to_string(), "bob".to_string()]);
    assert_eq!(summary.worst.as_ref().unwrap().level, Level::Error);

    // --- Stage 5: report (borrowed) ---------------------------------------
    let report = render_report(&summary);
    println!("{report}");
    assert!(report.contains("total lines: 8"));
    assert!(report.contains("[db]"));
    assert!(report.contains("worst:"));

    // --- The builder pipeline, end to end ---------------------------------
    let summary2 = Pipeline::parse(SAMPLE_LOG)
        .expect("pipeline parse")
        .filter(|l| l.level >= Level::Warn)
        .into_summary();
    assert_eq!(summary2.total, 4); // 2 WARN + 2 ERROR
    assert_eq!(summary2.errors(), 2);
    assert_eq!(summary2.warnings(), 2);

    let pipeline = Pipeline::parse(SAMPLE_LOG).expect("pipeline parse");
    assert_eq!(pipeline.len(), 8);
    assert!(!pipeline.is_empty());
    let first_borrowed = &pipeline.lines()[0];
    assert_eq!(first_borrowed.component, "auth");

    // --- Lenient parsing tolerates bad lines ------------------------------
    let messy = "2026-11-05T10:15:00Z INFO [ok] fine\nBAD LINE HERE\n2026-11-05T10:15:01Z INFO [ok] also fine\n";
    let mut p = LogParser::new(messy);
    let (good, errors) = p.parse_lenient();
    assert_eq!(good.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(errors.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(errors[0].line_no, 2);
    println!("lenient parse: {} good, {} errors", good.len(), errors.len());

    println!("ownership_pipeline: all assertions passed");
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Unit tests
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    const ONE_LINE: &str = "2026-11-05T10:00:00Z WARN [svc] something happened ms=99 user=zoe";

    #[test]
    fn parses_a_well_formed_line() {
        let mut p = LogParser::new(ONE_LINE);
        let line = p.next_line().unwrap().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(line.timestamp, "2026-11-05T10:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(line.level, Level::Warn);
        assert_eq!(line.component, "svc");
        assert_eq!(line.message, "something happened");
        assert_eq!(line.field_value("ms"), Some("99"));
        assert_eq!(line.field_value("user"), Some("zoe"));
        assert!(p.next_line().unwrap().is_none());
    }

    #[test]
    fn parser_skips_comments_and_blank_lines() {
        let src = "# c\n\n2026-11-05T10:00:00Z INFO [a] hi\n\n# tail\n";
        let mut p = LogParser::new(src);
        let lines = p.parse_all().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(lines.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(lines[0].message, "hi");
    }

    #[test]
    fn parser_rejects_unknown_level() {
        let mut p = LogParser::new("2026-11-05T10:00:00Z FATAL [a] boom");
        let err = p.next_line().unwrap_err();
        assert_eq!(err.line_no, 1);
        assert!(err.reason.contains("unknown level"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn parser_rejects_unbracketed_component() {
        let mut p = LogParser::new("2026-11-05T10:00:00Z INFO auth boom");
        assert!(p.next_line().is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn parser_rejects_malformed_field() {
        // `=x` has an empty key.
        let mut p = LogParser::new("2026-11-05T10:00:00Z INFO [a] msg =x");
        assert!(p.next_line().is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn unescape_borrows_when_unchanged_and_owns_when_changed() {
        assert!(matches!(unescape_message("plain"), Cow::Borrowed(_)));
        let owned = unescape_message("a\\nb");
        assert!(matches!(owned, Cow::Owned(_)));
        assert_eq!(owned, "a\nb");
        // Trailing backslash is kept verbatim.
        assert_eq!(unescape_message("a\\"), "a\\");
        // Unknown escape is kept verbatim.
        assert_eq!(unescape_message("a\\qb"), "a\\qb");
    }

    #[test]
    fn collapse_spaces_behaviour() {
        assert_eq!(collapse_spaces("a  b   c"), "a b c");
        assert_eq!(collapse_spaces(" already tidy "), "already tidy");
        assert!(matches!(collapse_spaces("tidy"), Cow::Borrowed(_)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn owned_record_sorts_fields_and_renders() {
        let line = LogLine {
            timestamp: "t",
            level: Level::Info,
            component: "c",
            message: "m",
            fields: vec![Field::new("z", "1"), Field::new("a", "2")],
        };
        let rec = OwnedRecord::from_line(line);
        assert_eq!(rec.fields[0].0, "a");
        assert_eq!(rec.render(), "t INFO [c] m a=2 z=1");
    }

    #[test]
    fn summary_counts_and_worst() {
        let mk = |level: Level, comp: &str| OwnedRecord {
            timestamp: "t".into(),
            level,
            component: comp.into(),
            message: "m".into(),
            fields: vec![],
        };
        let mut s = Summary::default();
        s.ingest(mk(Level::Info, "a"));
        s.ingest(mk(Level::Error, "a"));
        s.ingest(mk(Level::Warn, "b"));
        assert_eq!(s.total, 3);
        assert_eq!(s.errors(), 1);
        assert_eq!(s.worst.unwrap().level, Level::Error);
        assert_eq!(s.by_component["a"].total, 2);
        assert_eq!(s.by_component["b"].total, 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn component_stats_avg_ms() {
        let mut stats = ComponentStats::default();
        assert_eq!(stats.avg_ms(), None);
        let mk = |ms: &str| OwnedRecord {
            timestamp: "t".into(),
            level: Level::Info,
            component: "c".into(),
            message: "m".into(),
            fields: vec![("ms".to_string(), ms.to_string())],
        };
        stats.record(&mk("10"));
        stats.record(&mk("20"));
        assert_eq!(stats.avg_ms(), Some(15.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn pipeline_filter_and_summary() {
        let s = Pipeline::parse(SAMPLE_LOG)
            .unwrap()
            .filter(|l| l.component == "web")
            .into_summary();
        assert_eq!(s.total, 1);
        assert_eq!(s.by_component["web"].total, 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn level_ordering() {
        assert!(Level::Error > Level::Warn);
        assert!(Level::Warn > Level::Info);
        assert!(Level::Info > Level::Debug);
        assert_eq!(Level::from_token("INFO"), Some(Level::Info));
        assert_eq!(Level::from_token("nope"), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn field_typed_accessors() {
        let f = Field::new("ms", "42");
        assert_eq!(f.as_i64(), Some(42));
        assert_eq!(Field::new("ok", "true").as_bool(), Some(true));
        assert_eq!(Field::new("ok", "maybe").as_bool(), None);
        assert_eq!(Field::new("ms", "abc").as_i64(), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn lenient_parse_collects_errors() {
        let src = "bad\n2026-11-05T10:00:00Z INFO [a] ok\nbad2\n";
        let mut p = LogParser::new(src);
        let (good, errors) = p.parse_lenient();
        assert_eq!(good.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(errors.len(), 2);
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/producer_consumer.rs ---
// producer_consumer.rs
//
// A producer/consumer pipeline built on the standard library only:
//   * a hand-written bounded channel (`Mutex` + two `Condvar`s) with
//     explicit backpressure: a sender blocks when the buffer is full,
//     a receiver blocks when it is empty, and `close()` lets receivers
//     drain and then exit cleanly;
//   * multiple producers and multiple consumers per stage;
//   * a three-stage text-processing pipeline (generate -> parse ->
//     analyse -> aggregate) with per-stage statistics: items handled,
//     total and average processing time, and the time each stage spent
//     blocked waiting for work or for downstream room;
//   * a backpressure demonstration that measures how a tiny buffer
//     throttles fast producers behind a slow consumer, and a graceful
//     shutdown in which every stage drains before the next one closes.
//
// Everything here is original code written for this demonstration.
// It uses only the Rust standard library and needs no crates.
//
// Run it:  rustc producer_consumer.rs -o producer_consumer && ./producer_consumer
// Tests:    rustc --test producer_consumer.rs -o producer_consumer_test && ./producer_consumer_test

use std::collections::{HashMap, VecDeque};
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, AtomicUsize, Ordering};
use std::sync::{Arc, Condvar, Mutex};
use std::thread::{self, JoinHandle};
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

// ===========================================================================
// 1. A bounded channel with explicit backpressure
// ===========================================================================

/// Error returned by `send` when the channel has been closed.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SendError<T>(pub T);

/// Error returned by `recv` when the channel is closed and drained.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct RecvError;

/// The shared state of a bounded channel. Two condition variables keep
/// the two directions independent: producers wait on `not_full`,
/// consumers wait on `not_empty`, so a wake-up never wakes the wrong
/// side by mistake.
struct ChannelState<T> {
    queue: Mutex<VecDeque<T>>,
    not_empty: Condvar,
    not_full: Condvar,
    capacity: usize,
    closed: AtomicBool,
    /// How many senders are currently blocked inside `send`.
    blocked_senders: AtomicUsize,
    /// How many receivers are currently blocked inside `recv`.
    blocked_receivers: AtomicUsize,
}

/// A multi-producer, multi-consumer bounded channel.
///
/// Backpressure is the point: when the buffer is full, `send` blocks
/// until a consumer makes room, so a fast producer can never run away
/// from a slow consumer and memory stays bounded. When `close` is
/// called, buffered items can still be received, but no new item can
/// be sent and `recv` returns `Err` once the buffer is empty.
pub struct BoundedChannel<T> {
    state: Arc<ChannelState<T>>,
}

impl<T> Clone for BoundedChannel<T> {
    fn clone(&self) -> Self {
        BoundedChannel {
            state: Arc::clone(&self.state),
        }
    }
}

impl<T> BoundedChannel<T> {
    /// Create a channel holding at most `capacity` items. `capacity`
    /// must be at least 1; a zero-capacity (rendezvous) channel is a
    /// different design and is rejected here on purpose.
    pub fn new(capacity: usize) -> Self {
        assert!(capacity >= 1, "bounded channel capacity must be >= 1");
        BoundedChannel {
            state: Arc::new(ChannelState {
                queue: Mutex::new(VecDeque::with_capacity(capacity)),
                not_empty: Condvar::new(),
                not_full: Condvar::new(),
                capacity,
                closed: AtomicBool::new(false),
                blocked_senders: AtomicUsize::new(0),
                blocked_receivers: AtomicUsize::new(0),
            }),
        }
    }

    pub fn capacity(&self) -> usize {
        self.state.capacity
    }

    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.state
            .queue
            .lock()
            .map(|q| q.len())
            .unwrap_or(0)
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.len() == 0
    }

    pub fn is_closed(&self) -> bool {
        self.state.closed.load(Ordering::Acquire)
    }

    pub fn blocked_senders(&self) -> usize {
        self.state.blocked_senders.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
    }

    pub fn blocked_receivers(&self) -> usize {
        self.state.blocked_receivers.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
    }

    /// Send an item, blocking while the buffer is full. Returns the
    /// item back in `Err` if the channel was closed first.
    pub fn send(&self, item: T) -> Result<(), SendError<T>> {
        let mut queue = self
            .state
            .queue
            .lock()
            .unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner());
        loop {
            if self.state.closed.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
                return Err(SendError(item));
            }
            if queue.len() < self.state.capacity {
                queue.push_back(item);
                // Wake one consumer: one new item, one consumer.
                self.state.not_empty.notify_one();
                return Ok(());
            }
            // Buffer full: wait for room. Count ourselves as blocked so
            // the backpressure demo can observe the throttling.
            self.state.blocked_senders.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
            queue = self
                .state
                .not_full
                .wait(queue)
                .unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner());
            self.state.blocked_senders.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
        }
    }

    /// Try to send without blocking. `Ok(false)` means the buffer is
    /// full right now; the caller may retry, drop the item, or wait.
    pub fn try_send(&self, item: T) -> Result<bool, SendError<T>> {
        if self.state.closed.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
            return Err(SendError(item));
        }
        let mut queue = self
            .state
            .queue
            .lock()
            .unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner());
        if queue.len() < self.state.capacity {
            queue.push_back(item);
            self.state.not_empty.notify_one();
            Ok(true)
        } else {
            Ok(false)
        }
    }

    /// Receive an item, blocking while the buffer is empty. Returns
    /// `Err` once the channel is closed *and* drained.
    pub fn recv(&self) -> Result<T, RecvError> {
        let mut queue = self
            .state
            .queue
            .lock()
            .unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner());
        loop {
            if let Some(item) = queue.pop_front() {
                // We freed a slot: wake one blocked sender.
                self.state.not_full.notify_one();
                return Ok(item);
            }
            if self.state.closed.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
                return Err(RecvError);
            }
            self.state
                .blocked_receivers
                .fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
            queue = self
                .state
                .not_empty
                .wait(queue)
                .unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner());
            self.state
                .blocked_receivers
                .fetch_sub(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
        }
    }

    /// Receive without blocking; `Ok(None)` means empty but still open.
    pub fn try_recv(&self) -> Result<Option<T>, RecvError> {
        let mut queue = self
            .state
            .queue
            .lock()
            .unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner());
        if let Some(item) = queue.pop_front() {
            self.state.not_full.notify_one();
            Ok(Some(item))
        } else if self.state.closed.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
            Err(RecvError)
        } else {
            Ok(None)
        }
    }

    /// Close the channel: no more sends are accepted, blocked senders
    /// are woken and fail, and receivers drain what is buffered and
    /// then see `Err`. Idempotent.
    pub fn close(&self) {
        if self
            .state
            .closed
            .compare_exchange(false, true, Ordering::AcqRel, Ordering::Relaxed)
            .is_ok()
        {
            self.state.not_empty.notify_all();
            self.state.not_full.notify_all();
        }
    }
}

// ===========================================================================
// 2. Per-stage statistics
// ===========================================================================

/// What one pipeline stage reports about itself. All atomics, so the
/// stage threads can update them without a lock and the main thread can
/// read a snapshot at any time.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct StageStats {
    pub name: &'static str,
    pub items_in: AtomicUsize,
    pub items_out: AtomicUsize,
    pub items_dropped: AtomicUsize,
    /// Total time spent doing the stage's real work.
    pub work_nanos: AtomicUsize,
    /// Total time spent blocked waiting for input.
    pub starved_nanos: AtomicUsize,
    /// Total time spent blocked waiting for downstream room.
    pub blocked_nanos: AtomicUsize,
}

impl StageStats {
    pub fn new(name: &'static str) -> Self {
        StageStats {
            name,
            ..StageStats::default()
        }
    }

    pub fn snapshot(&self) -> StageSnapshot {
        StageSnapshot {
            name: self.name,
            items_in: self.items_in.load(Ordering::Relaxed),
            items_out: self.items_out.load(Ordering::Relaxed),
            items_dropped: self.items_dropped.load(Ordering::Relaxed),
            work: Duration::from_nanos(self.work_nanos.load(Ordering::Relaxed) as u64),
            starved: Duration::from_nanos(self.starved_nanos.load(Ordering::Relaxed) as u64),
            blocked: Duration::from_nanos(self.blocked_nanos.load(Ordering::Relaxed) as u64),
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct StageSnapshot {
    pub name: &'static str,
    pub items_in: usize,
    pub items_out: usize,
    pub items_dropped: usize,
    pub work: Duration,
    pub starved: Duration,
    pub blocked: Duration,
}

impl StageSnapshot {
    pub fn avg_work(&self) -> Duration {
        if self.items_in == 0 {
            Duration::ZERO
        } else {
            self.work / self.items_in as u32
        }
    }

    pub fn throughput(&self) -> f64 {
        let secs = self.work.as_secs_f64();
        if secs <= 0.0 {
            0.0
        } else {
            self.items_in as f64 / secs
        }
    }
}

impl std::fmt::Display for StageSnapshot {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        write!(
            f,
            "{:<12} in={:>6} out={:>6} dropped={:>4} work={:>8.3?} avg={:>7.3?} \
             starved={:>8.3?} blocked={:>8.3?} ({:.0} items/s)",
            self.name,
            self.items_in,
            self.items_out,
            self.items_dropped,
            self.work,
            self.avg_work(),
            self.starved,
            self.blocked,
            self.throughput()
        )
    }
}

// ===========================================================================
// 3. The workload: synthetic log lines, parsed and analysed in stages
// ===========================================================================

/// Severity of a log line.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub enum Level {
    Debug,
    Info,
    Warn,
    Error,
}

impl Level {
    pub fn as_str(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Level::Debug => "DEBUG",
            Level::Info => "INFO",
            Level::Warn => "WARN",
            Level::Error => "ERROR",
        }
    }

    pub fn parse(text: &str) -> Option<Level> {
        match text {
            "DEBUG" => Some(Level::Debug),
            "INFO" => Some(Level::Info),
            "WARN" => Some(Level::Warn),
            "ERROR" => Some(Level::Error),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
}

/// One parsed log entry: level, source component, message, and the words
/// of the message already tokenised for the analysis stage.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct LogEntry {
    pub level: Level,
    pub component: String,
    pub message: String,
    pub words: Vec<String>,
}

/// Parse a raw line of the form `LEVEL [component] message...`.
/// Returns `None` for lines that do not match, so the parser stage can
/// count and drop them instead of crashing the pipeline.
pub fn parse_log_line(line: &str) -> Option<LogEntry> {
    let mut parts = line.splitn(3, ' ');
    let level_text = parts.next()?;
    let level = Level::parse(level_text)?;
    let component_part = parts.next()?;
    if !(component_part.starts_with('[') && component_part.ends_with(']')) {
        return None;
    }
    let component = component_part[1..component_part.len() - 1].to_string();
    if component.is_empty() {
        return None;
    }
    let message = parts.next()?.to_string();
    if message.is_empty() {
        return None;
    }
    let words = tokenize(&message);
    Some(LogEntry {
        level,
        component,
        message,
        words,
    })
}

/// Lowercase alphanumeric tokenisation, shared by the parser stage and
/// the word-frequency analysis.
pub fn tokenize(text: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut words = Vec::new();
    let mut current = String::new();
    for ch in text.chars() {
        if ch.is_alphanumeric() {
            current.extend(ch.to_lowercase());
        } else if !current.is_empty() {
            words.push(std::mem::take(&mut current));
        }
    }
    if !current.is_empty() {
        words.push(current);
    }
    words
}

/// Per-entry analysis produced by the analysis stage: the words that
/// matter (longer than two letters, so "a", "to", "of" do not dominate),
/// and a cheap "suspicion" score used by the aggregator.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct AnalysedEntry {
    pub level: Level,
    pub component: String,
    pub keywords: Vec<String>,
    pub word_count: usize,
    /// 0 for ordinary lines; higher when the line looks like trouble.
    pub suspicion: u32,
}

pub fn analyse_entry(entry: &LogEntry) -> AnalysedEntry {
    let keywords: Vec<String> = entry
        .words
        .iter()
        .filter(|word| word.len() > 2)
        .cloned()
        .collect();
    let mut suspicion = 0u32;
    if entry.level == Level::Error {
        suspicion += 10;
    } else if entry.level == Level::Warn {
        suspicion += 4;
    }
    for word in &entry.words {
        match word.as_str() {
            "failed" | "failure" | "timeout" | "panic" | "corrupt" | "denied" | "overflow" => {
                suspicion += 3
            }
            "retry" | "slow" | "deprecated" | "fallback" => suspicion += 1,
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    AnalysedEntry {
        level: entry.level,
        component: entry.component.clone(),
        keywords,
        word_count: entry.words.len(),
        suspicion,
    }
}

/// What the aggregator accumulates over the whole run.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct Aggregation {
    pub total_entries: usize,
    pub by_level: HashMap<Level, usize>,
    pub by_component: HashMap<String, usize>,
    pub keyword_counts: HashMap<String, usize>,
    pub total_words: usize,
    pub suspicious_entries: usize,
    pub max_suspicion: u32,
}

impl Aggregation {
    pub fn add(&mut self, entry: &AnalysedEntry) {
        self.total_entries += 1;
        *self.by_level.entry(entry.level).or_insert(0) += 1;
        *self.by_component.entry(entry.component.clone()).or_insert(0) += 1;
        for keyword in &entry.keywords {
            *self.keyword_counts.entry(keyword.clone()).or_insert(0) += 1;
        }
        self.total_words += entry.word_count;
        if entry.suspicion > 0 {
            self.suspicious_entries += 1;
        }
        self.max_suspicion = self.max_suspicion.max(entry.suspicion);
    }

    pub fn merge(&mut self, other: Aggregation) {
        self.total_entries += other.total_entries;
        for (level, count) in other.by_level {
            *self.by_level.entry(level).or_insert(0) += count;
        }
        for (component, count) in other.by_component {
            *self.by_component.entry(component).or_insert(0) += count;
        }
        for (keyword, count) in other.keyword_counts {
            *self.keyword_counts.entry(keyword).or_insert(0) += count;
        }
        self.total_words += other.total_words;
        self.suspicious_entries += other.suspicious_entries;
        self.max_suspicion = self.max_suspicion.max(other.max_suspicion);
    }
}

// ===========================================================================
// 4. The pipeline
// ===========================================================================

/// Configuration for one pipeline run.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct PipelineConfig {
    /// Lines each producer generates.
    pub lines_per_producer: usize,
    pub producer_count: usize,
    pub parser_count: usize,
    pub analyser_count: usize,
    /// Capacity of the channel between producers and parsers.
    pub raw_capacity: usize,
    /// Capacity of the channel between parsers and analysers.
    pub parsed_capacity: usize,
    /// Capacity of the channel between analysers and the aggregator.
    pub analysed_capacity: usize,
    /// If set, analysers sleep this long per item, simulating a slow
    /// downstream stage so backpressure becomes visible.
    pub analyser_delay: Duration,
}

impl Default for PipelineConfig {
    fn default() -> Self {
        PipelineConfig {
            lines_per_producer: 500,
            producer_count: 3,
            parser_count: 2,
            analyser_count: 2,
            raw_capacity: 32,
            parsed_capacity: 32,
            analysed_capacity: 32,
            analyser_delay: Duration::ZERO,
        }
    }
}

/// The outcome of a pipeline run: the aggregation plus every stage's
/// statistics, so the caller can check both the results and the flow.
pub struct PipelineOutcome {
    pub aggregation: Aggregation,
    pub producer_stats: Arc<StageStats>,
    pub parser_stats: Arc<StageStats>,
    pub analyser_stats: Arc<StageStats>,
    pub aggregator_stats: Arc<StageStats>,
    pub elapsed: Duration,
}

/// Deterministic synthetic log lines. The generator cycles through a
/// fixed set of templates, so the expected counts are computable and
/// the run is reproducible. `seed` shifts each producer's starting
/// point so producers do not all emit identical streams.
pub fn generate_log_lines(producer_index: usize, count: usize) -> Vec<String> {
    const TEMPLATES: &[(&str, &str, &str)] = &[
        ("INFO", "auth", "user login succeeded for session"),
        ("INFO", "cache", "cache hit for key lookup"),
        ("DEBUG", "auth", "token refresh scheduled soon"),
        ("WARN", "cache", "cache miss slow retry fallback"),
        ("ERROR", "db", "connection timeout failed retry"),
        ("INFO", "api", "request completed successfully"),
        ("WARN", "api", "slow response deprecated endpoint"),
        ("ERROR", "auth", "login denied panic overflow"),
        ("INFO", "db", "query completed successfully"),
        ("DEBUG", "cache", "eviction scheduled soon"),
        ("WARN", "db", "query slow fallback retry"),
        ("ERROR", "api", "request failed corrupt payload"),
    ];
    let mut lines = Vec::with_capacity(count);
    for i in 0..count {
        let (level, component, message) = TEMPLATES[(producer_index + i) % TEMPLATES.len()];
        lines.push(format!("{} [{}] {}", level, component, message));
    }
    lines
}

/// Expected aggregation for a run of the generator, computed serially.
/// Used by the demo and the tests as ground truth for the pipeline.
pub fn expected_aggregation(config: &PipelineConfig) -> Aggregation {
    let mut aggregation = Aggregation::default();
    for producer in 0..config.producer_count {
        for line in generate_log_lines(producer, config.lines_per_producer) {
            if let Some(entry) = parse_log_line(&line) {
                aggregation.add(&analyse_entry(&entry));
            }
        }
    }
    aggregation
}

/// Run the whole pipeline and return its outcome.
///
/// Wiring, in order:
///   producers --raw--> parsers --parsed--> analysers --analysed--> aggregator
///
/// Each stage closes its output channel once its workers have all
/// finished, which cascades the shutdown downstream: parsers see the
/// raw channel close and drain, then close the parsed channel, and so
/// on, until the aggregator sees the analysed channel close and exits
/// with the final aggregation.
pub fn run_pipeline(config: PipelineConfig) -> PipelineOutcome {
    let started = Instant::now();
    let raw = BoundedChannel::<String>::new(config.raw_capacity);
    let parsed = BoundedChannel::<LogEntry>::new(config.parsed_capacity);
    let analysed = BoundedChannel::<AnalysedEntry>::new(config.analysed_capacity);

    let producer_stats = Arc::new(StageStats::new("producers"));
    let parser_stats = Arc::new(StageStats::new("parsers"));
    let analyser_stats = Arc::new(StageStats::new("analysers"));
    let aggregator_stats = Arc::new(StageStats::new("aggregator"));

    // -- Producers ---------------------------------------------------------
    let mut producer_handles: Vec<JoinHandle<()>> = Vec::new();
    for index in 0..config.producer_count {
        let raw = raw.clone();
        let stats = Arc::clone(&producer_stats);
        let lines = generate_log_lines(index, config.lines_per_producer);
        producer_handles.push(thread::spawn(move || {
            for line in lines {
                let wait_start = Instant::now();
                match raw.send(line) {
                    Ok(()) => {
                        stats.items_out.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
                        stats
                            .blocked_nanos
                            .fetch_add(wait_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as usize, Ordering::Relaxed);
                    }
                    Err(_) => break, // channel closed early; stop producing
                }
            }
        }));
    }

    // -- Parsers ------------------------------------------------------------
    let mut parser_handles: Vec<JoinHandle<()>> = Vec::new();
    for _ in 0..config.parser_count {
        let raw = raw.clone();
        let parsed = parsed.clone();
        let stats = Arc::clone(&parser_stats);
        parser_handles.push(thread::spawn(move || {
            loop {
                let wait_start = Instant::now();
                let line = match raw.recv() {
                    Ok(line) => line,
                    Err(_) => break, // raw channel closed and drained
                };
                stats
                    .starved_nanos
                    .fetch_add(wait_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as usize, Ordering::Relaxed);
                stats.items_in.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
                let work_start = Instant::now();
                match parse_log_line(&line) {
                    Some(entry) => {
                        stats
                            .work_nanos
                            .fetch_add(work_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as usize, Ordering::Relaxed);
                        let wait_start = Instant::now();
                        if parsed.send(entry).is_ok() {
                            stats.items_out.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
                        } else {
                            break;
                        }
                        stats.blocked_nanos.fetch_add(
                            wait_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as usize,
                            Ordering::Relaxed,
                        );
                    }
                    None => {
                        stats.items_dropped.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
                        stats
                            .work_nanos
                            .fetch_add(work_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as usize, Ordering::Relaxed);
                    }
                }
            }
        }));
    }

    // -- Analysers ----------------------------------------------------------
    let mut analyser_handles: Vec<JoinHandle<()>> = Vec::new();
    for _ in 0..config.analyser_count {
        let parsed = parsed.clone();
        let analysed = analysed.clone();
        let stats = Arc::clone(&analyser_stats);
        let delay = config.analyser_delay;
        analyser_handles.push(thread::spawn(move || {
            loop {
                let wait_start = Instant::now();
                let entry = match parsed.recv() {
                    Ok(entry) => entry,
                    Err(_) => break,
                };
                stats
                    .starved_nanos
                    .fetch_add(wait_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as usize, Ordering::Relaxed);
                stats.items_in.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
                let work_start = Instant::now();
                if !delay.is_zero() {
                    thread::sleep(delay);
                }
                let result = analyse_entry(&entry);
                stats
                    .work_nanos
                    .fetch_add(work_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as usize, Ordering::Relaxed);
                let wait_start = Instant::now();
                if analysed.send(result).is_ok() {
                    stats.items_out.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
                } else {
                    break;
                }
                stats
                    .blocked_nanos
                    .fetch_add(wait_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as usize, Ordering::Relaxed);
            }
        }));
    }

    // -- Aggregator ---------------------------------------------------------
    let analysed_for_agg = analysed.clone();
    let aggregator_stats_for_thread = Arc::clone(&aggregator_stats);
    let aggregator_handle = thread::spawn(move || {
        let mut aggregation = Aggregation::default();
        loop {
            let wait_start = Instant::now();
            let entry = match analysed_for_agg.recv() {
                Ok(entry) => entry,
                Err(_) => break,
            };
            aggregator_stats_for_thread
                .starved_nanos
                .fetch_add(wait_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as usize, Ordering::Relaxed);
            aggregator_stats_for_thread
                .items_in
                .fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
            let work_start = Instant::now();
            aggregation.add(&entry);
            aggregator_stats_for_thread.work_nanos.fetch_add(
                work_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as usize,
                Ordering::Relaxed,
            );
        }
        aggregation
    });

    // -- Coordinated shutdown, stage by stage. ------------------------------
    // Producers finish, then the raw channel closes so parsers drain.
    for handle in producer_handles {
        handle.join().expect("producer thread panicked");
    }
    raw.close();
    for handle in parser_handles {
        handle.join().expect("parser thread panicked");
    }
    parsed.close();
    for handle in analyser_handles {
        handle.join().expect("analyser thread panicked");
    }
    analysed.close();
    let aggregation = aggregator_handle
        .join()
        .expect("aggregator thread panicked");

    PipelineOutcome {
        aggregation,
        producer_stats,
        parser_stats,
        analyser_stats,
        aggregator_stats,
        elapsed: started.elapsed(),
    }
}

// ===========================================================================
// 5. Backpressure demonstration
// ===========================================================================

/// Show backpressure directly: one fast producer, one deliberately slow
/// consumer, and a buffer of 1. The producer cannot finish until the
/// consumer has taken items, so the producer's elapsed time is bounded
/// below by the consumer's pace, and the channel's peak length never
/// exceeds its capacity.
pub struct BackpressureDemo {
    pub items: usize,
    pub producer_elapsed: Duration,
    pub consumer_elapsed: Duration,
    pub max_observed_len: usize,
    pub capacity: usize,
}

pub fn backpressure_demo(items: usize, capacity: usize, consumer_delay: Duration) -> BackpressureDemo {
    let channel = BoundedChannel::<usize>::new(capacity);
    let max_len = Arc::new(AtomicUsize::new(0));

    let producer_channel = channel.clone();
    let producer_max = Arc::clone(&max_len);
    let producer_start = Instant::now();
    let producer = thread::spawn(move || {
        for n in 0..items {
            producer_channel.send(n).unwrap();
            let len = producer_channel.len();
            producer_max.fetch_max(len, Ordering::Relaxed);
        }
        producer_channel.close();
        producer_start.elapsed()
    });

    let consumer_channel = channel.clone();
    let consumer_max = Arc::clone(&max_len);
    let consumer = thread::spawn(move || {
        let start = Instant::now();
        let mut received = 0;
        while let Ok(n) = consumer_channel.recv() {
            assert_eq!(n, received);
            received += 1;
            let len = consumer_channel.len();
            consumer_max.fetch_max(len, Ordering::Relaxed);
            thread::sleep(consumer_delay);
        }
        assert_eq!(received, items);
        start.elapsed()
    });

    let producer_elapsed = producer.join().expect("producer panicked");
    let consumer_elapsed = consumer.join().expect("consumer panicked");
    BackpressureDemo {
        items,
        producer_elapsed,
        consumer_elapsed,
        max_observed_len: max_len.load(Ordering::Relaxed),
        capacity,
    }
}

// ===========================================================================
// 6. Demonstration driver
// ===========================================================================

fn print_stage_stats(outcome: &PipelineOutcome) {
    println!("  stage statistics:");
    println!("    {}", outcome.producer_stats.snapshot());
    println!("    {}", outcome.parser_stats.snapshot());
    println!("    {}", outcome.analyser_stats.snapshot());
    println!("    {}", outcome.aggregator_stats.snapshot());
    println!("  total elapsed: {:?}", outcome.elapsed);
}

fn print_aggregation(aggregation: &Aggregation) {
    println!("  total entries: {}", aggregation.total_entries);
    println!("  total words: {}", aggregation.total_words);
    println!("  suspicious entries: {}", aggregation.suspicious_entries);
    println!("  max suspicion: {}", aggregation.max_suspicion);
    println!("  by level:");
    let mut levels: Vec<_> = aggregation.by_level.iter().collect();
    levels.sort_by_key(|(level, _)| **level);
    for (level, count) in levels {
        println!("    {:<6} {}", level.as_str(), count);
    }
    println!("  by component:");
    let mut components: Vec<_> = aggregation.by_component.iter().collect();
    components.sort_by(|a, b| b.1.cmp(a.1).then_with(|| a.0.cmp(b.0)));
    for (component, count) in components {
        println!("    {:<8} {}", component, count);
    }
    let mut keywords: Vec<_> = aggregation.keyword_counts.iter().collect();
    keywords.sort_by(|a, b| b.1.cmp(a.1).then_with(|| a.0.cmp(b.0)));
    println!("  top keywords:");
    for (keyword, count) in keywords.iter().take(8) {
        println!("    {:<12} {}", keyword, count);
    }
}

fn assert_aggregation_eq(actual: &Aggregation, expected: &Aggregation) {
    assert_eq!(actual.total_entries, expected.total_entries);
    assert_eq!(actual.total_words, expected.total_words);
    assert_eq!(actual.suspicious_entries, expected.suspicious_entries);
    assert_eq!(actual.max_suspicion, expected.max_suspicion);
    assert_eq!(actual.by_level, expected.by_level);
    assert_eq!(actual.by_component, expected.by_component);
    assert_eq!(actual.keyword_counts, expected.keyword_counts);
}

fn main() {
    // -- Demo 1: the full pipeline, checked against a serial run. ---------
    println!("=== Demo 1: full pipeline ===");
    let config = PipelineConfig::default();
    let expected = expected_aggregation(&config);
    let outcome = run_pipeline(config.clone());
    assert_aggregation_eq(&outcome.aggregation, &expected);
    assert_eq!(
        outcome.producer_stats.snapshot().items_out,
        config.producer_count * config.lines_per_producer
    );
    assert_eq!(
        outcome.aggregator_stats.snapshot().items_in,
        expected.total_entries
    );
    assert_eq!(outcome.parser_stats.snapshot().items_dropped, 0);
    print_aggregation(&outcome.aggregation);
    print_stage_stats(&outcome);
    // Ground truth, computed by hand from the template table: 12
    // templates, 3 producers x 500 lines, each producer offset by one.
    assert_eq!(outcome.aggregation.total_entries, 1500);
    assert_eq!(outcome.aggregation.by_level[&Level::Error], 375);
    assert_eq!(outcome.aggregation.by_level[&Level::Info], 500);
    assert_eq!(outcome.aggregation.by_level[&Level::Warn], 375);
    assert_eq!(outcome.aggregation.by_level[&Level::Debug], 250);

    // -- Demo 2: the same pipeline with tiny buffers and a slow stage. ----
    // Backpressure should show up as blocked time on the producers and
    // the parsers, while the results stay identical.
    println!("\n=== Demo 2: tiny buffers + slow analysers (backpressure) ===");
    let slow_config = PipelineConfig {
        lines_per_producer: 200,
        producer_count: 2,
        parser_count: 2,
        analyser_count: 1,
        raw_capacity: 2,
        parsed_capacity: 2,
        analysed_capacity: 2,
        analyser_delay: Duration::from_millis(1),
    };
    let expected = expected_aggregation(&slow_config);
    let outcome = run_pipeline(slow_config.clone());
    assert_aggregation_eq(&outcome.aggregation, &expected);
    print_stage_stats(&outcome);
    assert!(
        outcome.producer_stats.snapshot().blocked > Duration::ZERO
            || outcome.parser_stats.snapshot().blocked > Duration::ZERO,
        "with buffers of 2 and a 1ms analyser delay, some stage must have blocked"
    );
    println!("  backpressure observed: producers/parsers spent time blocked");

    // -- Demo 3: the raw backpressure measurement. -------------------------
    println!("\n=== Demo 3: backpressure, measured directly ===");
    let demo = backpressure_demo(20, 1, Duration::from_millis(10));
    println!(
        "  {} items through a capacity-{} channel, consumer at 10ms/item",
        demo.items, demo.capacity
    );
    println!("  producer elapsed: {:?}", demo.producer_elapsed);
    println!("  consumer elapsed: {:?}", demo.consumer_elapsed);
    println!("  max observed queue length: {}", demo.max_observed_len);
    assert!(demo.max_observed_len <= demo.capacity);
    // The producer cannot outrun the consumer: 20 items at 10ms each
    // means at least ~190ms of producer time (the last item needs no
    // wait after it is sent).
    assert!(
        demo.producer_elapsed >= Duration::from_millis(150),
        "producer finished too fast for a capacity-1 channel: {:?}",
        demo.producer_elapsed
    );
    println!("  producer was throttled to the consumer's pace, as expected");

    // -- Demo 4: the bounded channel on its own. ---------------------------
    println!("\n=== Demo 4: bounded channel semantics ===");
    let channel = BoundedChannel::<i32>::new(2);
    assert_eq!(channel.try_send(1), Ok(true));
    assert_eq!(channel.try_send(2), Ok(true));
    assert_eq!(channel.try_send(3), Ok(false), "buffer is full");
    assert_eq!(channel.try_recv(), Ok(Some(1)));
    assert_eq!(channel.try_send(3), Ok(true));
    assert_eq!(channel.try_recv(), Ok(Some(2)));
    assert_eq!(channel.try_recv(), Ok(Some(3)));
    assert_eq!(channel.try_recv(), Ok(None), "empty but still open");
    channel.close();
    assert_eq!(channel.try_recv(), Err(RecvError));
    assert_eq!(channel.try_send(4), Err(SendError(4)));
    channel.close(); // idempotent
    println!("  try_send/try_recv/close behave as documented");

    // A blocked sender is released by close() and gets its item back.
    let channel = BoundedChannel::<i32>::new(1);
    channel.send(1).unwrap();
    let sender_channel = channel.clone();
    let handle = thread::spawn(move || sender_channel.send(2));
    thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50));
    assert_eq!(channel.blocked_senders(), 1, "sender should be blocked on a full buffer");
    channel.close();
    assert_eq!(handle.join().unwrap(), Err(SendError(2)));
    println!("  blocked sender released by close() with its item returned");

    // -- Demo 5: many producers, many consumers, one channel. --------------
    println!("\n=== Demo 5: fan-in / fan-out ===");
    let channel = BoundedChannel::<usize>::new(8);
    let total = Arc::new(AtomicUsize::new(0));
    let mut consumers = Vec::new();
    for _ in 0..4 {
        let channel = channel.clone();
        let total = Arc::clone(&total);
        consumers.push(thread::spawn(move || {
            while let Ok(n) = channel.recv() {
                total.fetch_add(n, Ordering::Relaxed);
            }
        }));
    }
    let mut producers = Vec::new();
    for producer in 0..4 {
        let channel = channel.clone();
        producers.push(thread::spawn(move || {
            for n in 0..25 {
                channel.send(producer * 25 + n).unwrap();
            }
        }));
    }
    for handle in producers {
        handle.join().unwrap();
    }
    channel.close();
    for handle in consumers {
        handle.join().unwrap();
    }
    // Sum of 0..100.
    assert_eq!(total.load(Ordering::Relaxed), 4950);
    println!("  4 producers x 25 items -> 4 consumers, sum = 4950");

    println!("\nAll producer_consumer demos passed.");
}

// ===========================================================================
// 7. Unit tests
// ===========================================================================

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn channel_send_recv_fifo_order() {
        let channel = BoundedChannel::new(3);
        channel.send(1).unwrap();
        channel.send(2).unwrap();
        channel.send(3).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(channel.recv(), Ok(1));
        assert_eq!(channel.recv(), Ok(2));
        assert_eq!(channel.recv(), Ok(3));
    }

    #[test]
    fn channel_close_drains_then_errors() {
        let channel = BoundedChannel::new(2);
        channel.send(1).unwrap();
        channel.send(2).unwrap();
        channel.close();
        assert_eq!(channel.recv(), Ok(1));
        assert_eq!(channel.recv(), Ok(2));
        assert_eq!(channel.recv(), Err(RecvError));
        assert_eq!(channel.send(3), Err(SendError(3)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn channel_try_send_reports_full() {
        let channel = BoundedChannel::<i32>::new(1);
        assert_eq!(channel.try_send(1), Ok(true));
        assert_eq!(channel.try_send(2), Ok(false));
        assert_eq!(channel.recv(), Ok(1));
        assert_eq!(channel.try_send(2), Ok(true));
    }

    #[test]
    fn channel_blocks_sender_when_full() {
        let channel = BoundedChannel::<i32>::new(1);
        channel.send(1).unwrap();
        let sender_channel = channel.clone();
        let started = Instant::now();
        let handle = thread::spawn(move || {
            sender_channel.send(2).unwrap();
        });
        thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(100));
        assert!(!handle.is_finished(), "sender must block on a full buffer");
        assert_eq!(channel.recv(), Ok(1));
        handle.join().unwrap();
        assert!(started.elapsed() >= Duration::from_millis(90));
        assert_eq!(channel.recv(), Ok(2));
    }

    #[test]
    fn channel_unblocks_receiver_on_close() {
        let channel = BoundedChannel::<i32>::new(1);
        let receiver_channel = channel.clone();
        let handle = thread::spawn(move || receiver_channel.recv());
        thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50));
        assert!(!handle.is_finished(), "receiver must block on an empty buffer");
        channel.close();
        assert_eq!(handle.join().unwrap(), Err(RecvError));
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_log_line_accepts_well_formed_lines() {
        let entry = parse_log_line("ERROR [db] connection timeout failed").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(entry.level, Level::Error);
        assert_eq!(entry.component, "db");
        assert_eq!(entry.words, vec!["connection", "timeout", "failed"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn parse_log_line_rejects_malformed_lines() {
        assert!(parse_log_line("INFO db no brackets").is_none());
        assert!(parse_log_line("NOPE [db] message").is_none());
        assert!(parse_log_line("INFO [] empty component").is_none());
        assert!(parse_log_line("INFO [db]").is_none());
        assert!(parse_log_line("").is_none());
    }

    #[test]
    fn analyse_entry_scores_suspicion() {
        let entry = parse_log_line("ERROR [api] request failed timeout panic").unwrap();
        let analysed = analyse_entry(&entry);
        assert_eq!(analysed.suspicion, 10 + 3 + 3 + 3);
        assert!(analysed.keywords.contains(&"request".to_string()));
        let calm = parse_log_line("INFO [api] request completed successfully").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(analyse_entry(&calm).suspicion, 0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn pipeline_matches_serial_ground_truth() {
        let config = PipelineConfig {
            lines_per_producer: 100,
            producer_count: 2,
            parser_count: 2,
            analyser_count: 2,
            raw_capacity: 4,
            parsed_capacity: 4,
            analysed_capacity: 4,
            analyser_delay: Duration::ZERO,
        };
        let expected = expected_aggregation(&config);
        let outcome = run_pipeline(config);
        assert_aggregation_eq(&outcome.aggregation, &expected);
        assert_eq!(outcome.aggregation.total_entries, 200);
    }

    #[test]
    fn pipeline_with_single_thread_per_stage() {
        let config = PipelineConfig {
            lines_per_producer: 50,
            producer_count: 1,
            parser_count: 1,
            analyser_count: 1,
            raw_capacity: 1,
            parsed_capacity: 1,
            analysed_capacity: 1,
            analyser_delay: Duration::ZERO,
        };
        let expected = expected_aggregation(&config);
        let outcome = run_pipeline(config);
        assert_aggregation_eq(&outcome.aggregation, &expected);
    }

    #[test]
    fn backpressure_demo_never_exceeds_capacity() {
        let demo = backpressure_demo(10, 2, Duration::from_millis(5));
        assert!(demo.max_observed_len <= 2);
        assert!(demo.producer_elapsed >= Duration::from_millis(30));
    }

    #[test]
    fn aggregation_merge_combines_counts() {
        let mut a = Aggregation::default();
        let mut b = Aggregation::default();
        let entry = parse_log_line("ERROR [db] connection failed").unwrap();
        let analysed = analyse_entry(&entry);
        a.add(&analysed);
        b.add(&analysed);
        a.merge(b);
        assert_eq!(a.total_entries, 2);
        assert_eq!(a.by_level[&Level::Error], 2);
        assert_eq!(a.max_suspicion, 13);
    }

    #[test]
    fn tokenize_lowercases_and_strips_punctuation() {
        assert_eq!(
            tokenize("Hello, WORLD! 123 go."),
            vec!["hello", "world", "123", "go"]
        );
        assert!(tokenize("!!!").is_empty());
    }
}

--- SOURCE: rust_samples/regex_engine.rs ---
// regex_engine.rs — A small regular-expression engine written from scratch.
// Syntax supported:
//   .            any character except '\n'
//   * + ?        greedy quantifiers, with lazy forms *? +? ??
//   {n} {n,} {n,m}  counted repetition (also lazy with a trailing ?)
//   [abc] [a-z] [^a-z]  character classes, ranges, escapes inside classes
//   ^ $          start / end anchors    \b \B  word boundary / non-boundary
//   |            alternation            ( )  capturing groups
//   (?: )        non-capturing groups   \d \D \w \W \s \S  shorthands
//   \n \t \r \\ \. \* ...  escapes for literal characters
// Matching is a priority-ordered backtracking search over the parsed AST,
// so greedy quantifiers prefer the longest match, lazy ones the shortest,
// and alternation prefers its left branch — the usual regex semantics.
// Captures record (start, end) byte... character-index ranges for each group.
// Standard library only. main() asserts every behaviour it demonstrates.

use std::fmt;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// AST
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum Ast {
    Empty,
    Char(char),
    AnyChar, // '.' — any char except '\n'
    Class(CharClass),
    Start, // ^
    End,   // $
    WordBoundary,
    NotWordBoundary,
    Concat(Vec<Ast>),
    Alt(Vec<Ast>),
    Group { index: Option<usize>, node: Box<Ast> },
    BackRef(usize), // \1..\9 — matches the text captured by that group
    Repeat { node: Box<Ast>, min: u32, max: Option<u32>, greedy: bool },
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct CharClass {
    pub negated: bool,
    pub ranges: Vec<(char, char)>, // inclusive ranges, kept sorted/merged
}

impl CharClass {
    pub fn new(negated: bool, mut ranges: Vec<(char, char)>) -> Self {
        ranges.sort();
        // Merge overlapping / adjacent ranges.
        let mut merged: Vec<(char, char)> = Vec::new();
        for (lo, hi) in ranges {
            if let Some(last) = merged.last_mut() {
                if (lo as u32) <= (last.1 as u32) + 1 {
                    if hi > last.1 {
                        last.1 = hi;
                    }
                    continue;
                }
            }
            merged.push((lo, hi));
        }
        Self { negated, ranges: merged }
    }
    pub fn contains(&self, c: char) -> bool {
        let inside = self.ranges.iter().any(|&(lo, hi)| c >= lo && c <= hi);
        inside != self.negated
    }
    pub fn single(c: char) -> Self {
        Self::new(false, vec![(c, c)])
    }
    pub fn digit() -> Self {
        Self::new(false, vec![('0', '9')])
    }
    pub fn not_digit() -> Self {
        Self::new(true, vec![('0', '9')])
    }
    pub fn word() -> Self {
        Self::new(false, vec![('0', '9'), ('A', 'Z'), ('_', '_'), ('a', 'z')])
    }
    pub fn not_word() -> Self {
        Self::new(true, vec![('0', '9'), ('A', 'Z'), ('_', '_'), ('a', 'z')])
    }
    pub fn space() -> Self {
        Self::new(false, vec![('\t', '\r'), (' ', ' ')])
    }
    pub fn not_space() -> Self {
        Self::new(true, vec![('\t', '\r'), (' ', ' ')])
    }
}

fn is_word_char(c: char) -> bool {
    c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_'
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Parser
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct ParseError {
    pub message: String,
    pub position: usize,
}

impl fmt::Display for ParseError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "regex parse error at {}: {}", self.position, self.message)
    }
}
impl std::error::Error for ParseError {}

pub struct Parser {
    chars: Vec<char>,
    pos: usize,
    groups: usize,
}

impl Parser {
    pub fn new(pattern: &str) -> Self {
        Self { chars: pattern.chars().collect(), pos: 0, groups: 0 }
    }
    fn peek(&self) -> Option<char> {
        self.chars.get(self.pos).copied()
    }
    fn peek_at(&self, off: usize) -> Option<char> {
        self.chars.get(self.pos + off).copied()
    }
    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<char> {
        let c = self.peek();
        if c.is_some() {
            self.pos += 1;
        }
        c
    }
    fn expect(&mut self, c: char) -> Result<(), ParseError> {
        if self.next() == Some(c) {
            Ok(())
        } else {
            Err(self.err(format!("expected '{}'", c)))
        }
    }
    fn err(&self, msg: impl Into<String>) -> ParseError {
        ParseError { message: msg.into(), position: self.pos }
    }
    fn eat(&mut self, c: char) -> bool {
        if self.peek() == Some(c) {
            self.pos += 1;
            true
        } else {
            false
        }
    }

    pub fn parse(&mut self) -> Result<(Ast, usize), ParseError> {
        let ast = self.parse_alt()?;
        if self.pos != self.chars.len() {
            return Err(self.err("unexpected trailing characters"));
        }
        Ok((ast, self.groups))
    }

    fn parse_alt(&mut self) -> Result<Ast, ParseError> {
        let mut branches = vec![self.parse_concat()?];
        while self.eat('|') {
            branches.push(self.parse_concat()?);
        }
        if branches.len() == 1 {
            Ok(branches.pop().unwrap())
        } else {
            Ok(Ast::Alt(branches))
        }
    }

    fn parse_concat(&mut self) -> Result<Ast, ParseError> {
        let mut items = Vec::new();
        while let Some(c) = self.peek() {
            if c == '|' || c == ')' {
                break;
            }
            items.push(self.parse_repeat()?);
        }
        match items.len() {
            0 => Ok(Ast::Empty),
            1 => Ok(items.pop().unwrap()),
            _ => Ok(Ast::Concat(items)),
        }
    }

    fn parse_repeat(&mut self) -> Result<Ast, ParseError> {
        let atom = self.parse_atom()?;
        let mut node = atom;
        // Only one quantifier applies to an atom (a** is rejected).
        let (min, max) = match self.peek() {
            Some('*') => {
                self.pos += 1;
                (0, None)
            }
            Some('+') => {
                self.pos += 1;
                (1, None)
            }
            Some('?') => {
                self.pos += 1;
                (0, Some(1))
            }
            Some('{') => self.parse_counted()?,
            _ => return Ok(node),
        };
        let greedy = !self.eat('?');
        node = Ast::Repeat { node: Box::new(node), min, max, greedy };
        // A second quantifier is an error rather than silently accepted.
        if matches!(self.peek(), Some('*') | Some('+') | Some('?') | Some('{')) {
            return Err(self.err("multiple quantifiers on one atom"));
        }
        Ok(node)
    }

    fn parse_counted(&mut self) -> Result<(u32, Option<u32>), ParseError> {
        // Consumes '{n}', '{n,}', '{n,m}'. On failure the '{' is literal:
        // callers only reach here when peek() == '{', so rewind on error.
        let start = self.pos;
        self.pos += 1; // '{'
        let mut digits = String::new();
        while let Some(c) = self.peek() {
            if c.is_ascii_digit() {
                digits.push(c);
                self.pos += 1;
            } else {
                break;
            }
        }
        if digits.is_empty() {
            self.pos = start;
            return Err(self.err("expected a number in counted repetition"));
        }
        let min: u32 = digits.parse().map_err(|_| self.err("repetition count too large"))?;
        if self.eat('}') {
            return Ok((min, Some(min)));
        }
        if !self.eat(',') {
            self.pos = start;
            return Err(self.err("expected ',' or '}' in counted repetition"));
        }
        let mut digits2 = String::new();
        while let Some(c) = self.peek() {
            if c.is_ascii_digit() {
                digits2.push(c);
                self.pos += 1;
            } else {
                break;
            }
        }
        if !self.eat('}') {
            self.pos = start;
            return Err(self.err("expected '}' in counted repetition"));
        }
        if digits2.is_empty() {
            return Ok((min, None));
        }
        let max: u32 = digits2.parse().map_err(|_| self.err("repetition count too large"))?;
        if max < min {
            return Err(self.err("repetition {n,m} with m < n"));
        }
        Ok((min, Some(max)))
    }

    fn parse_atom(&mut self) -> Result<Ast, ParseError> {
        match self.next() {
            None => Err(self.err("unexpected end of pattern")),
            Some('(') => {
                // (?:...) non-capturing, (?P<name>...) is not supported.
                let mut index = None;
                if self.peek() == Some('?') {
                    self.pos += 1;
                    if self.eat(':') {
                        // non-capturing
                    } else if self.peek() == Some('P') && self.peek_at(1) == Some('<') {
                        return Err(self.err("named groups (?P<name>...) are not supported"));
                    } else {
                        return Err(self.err("unsupported (?...) group syntax"));
                    }
                } else {
                    self.groups += 1;
                    index = Some(self.groups);
                }
                let inner = self.parse_alt()?;
                self.expect(')')?;
                Ok(Ast::Group { index, node: Box::new(inner) })
            }
            Some('[') => Ok(Ast::Class(self.parse_class()?)),
            Some('.') => Ok(Ast::AnyChar),
            Some('^') => Ok(Ast::Start),
            Some('$') => Ok(Ast::End),
            Some('\\') => self.parse_escape(),
            Some(c) => Ok(Ast::Char(c)),
        }
    }

    fn parse_escape(&mut self) -> Result<Ast, ParseError> {
        match self.next() {
            None => Err(self.err("pattern ends with a backslash")),
            Some('d') => Ok(Ast::Class(CharClass::digit())),
            Some('D') => Ok(Ast::Class(CharClass::not_digit())),
            Some('w') => Ok(Ast::Class(CharClass::word())),
            Some('W') => Ok(Ast::Class(CharClass::not_word())),
            Some('s') => Ok(Ast::Class(CharClass::space())),
            Some('S') => Ok(Ast::Class(CharClass::not_space())),
            Some('b') => Ok(Ast::WordBoundary),
            Some('B') => Ok(Ast::NotWordBoundary),
            Some('n') => Ok(Ast::Char('\n')),
            Some('t') => Ok(Ast::Char('\t')),
            Some('r') => Ok(Ast::Char('\r')),
            Some('f') => Ok(Ast::Char('\u{0C}')),
            Some('v') => Ok(Ast::Char('\u{0B}')),
            Some('0') => Ok(Ast::Char('\0')),
            Some(c) if ('1'..='9').contains(&c) => {
                Ok(Ast::BackRef(c.to_digit(10).unwrap() as usize))
            }
            Some(c) if c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() => {
                Err(self.err(format!("unsupported escape \\{}", c)))
            }
            Some(c) => Ok(Ast::Char(c)), // \. \* \\ \/ etc: literal char
        }
    }

    fn parse_class(&mut self) -> Result<CharClass, ParseError> {
        // self.pos is just past '['.
        let negated = self.eat('^');
        let mut ranges: Vec<(char, char)> = Vec::new();
        // A ']' as the very first character is a literal member.
        let mut first = true;
        loop {
            let c = match self.next() {
                None => return Err(self.err("unterminated character class")),
                Some(c) => c,
            };
            if c == ']' && !first {
                break;
            }
            first = false;
            // Read one class item (a char, or a shorthand class).
            let mut items: Vec<(char, char)> = Vec::new();
            if c == '\\' {
                match self.next() {
                    None => return Err(self.err("class ends with a backslash")),
                    Some('d') => items.push(('0', '9')),
                    Some('w') => {
                        items.extend([('0', '9'), ('A', 'Z'), ('_', '_'), ('a', 'z')]);
                    }
                    Some('s') => items.extend([('\t', '\r'), (' ', ' ')]),
                    Some('n') => items.push(('\n', '\n')),
                    Some('t') => items.push(('\t', '\t')),
                    Some('r') => items.push(('\r', '\r')),
                    Some('D') | Some('W') | Some('S') => {
                        return Err(self.err("negated shorthands are not allowed inside a class"));
                    }
                    Some(other) => items.push((other, other)),
                }
            } else {
                items.push((c, c));
            }
            // A '-' followed by something other than ']' forms a range.
            if self.peek() == Some('-') && self.peek_at(1).is_some() && self.peek_at(1) != Some(']') {
                self.pos += 1; // consume '-'
                let hi = match self.next() {
                    None => return Err(self.err("unterminated character class")),
                    Some('\\') => match self.next() {
                        Some('n') => '\n',
                        Some('t') => '\t',
                        Some('r') => '\r',
                        Some(other) => other,
                        None => return Err(self.err("class ends with a backslash")),
                    },
                    Some(other) => other,
                };
                // Only single-char items can be a range start.
                if items.len() == 1 && items[0].0 == items[0].1 {
                    let lo = items[0].0;
                    if hi < lo {
                        return Err(self.err("character class range is reversed"));
                    }
                    ranges.push((lo, hi));
                } else {
                    ranges.extend(items);
                    ranges.push(('-', '-'));
                    ranges.push((hi, hi));
                }
            } else {
                ranges.extend(items);
            }
        }
        Ok(CharClass::new(negated, ranges))
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Backtracking matcher
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Captures are character-index ranges: slot 2*i is group i's start,
// slot 2*i+1 is its end. Group 0 is the whole match.
pub type Captures = Vec<Option<(usize, usize)>>;

pub struct Matcher<'a> {
    text: &'a [char],
    /// Safety valve against pathological patterns (e.g. (a+)+$ on a long
    /// string of a's). Each recursive expansion consumes one unit of fuel;
    /// running out makes the match fail rather than hang forever.
    fuel: u64,
}

impl<'a> Matcher<'a> {
    pub fn new(text: &'a [char]) -> Self {
        Self { text, fuel: 2_000_000 }
    }
    fn take_fuel(&mut self) -> bool {
        if self.fuel == 0 {
            return false;
        }
        self.fuel -= 1;
        true
    }
